**Windows, nesting and barrier height.** The sub-items used for a single $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ must all correspond to the same declared observation window and the same accounting scope, and be allocated mutually exclusively by an explicit rule. $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ is not an extra cost parallel to the complete $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$; the very premise of the three-way decomposition is that $E_{\mathrm{struct}}=E_{\mathrm{barrier}}+E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ and that the three sub-items cover the same scope without overlapping, so that $E_{\mathrm{eff}} = E_{\mathrm{barrier}}+E_{\mathrm{survival}}+E_{\mathrm{causal}}$. $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ is a structural cost amortized by the model, not the height of a microscopic transition barrier (the latter is recorded as $\Delta U_{\mathrm{barrier}}$). If a one-time construction or writing cost is to be included in the viability potential, it must be declared whether it is counted in full, amortized over lifetime, or reported separately as a historical cost only. If structural maintenance and causal processing share one energy consumption, it must be apportioned by a verifiable functional or incremental-cost protocol; where it cannot be apportioned, it should be kept as a merged cost and the numerical computation of the corresponding sub-item or of $V$ suspended, rather than counting it in full on both sides. $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ must be an energy integral over the window and not a power. In the passive phase, $E_{\mathrm{eff}}=E_{\mathrm{struct}}=E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ holds only when $E_{\mathrm{causal}}=0$ and the structural-cost convention is consistent.

**Comparison rule for $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$.** $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ is the barrier cost of maintaining structural order and resisting the flattening by entropy increase. With $I$ and $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ equal, the lower $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ is, the higher $V$ is; if a change of barrier also changes $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$, it is a trade-off between different $(I,T,E)$ combinations and not a general law that "raising the barrier raises $V$".

**Measurement boundary of $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ and its social extension.** The basic definition is the actual causal-processing energy consumption attributed to the specified holder within the observation window; a concrete model may split it by operation type into $E_{\mathrm{compute}}+E_{\mathrm{refresh}}$, and must ensure that the sub-items do not overlap and cover the same window and holder boundary. If one physical system bears structural maintenance and causal processing at once, the whole metabolism or running energy consumption must not be counted in full in both $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$; it should be apportioned by operation-level measurement or an explicit incremental-cost protocol, and where it cannot be reliably separated the decomposition is marked unidentifiable. $E_{\mathrm{decode}}$ is currently only a functional label; its relation to the quantifiable sub-items of $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ has not been established, and it may overlap with the compute and refresh categories; without an operational definition, a common window, a holder boundary and a de-duplication rule it must not be added again. Non-energy expenditures such as purchase and storage are not themselves energy consumption and serve only as qualification evidence. For social and economic systems composed of populations, the activity energy consumption that a population of holders spends on a target output (labor, organizational operation) may, within a declared composite accounting scope, be counted into $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ by operation type, with the holder scope, common window and removal of duplicates declared at the same time; where these are undeclared, labor dissipation, armaments and organizational internal friction serve only as qualitative illustrations.

**Degeneration for steady-state dissipative structures.** When a steady-state dissipative structure is maintained at mean power $\dot E_{\mathrm{avg}}$, the structural energy cost within the window can be estimated as $E_{\mathrm{struct}} \approx E_{\mathrm{barrier}} + \dot E_{\mathrm{avg}}\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}$, and so

$$
V_{\mathrm{s}} = \frac{I\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}}{E_{\mathrm{barrier}} + \dot E_{\mathrm{avg}}\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}} \;\xrightarrow{\;E_{\mathrm{barrier}} \to 0\;}\; \frac{I}{\dot E_{\mathrm{avg}}}
$$

For this class of structure $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ cancels out and $V$ degenerates to the ratio of information to power (a fuel-limited star is a special case: $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ equals the fuel reserve divided by $\dot E_{\mathrm{avg}}$), and the independent role of $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ shows mainly in barrier-type structures and storage refresh. If $E \propto T$ in data of the same kind, a power-law regression can identify only $\beta-\gamma$.

### 2.3 The $T$ Family (the Time Family)

The $T$ family quantifies the various time scales involved in a persistent structure; the basic unit is $\mathrm{s}$.

The time quantities are defined as follows: $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ is the time during which the structure stays identifiable within the observation window; $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}$ is the model-estimated upper bound on lifetime (this is the notation always used for the "extreme persistence upper limit"); $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ is the total length of time during which the target effect is identifiable, produces a response, and the holder pays a measurable cost; $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is the endogenous time horizon; $T_{\mathrm{min}}$ is the minimum physical or computational temporal granularity; $T_{\mathrm{env}}$ is the environmental characteristic time; $t_{\mathrm{refresh}}$ is the storage refresh period; all units and the summary are in Appendix A.

Ambient temperature is recorded as $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ (unit $\mathrm{K}$), strictly distinguished from the time scale $T_{\mathrm{env}}$ (unit $\mathrm{s}$). $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ without superscripts denote the measured structural cost and persistence time under a given protocol and observation window; theoretical lower and upper bounds are written $E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}$ and $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}$, and when used for potential estimation the superscripted bound quantities must be used explicitly; estimates of lower and upper bounds must not be compared directly as if they were measured values.

**Rule for time symbols.** $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ is an objective quantity and uses $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ (structural channel) and $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ (causal channel); $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ and $V^{\mathrm{val}}$ are predictive quantities, and the cognitive channel uses $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$. **$T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ describes how long the actual effect lasts, and $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ describes how far the system can see; the two are not interchangeable.**

**The physical status of $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$.** In classical mechanics time $t$ is an exogenous coordinate parameter; in SRVD's active phase, $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is a state variable internal to the system, which can be extended by cognitive planning and can also collapse to $T_{\mathrm{min}}$ under myopic pressure. In living systems it forms a continuous spectrum: bacteria approach $T_{\mathrm{min}}$ ($T_{\mathrm{pred}}\to 0^{+}$), plants can predict days to months ($10^{0}\sim 10^{2}$ days), and humans and higher-order cognitive systems can predict years to decades ($\gg 1$ year). All living systems with autonomous metabolism and a genetic template have $T_{\mathrm{pred}} > T_{\mathrm{min}}$, but minimal-cognition life clings to the lower limit.

**Time-horizon constraint:**

$$
T_{\mathrm{min}} < T_{\mathrm{pred}} \le \min(T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max},\, T_{\mathrm{env}}) \tag{I-2.2}
$$

The endogenous time horizon cannot exceed the objective physical lifetime of the structure, nor the characteristic time scale of the environment. When a system sets $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ beyond this upper bound, a decoupling deviation $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ arises.

![Figure Ⅰ-4. Endogenous Time Horizon: Upper Bound and Two-Dimensional Characterization](figures/release/fig_i4_endogenous_time_horizon.pdf)

**Figure Ⅰ-4. Endogenous Time Horizon: Upper Bound and Two-Dimensional Characterization.** The endogenous time horizon is constrained by $T_{\min} < T_{\mathrm{pred}} \le \min(T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}, T_{\mathrm{env}})$; the horizon length $L_{\mathrm{pred}}$ and the time resolution $G_{\mathrm{pred}}$ are two orthogonal components — the horizon can be simultaneously long and fine, or short and coarse. $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is not the classical time $t$ and is not interchangeable with $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$. Conceptual figure, no numerical values.

### 2.4 The $V$ Family (the Viability-Potential Family)

The $V$ family quantifies the viability efficiency, self-assessment and behavioral drive of a persistent structure; the basic unit is $\mathrm{bit \cdot s / J}$.

The definitions and formulas of the viability-potential quantities: $V$ is the viability potential (the formal notation, with the low-order approximation $V\approx I\cdot T/E$); $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}+V_{\mathrm{c}}$ is the bookkeeping decomposition of the objective viability potential (the aggregation operator is provisional); $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ is the internal simulated value generated by the system through $D_{\mathrm{cog}}$ (a self-modeling special case with parameters omitted, a two-term mirror isomorphic to the objective formula); $V^{\mathrm{val}}=w_{\mathrm{s}}V_{\mathrm{s}}^{\mathrm{virt}}+w_{\mathrm{c}}V_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{virt}}$ is the behavioral goal actually optimized, with $w_{\mathrm{s}}+w_{\mathrm{c}}=1$ the survival weight and the development weight respectively; $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}=\lvert V^{\mathrm{virt}}-V_{\mathrm{obj}}\rvert$ is the decoupling deviation, and $\bar V$ is the long-term mean over a population of structures of the same kind; units are summarized in Appendix A.

**Dimensional reading.** When $I$ is measured in bit, $T$ in s and $E$ in J, the dimension of $V$ is $\mathrm{bit \cdot s / J}$, and can be interpreted as the information–time quantity corresponding to a unit of effective thermodynamic cost. The Landauer relation constrains the energy of a specified logically irreversible erasure operation, and does not automatically license eliminating bit from the viability-potential formula. If one instead adopts the convention that "the number of information bits is a dimensionless count", the units of $I$, $V$ and their boundary formulas must be rewritten in step; this book keeps bit as an explicit unit of measurement, so the upper bound of the viability potential must also keep the bit factor. Unless a reference potential is separately defined and $V/V_0$ is reported, $V$ itself must not be called a dimensionless quantity.

### 2.5 Elasticity Exponents and Other Parameters

| Symbol | Unit | Definition | Scope of application |
| :----- | :-------- | :---------------------------- | :--------------- |
| $\alpha$ | Dimensionless | Information elasticity exponent $\partial \ln V / \partial \ln I$ | All PSs |
| $\beta$ | Dimensionless | Time elasticity exponent $\partial \ln V / \partial \ln T$ | All PSs |
| $\gamma$ | Dimensionless | Forward energy elasticity exponent $-\partial \ln V/\partial \ln E_{\mathrm{eff}}$; whether the ratio $\alpha:\beta:\gamma$ is an invariant remains to be verified | All PSs |
| $\chi$ | $\mathrm{J/(bit\cdot s)}$ | Self-referential coupling strength: the recursive coupling coefficient between $I$ and $V$ | Active phase only |
| $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ | $\mathrm{J/(bit\cdot s)}$ | Critical coupling strength of the simplified $I$–$V$ subsystem, beyond which the positive equilibrium point disappears (saddle-node bifurcation) | Only the subsystem of Equation (I-5.12) |
| $\gamma_0$ | $\mathrm{s^{-1}}$ | Baseline depreciation rate (not the same quantity as the energy elasticity exponent $\gamma$) | All PSs |
| $\rho$ | Dimensionless | Competitive pressure index | Multi-subject environments |
| $\rho_I$ | Dimensionless | Cognitive return on investment $\alpha/\gamma$, a different quantity from $\rho$ | Active phase |
| $\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$ | $\mathrm{s^{-1}}$ | Damage rate | When damage is triggered |
| $\delta_{\min}$ | Dimensionless | Minimum effect size pre-registered at the operational layer (not the competitive pressure $\rho$) | Three-layer commitment |
| $L_{\mathrm{lev}}$ | $\mathrm{(bit\cdot s/J)/J}$ | Wisdom lever coefficient $\Delta V / E_{\mathrm{build}}$ | All PSs |
| $\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{e}}$ | $\mathrm{bit/s}$ | Cognitive processing rate $I_{\mathrm{net}}/T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ | Active phase only |
| $\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{e}}$ | $\mathrm{s/bit}$ | Cognitive lag coefficient $T_{\mathrm{pred}}/I_{\mathrm{net}} = 1/\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{e}}$ | Active phase only |
| $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{e}}$ | $\mathrm{bit/J}$ | Causal efficiency coefficient $I_{\mathrm{causal}}/E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, a proportional proxy for $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ at fixed $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ | Active phase only |
| $\alpha_0$ | Dimensionless | Pre-registered significance level for gating on the candidate paradigm-breaking signal | Active-phase gating only |

The reinforcement-learning discount factor is written $\gamma_{\mathrm{RL}}$, strictly distinguished from the energy elasticity exponent $\gamma$; the probability function is written $\Pr$.

**Dynamical parameters** ($k$, $k_I$, $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$, $V_{\mathrm{max}}$, $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$, $\sigma$, $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lr}}$, $k_D$, $k_{\mathrm{s}}$, $\lambda$, $T_{\mathrm{hor}}$, $\psi(\rho)$ and so on) are all model parameters to be calibrated and not measured quantities; their units are listed on the spot at the equation where each first appears and are summarized in Appendix A.

**Remaining symbols** ($r_{\mathrm{v}}$, $g_{\mathrm{net}}$, $\mu_{\mathcal F}$, $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{topo}}$, $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$, $L_{\mathrm{pred}}$, $G_{\mathrm{pred}}$, $E_{\mathrm{topo}}(D)$ and so on) are given their definitions and units on the spot at the equation where each first appears; all symbols and their bookkeeping units are listed together in Appendix A.

### 2.6 Structural Identity Criteria and the type–token Separation

#### 2.6.1 Passive-Phase Identity (for Structures without a Cognitive Decoder)

Identity is preserved if and only if: ① identity is preserved by the identification class of the reference decoder (the substrate may be replaced; continuity of the spatiotemporal boundary in the passive phase is a sufficient condition); ② $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ evolves gradually, with no instantaneous global rearrangement; ③ structural energy is not below the maintenance lower bound, $E_{\mathrm{struct}} \ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}$.

**Identification criterion and detection floor.** The identification criterion of a reader (a decoder or a public reading protocol) contains a detection floor on readings: when the structural reading falls below that floor, the reader cannot identify $P$ as the same structure, and identity ends. The detection floor belongs to the reader's side, and is not the same quantity as the maintenance threshold $I_{\mathrm{min}}$ on the structure's side in the active-phase criteria.

There are two physical mechanisms of structural collapse, which must be strictly distinguished: **destruction by external force** — topological collapse caused by high-energy external input, on the negative-effect information $I^{-}$ path, manifesting as $I_{\mathrm{struct}}\to 0$; **energy starvation** — thermodynamic destabilization caused by $E_{\mathrm{struct}}<E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}$.

**Three kinds of event must not share a single 0.** Destruction by external force and energy starvation are physical failures on the object side; a reading falling below the detection floor is an identification failure on the measurement side. All three may appear as a 0 or a blank in the record, but they mean different things: after external destruction $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ truly goes to zero; under energy starvation $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ may remain positive while what fails is $E_{\mathrm{struct}}\ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}$; below the detection floor the structure may be perfectly intact and merely unreadable under the current protocol.

When reporting, each reading must therefore carry a state (normally obtained / measured as zero / below the detection floor / undefinable under the definition / not obtained), together with a failure cause (external destruction / energy starvation / measurement failure / other / undetermined). State markers are record metadata: they enter no equation, do not enter the computation of $V$, and change none of the definitions of $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$, $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$, and the two persistence times. They prevent only one thing: writing "cannot be read" as "is gone".

#### 2.6.2 Active-Phase Identity (for Structures with a Cognitive Decoder)

On top of satisfying the passive-phase conditions, the following conditions are added. **Failure of any one of the first four declares that the structure no longer preserves identity**; the last, $T_{\mathrm{pred}}>T_{\mathrm{min}}$, is an **active-phase marker and does not belong to the identity conditions** — identity refers only to the persistent structure being identifiable (that is, the four qualification conditions of Volume IV §2.2), and it is kept apart from the "healthy state":

| Condition | Operational indicator | Physical meaning |
| :---------------- | :------------------- | :---------------------- |
| $I_{\mathrm{struct}} \ge I_{\mathrm{min}}$; when the causal channel is enabled, $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ must also not fall below its maintenance threshold | The information content of the declared channels is not below the maintenance threshold | Topological continuity is maintained |
| $E_{\mathrm{eff}} \le E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ | Under the same observation window and accounting boundary, the cumulative effective cost has not exceeded the damage threshold | By the phenomenological model, the damage term has not been triggered; physical mechanism and calibration to be established |
| $T_{\mathrm{pred}} > T_{\mathrm{min}}$ (**active-phase marker, not an identity condition**) | The endogenous time horizon has not collapsed | Has not yet entered the myopic runaway regime; failure does not mean the structure is no longer "the same one", only that it has entered the myopic region of the active phase |
| $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} \le \delta_{\mathrm{tol}}$ | The decoupling deviation lies within a pre-registered tolerance; $\delta_{\mathrm{tol}}$ is set by object domain and given with the report | Not captured by the illusion of the virtual potential |
| $D$ has not undergone fundamental drift | The decoder still extracts the original causal information | Semantic identity is preserved |

The role of the second row of the table is this: $E_{\mathrm{eff}} > E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ does not equal "identity fails at once and $V$ drops to zero at once". It is the switching condition of a dynamical branch; once triggered, the course of $V$ depends on the relative size of the net growth rate and the damage rate, and it may decay to zero or settle at a smaller positive equilibrium. This distinction must be kept when discussing damage dynamics, or a conditional conclusion will be read as an unconditional one.

**Identity judgment is decoder-relative, which is not the same as identity itself being relative.** The last row of the table requires that $D$ has not undergone fundamental drift, which means identity judgment depends on the continuity of the decoder itself. If the same object is read by two qualified decoders, different judgments may result: $D_1$, which takes functional causal organization as its identification basis, judges it continuous; $D_2$, which takes internal module organization as its basis, judges it to have been replaced.

In that case judgments should be reported decoder by decoder, rather than choosing one conclusion for the reader. Family-level aggregation rules — unanimity, majority, at least one — have not been argued for, and none may be assumed by default; the reporting rules for multiple boundaries cannot be transplanted directly into aggregation rules for identity judgment.

What can be established: **identity judgment is decoder-relative; whether the object's ontological identity is decoder-relative is not supported by the existing text.** A divergence of judgments does not prove that the object is, ontologically, both the original and not the original at the same time.

One further interface is consistent with the naming convention of RIF §12.10: that section distinguishes the reading detection floor, the two-object discrimination tolerance, and the structural-side floor $I_{\mathrm{min}}$ of this section. The three belong to two sides and are not interchangeable; "this protocol cannot distinguish two candidates" is not the same as "the two candidates are the same structure".

#### 2.6.3 The type–token Separation

**type**: an inheritable information template (such as a DNA sequence), characterized jointly by the stable parts of $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$; this is a parallel description and does not mean the two can be added directly as numbers. **token**: a concrete individual, characterized jointly by $I_{\mathrm{active}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$; until a semantic mapping is established, this notation is not to be interpreted as a measurable arithmetic sum.

**Evolutionary chain**: rock (no separation) → RNA replication (primitive separation) → multicellular organisms (individual token separated from type) → speciation (type splitting) → digital entities (token and type separation of $I_{\mathrm{net}}$).

**The type–token separation does not remove the recording gap.** The separation of type and token solves "template and instance are not on the same level"; it does not guarantee that the readings of $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$, $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$, and $I_{\mathrm{active}}$ retain the relational organization internal to the template or the instance. Under a fixed measurement protocol, two templates with different relational organization may still give the same I–T–E readings (a conditional illustration, not an existence proof); conversely, if an operationalization of $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ already encodes relational organization completely, the gap does not exist under that operationalization.

This volume therefore registers only a scope statement: **whether relational organization is retained by the readings depends on the operationalization, and is not guaranteed by the type–token distinction here.** This gap does not constitute a reason to add a fourth criterion, and does not enter $V$. Whether it holds depends on the protocol; this volume makes no universal assertion.

The same persistent structure may have multiple physical substrates. As long as these substrates continue to satisfy the identity conditions in structural information, causal information and decoder state, together they constitute one and the same persistent structure; when irreversible informational divergence appears among the substrates, the original persistent structure forks and forms new persistent structures.

***

## Chapter 3 — The Mathematical Structure of the Viability Potential

### 3.1 The Low-Order Approximation (**Proposition**)

$$
V \approx \frac{I \cdot T}{E} \tag{I-3.1}
$$

Here $V, I, T, E$ are all generalized conceptual quantities. This is SRVD's minimal persistence hypothesis, namely setting the elasticity exponents to $\alpha = \beta = \gamma = 1$ in the low-order approximation.

The multiplicative form rests on three structural requirements: (1) **irreplaceability**: $V(I, 0) = 0$, $V(0, T) = 0$; (2) **same-direction contribution**: with the other variables fixed, an increase in either $I$ or $T$ raises $V$; (3) under the specified assumption that the dependence on $I$ and on $T$ be separately linear, continuously differentiable and separable, the product $I\cdot T$ is the natural baseline form. Functions such as $\sqrt{IT}$ also satisfy vanishing at the boundary and monotonicity, but not linearity and separability at the same time. The product form is a chosen reference form, and the power law is its generalization.

These three requirements are themselves assumptions, not theorems deduced from the laws of physics: the "linear" and "separable" of requirement (3) are technical conditions added in choosing the reference form, and without them there are infinitely many functions satisfying vanishing and monotonicity. Equation (I-3.1) therefore has the status of a **chosen baseline**, whose value lies in supplying a dimensional anchor and a comparable starting point, and not in being the unique correct functional form. **Failure condition**: if in the same system $V$'s response to $I$ and $T$ is measured to contain a significant interaction term (for example, the marginal effect of $I$ changes sign as $T$ changes), the product form fails in that system, and a power law with interaction terms or a general functional form should be used instead.

**Division of labor with viability theory.** The viability of the Aubin tradition asks "under given constraints, does an everlastingly feasible trajectory exist", and its answer is a **set** (the viability kernel); the viability potential of this framework is a **scalar reading**, asking "how much effective information and time is maintained per unit of energy cost". The two share the word "feasible" but not a mathematical object: this framework gives no feasible set and does not guarantee that a trajectory maximizing $V$ exists. To connect with viability theory, one would take the constraint region of (I-5.19) as the constraint set and discuss its viability kernel separately — this volume does not do so.

### 3.2 The Objective Viability Potential: Two-Channel Superposition

The objective viability potential superposes two independent channels, physical-substrate maintenance ($V_{\mathrm{s}}$) and cognitive causal deduction ($V_{\mathrm{c}}$):

$$
V_{\mathrm{obj}} =
\begin{cases}
V_{\mathrm{s}} + V_{\mathrm{c}}, & E_{\mathrm{struct}} \ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min} \\[6pt]
\text{undefined (structural-maintenance condition fails)}, & E_{\mathrm{struct}} < E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}
\end{cases} \tag{I-3.2}
$$

The structural viability reserve is $V_{\mathrm{s}} = I_{\mathrm{struct}} T_{\mathrm{struct}} / E_{\mathrm{struct}}$. The causal channel is defined as below, so as to avoid $0/0$ for a passive structure when $I_{\mathrm{causal}}=E_{\mathrm{causal}}=0$:

$$
V_{\mathrm{c}} =
\begin{cases}
\dfrac{I_{\mathrm{causal}} T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}}, & E_{\mathrm{causal}}>0 \\[4pt]
0, & E_{\mathrm{causal}}=0 \land I_{\mathrm{causal}}=0 \\[4pt]
\text{undefined (causal information has a reading but no attributable cost)}, & E_{\mathrm{causal}}=0 \land I_{\mathrm{causal}}>0
\end{cases}
$$

If $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ is undefined, then $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}+V_{\mathrm{c}}$ is also not reported as a single value. Energy accounting is decomposed as

$$
E_{\mathrm{eff}} = E_{\mathrm{struct}} + E_{\mathrm{causal}} \tag{I-3.3}
$$

When the three-way decomposition is adopted, it must additionally hold that $E_{\mathrm{struct}}=E_{\mathrm{barrier}}+E_{\mathrm{survival}}$, and that all sub-items do not overlap within the same observation window and accounting scope.

**Domain of validity and thresholds.** The validity of $V_{\mathrm{s}}$ presupposes $E_{\mathrm{struct}} \ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}$ under the same observation window and structural boundary; $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ below this lower bound means the structural channel has not met the minimum maintenance energy in the definition, and under this model's identity criteria it is judged as failure of the persistence condition. The criterion should directly compare the structural-channel cost with its lower bound; $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the total cost including the causal channel and cannot replace this criterion without explanation; to define an independent lower bound on total cost, a separate variable must be set up and its physical basis given anew. Within the domain of validity of structural maintenance, $E_{\mathrm{eff}} \le E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ means the threshold damage term has not been triggered; if $E_{\mathrm{eff}} > E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$, $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ remains a numerical state variable, and its evolution switches to the damage dynamics of Eq. (I-5.3), rather than $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ itself being replaced by a verbal state. The threshold can be used only when object boundaries, observation windows and energy definitions are compatible and it has been calibrated on instances; the microscopic physical meaning and calibration of $E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ remain open problems. A passive structure has, by convention, $I_{\mathrm{causal}}=E_{\mathrm{causal}}=0$, hence $V_{\mathrm{c}}=0$ and $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}$.

**An enabled causal channel** must have an operationalizable causal effect and an attributable cost; only after a mapping from the actual amount of logically irreversible erasure to causal processing has been established may the conditional relation $E_{\mathrm{causal}} \ge E_{\mathrm{compute}} \ge E_{\mathrm{Landauer}}(I_{\mathrm{erased}})$ be used further. $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ must also declare whether the holder is the system itself or an external host; when the host pays, holder-by-holder reporting is the default, and no direct summation across hosts is made.

**The two-channel decomposition is a model-level aggregation assumption.** The $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}+V_{\mathrm{c}}$ of Eq. (I-3.2) cannot be derived from the energy accounting identity alone. To give a single $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, one must first declare the common accounting scope, the observation window and the attribution rules of the two channels, and make explicit the cost allocation and the removal of overlapping effects; otherwise $V_{\mathrm{s}}$ and the $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ of each holder should be reported separately, without claiming that a unique aggregate value exists. Cross-case numerical comparison additionally requires: the same decoder, or calibration to a common reference, with consistent protocol, environment, observation window and analysis scale; consistent operational definitions of $I$ and $T$ and consistent energy-apportionment rules; and similar energy composition, or independent calibration between different compositions. Only if unmodeled errors do act on all items as the same multiplicative bias can a relative ranking cancel that error, and cancellation must not be assumed; if one case is supported mainly by $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ and the other mainly by $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$, with no calibration, no numerical ranking is made. The low-order formula $V \approx I_{\mathrm{net}} T/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the low-order approximation of the formal notation $V$, and is not equal to the bookkeeping value of Eq. (I-3.2); this difference is not used to decide which of the two replaces the other, for it arises from the undetermined aggregation operator and from mixing an intensive quantity with an extensive one. How much they can differ can be computed: take $E_{\mathrm{struct}}=E_{\mathrm{causal}}=E$, $I_{\mathrm{struct}}=I_{\mathrm{causal}}=I$, and the time of both channels equal to $T$; then (I-3.2) gives $2IT/E$, whereas $I_{\mathrm{net}}T/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ gives $IT/(2E)$, a **difference of a factor of 4**. The root is that the energy-share-weighted average that forms $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is an intensive quantity, while the sum of the two channels' viability potentials is an extensive quantity; the two have the same dimension but different semantics, and cannot be substituted for each other, nor can they automatically repair an inconsistency in information definitions or energy attribution.

**Integral reading.** Define the cumulative viability $Q = \int I_{\mathrm{causal}}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t$ ($\mathrm{bit\cdot s}$). For a near-steady-state system $Q \approx I_{\mathrm{causal}}T$, and the causal channel is written $V_{\mathrm{c}} \approx Q/E_{\mathrm{causal}}$; only when a study explicitly adopts the coarse-grained single-denominator approximation, with structural cost negligible or the energy mapping calibrated, may one go on to write $V \approx Q/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$. It is not an identity transformation of the accounting decomposition (I-3.2).

![Figure Ⅰ-5. The Objective Viability Potential: Two-Channel Accounting](figures/release/fig_i5_objective_potential_dual_channel.pdf)

**Figure Ⅰ-5. The Objective Viability Potential: Two-Channel Accounting.** The structural channel $V_{\mathrm{s}}$ and the causal channel $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ are each booked as $I \cdot T / E$; they aggregate into $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}+V_{\mathrm{c}}$ only when the accounting conditions are met. $E_{\mathrm{struct}} \ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}$ is the structural-maintenance threshold; below it $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ is undefined. $V \approx I \cdot T/E$ is a low-order approximation of $V$; the aggregation operator of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ is provisional. Conceptual figure, no numerical values.

### 3.3 Elasticity Exponents and the Core Ordering Proposition

The power-law form is an empirical approximation at the coarse-grained level, describing the overall response of $V$ to $I, T, E$, and need not be strictly derived from the two-channel superposition; the two-channel decomposition is a finer channel-level description, and the two are not on the same description level. The ordering proposition is stated at the coarse-grained level and is not claimed to hold for each channel.

#### 3.3.1 Definition and Physical Meaning of the Elasticity Exponents

$$
V = V_0 \left(\frac{I}{I_0}\right)^{\alpha} \left(\frac{T}{T_0}\right)^{\beta} \left(\frac{E_{\mathrm{eff}}}{E_0}\right)^{-\gamma} \tag{I-3.4}
$$

$\alpha, \beta, \gamma$ are dimensionless **elasticity exponents**, defined as log–log slopes. Because the quantities in parentheses are all dimensionless ratios, $V_0$ must have the dimension of $V$; with $I$ in bit, $T$ in s and $E$ in J, $V_0$ is in bit·s/J. Only by separately defining $v:=V/V_0$ does one obtain a dimensionless index $v$, which must not be conflated with the dimensional $V$.

$$
\alpha := \left.\frac{\partial \ln V}{\partial \ln I}\right|_{E,T}, \qquad
\beta := \left.\frac{\partial \ln V}{\partial \ln T}\right|_{I,E}, \qquad
\gamma := -\left.\frac{\partial \ln V}{\partial \ln E_{\mathrm{eff}}}\right|_{I,T} \tag{I-3.5}
$$

In these formulas $I$ means $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ by the terminology convention. Because the channel weights $\varphi$ of $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ are taken from energy shares, changing $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ moves $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ at the same time; in estimating $\alpha$ and $\gamma$, channel-by-channel readings must be used or $\varphi$ fixed, or the direct energy-consumption effect and the weight effect will be confounded. The meaning of the elasticity exponents must be given in two layers: under the baseline form the coefficients are identically 1, which is a direct consequence of the definition and not an empirical law; under the general form, the concrete values of $\alpha,\beta,\gamma$ are not universal constants, and what is treated as a theoretical invariant awaiting verification is the ratio ordering $\alpha/\gamma>1>\beta/\gamma$, an ordering that may change with system type, material substrate and energy composition. The currently better analytical anchor is the break-even surface of the cognitive return on investment, $\rho_I=\alpha/\gamma=1$, which defines the boundary of the cognitive-phase interval relative to a specific system type. All content concerning the ordering of the elasticity exponents or differences in temperature-zone strategy is labeled "candidate corollary" or "hypothesis to be verified"; without an independent experimental anchor, no explicit functional form of the elasticity exponents' variation with temperature is given.

The shadow price is the **marginal effective value** of a resource under given constraints; it fluctuates sharply with local survival constraints and must not be confused with an elasticity exponent.

#### 3.3.2 Invariants of the Elasticity Exponents and the Ordering Proposition (**Hypothesis**)

The elasticity exponents are log–log slopes, and merely changing the units of $I,T,E,V$ does not change these slopes: a unit conversion translates the numerator and denominator together. What does change the estimates is the operational definition, the proxy indicator, the domain of observation and the fitted model, not the units. Ratios, like individual elasticity exponents, are invariant under unit conversion; the difference is that ratios may be more stable across systems, and this stability is an empirical question. Whether the ratio $\alpha:\beta:\gamma$ constitutes a cross-domain theoretical invariant remains to be verified, so the ordering proposition is stated in the form of dimensionless ratios:

$$
\frac{\alpha}{\gamma} > 1 > \frac{\beta}{\gamma} \tag{I-3.6}
$$

This is a qualitative conjecture based on the relative scarcity of three classes of resource: information is the scarcest resource in an entropy-increasing universe, energy is abundant but must be actively acquired, and time is given passively and cannot be produced. It has not yet received a first-principles derivation or reliable empirical support, and is a hypothesis to be verified; the concept of elasticity exponents itself is unaffected. In contexts that discuss only ratios, $\alpha/\gamma$ may be abbreviated as $\alpha$ and $\beta/\gamma$ as $\beta$, so that the notation degenerates to $\alpha>1>\beta$; this is only a notational shorthand. The baseline form $\alpha=\beta=\gamma=1$ is a metrological reference point, not a predictive formula; $\gamma\approx 1$ is tested separately as an independent empirical proposition, and its falsification does not affect the ordering proposition.

Taking the total differential of the potential function gives the marginal-return equation:

$$
\frac{\Delta V}{V} \approx \alpha \cdot \frac{\Delta I}{I} + \beta \cdot \frac{\Delta T}{T} - \gamma \cdot \frac{\Delta E_{\mathrm{eff}}}{E_{\mathrm{eff}}} \tag{I-3.7}
$$

**Resource-allocation inference (candidate corollary, valid if the ordering proposition (I-3.6) holds)**: **Note before use** — the baseline $\alpha=\beta=\gamma=1$ makes $\rho_I=\alpha/\gamma=1$, which falls exactly on the active/passive phase boundary and so has **no discriminating power** for phase discrimination; the order relation $\alpha/\gamma>1>\beta/\gamma$ is an assumption and must be estimated separately (Volume II Appendix C.2 makes the same declaration). (1) **Information first** — when the cost of acquiring an information increment is lower than $\alpha/\gamma$ times, invest in information rather than in extending time; (2) **Time stop-loss** — when there is no information increment within the window, continuing to sustain energy consumption is a net loss, and energy consumption must be contracted during stagnation; (3) **Energy red line** — once the information dividend is exhausted and the system has reached its optimal-scale boundary, adding energy directly causes the viability potential to fall.

If an apparent $\alpha/\gamma \le 1$ is measured, first run a pre-registered measurement-validity test: repeated measurements in the same domain agree; $I$, $T$, $E$ are taken from the same window and the same protocol; and the correlation between proxy and target quantity reaches the registered threshold. If the test passes and still $\alpha/\gamma \le 1$ is obtained, it counts as a counterexample to the ordering proposition, and the domain of validity contracts accordingly; only if the test fails may measurement failure be diagnosed. Thresholds are locked before measurement.

#### 3.3.3 Constancy of the Elasticity Exponents

That the ordering is conjectural does not mean that the values are constant, and three possibilities coexist: the **strong hypothesis** (the three exponents take the same values in all systems, with no direct evidence), the **medium hypothesis** (constant within systems of one kind, different across types) and the **weak hypothesis** (only $\alpha/\gamma>1>\beta/\gamma$ is universal, with concrete values varying across systems). What is retained at present is the weak hypothesis: whether this ordering holds depends on four layers of conditions — environmental parameters, material substrate, energy composition and decoder — and it holds as a candidate ordering within the class of environments with room temperature and ample free-energy flux, and may fail in other temperature zones or in environments without an energy source. The constancy of the values of the three exponents can be judged only after a unified measure of informational complexity has been established.

#### 3.3.4 The Current State and Limits of Numerical Precision

The fundamental limit on quantitative comparison at present is that different system types define $I$ in fundamentally different ways, and these cannot at present be unified at the cross-domain scale.

| System type | Natural definition of $I$ | Measurement method | Current status |
| :---------- | :------------------- | :------------- | :--------------- |
| DNA sequences | The minimum code length required to generate that genome | LZMA compression | Measurable, with in-domain consensus |
| Neural networks | The effective dimension or rank of the network parameters | Parameter counts, Fisher rank | Comparable within the same domain |
| Images, art | Visual structural complexity | Visual encoders | No mature definition |
| Social structures | Complexity of social organization | — | Entirely undefined |

The measurement methods in the table are all existing proxies, not the only or final protocol for $I$; the relation between a proxy and $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ itself (for example, compression length is largest for a random sequence) must be calibrated within each domain. Therefore precise relative numerical comparison is possible within a domain, while across domains only qualitative statements are allowed, and side-by-side comparison of the values of $\alpha, \beta, \gamma$ across different system types is explicitly prohibited.

### 3.4 The Virtual Viability Potential

**Definition**: The virtual viability potential is the internal simulated value that a system generates, through its own cognitive decoder $D_{\mathrm{cog}}$, for the viability potential of **any persistent structure $P$** (which may be itself or any other PS) under **any specified decoder $D$**, written $V^{\mathrm{virt}}(P, D)$. It is the general output interface of the system's internal predictive model: the system can equally use it to simulate the viability condition of a competitor, a collaborator, prey or any other PS under some decoder, and this is the computational basis of all behaviors of "anticipating others" — games, predation, social cognition.

$$
V^{\mathrm{virt}}(P, D) = \frac{I_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{virt}}\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{virt}}}{E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{virt}}} + \frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}\,T_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}} \tag{I-3.8}
$$

Variables carrying the superscript $^{\mathrm{virt}}$ are all the system's internal estimates, isomorphic to the objective variables but not equal to them, and all implicitly take $(P, D)$ as parameters; the simulations for different target structures and different reference decoders are each an independent set of variables. The virtual viability potential is the system's **subjective cognitive mirror** of some target $(P,D)$, and is not required to agree with that target's objective viability potential.

**Same-horizon convention.** The causal channel of (I-3.8) is uniformly written $T_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}$, while the $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ of (I-3.2) is measured with $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$, and the two have different meanings; without alignment, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ is not zero even if the prediction is entirely accurate. It is therefore stipulated that $T_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}:=\min(\hat T_{\mathrm{causal}},\,T_{\mathrm{pred}})$ — the system can assess causal persistence only within the window it can see, and the assessed value takes the smaller of the objective estimate and the prediction horizon; the subtraction in $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ is carried out only on this same horizon.

**Self-modeling is a special case of it.** When $P$ is the system itself and $D$ is taken as its own decoder $D_{\mathrm{self}}$, $V^{\mathrm{virt}}(P_{\mathrm{self}}, D_{\mathrm{self}})$ is abbreviated $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$. The decoupling deviation $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ and the myopic runaway regime discussed later both concern this special case: only when $P=P_{\mathrm{self}}$ can $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ be subtracted from the system's own $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ to define a deviation, and thereby drive its own decisions; simulated values for other targets do not enter $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ and serve as inputs to the game and social-cognition modules.

The value of $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ is independent of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$: what the system feeds in when computing it is the internal model's subjective estimate of each parameter, determined by the current cognitive state and not by external thermodynamic measurement. So even while the objective physical parameters deteriorate, $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ can stay high, or even keep inflating, through optimistic estimates. The systematic deviation between $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ and $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ is the mathematical root of cognitive decoupling, self-deception and irrational decision-making.

### 3.5 The Value Viability Potential

$$
V^{\mathrm{val}} = w_{\mathrm{s}} V_{\mathrm{s}}^{\mathrm{virt}} + w_{\mathrm{c}} V_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{virt}}, \qquad w_{\mathrm{s}} + w_{\mathrm{c}} = 1 \tag{I-3.9}
$$

where $V_{\mathrm{s}}^{\mathrm{virt}} = I_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{virt}} T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{virt}} / E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{virt}}$ and $V_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{virt}} = I_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}} T_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{virt}} / E_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}$; $w_{\mathrm{s}}$ and $w_{\mathrm{c}}$ are the value-goal weights, called the survival weight and the development weight respectively.

**Core proposition (framework proposition): what the system actually optimizes is $V^{\mathrm{val}}$.** When $w_{\mathrm{s}}$ and $w_{\mathrm{c}}$ are extremely biased (such as $w_{\mathrm{s}}\to 0$ and $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\gg 0$), the system may shift from "pursuing objective persistence" to "pursuing virtual, self-destructive goals". The complete dynamics of the phase transition in the value-goal weights (including threshold conditions and steady-state analysis) awaits later work. **Failure condition**: if, in some system, behavioral choice can be fully explained by the current gradient of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, and introducing $w_{\mathrm{s}}, w_{\mathrm{c}}$ brings no gain in predictive power, then $V^{\mathrm{val}}$ need not be introduced in that system.

**Symmetric hijacking.** Myopic runaway does not hijack only the $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ channel: $w_{\mathrm{s}}$ and $w_{\mathrm{c}}$ are symmetric, any weight overweighted by a virtual signal may take part in self-hijacking, and the subjective-assessment component of the structural channel, $V_{\mathrm{s}}^{\mathrm{virt}}$, is likewise a potential target of hijacking. "Triple splitting acts only on $V_{\mathrm{c}}$" is a common misreading.

### 3.6 Selection Dynamics: The Viability-Potential Form of the Price Covariance (**Theorem**)

The first three sections gave the static decomposition of $V$; this section adds a dynamical anchor: when a population of structures evolves by replicator selection, the rate of change of the mean viability potential has a closed form. **This equation is a direct application of the covariance selection mathematics of Price (1970)**; its derivation is not repeated here, only its reading under this framework's variables is given, together with the conditions under which it is merely an identity (see the end of this section).

Take a set of structures $i=1,\ldots,N$ whose frequencies $x_i$ satisfy the replicator equation

$$
\dot x_i\ =\ x_i\left(r_i-\bar r\right),\qquad \bar r\ =\ \sum_j x_j r_j \tag{I-3.10}
$$

If the viability potential $V_i$ of each structure is fixed within the observation window, and the replication rate $r_i=r(V_i)$ is strictly increasing in $V$, then the mean viability potential satisfies

$$
\frac{\mathrm{d}\langle V\rangle}{\mathrm{d}t}\ =\ \operatorname{Cov}\!\left(V,\,r(V)\right)\ \ge\ 0,
\qquad \langle V\rangle\equiv\sum_i x_i V_i \tag{I-3.11}
$$

**Premises**: (i) $V_i$ is fixed within the window; (ii) $r$ is a strictly increasing function of $V$; (iii) the population is closed, with no immigration or emigration; (iv) $x_i$ evolves continuously according to (I-3.10).
**Result**: the mean viability potential is monotonically non-decreasing, and its rate of change is exactly the covariance of $V$ and the replication rate.
**Failure conditions**: when $V_i$ varies with frequency (frequency-dependent selection), when there is immigration or emigration, or when $r$ is not monotonic in $V$, (I-3.11) does not hold.

**Proof**: from (I-3.10),

$$
\frac{\mathrm{d}\langle V\rangle}{\mathrm{d}t}=\sum_i V_i\dot x_i
=\sum_i V_i x_i\left(r_i-\bar r\right)
=\langle Vr\rangle-\langle V\rangle\langle r\rangle
=\operatorname{Cov}(V,r).
$$

The nonnegativity of the covariance follows from $r$ increasing in $V$: the covariance of two simultaneously increasing quantities over the same distribution is not negative (in the discrete case, this is Chebyshev's sum inequality).

**What it proves and what it does not.** What it proves is "**if** selection proceeds monotonically in viability potential, **then** the mean viability potential cannot decline" — a result at the level of an identity, not an empirical prediction. It does not guarantee that $r$ really increases with $V$, nor that $V$ is the quantity on which selection actually operates; the latter two are empirical questions, see Chapter 8. Hence this item is labeled **Theorem** only with respect to (I-3.11) itself, and is not extrapolated to any real system.

***

## Chapter 4 — The Decoder Framework

### 4.1 The Principle of Decoder-Centered Description

SRVD does not describe the world directly, but describes the interaction of persistent structures with the world through the decoder $D$; information, prediction, value and measurement are all defined through the decoding process. This is its fundamental difference from most fixed-observer frameworks: a structure not only changes its own state but also changes the way it knows the world.

**The physical-entity status of the decoder.** $D$ is not a mathematical operator or a metaphysical rule of interpretation, but a selective extraction mechanism realized on a concrete substrate and borne by some physical system, such as neural tissue or silicon circuits. Decoding activity at the level of culture, theory or language also depends on a physical substrate, but the maintenance cost of that substrate must be accounted separately under an explicit subject boundary. If, within a specified window, the operations of the decoder have been mapped to $I_{\mathrm{erased}}(D)$ logically irreversible erasures, and the erasure cost uses the same accounting boundary as $E_{\mathrm{eff}}(D)$, a conditional lower bound can be written:

$$
E_{\mathrm{eff}}(D) \ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}(D) + k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}} \ln 2 \cdot I_{\mathrm{erased}}(D) \tag{I-4.1}
$$

Here $E_{\mathrm{struct}}(D)$ and $E_{\mathrm{eff}}(D)$ must adopt the same decoder boundary and observation window; $I_{\mathrm{erased}}(D)$ is the actual amount of logical erasure within that window, and is not the same as the $I_{\mathrm{min}}(D)$ needed to maintain decoding capacity. Without an erasure map, a Landauer cost lower bound cannot be deduced from $I_{\mathrm{min}}(D)$ alone; nor does this equation guarantee that all readout, computation or refresh processes reach that bound.

**Measurement and observation.** All core variables depend on the definition of a concrete decoder. The observer is itself a persistent structure, and every measurement corresponds to information extraction under particular decoding conditions; observational capacity is constrained by energy cost, time resolution and signal-to-noise ratio, and these boundaries are fixed by the physical channel and do not come from any hyper-physical assumption.

### 4.2 Information Polarity

Physical causal interaction is not effective causal information. According to its influence on the topological direction of a system's long-term persistence phase space, information falls into three classes: **positive-effect information $I^{+}$** — inputs that enlarge the system's long-term reachable state space and strengthen structural plasticity; **negative-effect information $I^{-}$** — inputs that damage the structure, cause irreversible entropy increase or compress the state space; **neutral noise $I^{0}$** — inputs that produce physical causation but are not mapped to persistence polarity.

**The relational nature of polarity.** The physical basis of information is an objective topological configuration, but its polarity is determined by its relation to the decoder $D$. The same object can show completely different polarities to different decoders: a book supplies survival knowledge to a reader ($I^{+}$), the same virus causes structural destruction to a host cell ($I^{-}$), and random noise cannot form an effective match with any decoder ($I^{0}$). Causal efficacy is a measure of the degree of match between a topological configuration and the structure of the decoder.

**Measurement-theoretic statement (applies to the active phase).** For a system with $D_{\mathrm{cog}}$, the polarity determination of $I^{+}$ and $I^{-}$ is a dynamic label made in real time within the $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ window on the basis of the predictive gradient of $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ (a forward-looking assessment), not a retrospective classification awaiting the actual unfolding of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$; the system uses this label to drive the allocation of $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$. When $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}>0$, this forward-looking assessment may deviate systematically from objective reality, and this deviation is precisely the fuel of the myopic runaway regime.

**Paradigm levels.** Within $I^{+}$, a further grading is possible by the depth to which the decoder's topology is reconstructed. Three often-confused objects must be separated here; their layering was given in §2.1 (candidate signal $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ / ex post quantity $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ / event $\mathcal{T}$), and only two operational consequences are added here: **only layer one can enter the gate**, because only it is defined and computable before the transition, gating on the ex post quantity is circular, and gating on the event is a tautology; **a gate is not a conclusion**: a proxy reading that crosses the threshold shows only that the existing decoder is markedly failing on that target, the failure may come from noise, drift, instrument fault or the task being unlearnable, or from a genuinely new dimension, and what the gate screens out is only the candidate set "worth investing trial-and-error cost in", whose purpose is to concentrate the limited $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ on a few candidates and to cut losses quickly when they are falsified.

Neither $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ nor the paradigm-interpretive information $I_{\mathrm{interp}}$ is a directly measurable number of bits; the distinction between them takes as its candidate criterion whether a topological transition of the decoder is triggered ($D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ increases): paradigm-breaking information opens an entirely new causal dimension, its marginal efficacy does not decay with replication, and it can forcibly reset the topological distance of a niche; paradigm-interpretive information is high-quality execution or local parameter optimization within the existing paradigm, and its marginal efficacy decays rapidly with homogenization ($\Delta I\to 0$). This distinction is the physical basis for explaining the homogenization crisis of the age of AI.

![Figure Ⅰ-6. Information Polarity Is Determined by the Relation to the Decoder](figures/release/fig_i6_decoder_relative_polarity.pdf)

**Figure Ⅰ-6. Information Polarity Is Determined by the Relation to the Decoder.** The same physical input carries no fixed polarity: after mapping by the decoder $D$ it may be positive-effect information $I^{+}$ (expands the long-term reachable state space), negative-effect information $I^{-}$ (damages the organization's structure), or neutral noise $I^{0}$ (not mapped to viability polarity). Polarity is a dynamic tag relative to a specific $D$, not an intrinsic property of the signal; $I^{+}$ / $I^{-}$ apply only in the active phase. Conceptual figure, no numerical values.

### 4.3 Relation to Existing Frameworks

SRVD is a coarse-grained macroscopic dynamical framework; it does not compete with the established theories below, but maps them as special cases or boundary inputs.

| Framework | Role in SRVD | SRVD's extension and difference |
| :-------------- | :----------------------- | :------------------- |
| The second law of thermodynamics | Core constraint, not violated | Describes structures that locally resist entropy increase, with global entropy increase as the intrinsic driving force |
| I. Prigogine's dissipative-structure theory | Describes the interval dominated by $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ in the active-persistence domain | Adds the $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ and $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ components and goes beyond near-equilibrium states |
| Ulanowicz's theory of ascendency (1997) | $A=T_{\mathrm{ST}}\cdot\mathrm{AMI}$ resembles the numerator of $V$ in form but differs in meaning ($T_{\mathrm{ST}}$ is not a time) | Supplies the denominator $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$, making cross-substrate comparison possible |
| Still et al. (2012) | An information–energy efficiency trade-off on a single-step time scale, isomorphic in form to $V$; does not constitute a premise for deriving the $V$ formula | $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ carries polarity and decoder relativity; its $T$ is folded into a rate, with no independent time dimension |
| The Landauer principle | Supplies the erasure lower bound $E_{\mathrm{compute}}\ge k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2\cdot I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ | Serves only as a basis for $E_{\mathrm{compute}}$ and does not apply to $E_{\mathrm{refresh}}$ |
| The free-energy principle, general relativity, quantum mechanics, Shannon information theory, complex-adaptive-systems theory | Respectively: a local analogy for a single energy channel; a boundary input from gravity and black-hole thermodynamics; the microscopic source of Landauer's principle and the decoherence lower bound; the probabilistic limit after polarity is removed; a qualitative mechanism of rule set–selection–emergence | This volume either takes only their conclusions as boundary inputs (without rewriting the original equations), or distinguishes itself by the decoder relativity and persistence polarity of $I_{\mathrm{net}}$, or uses $V$ to scalarize fitness |

***

## Chapter 5 — Extended Dynamics

### 5.1 The Fivefold Recursion Architecture

The fivefold recursion is the core architecture by which SRVD describes the evolution of active-phase systems: the five variables $I, V, T, E, D$ couple to one another and give a qualitative picture of evolution. Low-order passive structures do not activate this architecture, and their dynamics degenerate to static estimation of the viability potential.

The recursion formulas of this section are a **heuristic discrete model awaiting rigorization**; their value lies in revealing the qualitative direction of the couplings and the existence of phase transitions, and they are not exact functional forms that can be fitted directly to data. The soundest mathematical foundations of this chapter are the ontological mechanism of triple splitting and the dynamics of the decoupling deviation, which have strict ordinary-differential-equation forms and explicit solutions.

**Linearization assumption.** The following assumes that the dominant coupling direction among the variables is one-way ($I\to V\to T\to E\to D$), corresponding to a time-scale separation of "fast driving, slow response". Real systems may contain bidirectional feedback loops; the complete coupled system is an open problem, and what is retained for now is a first-order approximation by discrete recursion, for numerical illustration and qualitative discussion.

The two operators of this chapter have a clear division of labor and must not be mixed: $\mathcal{F}$ is the **recursive unfolding operator**, which adds information within a given topology and is bounded by capacity; $\mathcal{T}$ is the **topological transition operator**, which changes the topology itself, pays $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ once and resets the capacity. Saturation of $\mathcal{F}$ does not mean that $\mathcal{T}$ occurs, which is a common premise of several arguments in this chapter.

![Figure Ⅰ-7. The Fivefold Recursion: Dominant Coupling Chain and Operator Division of Labor](figures/release/fig_i8_fivefold_recursion.pdf)

**Figure Ⅰ-7. The Fivefold Recursion: Dominant Coupling Chain and Operator Division of Labor.** This volume uses a one-way discrete approximation under fast–slow scale separation ($I \to V \to T \to E \to D$); the update of $D$ enters the next round of information extraction. $\mathcal{F}$ (the recursion-expansion operator) adds information within a given topology, while $\mathcal{T}$ (the topological-transition operator) changes the topology itself and pays $E_{\mathrm{build}}$; saturation of $\mathcal{F}$ does not mean that $\mathcal{T}$ occurs. Fully bidirectional coupling remains an open problem. Conceptual figure, no numerical values.

#### 5.1.1 The $I$ Recursion (Self-Amplification of Information)

$$
I_{\mathrm{net},n+1} = I_{\mathrm{bas}} \left(1 + \chi \cdot V_{\mathrm{obj},n}\right) \tag{I-5.1}
$$

$I_{\mathrm{bas}}$ is the system's baseline $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ at $V=0$; $\chi$ is the self-referential coupling strength, with dimension $[V]^{-1}$ (that is, $\mathrm{J/(bit\cdot s)}$), so that $\chi V$ is dimensionless. **The phenomenological status of $\chi$.** $\chi$ is an intrinsic property of the structural features of the decoder $D$ itself and phenomenologically characterizes the strength of the self-referential loop by which "the system uses feedback from its current persistence state to accelerate information extraction"; it is the core parameter that decides which path the system takes after the splitting. A complete microscopic derivation awaits later work; for now it is treated as a phenomenological parameter whose value is inferred back from the system's statistical behavior.

**Preconditions for numerical use.** $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ in the equation is used as a numerical state variable only when the four calibration conditions of §2.1 are met; when they are not met, only the ordinal interpretation is retained, and no quantitative growth rate or capacity is reported on its basis. **Domain of validity.** The linear form holds only in the active persistence domain $V_{\mathrm{obj},n}>0$; when $V_{\mathrm{obj},n}\le 0$ the system has entered the interval of structural degradation or disintegration, and the evolution of $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is jointly determined by dissipative runaway and damage terms. Where continuity and positive-definiteness are needed over the whole domain, the exponential form $I_{\mathrm{net},n+1} = I_{\mathrm{bas}} \exp(\chi V_{\mathrm{obj},n})$ can be used instead, which tends asymptotically to zero without turning negative as $V_{\mathrm{obj},n}\to -\infty$; the linear form is retained as a low-order approximation.

**Unified information-evolution equation.** Eq. (I-5.1) is a discrete map. To be compatible at once with continuous-time decay and discrete topological transitions, this volume writes information evolution as a hybrid dynamical system of **continuous flow plus discrete jumps**:

$$
\begin{aligned}
\frac{\mathrm{d}I_{\mathrm{net}}}{\mathrm{d}t}
&= \underbrace{\mu_{\mathcal F}\, I_{\mathrm{net}}\left(1 - \frac{I_{\mathrm{net}}}{I_{\max}(D)}\right)}_{\text{recursive unfolding }\mathcal F\text{: capacity-limited logistic growth}}\\
&\quad- \underbrace{\frac{I_{\mathrm{net}} - I_{\mathrm{eq}}}{\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}}}_{\text{thermodynamic decay: relaxation toward the thermal background}}
\end{aligned}
\tag{I-5.1a}
$$

$$
\begin{aligned}
&\text{jump map (}\mathcal T\text{ event):}\\
&I_{\mathrm{net}}^{+} = I_{\mathrm{net}}^{-} + \Delta I_{\mathcal{T}},\qquad
\Delta I_{\mathcal{T}} \equiv \kappa_{\mathrm{topo}}\,\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}\ \text{(to be calibrated)},\\
&I_{\max}(D^{+}) > I_{\max}(D^{-})
\end{aligned}
\tag{I-5.1b}
$$

**Division of labor and de-duplication.** The continuous flow (I-5.1a) is responsible only for $\mathcal F$ unfolding and thermal relaxation, and the jump map (I-5.1b) only for the information step and capacity reset caused by a topological transition. The same jump must not be counted in both terms; if an implementation chooses to express the capacity reset with (I-5.1b), it must not write it again as a continuous impulse term in (I-5.1a). Recording one transition both as an "impulse increase" and as a "capacity rise" would make the same $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ yield returns twice, and the valuation would drift with the way of bookkeeping.

**The fixed point without $\mathcal T$.** Setting the right-hand side of (I-5.1a) to zero and taking the positive root gives the unique physical fixed point

$$
I_{\mathrm{net}}^{*}
= \frac{I_{\max}(D)}{2\mu_{\mathcal F}}
\left[\,\left(\mu_{\mathcal F} - \tau_{\mathrm{decay}}^{-1}\right)
+ \sqrt{\left(\mu_{\mathcal F} - \tau_{\mathrm{decay}}^{-1}\right)^{2}
+ \frac{4\,\mu_{\mathcal F}\,I_{\mathrm{eq}}}{\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}\,I_{\max}(D)}}\ \right] \tag{I-5.1c}
$$

It is stable ($f'(I^{*})<0$). Two limiting cases deserve separate mention: when $I_{\mathrm{eq}}=0$ (no thermal-background information), $I^{*} = I_{\max}(D)(1-1/(\mu_{\mathcal F}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}))$, and the necessary and sufficient condition for a positive fixed point to exist is

$$
\mu_{\mathcal F}\,\tau_{\mathrm{decay}} > 1 \tag{I-5.1d}
$$

that is, the gain of recursive unfolding must outrun thermal relaxation, otherwise information decays monotonically to zero, which is a falsifiable quantitative criterion; as $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}\to\infty$ (no thermal relaxation) $I^{*}\to I_{\max}(D)$, degenerating to pure logistic saturation.

**Three-line summary of criterion (I-5.1d)**: (i) premises — logistic growth, constant $\mu_{\mathcal F}$ and $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$, $I_{\mathrm{eq}}=0$, no topological transition; (ii) result — $\mu_{\mathcal F}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}>1$ is the necessary and sufficient condition for the existence of a positive fixed point; at the critical value $\mu_{\mathcal F}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}=1$ the zero fixed point and the positive fixed point exchange stability, a **transcritical bifurcation**, not a saddle-node bifurcation; this volume's real saddle-node bifurcation lies at the $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ of §5.2.3; (iii) failure conditions — if $I_{\mathrm{eq}}>0$ (thermal-background information is injected), a positive fixed point exists even when $\mu_{\mathcal F}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}<1$, and the criterion must use the complete form of (I-5.1c).

**Dynamical reading.** The system's structural persistence state feeds back into the decoder's efficiency of information extraction: the higher the structural stability and redundancy, the stronger the capacity to maintain complex predictive structure, and the efficiency of extracting effective causal information rises accordingly. When $\chi$ is too high, the endogenous virtual viability potential decouples from physical constraints and enters nonlinear inflation, finally triggering the myopic runaway regime.

#### 5.1.2 The $V$ Recursion (Updating the Viability Potential)

The logistic equation of this section is a **reduced model of a single evolutionary channel**, not the general dynamics of the two-channel total $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}+V_{\mathrm{c}}$. Its derivation requires that the structural channel be negligible within the observation window ($V_{\mathrm{s}}\approx0$), so that $V_{\mathrm{obj}}\approx V_{\mathrm{c}}$; at the same time, the information input must satisfy $I_{\mathrm{net}}\approx I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ under the in-domain calibration of §2.1, or the information state of (I-5.1a) cannot be mapped directly to the state of the causal channel; if $V_{\mathrm{s}}$ is nonzero but approximately constant, the equation applies at most to the evolving component after that fixed baseline is deducted, and the total $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ cannot be substituted directly. Where the structural channel is not negligible or both channels change at once, the channel-by-channel accounting form (I-5.20a) should be used instead of this section's logistic trajectory. **$r_{\mathrm{v}}$ takes a constant within the reduced observation window ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$)** and is not a function of the reduced state. It is not a freely assignable empirical parameter: within the range where (I-5.2) serves as the quasi-static image of (I-5.1a), $r_{\mathrm{v}}=\mu_{\mathcal F}$ and $\gamma_0=\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}^{-1}$ (see "The relation between Eq. (I-5.2) and (I-5.1a)" at the end of this section); resource availability and the constraint geometry of the structure enter the model through $\mu_{\mathcal F}$ and $I_{\max}(D)$, and no independent drive rate parallel to them is set separately:

$$
\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{\mathrm{obj}}}{\mathrm{d}t} = V_{\mathrm{obj}} \cdot (r_{\mathrm{v}} - \gamma_0) \cdot \left(1 - \frac{V_{\mathrm{obj}}}{V_{\mathrm{max}}}\right) \tag{I-5.2}
$$

In this model's phenomenological approximation, if $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ under the same accounting boundary and observation window exceeds the calibrated $E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$, an irreversible damage term is triggered; this threshold relation is not a known physical law:

$$
\frac{\mathrm{d}V_{\mathrm{obj}}}{\mathrm{d}t} = V_{\mathrm{obj}} \cdot (r_{\mathrm{v}} - \gamma_0) \cdot \left(1 - \frac{V_{\mathrm{obj}}}{V_{\mathrm{max}}}\right) - \delta_{\mathrm{dam}} \cdot V_{\mathrm{obj}} \cdot \mathbf{1}\left[E_{\mathrm{eff}} > E_{\mathrm{thresh}}\right] \tag{I-5.3}
$$

Eq. (I-3.2) defines the numerical state $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, and Eq. (I-5.3) adds a continuous-time damage term when the threshold is exceeded; the threshold is the switching condition of a dynamical branch and does not replace the state variable with words. If (I-5.3) is applied to an actual trajectory, one must declare in advance whether the accounting window $W$ of $E_{\mathrm{eff}}(t;W)$ is fixed, rolling or cumulative up to time $t$, and state the window-reset rule; where this is not specified it can be used only for static diagnosis of a specified window, and cannot claim to have determined the instant of triggering.

**After triggering, $V$ does not automatically drop to zero.** Writing the threshold indicator function as "once exceeded, $V\to 0$" is wrong: Eq. (I-5.3) still has a growth term, a saturation term and a damage term in the triggered domain, and its own fixed points decide where $V$ goes. The effective net growth rate in the triggered domain is $r_{\mathrm{net}}\equiv r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0-\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$, uniformly written $g_{\mathrm{net}}\equiv (r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0)-\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}\mathbf{1}[E_{\mathrm{eff}}>E_{\mathrm{thresh}}]$, and in the triggered domain $g_{\mathrm{net}}=r_{\mathrm{net}}$. Accordingly three cases must be stated.

**Case (a): $0\le r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0<\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$ (collapse to zero).** In the positive state domain of the reduced model, the effective net growth rate is negative, the "growth minus damage" in the brackets is negative on $0<V\le V_{\mathrm{max}}$, so for every positive state $\mathrm{d}V/\mathrm{d}t<0$ and $V$ decays monotonically to 0. This is the true collapse case: the damage rate overpowers the net growth rate, the structure cannot be saved however large it is, and the initial decay rate is about $\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}-(r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0)$. $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0=\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$ is the critical boundary, where $\mathrm{d}V/\mathrm{d}t = -(r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0)V^{2}/V_{\mathrm{max}}$, and the decay changes from exponential to algebraic ($V\sim V_{\mathrm{max}}/((r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0)t)$), still tending to 0 but with a stretched time scale.

**Case (b): $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0>\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}>0$ (degraded equilibrium).** The effective net growth rate is positive, and in the triggered domain there exists a **smaller positive equilibrium**

$$
V^{*} = V_{\mathrm{max}}\left(1 - \frac{\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}}{r_{\mathrm{v}} - \gamma_0}\right) > 0
$$

and it is stable: differentiating with respect to $V$ at $V^{*}$ gives $-(r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0)V^{*}/V_{\mathrm{max}}<0$. Thus damage triggering does not necessarily knock $V$ down to zero but pushes the system to a lower positive equilibrium: if $V>V^{*}$ at triggering, $V$ falls monotonically to $V^{*}$; if $0<V<V^{*}$ at triggering, $V$ rises monotonically to $V^{*}$. Physically this corresponds to "running on with permanent damage": part of the productive capacity is eaten by the damage term, and what remains just sustains a reduced scale. The ratio $1-\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}/(r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0)$ is the "discount rate" of the damage: the stronger the damage and the weaker the net growth rate, the harsher the discount; as $\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}\to r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0$, $V^{*}\to 0$, and case (b) degenerates continuously into case (a).

**Case (c): $r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0$ and the threshold already triggered (accelerated collapse).** Within the state domain $0<V_{\mathrm{obj}}\le V_{\mathrm{max}}$ of the reduced model, the growth term and the damage term have the same sign: $\mathrm{d}V/\mathrm{d}t = V[(r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0)(1-V/V_{\mathrm{max}})-\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}]$, both terms in the brackets are negative, the decay rate is larger than either single term, and the collapse is accelerated. In such systems the damage steps down hard on a decline that has already begun; its signature is a collapse time markedly shorter than the time estimated from the damage rate alone.

![Figure Ⅰ-8 Terminal phase diagram and bistable basins](figures/release/fig_i1_terminal_phase.pdf)

**Figure Ⅰ-8. Terminal phase diagram and bistable basins.** (a) Phase diagram of the terminal $V/V_{\max}$ on the $(g_0,\delta_{\mathrm{dam}})$ plane, the white dashed line being $g_0=\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$; (b) the deviation curve of a moving target and the admissible drift upper limit — when the drift rate reaches 0.8% of the net growth rate the deviation already exceeds 10%; (c) the bistable basins at $\chi=0.9\chi_{\mathrm c}$, with divergent initial values accounting for 29%. The whole region to the right of the white line is "running with damage": triggering the threshold does not mean zero, and the damaged plateau $V^{*}>0$ occupies a considerable region.

**Common premises and boundaries of the three cases.** The analysis above presupposes that $V_{\mathrm{max}}$, $\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$, $r_{\mathrm{v}}$, $\gamma_0$ are constants within the observation window; any time-varying parameter turns the fixed point into a moving target, in which case only piecewise approximation is possible — but the "piecewise approximation" must be given an order of magnitude: writing $\varepsilon$ for the relative drift rate of $r_{\mathrm{v}}$, the prediction from a frozen fixed point agrees with the true trajectory when $\varepsilon/g_0\lesssim10^{-2}$ (deviation $<10\%$); beyond this order of magnitude the fixed point can serve only as an **instantaneous reference** and not as a prediction. The reason is that $\partial V^{*}/\partial g_0=\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}/g_0^{2}$, which diverges as $g_0\to\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}^{+}$. A worked example: when the drift rate reaches $0.8\%$ of the net growth rate, the prediction deviation already exceeds $10\%$ (Appendix F Table F-6; Figure Ⅰ-8(b)). $V_{\mathrm{max}}$ itself is a parameter to be calibrated, so the value of $V^{*}$ carries the same uncertainty; in this volume's placement, $V^{*}$ is a structural prediction ("there is a nonzero low plateau rather than collapse to zero") and not a value that can be compared directly with measurement. **Failure condition**: if a measured trajectory falls straight to the reading floor after the threshold is triggered, with no plateau segment, then case (b) does not hold in that system, and one should return to case (a) and check the calibration of $\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$.

**Closed-loop special case (Allee type).** If a class of systems is indeed driven in growth by $V$ itself, that is, $r_{\mathrm{v}}=k_{\mathrm{v}}V$, then (I-5.2) becomes $\dot V=k_{\mathrm{v}}V^{2}(1-V/V_{\mathrm{max}})-\gamma_0V$, and an unstable threshold $V_{\mathrm{A}}=\tfrac{V_{\max}}{2}\bigl[1-\sqrt{1-4\gamma_0/(k_{\mathrm{v}}V_{\max})}\bigr]$ appears: below it the system tends to zero even without damage, and only above it does it rise toward the capacity upper limit ($\gamma_0/k_{\mathrm{v}}$ is only the first-order limit as $V_{\max}\to\infty$, not the threshold itself). This is another dynamics, whose criteria cannot be borrowed from the three cases above. The three above belong to the branch where "the drive is independent of $V$", and every $r_{\mathrm{v}}$ in the rest of this section means the constant of this branch; the closed-loop special case is the only exception, and then $r_{\mathrm{v}}$ is no longer fixed by the image of (I-5.1a).

$V_{\mathrm{max}}$ is set in the model as the thermodynamic capacity upper bound and is assumed to be related to the environmental available free-energy flux $\dot{F}_{\mathrm{avail}}$ and the minimum entropy production rate $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}$; but this volume has not yet given a calibration formula that maps these physical quantities to the dimension of $V$, so $V_{\mathrm{max}}$ can for now only serve as a parameter to be calibrated and cannot be computed directly from the two quantities above. The $V_{\mathrm{max}}$ of this equation is the supremum of attainable $V$, $V_{\mathrm{max}}(D,S)$, and denotes the same quantity as the $V_{\mathrm{max}}$ in its scaling relation; that scaling relation gives only a proportionality, whose coefficient is to be calibrated.

**Conditional merging rule for two sources.** $V_{\mathrm{max}}$ has two candidate upper bounds: the information-capacity mapping given by the single-channel reduced model of (I-5.1a), and the niche-capacity scaling of (I-6.7). Only when both constraints concern the same state variable (and not one constraining $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ and the other the total $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$), the same system boundary and the same observation window is the tighter one taken:

$$V_{\mathrm{max}}=\min\Bigl\{\,\zeta_V I^{*},\ \ \alpha_{\mathrm{cap}}\,\dot F_{\mathrm{avail}}/(\sigma_{\min}\gamma_0)\,\Bigr\}$$
