$\alpha_{\mathrm{cap}}$ is the proportionality coefficient of (I-6.7), to be calibrated (hereafter the capacity coefficient), and is unrelated to the information elasticity exponent $\alpha$; $\zeta_V$ is the proportionality coefficient from information capacity to reduced viability-potential capacity, in units of $\mathrm{s/J}$, and must be calibrated in the target domain. The $I^{*}$ in the first branch takes the positive equilibrium root of (I-5.1c), and reduces to $I_{\max}(1-\gamma_0/r_{\mathrm{v}})$ only when $I_{\mathrm{eq}}=0$. Reason: a capacity upper bound is "the supremum attainable under the declared constraints"; when two constraints act on the same state variable at once, the attainable upper bound is fixed by the tighter one. If the state variables or accounting boundaries differ, taking the minimum has no mathematical meaning, and they must be reported separately. If the object of study is subject to only one of the constraints (for example $I_{\max}$ is given by protocol while the niche flux is unrestricted), it must be stated "which branch only this section uses"; **the two equations must not be cited together without explanation** — otherwise the same $V_{\mathrm{max}}$ would give two different capacity upper bounds, and the thresholds for $V^{*}$ and for the saturated state would no longer be comparable.

**Saturated state.** A system enters the stable saturated state if and only if the three conditions $E_{\mathrm{struct}}\ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}$, $\rho\to 0$ and $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\to 0$ are satisfied together; these conditions, together with $g_0\equiv r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0>0$ and the reduced single-channel state reaching its capacity upper bound, jointly define the saturation manifold of this reduced model; only when $V_{\mathrm{s}}\approx0$ may that state be written $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{max}}$. **The condition for saturation is $g_0>0$, not $g_0=0$**: when $g_0=0$, (I-5.2) gives $\mathrm{d}V/\mathrm{d}t\equiv0$, $V$ stays at its initial value, neither saturated nor collapsing; only $g_0>0$ makes $V\to V_{\mathrm{max}}$. The discrete update form is

$$
V_{\mathrm{obj},n+1} = \frac{I_{\mathrm{struct}}\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}}{E_{\mathrm{struct}}} + V_{\mathrm{c},n+1} \tag{I-5.4}
$$

where $V_{\mathrm{c},n+1}$ takes its value according to the piecewise definition of $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ in §3.2: it takes $0$ when the causal channel is not enabled; if causal information has a nonzero reading but no attributable energy consumption, the update value is undefined, and $0/0$ is not computed directly.

**The status of Eq. (I-5.2).** Eq. (I-5.2) is the trajectory of the single-channel reduced state, and does not explicitly contain $I,T,E$; (I-5.12) is another auxiliary subsystem, and the two are not coupled. **Eq. (I-3.2) is always the only two-channel reading definition**; in the general case the change of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ is booked channel by channel by (I-5.20a). Only when $V_{\mathrm{s}}$ is negligible, the causal channel dominates and the corresponding variables satisfy the freezing conditions may (I-5.2)/(I-5.3) serve as the reduced trajectory of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}\approx V_{\mathrm{c}}$, with coefficients fixed by (I-5.1a) (see the next paragraph). Therefore the full two-channel case uses (I-5.20a), and (I-5.2)/(I-5.3) are used only when the single-channel reduction conditions above are met. Wherever (I-5.2)/(I-5.3) hold, the variations of $I,T,E$ have been absorbed by the freezing conditions into $r_{\mathrm{v}}$ and $V_{\mathrm{max}}$, and exert no further dynamical effect on $V$ separately. The complete constraint phase diagram is left to later work; see the open problems of Chapter 10.

**Accounting identities (I-5.20a)/(I-5.20b).** Since (I-3.2) is $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}+V_{\mathrm{c}}$ and $V_k=I_kT_k/E_k$, first write the channel-by-channel form

$$
\dot V_{\mathrm{obj}}
=\sum_{k\in\{\mathrm{s},\mathrm{c}\}}V_k\left(
\frac{\dot I_k}{I_k}+\frac{\dot T_k}{T_k}-\frac{\dot E_k}{E_k}\right)
\tag{I-5.20a}
$$

In the single-channel approximation $V\approx I\,T/E$ it degenerates to

$$
\frac{\dot V}{V}=\frac{\dot I}{I}+\frac{\dot T}{T}-\frac{\dot E_{\mathrm{eff}}}{E_{\mathrm{eff}}}
\tag{I-5.20b}
$$

Both are identities of definition, not new assumptions. (I-5.20b) must not be taken as a substitute for (I-5.20a): for the same set of $I,T,E$ the two differ numerically by a factor of 4 (§3.2).

**The relation between Eq. (I-5.2) and (I-5.1a).** (I-5.2) is the image of (I-5.1a) in the quasi-static limit. Under the single-channel baseline form $V=I\,T/E$, with $I_{\mathrm{eq}}=0$ and the mapping $I_{\mathrm{net}}\approx I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ calibrated, if the structural channel is frozen within one viability-potential time scale ($\dot I_s=\dot T_s=\dot E_s\approx0$), and the time and energy consumption of the causal channel are also frozen ($\dot T_c=\dot E_c\approx0$), then the accounting identity (I-5.20a) degenerates to $\dot V_c/V_c=\dot I_c/I_c$, that is, $V_c\propto I_c$. Substituting into the continuous-flow form of (I-5.1a) and comparing coefficients with (I-5.2) gives

$$
r_{\mathrm v}=\mu_{\mathcal F},\qquad \gamma_0=\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}^{-1},\qquad V_{\max}=\zeta_V\,I_{\max}\left(1-\frac{\gamma_0}{r_{\mathrm v}}\right)
$$

that is, the net growth rate and decay rate of (I-5.2) are not free parameters: they correspond respectively to the growth term and decay term of (I-5.1a), and are automatically consistent with the criterion $r_{\mathrm v}>\gamma_0\iff\mu_{\mathcal F}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}>1$ of (I-5.1c) and (I-5.1d). It should be noted that (I-5.2) contains only the net growth rate $r_{\mathrm v}-\gamma_0$ and its quotient, so the **separate** values of the two parameters correspond according to the mechanism sources of the two terms; a different split does not change the trajectory, only the labels. This correspondence is an algebraic identity, introduces no new symbols and does not depend on calibration.

**Boundary.** The above reduction requires $I_{\mathrm{eq}}=0$ (declared in the three-line summary of (I-5.1d)) and that the causal channel be quasi-static on the time scale of the evolution of $I$. If $\dot T_c$ or $\dot E_c$ is of the same order as $\dot I_c$, $V_c\propto I_c$ does not hold, (I-5.2) is no longer the image of (I-5.1a), and one must return to the complete form of (I-5.20a). **Test procedure**: from $V_c=I_cT_c/E_c$ one has $\mathrm d\ln V_c=\mathrm d\ln I_c+\mathrm d\ln(T_c/E_c)$, so the condition for $V_c\propto I_c$ is that the cumulative drift of the ratio $T_c/E_c$ within one observation window be negligible. Take a pre-registered tolerance $\varepsilon^{*}$ (back-derived from the acceptable $|\Delta V_c/V_c|$); the criterion is

$$\varepsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}\equiv\frac{\left|\int_{t_0}^{t_1}\bigl(\dot T_c/T_c-\dot E_c/E_c\bigr)\,\mathrm dt\right|}{\left|\int_{t_0}^{t_1}(\dot I_c/I_c)\,\mathrm dt\right|}=\frac{\bigl|\Delta\ln(T_c/E_c)\bigr|}{\bigl|\Delta\ln I_c\bigr|}$$

When $|\varepsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}|\le\varepsilon^{*}$ this reduction holds within that window; otherwise one must return to the complete form of (I-5.20a). The numerator is a **signed difference**, not a sum of absolute values: when $T_c$ and $E_c$ drift in the same direction they cancel in $V_c$ and do not affect the validity of $V_c\propto I_c$; taking a sum of absolute values would misjudge this case as a failure, and misjudge same-direction accumulation (a true failure) as a pass. When the window is short enough the formula degenerates to the instantaneous form $(\dot T_c/T_c-\dot E_c/E_c)/(\dot I_c/I_c)$, but the conclusion is valid only for the window used. The numerical value of $V_{\max}$ is not involved here — it remains a parameter to be calibrated; its information-capacity branch is uniformly written $\zeta_V I^{*}$, in which $I^{*}$ takes the positive equilibrium root of (I-5.1c).

#### 5.1.3 The $E$ Recursion (Two Runaway Modes of Thermodynamic Dissipation)

**Mode one: surge of myopic dissipation (acute destabilization).** When $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ collapses, the system loses long-term planning capacity and turns to extracting extreme short-term returns; $E_{\mathrm{flow}}$ means the cumulative energy budget within the same accounting window and not instantaneous power, and this budget increases as $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ shortens:

$$
E_{\mathrm{flow},n} = E_{\mathrm{survival}} + E_{\mathrm{causal}} + \frac{k_{\mathrm{s}}}{T_{\mathrm{pred},n}} \tag{I-5.5}
$$

$k_{\mathrm{s}}$ is the metabolic sensitivity coefficient ($\mathrm{J\cdot s}$), making the unit of $k_{\mathrm{s}}/T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ J; $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ and $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ must be cumulative energies within the same accounting window, and instantaneous power cannot be substituted. $k_{\mathrm{s}}/T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is an additional budget term given by the model, whose microscopic origin has not been derived or calibrated, and it cannot be taken directly as a verified new actual expenditure: if it represents new energy consumption not included in the measured $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ or $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, it must be explicitly assigned to the corresponding channel before the total cost is summed or compared with the threshold; if it is only a proxy for items already counted in the basic sub-items, it must not be added again. $E_{\mathrm{flow}}$ itself is not an independent sub-item to be added again to $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$. The function is linear in $T_{\mathrm{pred}}^{-1}$ and convex in $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ itself: as the horizon shortens, the budget grows in accelerating inverse proportion, reaching a finite upper limit $E_{\mathrm{flow,max}} = E_{\mathrm{survival}}+E_{\mathrm{causal}}+k_{\mathrm{s}}/T_{\mathrm{min}}$ at $T_{\mathrm{pred}}=T_{\mathrm{min}}>0$. If this budget is compared with $E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$, the two must adopt the same declared accounting scope, observation window and cost-apportionment rule; if the threshold is defined for a single PS, it cannot be compared with a budget containing the full cost of external holders.

**Mode two: meta-level dissipation (chronic damping).** To maintain persistence, the system evolves a supervisory module; but "how to save energy" itself also consumes energy, producing meta-level dissipation $E_{\mathrm{eff}} = E_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{base}} + E_{\mathrm{meta}}$. If the supervisory layer can nest without limit, $E_{\mathrm{meta}}$ will keep growing and consume free energy (this volume gives no derivation of the growth rate). This can serve as a candidate mechanism for the bureaucratic inertia of large organizations and for computational deadlock, awaiting test.

#### 5.1.4 The $T$ Recursion (Self-Realization of the Endogenous Time Horizon)

$$
T_{\mathrm{pred},n+1} = \max\left(T_{\mathrm{min}},\; \frac{T_{\mathrm{hor}}}{1 + \lambda\,(\chi V^{\mathrm{virt}}_{n})^2}\right) \tag{I-5.6}
$$

$\chi V^{\mathrm{virt}}_{n}$ is already dimensionless; $\lambda$ is a dimensionless positive real number, acting as an adjuster of compression strength; $T_{\mathrm{hor}}$ has dimension $\mathrm{s}$, consistent with $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$. **The independent variable is the virtual viability potential, not the objective viability potential**: the model claims "the more outrageously the self-assessment is inflated, the narrower the horizon is squeezed"; if $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ were taken, a healthy structure with a very large objective potential and undistorted assessment would also have its horizon narrowed, completely contrary to the narrative of the whole book.

- **Feedforward regulation (long-term investment)**: when the horizon is wide, the system tends to invest current thermodynamic dissipation in infrastructure building, thereby raising $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$; phenomenologically this appears as building self-confidence and changing the future — predictability of the future rises, and the system is willing to pay a present cost for distant returns. This is a description of feedforward regulation and introduces no new variable.
- **Reverse phase transition (collapse of the time horizon)**: there are two sources, the first an internal positive-feedback runaway (a high $\chi$ inflates the virtual viability potential, and the system spontaneously falls into myopia), the second an external negative-effect shock (an $I^{-}$ event directly compresses the time horizon and triggers a defensive surge of dissipation).

The quadratic compression form is chosen because it captures the qualitative feature of superlinear collapse of the horizon as recursive amplification increases; the qualitative conclusions (the existence of the phase transition, the early-warning window) depend only on the monotonicity of the compression, not on the concrete functional form. The time-horizon compression formula is the compression rule given $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$, and $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ is the threshold of the $I$–$V$ closed loop; the two belong to different equation levels and cannot be substituted for each other.

**Two orthogonal components of the horizon.** Where finer division is needed, $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ can be split into horizon length $L_{\mathrm{pred}}$ (the largest prediction time domain satisfying the confidence threshold, $\mathrm{s}$) and horizon resolution $G_{\mathrm{pred}}=\log_2(T_0/T_{\mathrm{min}})$ ($\mathrm{bit}$), with $T_0$ a reference time scale. As $T_{\mathrm{pred}}\to T_{\mathrm{min}}$ both tend to zero, and the system loses cross-period resolving power — consistent with the $T$-lock-in criterion: it sees short, and it also sees coarse.

#### 5.1.5 The $D$ Recursion (Decoder Evolution: The Gradient-Drift Form)

The evolution of the decoder is described as a competition between information-gradient ascent and energy-gradient descent:

$$
D_{n+1} = D_n + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{lr}} \left( \nabla_{\Theta} I_{\mathrm{net}} - k_D\,\frac{I_0}{E_0}\, \nabla_{\Theta} E_{\mathrm{causal}} \right) \tag{I-5.7}
$$

$\Theta$ is the parameter space of that system type, $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lr}}$ is the learning rate, and $k_D$ is the energy-penalty coefficient (**dimensionless**; in the formula $I_0=1\ \mathrm{bit}$ and $E_0=1\ \mathrm{J}$ serve only to close the dimensions, written the same way as Volume IV (I-3.1)). Parameter coordinates are to be understood as nondimensionalized coordinates; if physical parameters with units are used, an appropriate scale factor must be introduced coordinate by coordinate, and a single scalar learning rate cannot be shared directly.

| System type | Examples of the parameter space $\Theta$ |
| :------------------- | :--------------------------------------- |
| Neural networks | Weight matrices, biases, architecture hyperparameters |
| Biological systems | Gene-expression levels, neural connection strengths, synaptic plasticity parameters |
| Social organizations | Resource-allocation ratios, institutional-rule parameters, power-structure variables |

The decoder has two evolutionary paths: **decoder drift** adjusts smoothly by gradient ascent or descent; **topological transition** is a paradigm-revolution-style structural reconstruction, in which, after the candidate paradigm-breaking signal $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ crosses its threshold, the trial-and-error investment injected triggers $\mathcal{T}$, making $I_{\max}(D_{n+1})>I_{\max}(D_n)$. The division of labor must be written clearly: drift moves parameters within existing dimensions, and $\mathcal{T}$ opens a new dimension; the former is continuous, differentiable and predictable, the latter discrete, nondifferentiable and confirmable only after the fact.

#### 5.1.6 The Three-Condition Canonical Form of the Topological Transition Operator $\mathcal{T}$ (**Proposition**)

$\mathcal{T}$ takes the decoder from $D_n$ to $D_{n+1}$ and resets the capacity. This volume fixes the transition condition as a canonical form of three conditions that hold simultaneously:

$$
\mathcal{T}:\ D_n \to D_{n+1}
\ \Longleftrightarrow\ (\mathrm{C}1) \wedge (\mathrm{C}2) \wedge (\mathrm{C}3) \tag{I-5.8}
$$

$$
\begin{aligned}
(\mathrm{C}1)\ \text{signal availability:}&\quad \tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}} \ge -\log_2\alpha_0\ \text{(} k \text{ consecutive times)} \ \wedge\ D_n(\text{new dimension}) \approx 0 \\
(\mathrm{C}2)\ \text{energy availability:}&\quad E_{\mathrm{available}}(t) > E_{\mathrm{build}} \\
(\mathrm{C}3)\ \text{wisdom lever:}&\quad L_{\mathrm{lev}} \equiv \frac{V(D_{n+1}) - V(D_n)}{E_{\mathrm{build}}} > L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}
\end{aligned} \tag{I-5.9}
$$

**C1 uses only an observable proxy.** The three-layer decomposition was given in §2.1: only the candidate signal $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ can enter C1, being the only quantity defined and computable before the transition; taking the ex post quantity $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ as the criterion is circular, and taking the event $\mathcal{T}$ itself as the criterion is a tautology. **$\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ crossing the threshold does not mean that a transition will necessarily occur**: C1 shows only that the existing decoder is indeed failing on that target and that the signal is rare enough; the failure may come from noise, drift or instrument fault, or from a genuinely new dimension; the role of C1 is to concentrate the limited $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ on a few candidates. $\alpha_0$ and the number $k$ of consecutive threshold exceedances must be pre-registered, the upper limit of the false-alarm rate is $N\alpha_0$ ($N$ being the number of independent gatings), and post hoc adjustment does not count as passing.

**Comparability premise of C3.** $V(D_{n+1})$ and $V(D_n)$ may use different readings, capacities, backgrounds and information mappings, and their difference is a comparable numerical value only when all of the following conditions are met: the same target object, the same observation window, the same energy-apportionment rule, the same reference protocol, the same cost de-duplication rule. When they are not met, only a **qualitative transition** can be reported, and $\Delta V/E_{\mathrm{build}}$ must not be taken as a comparable numerical criterion. This restriction is not formalism: the $V$ of the old and new decoders is often given by different reading protocols, and subtracting two such numbers directly and dividing by the construction cost would count a protocol difference as a leverage gain.

**Endogenous risk discount rate.** Competitive pressure and the time horizon jointly determine the system's intensity of discounting the future:

$$
r_{\mathrm{eff}} \;\equiv\; \gamma_0 + \psi(\rho) + \frac{1}{T_{\mathrm{pred}}} \qquad (\mathrm{s^{-1}}) \tag{I-5.10}
$$

The three terms are the baseline depreciation $\gamma_0$, the competitive premium discount rate $\psi(\rho)$ ($\psi(0)=0$, $\psi'(\rho)>0$), and the myopic discount $1/T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ produced by the finite time horizon. The larger $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is, the lower the present value of the same future persistence return; the effective discount horizon is $1/r_{\mathrm{eff}}$. Three directly testable monotonic relations follow (other conditions unchanged): a shorter $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ $\Rightarrow$ a higher $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ $\Rightarrow$ the present value of long-term investment is pressed down and the system turns to short-term extraction; a higher $\rho$ $\Rightarrow$ a higher $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$, that is, competitive excess drive itself aggravates myopia; $1/r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the time scale of commitment, and any investment whose payback period exceeds it has no persistence value in that system.

**Three-line summary**: (i) premises — $V(D_n)$ and $V(D_{n+1})$ satisfy the five comparability conditions of C3, the background distribution of $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ and $\alpha_0$ are pre-registered, and $\psi$ depends only on $\rho$; (ii) result — $\mathcal T$ occurs if and only if C1, C2 and C3 hold at once, and $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ rises monotonically with $1/T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ and $\psi(\rho)$; (iii) failure conditions — when the comparability conditions are not met, C3 can be used only qualitatively; when the false-alarm rate of $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ is not controlled, C1 does not constitute an effective gate; as $T_{\mathrm{pred}}\to T_{\mathrm{min}}$, $1/T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ diverges and the linear-superposition approximation of $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ fails.

### 5.2 The Triple Splitting of the Viability Potential

#### 5.2.1 Unification in Low-Order Structures

In low-order physical structures time is an exogenous coordinate parameter, the system cannot intervene endogenously, and there are no independent prediction and value modules, so the three terms merge into one value:

$$
\text{low-order structure:}\; V_{\mathrm{obj}} = V^{\mathrm{virt}} = V^{\mathrm{val}} = V \tag{I-5.11}
$$

#### 5.2.2 The Endogenization of $T$: The Physical Trigger of the Triple Splitting

After $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ emerges, the originally unified viability potential splits into three variables that belong to one family but differ sharply in function and constraint. The **objective viability potential $V_{\mathrm{obj}}(D)$** is the real persistence strength of any PS under a given decoder, bound by thermodynamic boundaries, unable to be deceived, unable to be borrowed across periods; when $D=D_{\mathrm{self}}$ it is the sole physical reference baseline of all viability-potential variables. The definitions and formulas of the **virtual viability potential $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$** and the **value viability potential $V^{\mathrm{val}}$** are in §3.4 and §3.5; the former is an internal simulated value, the latter a behavioral objective function that specifies on whose $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, of which PS, the system allocates its limited $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$.

**The viability-potential selection principle.** Structures with higher $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ are, in a statistical sense, more likely to be maintained, expanded or replicated. Low-order structures do not actively maximize $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ but passively accept thermodynamic and environmental selection; higher-order structures predict their own future states through $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ and select behavioral goals through $V^{\mathrm{val}}$. In the great majority of cases $V^{\mathrm{val}}$ is anchored to the structure's own $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, and in a few cases turns, through decoder drift, to the $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ of other PSs; the criterion for such transfer is an open problem.

**Ontological clarification.** The triple splitting is not a splitting of $V$ into three independent entities, but three internal relations that an active-phase system builds around one and the same $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$: the objective state (physical adjudication), the predictive representation (internal estimate), the optimization target (behavioral interface). The latter two do not constitute new physical quantities independent of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$.

**Paths after the splitting, and decoupling.** The triple splitting is a neutral structure: it lets the system represent the future and select goals beyond $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, is a necessary condition for higher-order cognition, and does not in itself foreshadow runaway. After it there are at least two paths, depending on $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ and $\chi$: one puts the virtual potential to use for long-term investment, and in the other $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ is self-referentially amplified and enters myopic runaway. The absolute value of the prediction error $V^{\mathrm{virt}}-V_{\mathrm{obj}}(D_{\mathrm{self}})$ is $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$, and its being nonzero is called **passive decoupling**; when the anchor of $V^{\mathrm{val}}$ shifts from the system itself to another PS, it is called **active decoupling**.

![Figure Ⅰ-9. The Triple Splitting of the Viability Potential](figures/release/fig_i9_triple_split.pdf)

**Figure Ⅰ-9. The Triple Splitting of the Viability Potential.** The objective viability potential $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ (physical accounting layer), the virtual viability potential $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ (internal-model layer, generated by the estimate of $D_{\mathrm{cog}}$), and the valuation viability potential $V^{\mathrm{val}}$ (behavioral-objective layer, the objective function actually optimized) are three distinct objects; the decoupling deviation $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} = |V^{\mathrm{virt}} - V_{\mathrm{obj}}|$ is measurable. $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}+V_{\mathrm{c}}$ is only a bookkeeping decomposition, with the aggregation operator provisional. The triple splitting applies only to active cognitive systems, not to all PSs. Conceptual figure, no numerical values.

#### 5.2.3 Classification of Equilibrium Points and the Two Paths (**Proposition**)

Write the effective elasticity exponent $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}>0$. Making the discrete map continuous in relaxation form, and pairing it with a separately posited viability-potential growth equation (it is not a direct coupling with the viability drive equation, and the coefficient $k_I$ is not the same quantity as the $k_{\mathrm{v}}$ in the closed-loop special case $r_{\mathrm{v}}=k_{\mathrm{v}}V$; $I_{\mathrm{bas}},k_I,\gamma_0,\tau_I>0$, $\chi\ge 0$, $V>0$):

$$
\dot{I} = \frac{I_{\mathrm{bas}}(1 + \chi V) - I}{\tau_I}, \qquad \dot{V} = \frac{k_I\, I^{\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}}}{V} - \gamma_0 V \tag{I-5.12}
$$

**Equilibrium points.** Setting $\dot I=\dot V=0$ gives $I^{*} = I_{\mathrm{bas}}(1+\chi V^{*})$ and $V^{*2} = k_I I^{*\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}}/\gamma_0$. Eliminating $I^{*}$, the positive equilibrium points are the positive roots of

$$
h(V) = V^{2} - \kappa\,(1 + \chi V)^{\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}} = 0, \qquad \kappa = \frac{k_I I_{\mathrm{bas}}^{\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}}}{\gamma_0}
$$

The trace of the Jacobian is identically $-1/\tau_I-2\gamma_0<0$, and the determinant is

$$
\det J = \frac{\gamma_0}{\tau_I}\left[2 - \frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}\,\chi V^{*}}{1 + \chi V^{*}}\right] = \frac{\gamma_0}{\tau_I V^{*}}\, h'(V^{*}),
$$

so an equilibrium point with $h'(V^{*})>0$ is stable, and one with $h'(V^{*})<0$ is a saddle.

**Classification.** (1) $0<\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}<2$: for any $\chi\ge 0$ there is a unique stable positive equilibrium point. (2) $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}=2$: the equation reduces to $V=\sqrt{\kappa}(1+\chi V)$; when $\chi<1/\sqrt{\kappa}$ there is a stable finite equilibrium point, and when $\chi\ge 1/\sqrt{\kappa}$ there is no finite positive equilibrium point. (3) $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}>2$, $\chi=0$: a unique equilibrium point $V=\sqrt{\kappa}$, stable. (4) $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}>2$, $\chi>0$: when $\chi<\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ there are two positive equilibrium points, the lower branch stable and the upper branch a saddle; when $\chi=\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ the two merge (saddle-node bifurcation); when $\chi>\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ there is no positive equilibrium point. Here

$$
\chi_{\mathrm{c}} = \frac{2}{(\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}} - 2)\sqrt{\kappa}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}} - 2}{\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}}\right)^{\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}/2} \tag{I-5.13}
$$

As $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}\to 2^{+}$, $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}\to 1/\sqrt{\kappa}$, joining onto class 2.

![Figure Ⅰ-10. Saddle-Node Bifurcation of the I–V Subsystem](figures/release/fig_i10_iv_saddle_node.pdf)

**Figure Ⅰ-10. Saddle-Node Bifurcation of the I–V Subsystem.** Only the branches of the reduced subsystem at $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}} > 2$ are shown ($\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}} = 3$, $\kappa = 1$, logarithmic vertical scale): for $\chi < \chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ two positive equilibria coexist (stable lower branch, saddle upper branch); at $\chi = \chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ the two branches merge; for $\chi > \chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ there is no positive equilibrium. The parameter section is a model illustration, not empirical data; $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ belongs only to the I–V reduced subsystem.

**Candidate dynamical criterion for path two.** When the equilibrium point disappears ($\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}>2$ and $\chi>\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$, or $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}=2$ and $\chi\ge 1/\sqrt{\kappa}$), under the approximation that $I$ follows $V$ quickly, $\dot{V}\approx -\gamma_0 h(V)/V>0$, corresponding to a candidate state of continued growth: $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}=2$, $\chi=1/\sqrt{\kappa}$ gives asymptotically linear growth, and $\chi>1/\sqrt{\kappa}$ gives exponential growth; the conclusion of finite-time divergence for $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}>2$ must hold under the fast-following condition above, and cannot be inferred directly from the nonexistence of equilibrium points alone. When a finite stable equilibrium point exists it corresponds to path one (or conservative maintenance), but when $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}>2$, $0<\chi<\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ the system is bistable: the stable manifold of the upper-branch saddle divides the phase space into two parts, initial states below it converge to the stable equilibrium point, and those above it still diverge, so $\chi<\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ cannot uniformly be taken as path one (order-of-magnitude example, a worked value within the model family and not an empirical value: at $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}=3$, $\chi=0.9\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ about thirty percent of initial values still diverge; Appendix F Table F-7, Figure Ⅰ-8(c)). A system with $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}<2$ has no such growth state. $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ depends on $(k_I,I_{\mathrm{bas}},\gamma_0,\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}})$ and is calibrated by system type.

**Layering of the result.** The classification above is a mathematical conclusion within the $I$–$V$ subsystem of Eq. (I-5.12), and is not a sufficient proof of the myopic runaway path after triple splitting: what Eq. (I-5.12) directly evolves is $I$ and $V$, and it contains no $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$, $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ or $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$; this volume does not specify how $V$ corresponds to these variables. To lift it to a complete path criterion, these correspondences must be made explicit, and one must test whether the mapping can produce the causal chain of myopic runaway. Therefore $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ is first of all the critical coupling parameter of a simplified subsystem, and whether it agrees with the threshold at which a full cognitive system enters the myopic runaway regime remains a question to be tested.

**Declaration of model level (the attribution of $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$).** The $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ of this volume belongs to the $I$–$V$ subsystem and is the saddle-node bifurcation point of the pair of equations (I-5.12); it is **not** a threshold of the decoder quality equation. The decoder quality $\mathcal{Q}_D$ has its own equation (of the form $\dot{\mathcal{Q}}_D=\mu_{\mathrm{rec}}\mathcal{Q}_D(1-\mathcal{Q}_D/\mathcal{Q}_{D,\max})-\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}\mathcal{Q}_D$), in which the recovery rate $\mu_{\mathrm{rec}}$, the degradation rate $\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$ and the capacity $\mathcal{Q}_{D,\max}$ have no conversion relation to this section's $k_I$, $I_{\mathrm{bas}}$, $\gamma_0$, $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$; its critical condition must be solved independently, and the stability of the zero equilibrium point is determined by the sign of $\mu_{\mathrm{rec}}-\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$, irrespective of $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$. The two models belong to different levels: $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ describes "at what coupling strength the self-referential loop of information and viability potential loses its positive equilibrium", and the $\mathcal{Q}_D$ model describes "how the decoder's maintained quality stabilizes between recovery and degradation". No value of $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ in this volume may be carried over into the discussion of the decoder quality equation, nor the reverse. Carrying a threshold across levels produces no dimensional conflict, only a number that looks reasonable and has no basis, and so is the hardest to detect.

**Distinction between the two exponents.** $\alpha$ is the information elasticity exponent, a parameter for the response strength of the viability potential to information resources; $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the effective elasticity exponent of $I$ in the growth source term of $V$, a parameter of the closed-loop model. The two belong to different levels and must not be conflated.

**Boundary of the scope of application.** The triple splitting and the myopic runaway regime can be activated only in systems whose $D_{\mathrm{cog}}$ lies in the **symbolic region** ($V_{\mathrm{c}}\gg 0$). A system in the embodied region ($V_{\mathrm{c}}\to 0^{+}$), though it has a nonzero $V_{\mathrm{c}}$, has an extremely short $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ and an extremely low $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$, and the model stipulates that its self-referential coupling strength $\chi$ is far below the critical value $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ of the simplified subsystem, so it does not enter the myopic runaway regime within this model; both this stipulation and the applicability of $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ to full systems remain to be tested. Systems with $D=\varnothing$ do not take part in this mechanism, and their collapse is determined directly by thermodynamic limits.

**Evidence boundary of the three statements.** (i) **Premises**: the two-equation form of Eq. (I-5.12), $I_{\mathrm{bas}},k_I,\gamma_0,\tau_I>0$, $\chi\ge 0$, and the scale separation in which $I$ follows $V$ quickly. (ii) **Result**: the four-way classification of the number and stability of equilibrium points by $(\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}},\chi)$, and the explicit formula for $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$. (iii) **Failure conditions**: if $\tau_I$ and the evolution time of $V$ are of the same order, the fast-following assumption fails and the classification no longer applies; if $\chi$ varies with $V$ (rather than being a constant), the saddle-node structure may become another bifurcation, and the formula for $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ no longer holds.

### 5.3 Decoupling-Deviation Dynamics (**Proposition**)

$\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} = \lvert V^{\mathrm{virt}} - V_{\mathrm{obj}} \rvert$ is the **prediction deviation**: $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ is an estimate of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, and their difference reflects the distance between the cognitive model and physical reality. Its evolution is controlled by the learning rate $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$ and the environmental noise intensity $\sigma$:

$$
\frac{\mathrm{d}\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}}{\mathrm{d}t} = -\alpha_{\mathrm{L}} \cdot I_{\mathrm{net}} \cdot \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} + \sigma \tag{I-5.14}
$$

The equation is defined on $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\ge 0$; when $\sigma>0$ the right derivative at zero is $\sigma>0$, so the trajectory does not cross zero (the absolute value is not differentiable at zero, and the right derivative is taken here). With $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ treated as a constant, the equation has a strict explicit solution:

$$
\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}(t) = \frac{\sigma}{\alpha_{\mathrm{L}} I_{\mathrm{net}}} + \left(\Delta_0 - \frac{\sigma}{\alpha_{\mathrm{L}} I_{\mathrm{net}}}\right) e^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{L}} I_{\mathrm{net}} t} \tag{I-5.15}
$$

From this the equilibrium deviation follows

$$
\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*} = \frac{\sigma}{\alpha_{\mathrm{L}} I_{\mathrm{net}}} \tag{I-5.16}
$$

**Physical reading.** Whatever positive value $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ takes, the deviation converges exponentially to the bounded equilibrium value $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*}$, at the convergence rate $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}}$; the equilibrium deviation is inversely proportional to $I_{\mathrm{net}}$: the stronger the sustained information flow, the better it suppresses environmental noise, and the closer the subjective assessment comes to objective reality; when $\sigma>0$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*}>0$, which reflects the fundamental limit that "no learner, however good, can fully overcome environmental noise". Taking the deviation tolerance $\delta_{\mathrm{tol}}$ (unit $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$, the dimension of $V$) as the tolerance, the corresponding critical information quantity is $I_{\Delta,\mathrm{crit}}=\sigma/(\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}\delta_{\mathrm{tol}})$ (dimensionally in bit): when $I_{\mathrm{net}}<I_{\Delta,\mathrm{crit}}$ the equilibrium deviation exceeds the tolerance, and the system cognitively loses an accurate model of its environment; when $I_{\mathrm{net}}>I_{\Delta,\mathrm{crit}}$ the deviation is suppressed within the tolerance.


Eq. (I-5.14) is the continuous dynamical equation for the decoupling deviation for which this volume currently gives an explicit solution; Eq. (I-5.12) is another subsystem, describing the feedback between information and viability potential. Their variables, assumptions and domains of applicability differ, and they do not yet form a complete unified coupled model: the former describes the bounded equilibrium of learning against noise, the latter growth or divergence under self-referential amplification. The premise of the formula above is that $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is a constant and $\sigma$ exogenous, corresponding to path one; in path two $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is self-referentially amplified by $\chi$, the source term of $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ is produced endogenously by Eq. (I-5.12), and $\Delta^{*}_{\mathrm{VV}}\propto 1/I_{\mathrm{net}}$ no longer applies. **Three-line summary**: (i) premises — $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ constant, $\sigma$ constant and exogenous, $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}}>0$; (ii) result — the deviation converges exponentially to $\sigma/(\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}})$, with critical information quantity $\sigma/\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$; (iii) failure conditions — the linear approximation fails when $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ varies with time, when $\sigma$ depends on the state, or when the domain of $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ crosses the structural-degradation region of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}\to 0$.

**Gap (path two).** The $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ source term $\sigma(\chi,V)$ of path two needs an explicit form (given in the next paragraph); (I-5.14) holds only when $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is a constant, and the equilibrium deviation $\Delta^{*}_{\mathrm{VV}}=\sigma/(\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}})$ it gives **decreases** with $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ — that is, $\chi$ amplifying $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ in fact **lowers** the equilibrium deviation. Therefore "$\chi>\chi_{\mathrm{c}}\Rightarrow\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ amplifies" **is a property this model has not yet derived**, and is a goal that a model still to be built (including an explicit form of the endogenous source term $\sigma(\chi,V)$) must reach. The chain of §5.4 takes it as a premise awaiting verification; the minimal closure of this premise is given below.

**Minimal closure of the source term.** What the gap requires is a function that **can flip the sign**: $\sigma$ must grow with $\chi$ faster than $I_{\mathrm{net}}$. Take

$$
\sigma(\chi,V)=\sigma_0\left[1+\left(\frac{\chi V}{\chi_{*}}\right)^{m}\right],\qquad \sigma_0>0,\ \chi_{*}>0,\ m>0 \tag{I-5.16a}
$$

Three meanings: $\sigma_0$ is the environmental noise floor independent of coupling; $\chi_{*}$ is the self-referential coupling strength at which the noise is amplified to twice the baseline; $m$ is the superlinearity of the amplification. The formula introduces no new state variable and only writes the qualitative requirement "the source term strengthens with the self-referential loop" into a substitutable shape; **it is a closure assumption, not a result derived from (I-5.12)**, and $m$ cannot be inferred from existing parameters and must be calibrated. Why at least quadratic: noise arises from the **use** of $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ and not from its **possession**; the frequency and amplitude of use both rise with $\chi V$, and the intensity of use itself already contains a factor of $\chi V$, so the error-generation rate is at least of the same order as $(\chi V)^{2}$; $m=1$ is the neutral point — when $\sigma\propto I_{\mathrm{net}}$, $\Delta^{*}_{\mathrm{VV}}$ is independent of $\chi$ and there is no amplification to speak of. Dimensionally $(\chi V/\chi_{*})$ is dimensionless, and $\sigma_0$ and $\sigma$ are both $\mathrm{bit/J}$, so (I-5.16a) can be substituted directly into (I-5.14).

**The scope of $I$.** (I-5.12) evolves the bare symbol $I$, and the denominator of (I-5.16) is $I_{\mathrm{net}}$; in this text these are not two quantities: by the terminology convention of §2.1, the bare symbol $I$ in formulas defaults to $I_{\mathrm{net}}$; and the discrete ancestor of (I-5.12), (I-5.1), is written word for word as $I_{\mathrm{net},n+1}=I_{\mathrm{bas}}\left(1+\chi V_{\mathrm{obj},n}\right)$, whose source term is exactly the same as that of (I-5.12). Therefore within the (I-5.12) subsystem this section takes $I\equiv I_{\mathrm{net}}$, with its numerical use inheriting the four conditions of §2.1; where the four are not complete, the deviation below serves only as an ordinal conclusion and not as a numerical one. This sentence is not a new assumption, but spells out a convention already in use — without spelling it out, the denominator of (I-5.16) and the variable of (I-5.12) could be read as two quantities.

Substituting into (I-5.16) gives $\Delta^{*}_{\mathrm{VV}}=\sigma_0\left[1+(\chi V^{*}/\chi_{*})^{m}\right]/(\alpha_{\mathrm L}I^{*})$, where $V^{*}$ and $I^{*}$ take the stable equilibrium branch of (I-5.12); its trend with $\chi$ is in the closed form (I-5.16b) of §5.4.

### 5.4 The Myopic Runaway Regime: The Causal Chain of Collapse (**Hypothesis**)

This is one of the paths after the triple splitting, not the only path. The myopic runaway regime is a dynamical self-consuming process in which a system continually spends its objective viability potential in order to sustain extreme values of the virtual viability potential.

**Model interface and scope of application.** Eq. (I-5.12) describes the information–viability-potential feedback in the $I$–$V$ subsystem, and Eq. (I-5.6) describes the compression rule of the time horizon given $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$. The two have not yet formed a complete bidirectionally coupled system, so the divergence of (I-5.12) alone cannot imply that the horizon in (I-5.6) must collapse, nor can it directly prove that the objective stock must deteriorate. What the chain below gives is a candidate mechanism chain, whose links and order must be verified through variable mappings, coupled equations or independent empirical observation; until then this volume treats the chain as a testable dynamical hypothesis. The quantitative form of link (1) is given by the closure (I-5.16a) of §5.3. On the stable equilibrium branch of (I-5.12), taking $\chi V^{*}\gg1$, $\chi<\chi_{\mathrm c}$, $0<\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}<2$ and taking the logarithmic derivative with respect to $\chi$ gives the closed form of the first link

$$
\frac{\mathrm d\ln\Delta^{*}_{\mathrm{VV}}}{\mathrm d\ln\chi}=\frac{2}{2-\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}}\left[\frac{m\,(\chi V^{*}/\chi_{*})^{m}}{1+(\chi V^{*}/\chi_{*})^{m}}-1\right] \tag{I-5.16b}
$$

It rises monotonically from $-2/(2-\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}})$ to $\dfrac{2}{2-\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}}(m-1)$, and **its zero crossing is always at $m=1$, independent of $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$**. Three inferences: (i) before the source term is activated, the deviation falls with $\chi$, which is the behavior of path one (at $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}=1.5$ the slope is $-4$; see Appendix F Table F-2 and Table F-9); (ii) when $m\le1$ the deviation always falls, and the chain breaks at the first link; (iii) when $m>1$ and $\chi$ is large enough the deviation turns upward, the starting point of the rising segment being fixed by $\chi_{*}$ and its slope jointly by $m$ and $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$. Hence "$\chi>\chi_{\mathrm{c}}\Rightarrow\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ amplifies" requires the endogenous source term to be **superlinear** in $\chi V$ ($m>1$), which corroborates the "Gap (path two)" of §5.3 and is corroborated by it.

**The testable criterion is therefore tightened.** It is no longer the one sentence "log–log slope $>1$", but this: on samples spanning $\chi$, the slope of $\log\Delta^{*}_{\mathrm{VV}}$ against $\log(\chi V^{*})$ should pass from $-2/(2-\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}})$ to $\frac{2}{2-\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}}(m-1)$; **the location of the transition point gives $\chi_{*}$** (write $u\equiv\chi V^{*}$; the minimum lies at $u_{\min}=\chi_{*}(m-1)^{-1/m}$), and the slopes at the two ends give $m$. The original criterion compressed two estimable parameters into one sign judgment, at the price of being unable to tell "$m$ slightly above 1" from "$\chi$ has not yet entered the transition zone", and of failing to obtain $\chi_{*}$.

**Failure boundaries (three; they must be cited together with the conclusion).** Outside $0<\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}<2$ there is no unique stable positive equilibrium point, the closed form does not apply, and the four-way classification of §5.2.3 is used instead; when $\chi V^{*}\gg1$ does not hold the elasticity returns to $-2/(2-\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}})$, the two formulas being continuous at the handover; when $\chi\ge\chi_{\mathrm c}$ the equilibrium disappears and the equilibrium deviation is undefined, so one can speak only of trajectories. When $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}>2$ and $\chi<\chi_{\mathrm c}$ the system is bistable, and the closed form holds only for the stable lower branch.

**What the closed form does not cover.** It quantifies only the direction and order of magnitude of link (1) within the $I$–$V$ subsystem; the correspondence of $V$ to $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ and $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ has still not been established (§5.2.3 declared that this correspondence is not specified), so links (2)–(5) remain hypotheses, and the overall conclusion of the chain is not upgraded by the closed form.

$$
\begin{aligned}
&V^{\mathrm{virt}}\uparrow \;\xrightarrow{(1)}\; r_{\mathrm{v}}^{\mathrm{virt}}\uparrow
\;\xrightarrow{(2)}\; V^{\mathrm{val}}\ \text{hijacked}\\
&\;\xrightarrow{(3)}\; E_{\mathrm{eff}}\uparrow \;\xrightarrow{(4)}\; V_{\mathrm{obj}}\ \text{deteriorates}
\;\xrightarrow{(5)}\; V_{\mathrm{obj}}\ \text{enters a decay segment}
\end{aligned}
\tag{I-5.17}
$$

1. **Inflation of the virtual potential**: a high $\chi$ activates the $I$–$V$ closed loop; $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ grows while $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ collapses.
2. **Surge of the drive rate**: the inflated virtual potential is mapped to an inflated growth-rate signal.
3. **The value goal is hijacked** (either channel, $w_{\mathrm{s}}$ or $w_{\mathrm{c}}$, is overweighted by the virtual signal): the behavioral interface optimizes the virtual signal rather than the objective potential, and $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is poured into the self-referential loop.
4. **The objective potential deteriorates**: $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ rises and $T_{\mathrm{pred}}\to T_{\mathrm{min}}$, so the objective assessment $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ is squeezed from both ends.
5. **The physical stock enters a decay segment**: $E_{\mathrm{eff}}>E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ triggers the damage term; thereafter $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ evolves according to the three cases of §5.1.2 — it decays to zero when $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0<\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$, falls to a smaller positive equilibrium $V^{*}$ rather than to zero when $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0>\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$, and decays doubly, with accelerated collapse, when $r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0$. The end point of the chain is therefore "entering the damage domain and evolving along the damage dynamics".

**The key asymmetry.** Steps 1 to 3 occur on the **latent (assessment) layer** and need no physical cost; step 5 is irreversible. The system burns real structure to chase an internal phantom. $V^{\mathrm{val}}$ never counts self-destruction as a loss, because what it computes is the virtual viability potential and not the objective one. This offers a candidate mechanism for the behavior of higher-order intelligent subjects "sacrificing themselves for short-term extraction of returns", awaiting test.

### 5.5 Competitive Dynamics and the Lock-In States (**Proposition**)

In a multi-subject environment, the viability potential of an individual does not evolve independently but is jointly influenced by the competitive pressure $\rho$ (the integral of competitive dissipative energy within the same observation window divided by the total available free-energy budget within that window, $0\le \rho\le 1$). When $\rho>0$, the symmetric Nash equilibrium is forced to satisfy

$$
r_{\mathrm{v,eq}} = \gamma_0 + \psi(\rho) > \gamma_0 \tag{I-5.18}
$$

At equilibrium all subjects are pushed into a permanent state of **excess drive** (the scope of this sentence is the subjects satisfying the premises above, not an observable population): the equilibrium drive rate is strictly higher than the depreciation rate, meaning that the drive intensity required to maintain the status quo is higher than the no-competition baseline, and this is **not** under-saturation in the sense of "below the capacity upper limit". $\psi(\rho)$ must satisfy two minimal properties: $\psi(0)=0$ and $\psi'(\rho)>0$. These two suffice to support this section's qualitative conclusions; the complete game-structure assumptions and an analytic derivation of $\psi(\rho)$ belong to later work.

| Lock-in type | Main symptoms and SRVD diagnostic criterion | Typical examples | Path out |
| :----- | :-------------------- | :------------ | :------------------- |
| **$E$-lock-in** | Energy expenditure surges with no gain in output; $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ keeps rising while $\Delta I_{\mathrm{net}}\to 0$ | Arms races, bureaucracies consumed by internal friction | Cut unproductive expenditure, shift the budget to cognitive expansion, and lower the share of defensive expenditure in $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ |
| **$I$-lock-in** | Collapse of informational diversity, homogenization; the effective dimension of $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ falls, $\nabla I\to 0$ | Thought censorship, algorithmic information cocoons | Inject $I^{+}$, introduce external diversity, and reactivate $\nabla I$ |
| **$T$-lock-in** | Collapse of the horizon, extreme short-termism; $T_{\mathrm{pred}}\to T_{\mathrm{min}}$ (equivalently $L_{\mathrm{pred}}\to 0$, $G_{\mathrm{pred}}\to 0$) | High-frequency trading, quarter-driven management | Lengthen $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ and lower the discount rate $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ |
| **$D$-lock-in** | Paradigm rigidity, inability to adapt to a new environment; decoder gradient $\nabla D\to 0$, rejection of candidate signals whose $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ crosses the threshold | Institutional rigidity, paradigm closure | Pay $E_{\mathrm{build}}$, trigger a $\mathcal{T}$ transition, and reconstruct the decoder |

**Diagnostic tools.** A sustained decline of the cognitive processing rate $\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{e}} = I_{\mathrm{net}}/T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ ($\mathrm{bit/s}$) signals that the system is approaching some lock-in state; the cognitive lag coefficient $\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{e}} = T_{\mathrm{pred}}/I_{\mathrm{net}} = 1/\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{e}}$ ($\mathrm{s/bit}$) is the core criterion for a lock-in state, and $\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{e}}\to\infty$ is lock-in.

**Three-line summary**: (i) premises — a symmetric Nash equilibrium exists, $\psi$ depends only on $\rho$, and the dissipation of the competing subjects can be accounted on one basis; (ii) result — the equilibrium drive rate is strictly higher than the depreciation rate, and all subjects are in excess drive; (iii) failure conditions — if an alternative niche that can be exited exists ($\rho$ can be lowered), or if costs are not comparable across subjects so that no common $\rho$ can be defined, "permanent excess drive" does not hold.

### 5.6 Mathematical Conditions of the Four Kinds of Limiting Behavior (**Proposition**)

At the limit $t\to\infty$, the trajectory of a system's state falls, within the model family examined, into the following four classes:

| Limiting behavior | Mathematical conditions in the limit domain | Dynamical result |
| :--------- | :-------------------- | :--------------------------- |
| **Saturated state** | $\inf E_{\mathrm{eff}}>E_{\mathrm{floor}}>0$, $\rho\to 0$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\to 0$, $\sup T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}<\infty$ | $V(t)\to V_{\mathrm{max}}$ |
| **Collapse manifold** | Threshold triggered **and $\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}>0$** (not tied to the sign of $g_{\mathrm{net}}$), $\rho\gg 0$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}>0$, $E_{\mathrm{eff}}<\infty$ | Three kinds according to $g_{\mathrm{net}}$: $g_{\mathrm{net}}>0$ → falls to $V^{*}=V_{\mathrm{max}}(1-\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}/(r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0))$ and stops (degraded-equilibrium type); $g_{\mathrm{net}}<0$ and $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0\ge0$ → tends monotonically to $0$ (collapse-to-zero type); $g_{\mathrm{net}}<0$ and $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0<0$ → accelerated collapse to zero |
| **Topological transition** | The effective upper bound of $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}$ or $I_{\mathrm{max}}$ is raised (bound by hard upper limits, not tending to infinity) | $\mathrm{d}V_{\mathrm{max}}/\mathrm{d}t>0$ |
| **Metric replacement** | $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\gg 0\Rightarrow D\to D'$ | $V\to 0$ under the original measure, and the new measure $D'$ dominates the flow field |

**The "collapse manifold" row must be aligned with the three cases.** The row must not be written as "when $\rho\gg0$ and $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}>0$, $r_{\mathrm{v}}>\gamma_0$ is truncated and $\mathrm{d}V/\mathrm{d}t\ll0$", which mixes together the triggering condition, the sign of the net growth rate and the limit value of $V$. They must be separated: **collapse-to-zero type (a)** $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0<\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$, $V$ decays monotonically to $0$, the only case that can be called "collapse to zero"; **degraded-equilibrium type (b)** $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0>\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}>0$, $V$ falls to $V^{*}$ and stays there, the stock pressed down but the structure still running; **accelerated type (c)** $r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0$ and triggered, with a decay rate larger than the sum of the single terms. The "collapse manifold" is therefore a **region** and not a point, its interior divided by $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0=\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$ into two end states, collapse to zero and degraded equilibrium; drawing the whole region as "$V\to 0$" would misjudge a structure running with damage as already extinct. One more mixing of criteria must be corrected at the same time: "$r_{\mathrm{v}}>\gamma_0$ is truncated" is a phase-state description of competitive truncation, not equivalent to the net-growth-rate condition "$r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0$ persists", and this table uniformly adopts the sign of $g_{\mathrm{net}}$ as the sole criterion.

Topological transition and metric replacement are events or switches, not attractors in the asymptotic sense; the four classes are only a classification of limiting behavior within this model family. **Three-line summary**: (i) premises — parameters are constant within the observation window, $V_{\mathrm{max}}$ is finite, and the threshold is on the same basis as $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$; (ii) result — the four kinds of limiting behavior and the conditions under which they hold; (iii) failure conditions — the classification does not apply when parameters vary in time, when $V_{\mathrm{max}}$ is unbounded, or when the threshold and the energy accounting are on different bases.

### 5.7 The Complete Inequality-Constraint System (**Proposition**)

Here $E_{\mathrm{available}}$ denotes the upper limit of the available free-energy budget and $E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ is the candidate damage-trigger threshold; the two are not the same parameter, and no fixed size relation between them is presupposed. Before the threshold and the available budget have been calibrated on instances, the relations below define only the model's candidate constraint region, and cannot by themselves be used to assert that a real structure must collapse.

$$
\begin{aligned}
& I_{\mathrm{min}} \le I_{\mathrm{net}} \le I_{\mathrm{max}}(D), \\
& T_{\mathrm{min}} \le T_{\mathrm{struct}} \le T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}, \\
& E_{\mathrm{struct}} \ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}, \qquad E_{\mathrm{eff}} \le E_{\mathrm{available}}, \\
& 0 \le \rho \le 1, \quad \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} \ge 0, \\
& \text{coarse-grained estimate (not a definition):}\quad V_{\mathrm{cg}} \approx \frac{I_{\mathrm{net}} \cdot T}{E_{\mathrm{eff}}}, \\[4pt]
& \text{saturation condition:}\ (E_{\mathrm{struct}} \ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}) \land (\rho \to 0) \land (\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} \le \delta_{\mathrm{tol}}) \land (g_0 \equiv r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0 > 0), \\[4pt]
& \text{damage-trigger condition:}\ E_{\mathrm{eff}} > E_{\mathrm{thresh}} \Rightarrow \text{damage term}\ \delta_{\mathrm{dam}}\ \text{activated},\\
& \text{net-growth criterion:}\ g_{\mathrm{net}} \equiv (r_{\mathrm{v}} - \gamma_0) - \delta_{\mathrm{dam}}\,\mathbf{1}[E_{\mathrm{eff}} > E_{\mathrm{thresh}}],\\
& \text{collapse condition:}\ \big[(E_{\mathrm{eff}} > E_{\mathrm{thresh}}) \land (r_{\mathrm{v}} - \gamma_0 < \delta_{\mathrm{dam}})\big] \lor (r_{\mathrm{v}} < \gamma_0\ \text{irreversibly persistent}) \\
& \qquad\qquad \Rightarrow V \to 0, \\
& \text{impaired-equilibrium condition:}\ (E_{\mathrm{eff}} > E_{\mathrm{thresh}}) \land (r_{\mathrm{v}} - \gamma_0 > \delta_{\mathrm{dam}} > 0) \\
& \qquad\qquad \Rightarrow V \to V^{*} = V_{\mathrm{max}}\left(1 - \frac{\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}}{r_{\mathrm{v}} - \gamma_0}\right) > 0
\end{aligned} \tag{I-5.19}
$$

**Four judgments must be kept apart and not merged.** (1) **Threshold triggering $\neq$ net growth turning negative**: activation of the damage term means only one more dissipative channel $-\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}V$; if $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0>\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$, the system still has a positive stable equilibrium $V^{*}>0$. (2) **Net growth turning negative $\neq$ the viability potential going to zero**: $\mathrm{d}V/\mathrm{d}t<0$ describes the level falling, and a negative growth rate does not imply a limit of zero unless "persistently negative" is added — which is exactly why the collapse condition says "irreversibly persistent". (3) **$r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0$ and "$r_{\mathrm{v}}>\gamma_0$ is truncated" are not the same criterion**: the former is a sign condition on the net growth rate, the latter a phase-state description of competitive truncation, and the phase table uniformly uses the sign of $g_{\mathrm{net}}$. (4) **Impaired equilibrium is a fourth end state**: when the net growth rate remains positive after triggering, the system settles at $V^{*}$, the stock permanently depressed but the structure still running; counting it as collapse would, in diagnosis, judge a repairable system as written off, and so give the wrong intervention priority.

Hence "$E_{\mathrm{eff}}>E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ necessarily leads to $V\to 0$" is a strong assertion that **does not hold**; the correct statement is: threshold triggering switches the system to the dynamical branch containing the damage term, and whether it goes to zero is decided by the sign of $g_{\mathrm{net}}$ on that branch and its persistence. The sign of $g_{\mathrm{net}}$ directly gives three courses: $g_{\mathrm{net}}<0$ with $r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0$ is accelerated collapse (case c); $g_{\mathrm{net}}<0$ with $r_{\mathrm{v}}\ge\gamma_0$ is collapse-to-zero decay (case a); $g_{\mathrm{net}}>0$ is the degraded-equilibrium type, tending to $V^{*}$ (case b). $g_{\mathrm{net}}=0$ is the critical surface, at which the decay is taken over by the algebraic type, and $V$ still tends to zero but with a diverging time scale.

**Three-line summary**: (i) premises — all inequalities are taken on the same basis within the same accounting boundary and observation window, and $E_{\mathrm{available}}$ and $E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ have both been calibrated; (ii) result — the four bounds of the constraint region, the saturation condition, the damage-trigger condition, the net-growth criterion, the collapse condition and the impaired-equilibrium condition; (iii) failure conditions — if $E_{\mathrm{available}}<E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ (the budget is exhausted before the threshold), the damage domain is unreachable in that system, collapse is determined by energy starvation and not by the damage term, and this section's conditions yield to the criterion of the structural energy lower bound.

***

## Chapter 6 — Thermodynamic Interface and Physical Constraints

This chapter explains the correspondence between SRVD and existing thermodynamic concepts; it introduces no new physical laws and does not redefine thermodynamic quantities. All constraints are external interfaces and take no part in deriving the core dynamical equations.

### 6.1 Entropy and Structural Order

In most physical settings, lower thermodynamic entropy is often associated with higher local order, but the two are not strictly equivalent. Boltzmann entropy $S_D(m)=k_B\ln\lvert\Omega_D^{-1}(m)\rvert$ depends on the definitions of system boundary, coarse-graining and distinguishable microstates; $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ is determined by the reading protocol of the specified domain. Only after a testable mapping has been established under the same boundary, observation window and coarse-graining convention can entropy variables be used to derive changes in structural information. This section therefore retains only the limited claim of "in-domain qualitative correlation": it neither takes $(-S_DT_{\mathrm{thermo}})/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ as a quantitative expression of $V_D$, nor automatically derives the viability-potential formula from the correlation between entropy and order. $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and $S$ are not in one-to-one correspondence — classical entropy characterizes the statistical properties of microstates under specified macroscopic constraints, the meaning of $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ depends on the domain's reading rules, and $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ additionally requires an observable causal effect relative to a holder; a quantitative mapping between it and entropy or negentropy must be established separately.

### 6.2 The Landauer Lower Bound

The Landauer principle (Landauer, 1961) states that in an environment at temperature $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$, irreversibly erasing $1\ \mathrm{bit}$ of information dissipates at least

$$
E_{\mathrm{L,bit}} = k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}} \ln 2 \approx 0.693\, k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}} \tag{I-6.1}
$$

It is the lower bound of the **cost of logically irreversible erasure**, and not a lower bound of **maintenance cost** in general. $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}$ denotes the ideal lower bound for a single erasure of an equiprobable (maximum-entropy) two-state logical bit when the conditions of applicability hold, and in accounting formulas is to be understood as $\mathrm{J/bit}$, giving energy (J) only after multiplication by the amount erased (bit); it is a different variable from the $E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}$ of the structural channel. To account for causal processing, the model provisionally splits it by operational function into two terms, $E_{\mathrm{causal}}=E_{\mathrm{compute}}+E_{\mathrm{refresh}}$, which must still satisfy the same boundary, observation window and de-duplication conditions.

| Sub-item | Meaning | Physical lower bound and conditions of applicability |
| :----- | :--------------- | :------------------------------------- |
| $E_{\mathrm{compute}}$ | Energy consumed by computation (including erasure) | If the operation includes logically irreversible erasure, then $\ge k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2\cdot I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ (Landauer) |
| $E_{\mathrm{refresh}}$ | Energy consumed by maintenance (refresh, error correction, fidelity) | Only when each refresh cycle corresponds to one logically irreversible erasure of the corresponding stored logical bit, and the number of erasures is measurable, may the Landauer lower bound be applied according to the cumulative number of erasures; this table does not claim that general refresh or error-correction processes have the unconditional lower bound given by that formula |

When the Arrhenius approximation is satisfied, the storage refresh period $t_{\mathrm{refresh}}$ is written as

$$
t_{\mathrm{refresh}} \sim \tau_0 \cdot \exp\left(\frac{\Delta U_{\mathrm{barrier}}}{k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}}}\right) \tag{I-6.2}
$$

where $\Delta U_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ is the barrier height (energy difference) in the Arrhenius formula, not equal to the actual structural cost $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ amortized to the observation window; the two must not share one symbol or be interchanged directly. If each refresh cycle does perform one logically irreversible erasure on each of $I_{\mathrm{stored}}$ logical bits, and the number of cycles within the window can be approximated as $t/t_{\mathrm{refresh}}$, then the cumulative refresh energy consumption can be estimated as $E_{\mathrm{refresh}} \ge I_{\mathrm{stored}}k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2\,(t/t_{\mathrm{refresh}})$; if refresh or error correction is reversible, uses side information, or the actual erasure amount differs from cycle to cycle, it must be recomputed from the concrete operations. This framework does **not** claim that $E_{\mathrm{causal}} \ge k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2\cdot I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ is a universal lower bound of maintenance cost.

**The classification of $E_{\mathrm{decode}}$ remains to be clarified.** It is currently only a functional label for cognitive decoding activity, has no independent physical sub-item, and has no established quantitative sub-item relation to $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, and it may cross-cut the compute and refresh classifications; until an operation-level definition, boundary and de-duplication protocol are completed, it is not added to these energy terms, and is not claimed to be mutually exclusive with them or independently measurable. **Distinction of symbols**: $t_{\mathrm{refresh}}$ (the storage refresh period) depends on the medium barrier and the temperature, and $\tau_0$ (the thermal-activation attempt time) is a microscopic constant; the two cannot replace each other.

### 6.3 Persistence Constraints

No persistence path violates the second law of thermodynamics. There are two basic mechanisms of resistance: **passive resistance** — the structure resists entropy increase by the microscopic barrier height $\Delta U_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ (chemical bonds, lattice barriers, gravitational potential wells), the corresponding window cost being recorded separately as $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$, with no need for continuous dissipation, $E_{\mathrm{flow}}=0$; **active resistance** — the structure dynamically compensates for entropy increase by a continuous energy flow $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ (metabolism, nuclear fusion) and causal processing $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, and once this is interrupted the structure begins to disintegrate at once.

**Information–time relation (a conditional coarse-grained relation).** Only when the single-denominator coarse-grained approximation $V_{\mathrm{cg}}:=I_{\mathrm{net}}T/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is explicitly adopted, and that approximation has been calibrated in the target domain, can one obtain from $V_{\mathrm{cg}}\le V_{\mathrm{max}}(D,S)$

$$
I_{\mathrm{net}} \cdot T \le V_{\mathrm{max}}(D, S) \cdot E_{\mathrm{eff}} \tag{I-6.3}
$$

Within the same accounting domain, if $V_{\mathrm{max}}(D,S)$ is an attainable supremum of $V_{\mathrm{cg}}$, then Eq. (I-6.3) is an algebraic result of that definition and the coarse-grained approximation; it is not an identity transformation of the bookkeeping decomposition (I-3.2), nor does it independently prove that $I$ and $T$ have an additional physical trade-off. To obtain a nontrivial trade-off relation, $V_{\mathrm{max}}$ must still be derived from independently verifiable physical or niche constraints, and cannot be proved by a circular definition alone.

### 6.4 Interfaces to Physical Constraints and Candidate Upper Bounds

Listed below are the external physical constraints that SRVD proposes to adopt, with candidate interfaces: the Landauer erasure bound can be used directly when its operational premises are met; formulas for computation rate must distinguish the logical operation rate from the information bit rate; the niche-capacity scaling is a phenomenological hypothesis. Together they do not constitute a proven "physical guarantee", and cannot replace calibration of SRVD's variables.

**Upper bound on the computational operation rate (Lloyd, 2000; Margolus and Levitin, 1998)**: let $\mathcal{E}_D$ denote the mean energy entering the operation-rate bound (as defined by that bound, counted as available energy above the ground state); then the logical operation rate satisfies

$$
\dot{N}_{\mathrm{op}} \le \frac{2\mathcal{E}_D}{\pi \hbar} \qquad (\mathrm{op/s}) \tag{I-6.4}
$$

The right-hand side has dimension $\mathrm{s^{-1}}$ and is a logical operation rate, not an automatically obtained $\mathrm{bit/s}$. Here Lloyd's operation-count upper-bound form for the physical computation rate is adopted, and the communication/information rate formulas in the Bremermann literature are not directly equated with a logical operation rate; for concrete application the operation definition and the system boundary must still be declared (Lloyd, 2000).

**Operation rate and bit rate must be written separately.** What Bremermann and Lloyd give are both upper bounds on the **logical operation rate**: how many irreversible logical operations (gates, flips, discriminations) can be completed per second, in units of $\mathrm{op/s}$. To turn an operation rate into an information rate, a declared **operation-to-causal-information mapping** must be inserted in between (this volume uniformly writes $\beta_{\mathrm{op}}\equiv1/\kappa_{\mathrm{map}}$, in units of $\mathrm{op/bit}$; Volume Z uses the same notation):

$$
\dot I_{\mathrm{net}} \le \frac{1}{\beta_{\mathrm{op}}}\cdot \dot{N}_{\mathrm{op}}, \qquad \beta_{\mathrm{op}}\equiv\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mathrm{map}}},\ [\beta_{\mathrm{op}}]=\mathrm{op/bit} \tag{I-6.5}
$$

$\kappa_{\mathrm{map}}$ is not the constant 1, and is not a physical constant; it depends on three things: how many distinguishable logical bits one operation changes on average (a multi-bit gate and a single-bit flip differ enormously); what fraction of them carries a difference meaningful to the target decoder (redundancy checks, error correction and ineffective refresh carry none); and at what granularity the reading protocol counts. If any of the three is undeclared, $\kappa_{\mathrm{map}}$ is unknown, and then only an upper bound on the operation rate can be written, not one on the information rate. Therefore Eq. (I-6.4) **must not** be written directly as $\dot I_{\mathrm{net}} \le \dot I_{\mathrm{Brem}}$ or $\dot I_{\mathrm{net}} \le 2\mathcal{E}_D/(\pi\hbar)$: the latter writing treats a quantity in $\mathrm{op/s}$ as one in $\mathrm{bit/s}$, which fails dimensionally. When this volume cites such upper bounds later, it always writes the operation rate first, then the mapping, then the information rate, and none of the three steps may be omitted; when the mapping is not calibrated, the information-rate entry is left blank.

**Causal-propagation time constraint (conditional)**: if $r_{\mathrm{v}}$ is operationalized as the frequency of one complete feedback loop spanning a scale $L$, and this loop is limited by causal propagation not exceeding the speed of light, then $T_{\mathrm{feedback}}\ge L/c$, and so within the model

$$
r_{\mathrm{v}} \le \frac{c}{L} \tag{I-6.6}
$$

When the above operationalization holds, an increase in scale lowers the theoretical maximum frequency of that feedback loop. Its implications for large structured organizations or for the dispersed form of an interstellar civilization are model inferences and still need independent verification.

**Niche-capacity scaling (phenomenological hypothesis)**, where $\dot{F}_{\mathrm{avail}}$ is the environmental available free-energy flux and $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}$ is the minimum entropy production rate needed to sustain the niche's resource cycling:

$$
V_{\mathrm{max}} \propto \frac{\dot{F}_{\mathrm{avail}}}{\sigma_{\mathrm{min}} \cdot \gamma_0} \tag{I-6.7}
$$

The proportionality coefficient $\alpha_{\mathrm{cap}}$ (unit $\mathrm{bit/(J\cdot K)}$, unrelated to the information elasticity exponent $\alpha$) is calibrated from data; this formula is not used as a hard physical upper bound. This scaling and the reduced-model capacity formula of §5.1.2 may be merged by the take-the-smaller rule only when the two constrain the same state variable, the same boundary and the same observation window; otherwise they are reported separately, and upper bounds of different levels must not be forcibly synthesized into one $V_{\mathrm{max}}$.

**Conditional upper bound on physical entropy and the number of distinguishable states**: when the corresponding isolated/gravitational-system assumptions are met and the boundary definitions of radius $R$ and total energy $E$ are made explicit, the Bekenstein bound can be written as

$$
I \le \frac{2\pi R E}{\hbar c \ln 2} \tag{I-6.8}
$$

and is also constrained by the holographic bound $I \le Ac^{3}/(4G\hbar\ln2)$ ($A=4\pi R^{2}$). These two bounds constrain physical entropy or the number of distinguishable states, and do not constrain arbitrary semantic-information proxies; when used, the region, effective radius or enclosing area, and the definition of energy must be listed. **Boundary of applicability of the mass-energy scale (not used as a universal disintegration threshold).** The energy scale corresponding to rest mass is $mc^{2}$, but this does not imply that the model's disintegration threshold must have $E_{\mathrm{thresh}}\le mc^{2}$, nor that a cumulative $E_{\mathrm{eff}}>mc^{2}$ within the window would cause the structure to undergo irreversible mass–energy conversion: cumulative energy consumption can consist of energy that the system continually acquires and consumes within the window, and one cannot judge that mass has been converted merely because the total exceeds the rest-mass energy. Only after the available rest-mass energy of the system, the sources of energy, the accounting boundary and the conversion mechanism have been separately delimited can a corresponding constraint be established. If the energy corresponding to a given mass is compressed within the corresponding gravitational radius, general relativity gives a constraint for horizon formation; this does not mean that all high-density computing systems will collapse, and when used in SRVD the system's mass, size, total energy and their boundary relations must be stated.

**Energy cost of extending the horizon (candidate)**: extending $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ requires acquiring causal information from a distance $R=c\,T_{\mathrm{pred}}$. Under a propagation model of isotropic point-source radiation and a fixed detection threshold, the emission power needed to sustain a detectable signal grows as the square of the distance:

$$
\text{emission power} \propto (c \cdot T_{\mathrm{pred}})^{2} \tag{I-6.9}
$$

Taking $R=c\,T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is a modeling assumption, and the proportionality coefficient is to be calibrated. In complex higher-order systems that need high-fidelity prediction, multi-stage causal chains or error correction, $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ may grow faster; when it approaches the system's total energy flow $E_{\mathrm{flow}}$, $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ can no longer be extended. This is a candidate energy mechanism for compressing the time horizon, parallel to self-referential amplification, to be calibrated.

### 6.5 The Landauer Limit: The Endogenous Lower Bound of $E_{\mathrm{compute}}$ (Stock Form) (**Candidate Corollary**)

The Landauer lower bound applies directly to the cost of logically irreversible erasure that meets the premises; only after a testable mapping has been established between the actual amount of erasure and $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ or $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ can it be connected to the corresponding channel. The processing rate constrained by the Bremermann limit likewise must adopt a basis compatible with this volume's information variables, and enters the bit rate only via $\kappa_{\mathrm{map}}$ (§6.4). Constraints in rate and power form depend on mappings not yet established, and this volume does not develop them.

$E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ is the total causal-processing energy consumption of the decoder's operation (a stock, $\mathrm{J}$). Under the condition that the amount of logically irreversible erasure has been defined, the Landauer principle gives the corresponding lower bound on computational energy consumption:

$$
E_{\mathrm{compute}} \ge k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}} \ln 2 \cdot I_{\mathrm{erased}} \tag{I-6.10}
$$

It constrains the information $I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ erased in irreversible computation, and not an arbitrarily defined $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$; the coarse-grained mixed quantity $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ likewise is not directly substituted for conversion. Only after it is made explicit that $E_{\mathrm{compute}}$ is contained in $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, and a testable mapping between $I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ has been established, can Eq. (I-6.10) be turned into an upper bound on the viability potential of the causal channel; before the mapping is established, it is only an independent constraint on computational energy consumption. The derivation below simultaneously assumes that the mapping has been established, that $E_{\mathrm{compute}}\le E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, and that all energy terms use the same observation window and accounting boundary:

$$
V_{\mathrm{c}} = \frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}\,T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}} \le \frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}\,T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}} \ln 2 \cdot I_{\mathrm{erased}}} \tag{I-6.11}
$$

In the pure information-processing limit ($E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ negligible, $V\approx V_{\mathrm{c}}$), $I_{\mathrm{causal}}\le I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ may be used only when the correspondence between $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ has been established and the two are compared under the same bit convention. Under this volume's convention of keeping bit as an explicit unit of information:

$$
V \le \frac{T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}} \ln 2} \tag{I-6.12}
$$

**Upper limit of the viability potential (conditional inference).** Under the conditions that $E_{\mathrm{compute}}\le E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, that the mapping of $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ has been established under the same bit convention with the former not larger than the latter, and that the structural maintenance energy consumption is negligible ($E_{\mathrm{compute}}\gg E_{\mathrm{struct}}$), Eq. (I-6.12) gives a candidate upper bound on $V$. The right-hand side increases with $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ and decreases with $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$; it does not itself prove that "the longer the time, the higher the minimum energy cost", which comes from the combination of the Landauer energy lower bound and a specified erasure amount. In particular $I_{\mathrm{causal}}\le I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ is an additional mapping assumption and not a relation automatically guaranteed by the Landauer principle; when one of the conditions is not met this formula does not apply, and it is not a universal conclusion.

**Three-line summary**: (i) premises — the mapping with $E_{\mathrm{compute}}\le E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{causal}}\le I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ is established, $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ is negligible, same window and same boundary; (ii) result — a candidate upper bound $V\le T_{\mathrm{causal}}/(k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2)$ ($k_BT\ln2$ has the unit $\mathrm{J/bit}$, so the quotient is in $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$); (iii) failure conditions — this formula does not apply when the mapping is not established, when $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ is not negligible, or when $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ changes markedly within the window.

***

## Chapter 7 — Construction Cost, the Thermodynamically Inert State, and the Wisdom Lever

### 7.1 Construction Cost and the Sunk-Cost Effect
