where $S_n$ is the structural state of the $n$-th generation and $\lambda_{\mathcal{F}}$ is a control parameter jointly constrained by $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{net}}$. When $\mathcal{F}$ is invariant under scale transformation, the structure shows fractal self-similarity; $\mathcal{T}$ corresponds to a discrete jump in $\lambda_{\mathcal{F}}$, breaking the old scale invariance.

### 2.4 Rule Depth: Static Definition, Depth Exponent, and Calibration Assumption

"Rule depth" in this volume refers to the **depth exponent**, which has two versions that must be named separately and used separately.

**(I) The static definition $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$.** Given an object $P$ and a tolerance $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{tol}}$, the static rule depth is the **minimum number of mutually independent rules** needed to reconstruct $P$ as a rule description within that tolerance. A "rule" means an executable rewriting instruction; "independent" means that after any one rule is removed the remaining rules cannot generate the output it covers; the tolerance is the largest permitted reconstruction error and must be stated in full when a figure is reported. A random configuration takes 0; a perfect crystal needs just one rule ("identical occupation"), $D_{\mathrm{depth}}=1$; a mechanical watch needs several rules that cannot be derived from one another, such as gear transmission, mainspring energy storage and hand indication. Repeated unfolding does not add depth: a snowflake dendrite is the same rule repeated at different scales.

**(II) The dynamical indicator.** This volume defines

$$
D_{\mathrm{depth}}^{\mathrm{dyn}}\equiv 1+\#\{\mathcal{T}\}, \tag{II-2.6}
$$

that is, the initial level is recorded as 1, and each topological transition thereafter adds one; this formula is defined only for objects whose static depth is not less than 1.

**(III) That the two are equal is a calibration assumption, not an equality derived from the definitions.** A single transition may introduce several mutually underivable rules at once (the compartmentalization of the eukaryotic cell brings the membrane system, the cytoskeleton and the nucleus together), or may merely reorganize existing rules, so $D_{\mathrm{depth}}\overset{?}{=}1+\#\{\mathcal{T}\}$ is marked a **calibration assumption**. The test procedure is to measure separately $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ (compressed description length and a determination of rule irreducibility) and $\#\{\mathcal{T}\}$ (a count of morphological innovation events, or the number of nodes of a phylogenetic event tree), regress $D_{\mathrm{depth}}=a+b\cdot\#\{\mathcal{T}\}$, and test $b=1$, $a=1$: the **prior expectation for this test should be $b>1$ rather than $b=1$**: as soon as a transition introduces on average more than one independent rule, the slope exceeds $1$ (a worked example within the model family: when each transition adds 1–3 rules and $20\%$ of transitions only reorganize, $\hat b\approx1.3$, $b=1$ is strongly rejected, $p<10^{-6}$; see RIF Appendix C Table C-6). Therefore $D_{\mathrm{depth}}^{\mathrm{dyn}}$ **is by default used on an ordinal scale** — one can say only who is deeper, not by how much; only when $b$ does not differ significantly from $1$ can it be upgraded to a cardinal scale. Writing "cardinal if the test passes" as the default path amounts to treating a test that will almost certainly fail as a routine expectation; "a transition introduces exactly one independent rule" is a special case, not the general case.

$D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ is orthogonal to $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$: an octopus underwent extensive $\mathcal{F}$ unfolding after one major transition, and so is very large but very shallow; some multilayer regulatory networks are very deep but not large. Depth further presupposes the **type–instance separation**: a type is an inheritable information template, an instance is a concrete physical individual (a rock has no separation, RNA has a primitive separation, a digital entity has a complete separation); without separation, the number of rules a structure can accumulate cannot exceed the number of rules that a single substrate can bear without disintegrating, and the substrate's lifetime is bounded by $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}$.

### 2.5 Evidence Levels and Notation

This volume's conclusions are labeled with four levels to mark their strength: those labeled **Theorem** must give a complete derivation, and those labeled **Hypothesis** may not be used as proven fact in the concluding chapter.

| Label | Conditions of use | Must be accompanied by |
| :---------- | :---------------------------- | :------------------- |
| **Theorem** | Premises explicitly listed, derivation complete, and the conclusion holds strictly under those premises | Premises, result, proof or derivation, domain of validity |
| **Proposition** | Has a formal statement, but the premises are to be calibrated, or some step of the derivation depends on an unproven modeling assumption | Premises, result, assumptions relied on, failure conditions |
| **Candidate Corollary** | Derived from some proposition under additional conditions, not independently tested | Parent proposition, additional conditions, measurable consequences |
| **Hypothesis** | An empirical or mechanistic conjecture with no formal derivation | Statement, supporting rationale, falsification condition |

A bare $I$ defaults to $I_{\mathrm{net}}$; $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ must not be interchanged; the six time notations must not be interchanged, nor may a bare $T$ be written to stand for any one of them. The complete notation is in Appendix A.

***

## Chapter 3 — A Unified Information-Evolution Equation: Continuous Flow and Discrete Jumps

The concepts of the previous chapter are assembled into a dynamical system: a segment of **continuous flow**, describing how information grows under the current decoder and how it decays toward the equilibrium state; and a string of **discrete jumps**, describing how topological transitions change state and capacity. Mixing the two into one equation containing a Dirac $\delta$ would crowd two different ledger entries, the "jump" and the "reset", into a single term.

### 3.1 The Form of the Hybrid Dynamical System

The state consists of three quantities: the amount of information $I(t)$ (bit), the decoder $D(t)$, and the capacity $I_{\max}(D)$ (bit). $D$ stays unchanged between two transitions, so the system is **piecewise smooth**: between two adjacent transitions $I$ satisfies an ordinary differential equation; at a transition time $t_j$, $I$ and the capacity change instantaneously according to the jump rules.

**(I) The continuous segment.** On $t\in(t_{j-1},t_j)$,

$$
\frac{\mathrm{d}I}{\mathrm{d}t}=\mathcal{F}(D,I)-\frac{I-I_{\mathrm{eq}}}{\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}}. \tag{II-3.1}
$$

$\mathcal{F}$ must depend explicitly on $I$: if it were only a function of $D$, the equation would grow linearly at any capacity, which contradicts diminishing marginal returns. This volume takes the capacity-constrained logistic form:

$$
\mathcal{F}(D,I)=\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\,I\left(1-\frac{I}{I_{\max}(D)}\right), \tag{II-3.2}
$$

where $\mu_{\mathcal{F}}>0$ is the unfolding-rate parameter ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$). At $I=0$ the unfolding rate is zero; as $I\to I_{\max}(D)$ it tends to zero; for $I$ slightly above $I_{\max}$ it is negative, and an over-capacity structure is pressed back within the capacity. Changing $\mathcal{F}$ changes the analytic formula of the equilibrium point, but "a positive equilibrium point exists, and its position shifts upward with capacity" holds across a broad family of unimodal saturating functions.

**(II) The jump map.** At a transition time $t_j$,

$$
I\left(t_j^{+}\right)=I\left(t_j^{-}\right)+\Delta I_{\mathcal{T},j},\qquad \Delta I_{\mathcal{T},j}\ge 0, \tag{II-3.3}
$$

where $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T},j}$ is the increment of information **actually written into the structure** by the $j$-th transition. It is **not the same quantity** as the candidate-signal reading $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$, and **the relation between the two has not been established** (**one may not write this as** $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T},j}\le\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig},j}$: a rarity reading is not a written amount, and the written amount must be calibrated separately). Only part of the presented signal can actually be written in by the new decoder.

**(III) The capacity reset.** The same transition also changes the capacity upper bound:

$$
I_{\max}\left(D_{n+1}\right)>I_{\max}\left(D_n\right),\qquad
D_{\mathrm{depth},n+1}^{\mathrm{dyn}}=D_{\mathrm{depth},n}^{\mathrm{dyn}}+1. \tag{II-3.4}
$$

The second equation holds only within a domain in which the calibration assumption has passed its test; otherwise only the first is retained, and depth records only an ordinal change.

**(IV) The impulse term and the reset operator must not be double-counted.** **The impulse term records only the instantaneous increase of the amount of information**; it changes the current value of $I$ and does not change the capacity; **the reset operator records only the change of the capacity upper bound**; it does not change the current value of $I$, so a reset **does not produce** a jump in $I$. Counting "the logistic term pulling $I$ toward the new upper limit after the capacity is raised" as a gain of the transition amounts to booking several years of future continuous growth to the account of the single instant of the transition.

From this follows the bookkeeping rule: **each transition has exactly one principal entry on the books** — (a) $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T},j}>0$ with the capacity rising (a regular transition); (b) $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T},j}=0$ with only the capacity rising (a pure-capacity transition: the rules are reorganized, the current amount of information is unchanged but the upper limit is raised). The second is not rare: the division of labor in multicellularity does not at present increase the total amount of information, but raises the future capacity ceiling by an order of magnitude.

**(V) Existence and regularity of solutions.** On $(0,I_{\max})$ $\mathcal{F}$ is continuously differentiable and the decay term is linear, so within each continuous segment the solution exists and is unique; jump times are triggered by the gating conditions of Chapter 4, and the whole system is a hybrid automaton. This volume sets a **minimum transition interval** $\tau_{\mathcal{T}}>0$: structural reorganization needs construction time; without it the model permits infinitely dense transitions (Zeno behavior), and infinitely dense transitions are physically equivalent to continuous change.

### 3.2 The Physical Meaning of the Three Terms

**Recursive unfolding** is continuous growth under the current decoder, and as $I\to I_{\max}(D)$ the marginal return tends to zero; crystals, viruses and replicating molecules operate mainly through $\mathcal{F}$. **Topological transition** is the discrete jump of absorbing a signal and reorganizing the decoder: **the marginal cost is paid once and is high ($E_{\mathrm{build}}$), and the marginal benefit is released over the long term**, which is exactly why it is a gating event. **Thermodynamic decay**: absent active maintenance, any ordered structure decays toward $I_{\mathrm{eq}}$ with characteristic time $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$, and a purely passive structure ($E_{\mathrm{causal}}=0$) is governed by this term alone; the relation of $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$ to temperature, barrier and substrate must be given by the specific mechanism (for example, a thermally activated process gives $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}\propto e^{\Delta U_{\mathrm{barrier}}/k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}}$), and it is a parameter to be calibrated.

### 3.3 Reduction to Special Cases

**Passive crystal.** $\mathcal{F}=0$, no transition, and the equation reduces to $\mathrm{d}I/\mathrm{d}t=-(I-I_{\mathrm{eq}})/\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$. Decay is suppressed by $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}\gg k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}$, $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$ is extremely large, and $I\approx\mathrm{const}$: the persistence of a crystal consists in a decay time far longer than the observation window.

**Replicating molecule.** $\mathcal{F}>0$ (template-directed replication), and transitions are rare. Setting the right-hand side of Eq. (II-3.1) to zero gives the positive equilibrium point

$$
I^{*}=\frac{I_{\max}(D)}{2}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}}\right)+\sqrt{\left(1-\frac{1}{\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{4I_{\mathrm{eq}}}{I_{\max}(D)\,\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}}}\right],
$$

and taking $I_{\mathrm{eq}}=0$ reduces it to $I^{*}=I_{\max}(D)\left(1-1/(\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}})\right)$, for which the necessary and sufficient condition for a positive fixed point is $\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}>1$. **When $I_{\mathrm{eq}}>0$ this condition is no longer necessary**: thermal-background injection allows a positive fixed point even when $\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}<1$, and the criterion must use the complete form above. Stability is given by $f'(I^{*})=-\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\sqrt{\left(1-1/(\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}})\right)^{2}+4I_{\mathrm{eq}}/(I_{\max}\mu_{\mathcal{F}}\tau_{\mathrm{decay}})}<0$, which holds for all parameters that make the radicand real. From this follows a threshold that can be decided by criterion: **in a system without thermal-background injection, the product of the unfolding rate and the decay time must exceed 1 for the structure to be maintained above the equilibrium state**; cryopreservation works precisely because cooling increases $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$.

**Actively evolving organism.** All three terms operate: the first two drive $I$ upward, the third requires continuing maintenance cost to be paid by $E_{\mathrm{survival}}+E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, and the equilibrium point lies where the growth rate equals the decay rate. A **D-lock-in system** has $\mathcal{F}\to 0$, with transitions blocked, and the equation degenerates to pure decay; "lock-in" is a dynamical statement (the net growth rate tends to zero) and not an inevitable structural collapse — a locked-in system can maintain the status quo for a long time with a reliable energy supply.

### 3.4 Lower Bound on Maintenance Power (**Proposition**)

The decay term in Eq. (II-3.1) is a purely informational term and contains no energy itself; to say that "the decay term sets the minimum energy requirement for persistence", one must supply a relation connecting informational decay with energy dissipation. What this volume supplies is a conditional proposition.

**Proposition 3.1 (Lower Bound on Maintenance Power)**
- **Premises**: (i) the system maintains its information by "erase and rewrite", that is, each maintained bit is erased and rewritten at least once in every $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$; (ii) erasure is a logically irreversible operation, whose per-bit energy lower bound is the Landauer bound $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}=k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln 2$ ($\mathrm{J/bit}$); (iii) the ambient temperature $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ is constant within the observation window; (iv) what is counted is the heat-generation lower bound of the erasure stage, excluding the extra overheads of writing, transmission, error correction and cooling.
- **Result**: the average power needed to maintain all $I$ bits satisfies

$$
\dot E_{\mathrm{maintain}}\ \ge\ k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln 2\cdot\frac{I}{\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}}. \tag{II-3.5}
$$

- **Derivation**: By (i), the number of bits to be erased in one decay period is $I$; by (ii), each bit consumes no less than $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}$, so the energy in a period is no less than $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}I$; by (iii), when this energy is spent uniformly over $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$ the power is no less than $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}I/\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$.
- **Assumptions relied on**: Eq. (II-3.5) depends entirely on premise (i). If the system uses reversible computation, external storage, error-correcting codes (with a refresh period longer than $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$) or a replay mechanism, premise (i) is violated, and Eq. (II-3.5) no longer gives a valid lower bound — the true maintenance power can be below this value, or above it because of error-correction overhead, in an indeterminate direction.
- **Failure conditions**: (a) information is maintained by non-erasing means (topologically protected quantum storage, reversible logic); (b) the temperature changes markedly within the window, in which case the integral form $\dot E_{\mathrm{maintain}}\ge k_B\ln 2\cdot\overline{T_{\mathrm{thermo}}I}/\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$ must be used; (c) when applied to biological systems, the accounting basis of metabolic power (chemical free energy rather than heat dissipation) differs from this formula's, and the two cannot be compared directly.

**Order-of-magnitude check.** Taking $I=10^{12}$ bit, $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}=10^{4}$ s, $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}=300$ K, then $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\approx2.87\times10^{-21}$ J, and Eq. (II-3.5) gives $\dot E_{\mathrm{maintain}}\gtrsim2.9\times10^{-13}$ W, about nine orders of magnitude below the maintenance power of real organisms or chips (typically of order $10^{-4}$ W). **The Landauer bound is an extremely weak lower bound on the maintenance cost of real systems**; the real cost is determined by chemical kinetics, material fatigue, error correction and heat dissipation. Eq. (II-3.5) gives a floor that cannot be crossed: any scheme claiming that "maintenance cost is below this value" must violate one of premises (i) to (iii).

The whole system can be viewed as a recursive dynamical system corrected by thermodynamic dissipation: $\mathcal{F}$ corresponds to attractor dynamics, $\mathcal{T}$ corresponds to bifurcation events, and the ascent sequence is therefore an **energy-gated bifurcation cascade**.

***

## Chapter 4 — Three Conditions for a Complexity Transition

Whether a topological transition occurs is jointly gated by three conditions. The three criteria differ greatly in operability: condition one depends on statistical readings, condition two on energy accounting, and condition three on cross-decoder comparison of $V$. Wherever comparability is not satisfied, this volume reports only a qualitative transition and does not report a leverage value.

### 4.1 Condition One: Signal Availability (with the Three-Layer Decomposition of $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$)

The original statement of condition one is: a paradigm-breaking signal must exist in the environment that the current decoder cannot extract ($D_n(I_{\mathrm{orig}})\approx0$) and the reorganized decoder can ($D_{n+1}(I_{\mathrm{orig}})\gg0$). This statement has a hidden circularity: if $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ can be confirmed only ex post, it cannot at the same time serve as a gating variable before the transition. This volume splits it into three layers, and only the first enters the gate.

**Layer one: the candidate paradigm-breaking signal $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ (an observable proxy).** It is defined as the rarity of $D_n$'s prediction residual on the target relative to its own historical residual distribution:

$$
\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}\equiv-\log_2\Pr_{B_n}\left(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{res}}\ge\varepsilon_{\mathrm{obs}}\right), \tag{II-4.1}
$$

where $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{res}}$ is the residual (the measure must be declared in advance), $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{obs}}$ is the present observed value, and $B_n$ is the historical residual distribution (which must be frozen before the transition and may not be re-estimated with a sample containing the present observation); the unit is bit, and the meaning is "how rare this failure is".

**Threshold and false-alarm rate.** The gating rule must be pre-registered before observation: (i) a significance level $\alpha_0$ (for example $0.01$), corresponding to $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}\ge-\log_2\alpha_0$; (ii) at least $m$ crossings of the gate in $N$ independent retests (for example $N=3$, $m=2$), to suppress a single anomalous reading; **the convention relative to Volume I**: Volume I §2.1 and §5.1.6 (C1) use "$k$ consecutive threshold exceedances" (example $\alpha_0=10^{-3}$), and this volume uses "at least $m$ out of $N$" (example $\alpha_0=0.01$); both are instances of a gating rule, with false-alarm rates of about $N_{\mathrm{test}}\alpha_0^{k}$ and $C(N,m)\alpha_0^{m}$ respectively. Each way of writing is legitimate, with the false-alarm rate as above; **which one is adopted must be fixed before observation, and aligned with Volume I's convention**; (iii) when several candidate signals are tested in the same window, $\alpha_0$ must be adjusted downward according to the number of tests. If any of the three is missing, a high reading says only that "some observation was very surprising".

**Layer two: the validated added information $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ (an ex post confirmed quantity).** Only after the transition has succeeded and the new decoder has reproduced a stable extraction capacity on an independent sample is the added information that the transition actually brought recorded as $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ (bit) and reported with an interval. The former is information that has been realized, and the latter is "the degree of surprise"; the values need not be equal.

**Layer three: the topological transition event $\mathcal{T}$.** The event of structural reorganization itself; it is an event and not a quantity, and when used with Eq. (II-3.3) its magnitude is given by $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T}}$.

The gate uses only layer one, and **crossing the gate does not mean that a transition will necessarily occur**: at $\alpha_0=0.01$, even with no real new paradigm, about one percent of windows will be misjudged; conversely, not crossing the gate does not exclude a transition, since low statistical power can make a real signal unreadable. Condition one is therefore an **exclusionary criterion**.

### 4.2 Condition Two: Energy Availability

The one-time construction cost must not exceed the available free-energy budget:

$$
E_{\mathrm{build}}\left(D_{n+1}\right)<E_{\mathrm{available}}. \tag{II-4.2}
$$

Here $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ is the one-time physical energy needed to create or upgrade a structure (J), $E_{\mathrm{available}}$ is the upper limit of energy the system can currently mobilize (J), and $E_{\mathrm{available}}\le E_{\max}$; $E_{\max}$ is the environment's ceiling, and $E_{\mathrm{available}}$ is the part the system can actually draw on.

This is a hard threshold, and it also explains why most organisms have never made a transition: the energy cost of reorganizing a decoder is usually several orders of magnitude greater than that of running the current one. Construction cost must be paid within the construction period, and the construction period is often longer than one generation, so the cost must be apportioned over many individuals or generations (the nests of social insects, intergenerational transmission of knowledge).

### 4.3 Condition Three: The Wisdom Lever (with the Comparability Premises for $V$)

The viability-potential gain from a transition must exceed a minimum threshold relative to its cost:

$$
L_{\mathrm{lev}}=\frac{V\left(D_{n+1}\right)-V\left(D_n\right)}{E_{\mathrm{build}}}>L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}. \tag{II-4.3}
$$

$L_{\mathrm{lev}}$ is the **wisdom lever coefficient**, with dimension $\mathrm{(bit\cdot s/J)/J}$; $L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$ is a contextual threshold of the same dimension. This condition captures competitive pressure: in an environment where the current decoder still yields returns ($I<I_{\max}(D_n)$), the system has no thermodynamic incentive to bear the cost of reorganization.

**Comparability premises for $V$.** The numerator of Eq. (II-4.3) is the difference of two $V$ values, and is a comparable value only when the following four are satisfied at the same time.

1. **The same target**: the two must address the same class of task or persistence problem; the old decoder's $V$ on foraging minus the new decoder's $V$ on navigation yields a task difference.
2. **The same observation window**: the $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ of the two must be integrated over the same time span; comparing with a short window systematically underestimates the benefit of the transition.
3. **The same energy-apportionment rule**: the ways of apportioning construction cost, maintenance cost and shared infrastructure must be consistent.
4. **A common reference protocol**: the two-channel readings must come from the same protocol, the same background reference and the same tolerance; readings across protocols cannot be subtracted.

When the four are not satisfied, this volume **reports only a qualitative transition** — stating that the topology has changed, the capacity has been raised and the extractable causal dimensions have increased, without reporting a numerical value of $L_{\mathrm{lev}}$.

### 4.4 Decidability Table of the Three Conditions

Judged in row order, because the observation cost of condition one is lowest and that of condition three highest.

**Table 4.1 Decidability Table of the Three Conditions for a Topological Transition**

| Condition | Criterion | Required observables | Phenotype upon failure |
| :------- | :--------------- | :----------------- | :----------------- |
| **I. Signal availability** | Under the frozen background $B_n$, $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}\ge-\log_2\alpha_0$, with at least $m$ of $N$ independent retests crossing the gate | Residual series $\{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{res}}\}$; historical residual distribution $B_n$; pre-registered $\alpha_0$, $N$, $m$; multiple-comparison correction scheme | No new dimension appears for a long time; morphological stasis (the deep-sea sessile type) |
| **II. Energy availability** | $E_{\mathrm{build}}(D_{n+1})<E_{\mathrm{available}}$, and power paid during the construction period does not exceed $\dot E_{\mathrm{available}}$ | Construction period $\tau_{\mathrm{build}}$; energy accounting of $E_{\mathrm{build}}$; the time curve of $\dot E_{\mathrm{available}}$; cost-apportionment mechanism | Construction stops midway and the structure is incomplete; or never starts; or is forced to scale down |
| **III. Wisdom lever** | $L_{\mathrm{lev}}=\Delta V/E_{\mathrm{build}}>L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$, with all four comparability premises satisfied | $V(D_n)$ and $V(D_{n+1})$ under the same target, the same window and the same apportionment rule; $E_{\mathrm{build}}$; $L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$ | The new decoder is abandoned after being built (a vestigial organ); the lineage reverts to a simplified route |

There are dependencies among the three rows: when condition two is not met the numerator of condition three cannot be realized, and when condition one is not met the numerator of condition three cannot be attributed.

### 4.5 Why the Three Are Necessary Conditions (**Proposition**)

**Proposition 4.1 (Necessity of the Three Conditions)**
- **Premises**: (i) a level transition is defined as a discrete event in which the decoder's topology changes and the capacity upper bound is raised (the jump map of Section 3.1); (ii) $V$ is the order parameter of selection, that is, under the same constraint geometry configurations with lower $V$ are statistically more likely to be eliminated; (iii) construction takes time, and the structure must sustain itself during the construction period.
- **Result**: If any condition is not satisfied, the level transition does not occur (or is not statistically retained). The three together are a subset of the set of necessary conditions, and do not constitute a sufficient condition.
- **Assumptions relied on**: Assumption (ii) is a statement of selection theory, not a corollary of thermodynamic first principles, and it needs additional assumptions about competition and replication. Without a competitive environment, the selection pressure on $V$ vanishes, and the necessity of condition three weakens accordingly — transitions can then occur but are not "retained".
- **Failure conditions**: If there is a path to capacity expansion that does not go through decoder reorganization (such as plugging directly into external storage without changing the internal rules), the statements of conditions one and three need rewriting; this volume assigns such cases to "externalization", whose criteria are discussed separately.

**Step one: without a signal, the jump map has no input.** By Eq. (II-3.3), the effect of a transition is an instantaneous increase of $I$. If the environment contains no regularity that the old decoder cannot read, a newly built decoder has nothing to do: the capacity is raised, but what can be extracted is the same as for the old decoder, and $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T}}=0$. Such a "purely formal reorganization" does not constitute a level transition — by definition (i), it requires an increase of the extractable causal dimensions. Signal availability is therefore a condition of **possibility**.

**Step two: without energy, construction cannot be completed.** By premise (iii), construction takes time and the structure must sustain itself during it. If $E_{\mathrm{build}}>E_{\mathrm{available}}$, construction either cannot start or stops midway; the intermediate form at the stopping point is usually functionally incomplete (a half-built eye supplies a low-quality signal while still consuming maintenance cost), and so falls below the pre-transition level in $V$. This is "worse than the starting point after failure". Energy availability is therefore a condition of **feasibility**.

**Step three: without leverage, a transition, even if it occurs, is not retained.** If the new decoder's $V$ is not higher than the old decoder's, then under assumption (ii) the lineage carrying the new decoder is at a disadvantage in competition; even if it appears by chance, it will be eliminated in subsequent selection. The leverage condition is therefore a condition of **desirability**.

The three steps give a division of labor: possibility, feasibility and desirability, corresponding respectively to "is there an object", "is there a resource" and "is there a net gain".

**Why they are not sufficient conditions.** Satisfying all three at once may still not produce a transition. First, **probabilistic**: a transition needs one successful reorganization event, reorganization is a search process, and the success rate depends on the size of the search space and the degree to which the structure can vary; the conditions guarantee only that "it is worth searching", not that "it will be found". Second, **temporal**: the three windows of signal appearance, energy abundance and sufficient competitive pressure need not coincide. Third, **competitive**: if another lineage has already occupied the same niche at lower cost, the first to transition does not necessarily win.

### 4.6 Classification of Failure Modes

Each of the three conditions corresponds to a class of failure; adding the class "conditions satisfied but marginal returns not exhausted" gives four main mechanisms of staying put.

| Failure mode | Mechanism | Examples |
| :------- | :------------------------------------ | :--------------- |
| **D-lock-in** (this volume's type) | Gradient saturation of the decoder ($\nabla D\to0$, that is, the marginal gain of continued unfolding tends to zero once $I$ approaches $I_{\max}$), together with $E_{\mathrm{build}}>E_{\mathrm{available}}$ blocking transition. **Reminder of homonymy**: the "D-lock-in" of Volume IV §3.1 refers to "approach lock-in / decay domain" ($\mu_{\mathrm{rec}}/\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$ close to or below 1) and is not the same mechanism as this row's "gradient saturation plus insufficient budget"; this volume's branch may be called **gradient lock-in**. Note also: the single item $E_{\mathrm{build}}>E_{\mathrm{available}}$ is the **energy lock-in** listed separately below; the two kinds of lock-in can hold at the same time, and must be reported separately. When these two kinds of lock-in are cited across volumes, the volume number must be given, to avoid homonymy | Horseshoe crabs (morphologically almost unchanged for 450 million years), cave fish that have lost the visual decoder |
| **Signal absence** | No usable signal in the current niche ($\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ stays below the threshold for a long time) | Deep-sea sessile organisms in a stable environment |
| **Energy lock-in** | $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ exceeds the mobilizable free energy $E_{\mathrm{available}}$ | Ectotherms with conservative energy budgets; large land animals whose construction period is longer than the generation length |
| **Marginal returns not exhausted** | $L_{\mathrm{lev}}<L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$: the current decoder still yields positive returns, and reorganization is not worthwhile | Organisms in highly stable, resource-rich environments |

The four have different observable signs (morphological stasis, a stable environment, variation without construction, marginal returns still high), and their implications for intervention differ accordingly.

***

## Chapter 5 — The Ascent Sequence: Shifts in Energy Dominance

Looking across the history of the organization of matter, each major complexity transition corresponds to a shift of the dominant component in the effective energy consumption $E_{\mathrm{eff}}=E_{\mathrm{barrier}}+E_{\mathrm{survival}}+E_{\mathrm{causal}}$.

### 5.1 Clarifying the "Order" Direction: The Crystal Route and the Life Route

The order of a crystal and the order of life are **two different ordering mechanisms**.

The order of a crystal rests on **averaging**: a great many atoms occupy identical periodic positions, passively locked in place by a deep barrier $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$, with few distinguishable configurations each of which is extremely stable; it is an order of **symmetry maximization**. The order of life rests on **differentiation**: DNA replication, protein folding and signaling pathways each have their own function, actively maintained by a continuing energy flow $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$, with many distinguishable configurations each of which needs continuing investment; it is an order of **symmetry-breaking maximization**. The two share one set of $V$ formulas and sit at the two ends of the two channels:

$$
V_{\mathrm{s}}=\frac{I_{\mathrm{struct}}\cdot T_{\mathrm{struct}}}{E_{\mathrm{barrier}}+E_{\mathrm{survival}}}\qquad\text{(crystal type: persisting by stillness)} \tag{II-5.1}
$$

$$
V_{\mathrm{c}}=\frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}\cdot T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}}\qquad\text{(life type: persisting by flow)} \tag{II-5.2}
$$

From crystal to life is a **switch of ordering mechanism**. Stickiness stems from two energy terms: $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ locks in once, and on exit the structure remains; $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ is maintained by a continuing flow, and on exit the structure disintegrates. So complexification is sticky in living lineages — because the exit cost is high.

**The correct statement of "irreversibility".** Writing this asymmetry as "once life took the direction opposite to the crystal, it has never turned back to this day" is writing a statistical tendency as a universal necessity. The statement of this volume is: **within the chosen sample and observation window, lineages that have entered the mode of active maintenance show a statistical tendency to evolve toward a higher share of $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$; reversion events exist but are rare.** Examples of reversion: crustaceans that live as parasites lose their compound eyes and part of their nervous systems; endosymbiotic bacteria lose large numbers of genes and hand their metabolic functions over to the host. "Turning back" is entirely possible mechanistically, but is not statistically the majority — reversion is usually accompanied by a narrowing of the niche, and narrowing in turn raises extinction risk.

### 5.2 The Ascent Sequence

The sequence characterizes each level by four items: "key transition + information state + energy dominance + new capability". It is a classification tool, not a timetable: different lineages cross the same level at different times, and some lineages never reach it.

| Stage | Key transition | $I$ state | Energy dominance | New capability |
| :-------- | :----------- | :----------- | :------------ | :------------- |
| **Crystal lattice** | Initial inorganic order | $I_{\mathrm{struct}}>0$, $\eta_D\approx0$ | $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ (passive barrier locking) | Stable structure |
| **Organic chemistry** | Inorganic $\to$ organic | $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ expands | $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}+E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ (active maintenance begins) | Chemical diversity |
| **Unicellular** | Molecule $\to$ self-maintenance | $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ expands explosively; $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ appears | $E_{\mathrm{survival}}+E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ (metabolism plus information processing) | Self-replication, autopoiesis |
| **Multicellular** | Independence $\to$ division of labor | $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ expands through communication | $E_{\mathrm{survival}}+E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ (communication cost rises) | Specialization and cooperation |
| **Nervous system** | Reaction $\to$ prediction | $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ grows exponentially; $T_{\mathrm{pred}}>0$ appears | $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ share rises sharply | Prediction and planning |
| **Language, civilization** | Individual $\to$ collective memory | New information injected; $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ externalized | $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ becomes the dominant cost | Cumulative cultural knowledge |
| **Artificial computation** | Biological $\to$ silicon-based recursion | $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ recursive explosion | $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ dominates; runaway risk emerges | High-speed learning, recursive self-improvement |

**Shift in energy dominance.** The dominant component moves from $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ (passive, zero maintenance) to $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ (active metabolic maintenance), and then to $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ (information processing and prediction). Each shift increases vulnerability to supply interruption, and also raises the upper limit of achievable $V$. This sequence describes lineages that take the $I$ route; lineages that choose the $T$ or $E$ route likewise satisfy the $\max V$ principle.

**The decoder–execution flywheel.** A stronger decoder extracts more information, achieves more efficient survival behavior, enlarges the habitable range, is exposed to new signals, and creates pressure for further upgrading. The flywheel is bounded by the three conditions: it stalls when the signal is exhausted or the energy-payment capacity is saturated, and the system enters D-lock-in or energy lock-in.

**Boundary of applicability.** The sequence is induced from two samples, the history of life on Earth and the history of human technology, and its claim to universality can only be "a similar shift in energy dominance should be observed in other samples"; "upward" holds only in windows of the order of a billion years, and within a window of a million years most lineages show fluctuation and stasis; the seven-level division is a product of coarse-graining, and the grain should serve the research question.

***

## Chapter 6 — Rule Depth, Information Geometry, and the Limits of Linear Ranking

### 6.1 Why Linear Complexity Rankings Fail

"Humans are more complex than bacteria" is right in one sense: humans do require more generating rules. What is wrong is to assign a structure a single numerical value $I=\log_2\Omega$ and rank linearly on it — the state spaces of different systems are incommensurable, $\Omega_{\mathrm{crystal}}\ne\Omega_{\mathrm{DNA}}\ne\Omega_{\mathrm{language}}$; putting the logarithms of the three spaces on one axis forces numbers in three different units into a single row. This volume therefore abandons linear ranking and uses coordinates instead.

### 6.2 The Three-Quantity Coordinate System of Rules

The information organization of a persistent structure is characterized by three independent coordinates. They are **discrete structural coordinates** and are not the same set of quantities as the continuous axes that characterize the decoder: the former describe the generative structure of the object itself, and the latter describe the efficiency with which a subject reads the object.

$$
I\;\longrightarrow\;\left(L_D,\;D_{\mathrm{depth}},\;F_{\mathrm{scale}}\right) \tag{II-6.1}
$$

**Rule length $L_D$ (bit).** The code length of the most compact rule set when the object is reconstructed under tolerance $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{tol}}$. It differs from static depth: $L_D$ measures the **total length** of the rule set, and $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ measures the **number of irreducible rules**. An object may have very few rules each of which is very long, or very many rules each of which is very short.

**Rule depth $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ (dimensionless).** The number of independent rules needed to reconstruct the object; the static version follows the definition of Section 2.4, and the dynamical version is $1+\#\{\mathcal{T}\}$; the version must be stated when a figure is reported.

**Scale factor $F_{\mathrm{scale}}$ (dimensionless).** The number of times, or the scale span over which, the same rule is recursively unfolded; a snowflake dendrite has a very large $F_{\mathrm{scale}}$ and a very low depth.

The three are orthogonal, so there is no "total complexity score".

**Configuration capacity** is the base-2 logarithm (bit) of the number of distinguishable configurations the structure can present while retaining its identity or function: it measures "how many states can be borne", and cannot be subtracted from "the rarity of one specific state". It also explains the apparent paradox of bacteria: a bacterium is a feasible configuration of low depth and low maintenance, and its $V$ may exceed that of a far more complex organism — its $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is extremely low while the numerator $I\cdot T$ is also low, and which product is larger depends on the specific readings.

### 6.3 Hard Limits on Cross-Domain Quantification

The $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ values of DNA sequences, neural-network parameters and social institutions are measured under incommensurable protocols. **Within a domain**: when the same decoder substrate $D$ and state space $\Omega$ are locked, relative quantitative comparison is valid. **Across domains**: only qualitative structural comparison can be guaranteed — one may assert that a nervous system has a higher rule depth than a unicellular organism, without claiming any specific ratio of $I$. **Prohibited** is cross-domain numerical ranking (such as "the human brain $=10^{15}$ bit, bacteria $=10^{6}$ bit"): no independent observation can test readings under two protocols at once.

### 6.4 Minimum Requirements for a Measurement Protocol

If any one is missing, the reported value should not enter a cross-system comparison: **lock the decoder substrate** (the type of $D$, the way $I_{\max}(D)$ and $\eta_D$ are estimated), **lock the state space** (the definition of $\Omega$, the rule for judging distinguishable configurations and the granularity of discretization), **lock the tolerance** (not reporting the tolerance is equivalent to not reporting the number), **lock the background**, **lock the window**; in addition, **report intervals and not point values**, and **distinguish ordinal from cardinal**.

***

## Chapter 7 — The Evolutionary Tree Has No Predetermined Summit

### 7.1 Evolution Is Gradient Search, Not Progress

Evolution is a gradient search in $V$ space: structures that achieve a higher viability potential under local thermodynamic constraints are statistically more likely to persist. There is no goal, only gradient.

First, rule depth can decrease as well as increase: when the cost of maintaining a constraint layer exceeds its contribution to $V$, that layer is shed. When a host bears the metabolic burden, a parasite simplifies its morphology; when photonic signals no longer carry usable information, cave fish lose their visual organs. These are local optimizations under the $\max V$ constraint, formalized as a negative topological transition:

$$
\mathcal{T}^{-}:\quad \Delta D_{\mathrm{depth}}^{\mathrm{dyn}}=-1 \quad\text{when}\quad \frac{\partial V}{\partial D_{\mathrm{depth}}}<0 \tag{II-7.1}
$$

The $-1$ in Eq. (II-7.1) depends on the calibration assumption of the depth exponent; when that has not passed its test, the equation means only "depth falls by one level".

Second, the evolutionary sequence is bidirectional:

$$
\cdots\xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{F}\cdots\qquad(\text{ascent}) \tag{II-7.2}
$$

$$
\cdots\xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{-}}\mathcal{F}^{-}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}^{-}}\cdots\qquad(\text{simplification}) \tag{II-7.3}
$$

$\mathcal{F}^{-}$ denotes "continued recursive unfolding under a reduced capacity". The direction is determined by the $V$ gradient and not by the notion of progress; the history of any real lineage is an alternation of the two, and the alternation ratio depends on how frequently the constraint geometry changes.

### 7.2 The Current Position of Humans

Humans are the first biological structure to externalize the decoder on a large scale to non-biological substrates (writing, mathematics, computation). This represents a topological transition possibly of the same order as the nervous system: previously the ceiling of rule depth was set by an individual's learning capacity and transmission bandwidth, and thereafter by the capacity and retrieval efficiency of collective storage media. The shift to artificial computation is a further externalization of the decoder — the $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ of the next-layer decoder is now distributed across global computing infrastructure. Whether the leverage condition is met is the core open empirical question in the development of artificial intelligence; this volume gives only the criterion and the quantities to be measured: a comparison of $V$ under the same target, the same window and the same apportionment rule, plus a complete accounting of construction and alignment costs.

This transition reveals the divide between $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{net}}$. At the end of the Cretaceous the dinosaurs had very high $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and a considerable stock of $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, but very low $I_{\mathrm{net}}$; when the negative-effect information shock $I^{-}$ exceeded the scale the stock could absorb, the buffer failed completely — a high stock raises the **threshold of resistance to shocks**, not infinite immunity. The difference of humans lies not in a larger $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ but in the capacity, brought by the externalization of $I_{\mathrm{net}}$, to **actively reshape the way $I^{-}$ acts**.

### 7.3 The Thermodynamic Ceiling Surfaces: Endogenous Constraints on Ascent

Each transition demands a higher core **power density** ($\mathrm{W/kg}$ or $\mathrm{W/cm^2}$), the upper limit being set rigidly by the medium's **maximum dissipation rate** $\dot E_{\mathrm{diss}}^{\max}$ (determined jointly by thermal conductivity and geometric area); each of the two directions yields one ceiling surface.

**Structural direction: the gravity tax.** As body mass increases, the share of support and locomotion costs in $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ rises with scale (Galileo's geometric scaling); maintenance power per unit mass falls with mass (the WBE metabolic scaling law $B\propto M^{3/4}$, sublinear, verified in the biological domain). When $\Delta E_{\mathrm{survival}}/\Delta I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ exceeds the elasticity threshold, the $V$ return on enlarging the body turns negative. Dinosaurs, elephants and large mammals may already be approaching this line: the $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ needed to keep increasing $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ exceeds the mobilizable budget, and the energy-availability condition is endogenously violated.

**Net effective causal information direction: the dissipation wall.** Raising power density produces waste heat. Every erased bit releases at least $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}=k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2$ ($\mathrm{J/bit}$, Landauer, 1961); waste heat accumulates with processing rate, while dissipation is constrained by thermal conductivity and geometric area. The nature of the constraint must be made clear: the Landauer bound is a **per-bit lower bound on the energy of erasure**, and must be multiplied by the erasure rate to give power; multiplying the Landauer bound directly by an "effective information processing rate" amounts to tacitly assuming that every processed bit is an irreversibly erased bit. This volume retains only the weak form: given an erasure rate $\dot I_{\mathrm{erase}}$, $\dot Q_{\mathrm{heat}}\ge E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\dot I_{\mathrm{erase}}$.

Under this weak form: the power density of the mammalian cerebral cortex is about $10\ \mathrm{mW/cm^2}$, and the power density of advanced chips is already close to the limits of their respective packaging and cooling schemes; breaking through this wall requires a change at the level of the medium (cryogenic computing, photonic interconnects, liquid-cooling restructuring), not iterative optimization of the current architecture. This is an **engineering trend statement**, and depends on the condition of "current cooling schemes".

**The thermodynamic meltdown wall as a Great Filter (candidate corollary).** If the heat-dissipation bottleneck cannot be broken by a medium transition, a civilization will face a hard threshold: $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ keeps rising while the gain in $I_{\max}(D_{n+1})$ tends to saturate, and the leverage condition is violated. This may be a thermodynamic form of the "Great Filter"; its consequences can be derived within the model but cannot be tested empirically — there is only one sample. A candidate path to break through it is to find a new physical substrate on which $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ does not grow superlinearly with depth.

### 7.4 The Question of Extraterrestrial Intelligence

A more advanced extraterrestrial civilization is not necessarily larger or more energetic, but would operate in causal dimensions inaccessible to human decoders; its detection signature is anomalously high assembly complexity. Non-detection does not mean that humans are the most complex — it may simply be that the combination of conditions needed for a transition is rare enough.

### 7.5 Convergence: Same Constraints, Finite Feasible Solutions

Convergence recurs in evolution: the eye evolved independently many times in different lineages; the decoder extended from oral rituals to writing, money and digital protocols. This framework's explanation is a selection argument: under finite energy, finite time and finite information-processing capacity, configurations that can maintain ordered structure at lower cost are statistically more likely to persist. This argument is compatible with the second law, but is not its direct corollary.

**Mechanism and objective function.** Selection is the mechanism, and $\max V$ is an objective-function-style summary of the result of selection (this volume's meta-postulate: under given constraints, the structures that are retained have a viability potential no lower than those that are eliminated), and for active-phase structures it is at the same time the direction actually optimized. $I/E$ is only a local projection of $V$ at fixed $T$; when $T$ can vary, lengthening persistence time is another route — tribes rely on kinship, states on institutions, firms on legal fictions, and looking only at $I/E$ cannot explain why states outlast tribes, whereas $V$ can. At the level of local strategy, which route to take is a real choice; the selection layer does not change the standard, and only selects among the consequences of these choices.

**What converges is the type of solution, not the destination.** Because the constraints are the same and the feasible solutions are finite, there is convergence; this does not contradict "no predetermined summit". Qualitative inferences about the future belong to the level of candidate corollaries.

***

## Chapter 8 — A Dialogue with Existing Frameworks

Eight external frameworks provide support, precedent or local validation, and each also leaves a gap that this volume fills.

| Framework | Support provided to this volume | Gap filled by this volume |
| :-------------- | :----------------------- | :--------------------- |
| Dissipative-structure theory (Prigogine, 1967) | Confirms that energy flow can locally produce ordered structure, providing a physical basis for the emergence of $V_{\mathrm{c}}>0$ from $V_{\mathrm{c}}=0$ | Does not treat information accumulation across transitions, and does not supply the dynamics of decoder evolution |
| Autocatalytic sets (Kauffman, 1993; Eigen, 1971) | Identifies the conditions for a unicellular-level transition at the chemical level | Confined to the moment of origin |
| Metabolic scaling theory (West, Brown & Enquist, 1997) | A precise scaling law within the biological domain, usable as a reference for the growth of $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ with mass | Not extended across levels or to cultural evolution |
| Energy rate density (Chaisson, 2001) | Records the monotonic increase of energy flow per unit mass, qualitatively consistent with the sequence of shifts in energy dominance | Energy is a necessary, not a sufficient, condition |
| Free-energy principle (Friston et al., 2017) | Describes the decoder's steady-state optimization under fixed boundary conditions | Does not treat the dissolution and reconstruction of the boundary itself, the transition event |
| Assembly theory (Cronin & Walker, 2021) | Provides an operational complexity measure, usable as an empirical proxy of rule depth | A static measure; no dynamical equations or transition conditions |
| Ecological thermodynamics (the exergy approach, the Jørgensen school) | Provides a measure of order at the ecological scale, "free-energy distance from the dead state", which with this volume's $V$ belongs to "energy-normalized order" | Contains no decoder and does not distinguish the structural channel from the causal channel; values depend strongly on the choice of dead-state reference |
| Resilience theory (Holling, 1973) | Distinguishes "whether the system can return to the basin of attraction after perturbation" from "whether the stock is stable", providing an external comparison for lock-in states | Asks only whether return is possible, does not measure the level of the stock, and does not explain the depressed steady-state $V^{*}$ |
| Cultural evolution (Henrich, 2015; Mesoudi, 2011) | Provides mechanistic evidence on transmission, selection and cumulative culture, supporting rule depth and cross-generational accumulation | Gives mechanisms of transmission but no cost accounting, and so cannot compare the energy books of "compiling versus inheriting" |

**Support and gap are two sides of the same thing**: a framework's focus determines its blind spots. The eight frameworks are not reduced to one; their different facets can be lined up under a single accounting language, so that predictions become comparable with one another — if dissipative-structure theory predicts that order rises as energy flow increases, this volume predicts that order no longer rises after capacity saturates, and the two can be distinguished in the same experiment.

***

## Chapter 9 — Falsifiable Predictions

This chapter gives six predictions, numbered HⅡ-1 to HⅡ-6: the test procedure must contain a sample, a statistic and a decision threshold, and the refutation condition must be **observable**.

**Table 9.1 Falsifiable Predictions of Volume II**

| No. | Prediction | Test procedure | Refutation condition |
| :--- | :-------------- | :------------------------ | :----------------- |
| **HⅡ-1** | A level transition occurs only when all three conditions are met at once (necessary, not sufficient) | Collect transition cases with detailed energy-budget records, and check one by one whether $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ crossed the gate and whether $E_{\mathrm{build}}<E_{\mathrm{available}}$ and $L_{\mathrm{lev}}>L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$ hold; report the results of checking the four comparability premises | Among recorded transitions, cases in which $E_{\mathrm{build}}>E_{\mathrm{available}}$ or $L_{\mathrm{lev}}<L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$ occurs and the event is nonetheless confirmed as a level transition (excluding measurement error and apportionment differences) |
| **HⅡ-2** | $E_{\mathrm{causal}}/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ shows a statistical tendency to rise in lineages taking the $I$ route | Compare the share of neural metabolism across stages within the same lineage (brain-to-body mass ratio, basal metabolic rate, brain glucose uptake rate), estimating the temporal slope with a mixed-effects model and controlling for body size and temperature | In some lineage the share allocated to information processing is significantly lower in the late stage than in the early stage, and this is repeated in several independent lineages |
| **HⅡ-3** | D-lock-in precedes extinction events statistically | Using cladistics together with the fossil record, count the morphological stasis periods of extinct clades and compare them with those of surviving sister clades, using the Mann–Whitney U test or survival analysis, $n\ge20$ | The morphological stasis period of extinct clades does not differ significantly from that of surviving clades ($p>0.05$), or the stasis period systematically comes later than the precursors of extinction |
| **HⅡ-4** | Within a domain in which the decoder substrate and the state space are locked, $\alpha/\gamma>1$ | In a model family of the same decoder architecture, with parameter count as the proxy for $I$ and training compute as the proxy for $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, fix $\varphi$ and regress $\ln V=\alpha\ln I-\gamma\ln E+\text{constant}$, reporting the point estimate and the interval | In a domain meeting the locking conditions, $\alpha/\gamma\le1$ is estimated, with an interval that excludes 1 |
| **HⅡ-5** | Negative topological transition occurs preferentially after $\partial V/\partial D_{\mathrm{depth}}<0$ has persisted for some time | In species with existing records of morphological simplification (cave fish, parasitic crustaceans), trace the time series of maintenance cost before simplification (metabolic rate, organ volume ratio, gene-loss rate) | Morphological simplification is observed but maintenance cost has not fallen, or simplification occurs before the cost change |
| **HⅡ-6** | Cross-scale co-directionality: under the same environment, the same decoder and the same protocol, the one with lower $V$ disintegrates first | Three control groups: (a) a crystal and an ordinary rock in the same environment; (b) two isolated settlements, one of which continually produces iterative culture; (c) two functional units in the same organization, one whose output covers its cost. First calibrate the $V$ ranking under the same protocol, then test whether the order of disintegration points the same way as the ranking | In controls meeting the same-domain conditions, the $V$ ranking and the order of disintegration are systematically in opposite directions |

**The test logic of HⅡ-1 is exclusionary**: since the three conditions are necessary, this prediction can be refuted only by "transition cases that violate a necessary condition". **HⅡ-4 is the most critical test**, but its dependent variable $V$ cannot be observed directly and must first be operationalized: take "downstream benchmark score per unit cost × effective service duration" as the proxy for $V$, and report a comparison of the proxy with existing neural scaling laws; **at the baseline $\alpha=\gamma$ this regression has no discriminating power** (under the baseline form the coefficients are identically 1, a consequence of definition and not an empirical law, see Appendix C.2), so the test must be done within a locked domain, with $\varphi$ fixed, pre-registered, with intervals reported and a multiple-comparison correction applied. If $\alpha/\gamma\le1$ holds within the locked domain, the directional source that "information investment has the highest marginal return" fails. **HⅡ-6 tests only whether the order relations point the same way**, among which group (c) most easily gives rise to measurement disputes and serves as auxiliary evidence.

***

## Chapter 10 — Open Questions and Limitations

Unresolved problems fall into two kinds: **unfinished derivations** (the structure is known, steps are missing) and **uncalibrated parameters** (the structure is unknown, data are missing): the former needs mathematics, and the latter needs measurement. For problems at the conceptual level, what is missing is a formulation of the problem itself.

### 10.1 Unfinished Derivations

**The explicit form of the elasticity exponents has not been derived.** Both routes to redemption — deriving explicit functions from the Landauer bound, Arrhenius rates and thermal fluctuations, or back-inferring from persistence consequences independent of $(I,T,E)$ — have failed to work. Partial progress is to replace "what the elasticity looks like as a function of temperature" with "where the break-even surface $\alpha/\gamma=1$ lies": the break-even point can be computed and compared (Appendix C.2), but has not yet been systematically compared with measured transition temperatures.

**The gap in the sufficiency of the three conditions has not been filled.** Formalizing the probabilistic, temporal and competitive aspects needs a transition-success-rate model: given the size of the search space, the degree of variability and the time curve of energy payment, estimate the probability that one reorganization succeeds. No such model exists, so "if the three conditions are met, the transition occurs with probability $p$" cannot be written. This is the main derivation gap of this volume.

**A global analysis of the hybrid system has not been done.** How many qualitative types of long-term behavior the parameter space $(\mu_{\mathcal{F}},\tau_{\mathrm{decay}},I_{\mathrm{eq}},I_{\max},\Delta I_{\mathcal{T}},\tau_{\mathcal{T}})$ has, and where the boundaries lie, are all unspecified; the local equilibrium points of Section 3.3 cover only the continuous segments.

**The additivity of $V$ and the depth calibration have not been tested independently.** The former requires measuring the two channels separately, to see whether the composed $V$ predicts persistence consequences better than either single channel. For the latter, Section 2.4 gave a regression method but no cross-domain calibration; until the test is completed, every conclusion that uses depth as a cardinal quantity (including Eq. (II-2.6) and the $-1$ of Eq. (II-7.1)) carries an additional condition.

### 10.2 Uncalibrated Parameters

**$E_{\mathrm{build}}$ and $L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$.** The former must be estimated retrospectively from the fossil record, comparative genomics and developmental biology; the latter depends on context (competition intensity, risk preference, availability of alternatives) and may be only a property of the environment.

**$\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{eq}}$.** The relation of the former to temperature, barrier and substrate must be given by the specific mechanism; the latter is harder — it is "the amount of information corresponding to the disordered state of the thermal background", and this reference itself depends on the protocol.

**The time variation of the channel weights $\varphi$.** Section 2.2 requires $\varphi$ to be fixed, but $\varphi$ is determined by energy composition, and energy composition changes markedly in evolution (the shift in energy dominance of Section 5.2 is exactly a systematic drift of $\varphi$). So "fixing $\varphi$" is feasible over a short window and infeasible over a long window; a long-window estimate needs an explicit model of $\varphi(t)$, and that model has not been built.

**Universal calibration of $\dot E_{\mathrm{diss}}^{\max}$.** The dissipation upper limit can be computed for a specific system, but comparison across systems needs a unified scheme (the same geometric assumption? the same ambient temperature? the same cooling method?); until the scheme is fixed, the quantitative argument for the dissipation wall can stay only at the level of orders of magnitude.

**Literature values cited in Appendix C.** The nematode dauer transition temperature, the radiation tolerance dose, the Arrhenius extrapolation span and the like should have cited ready-made data, but this book has not obtained verifiable sources, **so no specific values are listed**; until they are supplied they are not used as quantitative grounds.

### 10.3 Open Problems at the Conceptual Level

**The origin of the first decoder.** All three conditions presuppose that "a decoder already exists that can read signals and compute returns", and when the first decoder appeared neither premise held; its appearance was a fluctuation under an extreme energy gradient, and must be answered by prebiotic chemistry.

**The decoding efficiency of consciousness.** It can be stated precisely: there exists an $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$ that exists independently of whether any decoder can extract it, while the current $\eta_D$ is near zero; solving it is equivalent to constructing a decoder $D_{\mathrm{conscious}}$. This volume points out only that this formulation turns the problem into an operational research program.

***

## Chapter 11 — Conclusion

From crystals to civilizations, this volume writes the evolution of persistent structures as a combination of three accountable facts, and not as a teleological story.

**A candidate source of directionality**: if the elasticity ordering $\alpha/\gamma>1>\beta/\gamma$ holds, then the marginal return of information growth exceeds the marginal cost of energy growth. It is an empirical hypothesis and does not depend on measurement units; what changes the estimated values are the operational definition, the proxy indicator, the observation domain and the fitted model.

**The structure of the threshold**: a level transition requires a candidate signal, energy availability and a net-gain criterion to be satisfied at once, corresponding respectively to possibility, feasibility and desirability, and if one is missing the chain breaks at that link; even when all three are present, a transition may still not occur because of probability, timing and competition. This status of "necessary but not sufficient" is retained deliberately — writing them as sufficient conditions would make the theory stronger and more easily falsified, but that strength would be false.

**The consequence of upgrading**: each transition resets the upper limit of achievable viability potential, while raising maintenance cost and vulnerability. The capacity reset changes the upper limit and the impulse increment changes the current value, and the two are booked separately, so as to avoid booking future growth to the account of the present transition.

Most structures have not ascended: the current decoder is saturated, transition energy is unavailable, or the competitive environment has not yet made a demand, and so they persist at the original level, sometimes for geological ages. This is a local optimum under their constraint geometry; bacteria dominated in numbers, metabolic diversity and persistence time for more than three billion years, which is the strongest empirical support.

The universe has set no predetermined destination for this process, only a gradient ($\max V$ along the direction of available signals) and three conditions that govern whether any structure can follow it. If, within a locked domain, the elasticity ordering is observed to fail, a level transition is confirmed to occur in a case violating a necessary condition, or D-lock-in is found not to precede extinction at a statistical rate above chance, the core claims of this volume are weakened or falsified; the three carry different weights: failure of the ordering is the most fatal, violation of a necessary condition next, and failure of the D-lock-in test the lightest.

***

This table covers all symbols of this volume (including those that appear only once); the fourth column states the conditions under which each is meaningful, and must be read before any cross-system comparison.

### A.1 Information

| Symbol | Unit | Definition | Scope of application |
| :---- | :-------------- | :------------------- | :------------------- |
| $I$ | bit | Total amount of distinguishable ordered difference (parent variable) | A bare $I$ defaults to $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ |
| $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$; $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ | bit | Structural rarity relative to the background; effect rarity relative to the control | Background and tolerance must be locked / Control design and the same window required |
| $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ | bit | $\varphi_{\mathrm{s}}I_{\mathrm{struct}}+\varphi_{\mathrm{c}}I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ | After in-domain calibration |
| $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$; $I_{\mathrm{active}}$ | bit | Recoverable causal capacity that is not activated; $\eta_D I_{\mathrm{latent}}$ | Independent of the decoder / Not converted with $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ |
| $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ | bit | $-\log_2\Pr_{B_n}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{res}}\ge\varepsilon_{\mathrm{obs}})$ | Gating only, must be pre-registered |
| $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$; $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T}}$ | bit | Validated added information; amount written by a single transition | Ex post confirmation / Records only the instantaneous increment |
| $I_{\max}(D)$; $I_{\mathrm{eq}}$ | bit | Decoder capacity upper bound; equilibrium information level | Reset after a transition / To be calibrated |
| $I^{-}$ | bit | Perturbation that reduces $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ on net | Must have the same dimension |
| $L_D$ | bit | Code length of the most compact rule set | Tolerance must be reported |
| $\varphi_{\mathrm{s}},\varphi_{\mathrm{c}}$ | Dimensionless | Channel weights, summing to 1 | Must be fixed when estimating elasticity |
| $\Omega$; $B_n$ | — | State space; historical residual distribution | Incommensurable across domains / Frozen before the transition |
| $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{res}}$; $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{tol}}$ | Same as the prediction or reconstruction target | Prediction residual; reconstruction tolerance | Measure must be declared / Depth and length are sensitive |
| $\alpha_0$; $N$, $m$ | Dimensionless; count | Pre-registered significance level; number of retests and gating threshold | Registered together with the multiple-comparison correction |

| :--- | :------ | :------------------------------- | :--------------- |

| $E_{\mathrm{survival}}$; $\dot E_{\mathrm{survival}}$ | J; W | Active dissipation that compensates for entropy increase | Power is used for criteria |

| $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}$ | J/bit | $k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2$ | Irreversible erasure only |
| $\Delta U_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ | J (per particle) | Barrier height. **It can only be a single-particle energy**: it appears only in the numerator of the Arrhenius exponent $\Delta U_{\mathrm{barrier}}/(k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}})$, whose denominator is the single-particle $k_B$, and writing J/mol would make the exponent off by a factor of $N_A$; if the data are given as molar quantities, first divide by $N_A$, or replace the $k_B$ in the denominator with $R$ | Not $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ |
| $\dot E_{\mathrm{diss}}^{\max}$; $\dot E_{\mathrm{maintain}}$; $\dot E_{\mathrm{available}}$ | W | Maximum dissipation rate; average power for maintaining information; power payable during the construction period | Set by thermal conductivity and area / Limited to Proposition 3.1 / Compared with $E_{\mathrm{available}}/\tau_{\mathrm{build}}$ |
