### A.5 Decoder, Operators, and Geometry

| Symbol | Unit | Definition | Scope of application |
| :---------- | :--------- | :--------------------- | :---------------- |
| $D$ | — | Mechanism that extracts causal information | Subscript $n$ denotes generation |
| $\eta_D$ | Dimensionless | Decoding efficiency, $\in[0,1]$ | Not $\eta_{\lambda}$ |
| $\mathcal{F}$; $\mathcal{F}^{-}$ | — | Multiscale replication without adding dimensions; unfolding under a reduced capacity | Continuous segment / simplification segment |
| $\mathcal{T}$; $\mathcal{T}^{-}$ | — | Reorganization of structure that opens a new dimension; negative topological transition | Magnitude $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T}}$ |
| $\#\{\mathcal{T}\}$ | Count | Cumulative number of transitions | Component of depth |
| $\lambda_{\mathcal{F}}$; $S_n$ | — | Control parameter of the unfolding operator; state of generation $n$ | $\mathcal{T}$ is its jump / Eq. (II-2.5) |
| $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$; $D_{\mathrm{depth}}^{\mathrm{dyn}}$ | Dimensionless | Minimum number of irreducible rules; $1+\#\{\mathcal{T}\}$ | A random configuration takes 0 / Defined only for depth $\ge1$ |
| $F_{\mathrm{scale}}$ | Dimensionless | Number of unfoldings or scale span | Orthogonal to depth |
| Configuration capacity | bit | Logarithm of the number of presentable configurations | Not the rarity of a single state |
| $k_B$; $\ln 2$ | J/K; dimensionless | Boltzmann constant; approximately 0.6931 | Lower-bound calculation and bit conversion |
| $\Pr$; $P$ | — | Probability function; probability distribution | Not interchangeable |
| $\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}$; $\eta_{\lambda}$; $\lambda_{\mathrm{civ,opt}}$ | Dimensionless | $E_{\mathrm{ext}}/E_{\mathrm{causal}}$; its reciprocal; the value at which $\partial V/\partial\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}=0$ | Appendix B only |
| $T_{\mathrm{thermo,c}}$ | K | Switching temperature | Not comparable across species |

For any quantity marked "must be reported", the protocol parameters must be reported together with the figure; this table does not contain value weights.

***

## Appendix B — Civilizational Energy-Lever Extensions (Application-Level Auxiliary Parameters)

The quantities of this appendix are used only for strategy analysis; where they conflict with the core criteria, the core criteria govern.

### B.1 The Energy Dichotomy

Total energy consumption is split into $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ (consumed directly by the decoder) and $E_{\mathrm{ext}}$ (the external energy flow controlled by the decoder). The **civilizational energy lever** is

$$
\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}\equiv\frac{E_{\mathrm{ext}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}}. \tag{II-B.1}
$$

$\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}$ is dimensionless, and both sides must be integrated quantities over the same observation window — one an integral and the other a power is the most common dimensional error. Its reciprocal $\eta_{\lambda}$ is called the control efficiency.

### B.2 The Optimal Leverage Surface

Keep $V=IT/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and let $E_{\mathrm{eff}}=E_{\mathrm{causal}}(1+\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}})$ (**this appendix folds $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ into $E_{\mathrm{ext}}$; where it conflicts with the core formula $E_{\mathrm{eff}}=E_{\mathrm{struct}}+E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, the core formula governs**). Expansion acts in opposite directions on $I$ and $T$: $I$ increases with leverage, and $T$ shortens with it. Write $T=T_0f(\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}})$ with $f'<0$; then

$$
V\left(\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}\right)=\frac{I\left(\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}\right)\cdot T_0\cdot f\left(\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}\right)}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}\left(1+\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}\right)}. \tag{II-B.2}
$$

**Proposition B.1 (Existence of the Optimal Leverage Surface)**
- **Premises**: (i) $I(\lambda)$ is continuously differentiable and monotonically non-decreasing; (ii) $f(\lambda)$ is continuously differentiable, monotonically decreasing and always positive; (iii) the growth rate of $I$ decreases with $\lambda$; (iv) $\dfrac{\mathrm{d}\ln(If)}{\mathrm{d}\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}}$ is greater than 1 as $\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}\to0^{+}$, and less than $\dfrac{1}{1+\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}}$ as $\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}\to\infty$.
- **Result**: Under (i)–(iv), $V$ has an interior stationary point on $(0,\infty)$, and it satisfies

$$
\frac{\partial\ln I}{\partial\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}}+\frac{\partial\ln T}{\partial\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}}=\frac{1}{1+\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}}. \tag{II-B.3}
$$

- **Derivation**: Take the logarithm of Eq. (II-B.2): $\ln V=\ln I+\ln T_0+\ln f-\ln E_{\mathrm{causal}}-\ln(1+\lambda)$; differentiating and setting the derivative to zero gives the result.
- **Assumptions relied on**: The existence of the stationary point depends on premise (i). If expansion adds no information, the left side of Eq. (II-B.3) is identically negative and the right side positive, $V$ decreases monotonically, and expansion at any scale is a net loss.
- **Failure conditions**: (a) when the growth rate of $I$ does not decrease, the optimum lies on the boundary; (b) when $f$ reaches zero at $\lambda_c$, the optimum degenerates to a degenerate point; (c) the second-order condition must be checked and compared with $V(0)$.

The first-order condition reads: **the sum of the information-gain rate and the lifespan-loss rate of expansion equals the relative growth rate of the energy denominator**.

### B.3 Criteria for Civilizational Phase Transitions (E Phase and T Phase)

By the signs of $\mathrm{d}\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}/\mathrm{d}t$ and $\mathrm{d}V/\mathrm{d}t$, four cells can be distinguished: an expansion period (E phase, both positive, as in the Industrial Revolution and the explosion of computing power), a saturation period (both about zero, leverage stagnant), a dormancy period (T phase, leverage falls while $V$ rises, trading leverage for lifespan) and true decline (both negative, structural collapse). The four cells are a classification and not a trajectory: $\dot\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}<0$ does not necessarily equal decline, and real civilizations can move back and forth.

### B.4 A Historical Accounting Baseline

A conceptual order-of-magnitude estimate, rounded to one significant figure: $E_{\mathrm{causal}}\approx1.3\times10^{21}\ \mathrm{J}$; $E_{\mathrm{eff}}\approx3.2\times10^{22}\ \mathrm{J}$; hence $E_{\mathrm{eff}}/E_{\mathrm{causal}}=\lambda_{\mathrm{civ,avg}}+1\approx25$, that is, $\lambda_{\mathrm{civ,avg}}\approx24$. $V_{\mathrm{civ}}$ further requires $I$ and $T$ on the same basis, and this section reports no figure for it.

Order-of-magnitude check: $E_{\mathrm{ext}}\approx3.1\times10^{22}$ J and $E_{\mathrm{eff}}=3.2\times10^{22}$ J are of the same order, so $\lambda_{\mathrm{civ}}\approx24$.

***

## Appendix C — Three Temperature Zones, Intertemporal Strategies, and Engineering Projections (Application-Layer Candidate Corollaries)

> This appendix maps already-established thermodynamic constraints onto persistence strategies and discovers no new physics. All orderings concerning how elasticity varies with temperature are **candidate corollaries**. $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ is temperature (K), $T_{\mathrm{env}}$ is the environmental characteristic time (s), and the two may not be mixed. The literature values cited (the nematode dauer transition temperature, the radiation tolerance dose, the Arrhenius extrapolation span) lack verifiable sources in this book, and are not used as quantitative grounds until supplied.

### C.1 How Temperature Modulates the Three Dimensions

The three mechanisms do not point in the same direction: the Landauer lower bound $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}=k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2$ makes single-bit erasure more expensive after warming; the Arrhenius rate $\lambda_{\mathrm{flip}}\propto e^{-\Delta U_{\mathrm{barrier}}/k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}}$ makes reactions slower after cooling; and the thermal-fluctuation scale $\propto k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ makes structures more fragile at high temperature. So there is no monotonic "better", only trade-offs.

### C.2 The Value Determination of the Elasticity Exponents

After expansion, the baseline form $V=IT/E$ has elasticities identically equal to 1, which is a consequence of the definition; only the general form $V\propto I^{\alpha}T^{\beta}/E^{\gamma}$ carries the empirical claim $\alpha/\gamma>1>\beta/\gamma$, and taking the former as evidence for the latter is circular reasoning. Neither route to redemption has been completed — deriving the concrete form from Landauer, Arrhenius and thermal fluctuations, or back-inferring with a persistence indicator independent of $(I,T,E)$; the common premise is an **independent anchor**: with no observable consequence of $V$ constraining its functional form, $\alpha,\beta,\gamma$ are merely free parameters. At present there is only partial redemption: the anchor is the break-even surface of $\rho_I=1$, and the switching temperature $T_{\mathrm{thermo,c}}$ can be computed, but its absolute position cannot be trusted.

Elasticity also depends on the coupling between system and environment: the environment changes the relative costs of the three terms, the substrate determines barriers, rates and storage density, the energy composition determines where energy is directed, and the decoder determines the efficiency of extracting $I$; "elasticity varies with temperature" is shorthand for considering only the first layer. **External comparison**: England's dissipation-driven adaptation shares its origin with this framework; it predicts that active systems at low temperature accelerate dissipation, while this framework predicts that the information route dominates, and the two can be distinguished mechanistically (England's original is not included in this book's references; it is used here only for positioning, not as an argument).

### C.3 Conditions for Elasticity Reversal and the Cognitive-Phase Interval

$$
\rho_I\equiv\frac{\alpha}{\gamma}=\frac{\partial\ln V/\partial\ln I}{-\partial\ln V/\partial\ln E_{\mathrm{eff}}}. \tag{II-C.1}
$$

$\rho_I=1$ defines **the boundary of the cognitive-phase interval for that type of system**, not "the interval of life".

| Condition | Phase | Route |
| :------------- | :----------------------- | :--------------------- |
| $\rho_I\gg1$ | Active cognitive phase, high returns | $I$ route dominates |
| $\rho_I$ slightly above $1$ | Active cognitive phase, returns just sufficient | $T$ or $E$ route |
| $\rho_I=1$ | Phase transition surface | — |
| $\rho_I<1$ | Passive phase | No route; thermodynamic screening only |

$\rho_I=1$ has two readings: the **existence gate** and the **route divide**. Pines, sea turtles and parasites still have decoders, and merely direct more energy toward extending $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ or lowering $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$. The active cognitive phase must satisfy both $\rho_I>1$ and a sustainable $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$; extending $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ increases $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, enlarges $\gamma$ and compresses $\rho_I$, so the extension itself tightens the cognitive-phase interval. Each system has its own $\rho_I=1$ surface.

### C.4 Three Boundaries: The Complete Conditions of the Active Cognitive Phase

$\rho_I>1$ is not the only condition. The structure must still exist, cognition must be affordable, and the dynamics must be fast enough.

| Condition | Physical meaning | Consequence of failure | Tightness |
| :----------- | :--------------- | :---------------- | :-------------- |
| Structural integrity | The physical substrate does not fall apart | The structure disintegrates and perishes outright | **Tightest**: the wall most organisms hit |
| Cognitive affordability | Overhead does not exceed budget | Degrades to the passive phase | Very loose |
| Dynamical feasibility | Reaction rate not below decision rate | Freezing, too late for effective behavior | Depends on temperature |

This explains the counterintuitive phenomenon of radiation lethality: about 5 Gy of whole-body irradiation is lethal to humans, and the cause of death is the collapse of DNA and cellular integrity, that is, the structure falls apart first, and it is not that the "cognitive system fails". Tardigrades tolerate doses higher by several orders of magnitude (the specific multiple has no verifiable source listed, to be calibrated), the mechanism being a change of phase: free water is removed, the structure becomes a configuration extremely hard for radiation to damage, and after the change of phase it no longer retains the active cognitive phase.

### C.5 The Three-Temperature-Zone Framework

| Temperature zone | Dominant feature | Candidate elasticity tendency | Phase and route | Nature of the strategy |
| :----------- | :--------- | :------------- | :---------- | :------------ |
| Low temperature, passive | Static preservation | Information elasticity high but cannot be realized | $\rho_I<1$, passive phase | Freeze and wait |
| Low temperature, active | Slow processing | Information elasticity can still be high | $\rho_I$ slightly above $1$, $T$ and $E$ routes | Trade time for information |
| Moderate temperature (about 0–30 °C) | Balanced | $\alpha/\gamma>1>\beta/\gamma>0$ (candidate) | $\rho_I\gg1$, $I$ route | Fast and expensive |
| High temperature | Structure hard to maintain | Information elasticity may fall | $\rho_I<1$, passive phase | Tends toward energy saving |
| Extremely high temperature | Only gravitational structures | — | $\rho_I<1$, passive phase | No small-scale chemical-bond-based intelligences |

**The branching point of the low-temperature zone is the free-energy flux, not the temperature itself.** At low temperature with an active source, the cost per bit is extremely low and an enormous amount of information can be processed per unit energy, but the computation clock is stretched; the ordering in the moderate-temperature row is the candidate instance for that zone, and if the ordering reverses in another zone, it does not falsify the ordering itself (whose scope is "within a locked domain").

### C.6 Intertemporal Persistence and Dormancy Strategies

**A single-period $V$ cannot express dormancy.** Dormancy is an intertemporal decision: dynamic processing is shut down, structure is preserved, and reactivation waits until a future window opens. The completed form is: intertemporal viability potential = the current-period term of the structural channel + the expected value of the discounted future causal option. The current-period term is the energy consumed to maintain minimal physical integrity, and the discount factor is influenced by environmental uncertainty, the substrate's aging rate and the probability of external threats; this is a conceptual structure and not a computable quantity. Typical effects of the three temperature zones: in the deep-cold preservation zone, structural consumption is extremely low, but the energy cost of awakening is high and the response delay is large; the elastic buffer zone is usually the globally better interval, retaining basic repair and a faster response; in the ordinary-temperature active zone causal efficacy is greatest and current-period consumption highest, and long exposure accelerates degradation.

**Four model systems** (this section is an integration and reinterpretation, not a new experiment; the concrete numerical values of each system have no verifiable source listed in this book, and are all to be calibrated): the nematode dauer (high temperature plus food scarcity, single-stage intertemporal), the tardigrade tun (slow dehydration, single-stage intertemporal plus a formation cost, the key parameter being the dehydration-rate threshold), spore germination (temperature window, single-stage intertemporal, with upper and lower thresholds) and seed dormancy (multi-stage temperature and humidity signals, a **multi-stage intertemporal sequence**, with different temperatures and times at each stage).

The common structure: when the free-energy flux or the temperature deviates from the suitable window, the system shuts down dynamic processing, preserves structure, and waits for a future window. The switching temperatures of different systems cannot be compared across species.

### C.7 Inference on Time Perception (Candidate)

The internal reaction rates of intelligences in different temperature zones are constrained by Arrhenius kinetics, and subjective time scales may be badly mismatched with objective time: a low-temperature active one thinks deeply in a single thought, but needs long dormancy and has difficulty communicating; a high-temperature one thinks shallowly in a single thought and is short-lived, and can hardly accumulate a civilization; a moderate-temperature one lies in between. Subjective time depends on the number of prediction steps and not on objective seconds, so comparison across species can use only the objective $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$, and the number of prediction steps is comparable only within the same material substrate. Only a low-temperature active one could form an "intelligence that occasionally awakens"; a low-temperature passive one is a dormant body, not an intelligence. The related cosmological inference (for example the explanation of the Fermi paradox) currently has no observational evidence, and conflicts with the orientation that "civilizational evolution occurs mainly in constant-temperature environments"; this volume does not develop it, and does not list it among the falsifiable content.

### C.8 Engineering Projection: The Ember Persistence Temperature

The "ember persistence temperature" is an engineering projection of the same framework onto civilizational continuation and interstellar scenarios, and is the result of a dynamic optimization that varies with technological level, substrate material, energy power density and rotation period: it turns a physiological parameter traditionally regarded as fixed into an adjustable control variable. Three red lines: temperature is only one of several control variables and cannot by itself decide a strategy; any statement must specify relative to which decoder; it is an application-level inference and must not be mixed with the core ontological concepts. As to physical constraints, if the temperature is too high, the preservation cost is high, and if too low, intracellular ice forms; too rapid rewarming produces ice crystals, and too slow rewarming prevents metabolism from recovering; so the suitable interval is a narrow window, whose position and width must be calibrated system by system, and no absolute values are given.

### C.9 Testable Predictions and Current Gaps

The following seven are HⅡ-7 to HⅡ-13, with the three columns meaning the same as in the main text.

| No. | Prediction | Test procedure | Refutation condition |
| :--- | :---------------- | :------------------ | :-------------------- |
| **HⅡ-7** | Low-temperature active systems produce more information per unit energy than moderate-temperature systems, and less per unit objective time | Energy-consumption–output comparison of low-temperature quantum computing devices and room-temperature devices, reporting effective operations per joule and per second | Output per unit energy is not higher than in moderate-temperature systems (same class of task, same precision) |
| **HⅡ-8** | Intelligences based on complex chemical bonds should not appear in the high-temperature zone | A molecular-diversity survey of high-temperature planetary atmospheres, testing the correlation between complexity and temperature | A complex information-processing structure maintained by multi-step chemical-bond cascades is found in a high-temperature environment |
| **HⅡ-9** | The critical temperature of ordering reversal can be computed from the break-even surface | Write out the explicit break-even condition for $\rho_I=1$, then calculate the scaling relation of $T_{\mathrm{thermo,c}}$ | The critical temperature computed from the break-even surface does not agree with the measured scaling of the transition temperature (off by more than one order of magnitude and inconsistent in direction) |
| **HⅡ-10** | Dormancy transition temperatures correspond to intersections with the break-even surface | Fit the temperature thresholds of the nematode dauer and of spore germination, and compare with the predicted intersection | The transition temperature deviates systematically from the intersection, and this cannot be explained by differences in substrate parameters |
| **HⅡ-11** | The $T_{\mathrm{thermo,c}}$ of active dormancy is modulated by $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ | Compare the formation success rate and temperature threshold of the tardigrade tun at different dehydration rates | $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ is changed while $T_{\mathrm{thermo,c}}$ does not move at all |
| **HⅡ-12** | Each stage of multi-stage dormancy has an independent $T_{\mathrm{thermo,c}}$ | Fit separately the temperature thresholds of the seed radicle stage and the hypocotyl stage | The thresholds of the two stages are statistically indistinguishable |
| **HⅡ-13** | The lower the free-energy flux, the lower $T_{\mathrm{thermo,c}}$ should be | Compare dauer transition temperatures under different food-supply conditions | Flux and transition temperature are uncorrelated, or correlated in the opposite direction |

**Deferred item (unnumbered)**: the difficulty of communication caused by mismatched signal forms and time scales between low-temperature and high-temperature intelligences belongs to a cosmological direction; this volume defers developing it, and does not list it among the falsifiable content.

**Current gaps**: the explicit function of the elasticity exponents with temperature (partly taken over by the break-even surface); the absolute position of $T_{\mathrm{thermo,c}}$ (the scaling relation is usable); the reliability of the Arrhenius extrapolation; measurable proxies and comparability rules across temperature zones (not established); integration of data on dormant structures (not done); delimitation from r–K selection theory (not handled). Until the gaps are filled, no heuristic functional form of the elasticity exponents with temperature is given: a functional form without a data anchor is pseudo-precision.

<!-- VOLUME Ⅲ ｜ Compressed Intelligence and Cognitive Evolution: Offline-Compiled Computation, the Human Causal Increment, and the Boundary of Physical Embedding -->
## Volume III — Compressed Intelligence and Cognitive Evolution: Offline-Compiled Computation, the Human Causal Increment, and the Boundary of Physical Embedding

> **This volume = Volume III.**

## Abstract

Over the past decade, the growth in the capabilities of silicon-based intelligence has followed a single path: converting more online computation into better output. This path has two dead ends: heat, and data. This volume answers three questions: why online search has an upper limit that cannot be crossed by adding more computing power; under what conditions compiling search into structure ahead of time pays off; and what the functional position of **physically embodied systems** is within this configuration. Embodied AI belongs to the side of physical embedding and is not bound by the "bottleneck" claim of this chapter — the distinction drawn in this chapter is between "access to a physical channel" and "a closed digital system", not between "carbon" and "silicon".

The method has three layers. **The physical lower bound**: Landauer's principle gives an energy lower bound per bit of logically irreversible erasure, $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}=k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2$, not a lower bound per bit "processed"; between the two lies a mapping $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ that must be declared explicitly, so the lower bound on heat production is written $\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}\ge k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2\cdot\dot I_{\mathrm{erase}}$. **Optimality within the model**: "when to compile and how much to compile" is written as a two-stage optimal control problem with linear switching, with costs first made dimensionless (**the energy budget constraint does not affect $u^{*}$ in this model**, see §8.1.2). The criterion was originally taken to be $R\equiv C_{\mathrm{comp}}/E_{\mathrm{build}}>1$; but $R$ does not appear in the functional of this optimal control, so **$R>1$ (together with $N_{\mathrm{eff}}\ge2$) is only a sufficient condition for one-time budget recovery, and is neither an optimality criterion nor an affordability criterion** — affordability is written separately as $E_{\mathrm{build}}<E_{\mathrm{available}}$ (§8.1.5). What §8.1.5 actually gives is the first-order condition of the single-switch policy; the bang-bang structure obtained from it is a **Proposition** rather than a **Theorem**. **Hypotheses to be tested**: the physical-embedding premium, innovation uncertainty, involution lock-in, and the thermodynamic Red Queen, each of which gives measurable consequences and falsification conditions.

The main conclusions are ordered by strength. **Theorem**: the Landauer bound and its rate form; the domain of validity is logically irreversible erasure. **Proposition**: from heat production to damage requires four steps — net heat flow, heat-capacity temperature rise, dissipation curve, and dose accumulation — and if any one is missing the conclusion does not hold. **Proposition**: optimal compilation switches at most once. **Proposition**: once unfolding saturates under a fixed architecture, the net growth rate turns negative, but $V$ has a positive upper bound $T_{\mathrm{causal}}/k_{\mathrm{m}}$. **Proposition**: synthetic-data degradation: $N_{1/e}=-1/\ln\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{gen}}$ equals $1.44/2.80/3.48/4.48$ generations at $\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{gen}}=0.5/0.7/0.75/0.8$, and "3–5 generations" corresponds to $0.75$–$0.80$. **Hypothesis**: that the innovation success rate is no higher than a random baseline can be stated only with a statistical decision model. **Proposition**: the thermal-shell ratio $\Sigma\ge L_{\min}^{2}\rho_{s}e_{s}/(\kappa\Delta T\tau_{\mathrm{op}})$; without $L_{\min}$ there is no positive lower bound.

**Scope statement.** First, this volume asserts no cosmological bound on the total amount of information; the amount of data available to humans is a window estimate under assumptions about a specific population, lifespan, sensory rate, and encoding efficiency. Second, $V$ is a descriptive framework and does not constitute a normative criterion. Third, "intelligence" is limited to observable information processing and structure consolidation. Fourth, except for values with a cited source, all numbers throughout are order-of-magnitude estimates. Fifth, the bound on the switching count in §8.6 evaluates to $3.5\times10^{14}$ at typical parameters, a value for which the real-world premises fail; it is recorded only as a failure registration and is not listed as a main result.

**Keywords**: Structure Recursive Viability Dynamics; offline-compiled computation; Landauer bound; erasure map; heat-dissipation constraint; synthetic-data degradation; human causal increment

***

## Chapter 1 — The Thermodynamic Limit of Brute-Force Computation and Physical Heat-Dissipation Constraints

Online computing power has an upper limit that cannot be crossed by adding more computing power. This chapter first gives the strict form and domain of validity of the Landauer bound, then lifts it from "energy per bit" to "power", then fills in the two links that are often skipped between power and damage — the erasure map and the thermal chain — and finally lists the failure conditions link by link. The whole chapter contains only one **Theorem**; everything else is a **Proposition**.

### 1.1 Four Core Quantities: the Viability Potential, Net Information, the Decoder, and the Operators

A persistent structure (PS) is a physical organization that retains an identifiable identity within the observation window, such as a crystal, a cell, a person, or an organization. This volume treats only the class of the **active phase**: structures that must continuously take in free energy and continuously discharge waste heat in order to maintain a low-entropy state. The viability potential of passive-phase structures (rock, the frame of a building) is determined by potential barriers and lies outside the discussion.

To maintain viability, an active-phase structure must do two things: extract **causal information** from the environment, and **consolidate** the extracted result into its own structure. The former is online, energy-consuming, and paid for each time; the latter is offline, one-time, and reusable. The conversion between this pair of actions is called **offline compilation**, and the state after the conversion is complete is called **the offline-compiled computational state**.

The **viability potential $V$** is the product of the information and the time obtained per unit of energy cost:

$$
V=\frac{I\cdot T}{E} \tag{III-1.1}
$$

Its dimension is $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$. It is an efficiency reading of "how many bit·seconds are obtained per joule"; the bit factor is retained because the information channel and the energy channel are accounted for separately in $V$, and only the normalized $v=V/V_{0}$ is dimensionless.

**Equation (III-1.1) is the low-order baseline form used in this volume.** The dual-channel definition $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}+V_{\mathrm{c}}$ and the numerical relation between the two are given in Volume I §3.2: under the same set of symmetric parameters the two formulas can differ by a factor of 4. This volume uses (III-1.1) only where orders of magnitude and ordering relations are required; it does not substitute between (III-1.1) and the dual-channel value, and it does not report absolute values from (III-1.1).

**Net effective causal information $I_{\mathrm{net}}$** is a coarse-grained index synthesized according to energy shares:

$$
I_{\mathrm{net}}=\varphi_{\mathrm{s}}I_{\mathrm{struct}}+\varphi_{\mathrm{c}}I_{\mathrm{causal}},\qquad \varphi_{\mathrm{s}}=\frac{E_{\mathrm{struct}}}{E_{\mathrm{eff}}},\quad \varphi_{\mathrm{c}}=\frac{E_{\mathrm{causal}}}{E_{\mathrm{eff}}},\quad \varphi_{\mathrm{s}}+\varphi_{\mathrm{c}}=1 \tag{III-1.2}
$$

$I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ each have their own reading protocol and background; that both are measured in bit does not prove additivity. This composite index can be used numerically only when the same reading protocol, the same background, and the same observation window are in force and in-domain calibration has been completed; otherwise it provides ordering relations only. Without a subscript, "effective causal information" refers to $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$, the one measurable channel, and "net effective causal information" refers to the composite quantity of Eq. (III-1.2).

**The decoder $D$ and the rule depth $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$.** A decoder is a device that maps environmental signals to internal states; it may be a neural circuit, a theory, a program, or the conventions of an organization. Rule depth has two usages: static depth is a **definition**, namely the minimum number of independent rules needed to reconstruct the object within a specified tolerance; the dynamic form $D_{\mathrm{depth}}^{\mathrm{dyn}}=1+\#\{\mathcal{T}\}$ is the **depth exponent**, and "each topological transition adds exactly one independent rule" is a calibration assumption, not an equality that follows from a definition. The calibration method is to regress static depth on the number of transitions; when the slope differs significantly from 1, the depth exponent is used only as an ordinal quantity.

**Two operators.** Recursive unfolding $\mathcal{F}$ replicates, densifies, and amplifies across multiple scales within one and the same rule set, without changing the rule set itself; topological transition $\mathcal{T}$ reorganizes the rule set after absorbing external signals and opens a new causal dimension. Stacking computing power belongs to $\mathcal{F}$; a change of paradigm belongs to $\mathcal{T}$.

This volume does not answer four questions: the origin of the first decoder; the mechanism of consciousness (when "self" is discussed, it refers only to observable self-representation and self-evaluation responses); absolute quantification across domains; and timetables for specific technical routes (years and numbers of generations are order-of-magnitude illustrations).

### 1.2 Evidence Levels and Notation

Conclusions are labeled with their strength by a four-level tag. A tag is a commitment: whatever is tagged **Theorem** must give a complete derivation, and whatever is tagged **Hypothesis** may not be used as an established fact in the concluding chapter.

| Tag | Conditions of use | Must be accompanied by |
| :-------- | :--------------------------------- | :---------------- |
| **Theorem** | Premises explicitly listed; conclusions within the volume must be fully derived; when a previously proven result is cited, re-proof within the volume is not required, but the original source and the premises there must be noted — the two may not be mixed under one label | Premises, result, derivation or source, domain of validity |
| **Proposition** | Has a formal statement, but some premises remain to be calibrated, or some step depends on an unproven modeling assumption | Premises, result, assumptions relied on, failure conditions |
| **Candidate Corollary** | Derived from some proposition under additional conditions; not independently tested | Parent proposition, additional conditions, measurable consequences |
| **Hypothesis** | Empirical or mechanistic conjecture with no formal derivation | Statement, supporting rationale, falsification condition |

The notation conventions hold throughout the volume and are collected in Appendix A. In the information family, a bare $I$ by default means $I_{\mathrm{net}}$, and the two measurable channels are always written in full as $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$; a candidate paradigm-breaking signal is written $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$, and verified new information is written $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$. The five members of the time family $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$, $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$, $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$, $T_{\min}$, $T_{\mathrm{env}}$ may not be interchanged; $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ is a temperature and does not belong to the time family. In the energy family, stocks use $E$ and power is always $\dot E$, with $P$ not used so as to avoid confusion with probability. $\dot I_{\mathrm{erase}}$ refers specifically to the rate of logically irreversible erasure, and $\dot I_{\mathrm{active}}$ specifically to the current processing rate. Generation indices are always subscripts, as in $I_{\mathrm{net},n}$ and $D_{n+1}$.

### 1.3 The Landauer Bound: Strict Form, Domain of Validity, and Rate Form (**Theorem**)

**Theorem 1.1 (Landauer Bound)**

- **Premises**: (i) the $n$ bits of logical state to be erased are, before the operation, equiprobably distributed over $2^{n}$ configurations; (ii) the operation maps this distribution onto a single configuration, that is, it is logically irreversible; (iii) the operation is in contact with a heat bath at temperature $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$, and the process is quasi-static; (iv) the bath temperature is constant during the operation.
- **Result**: The heat released to the bath by the operation satisfies $E_{\mathrm{heat}}\ge n\,k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2$, with equality attained in the reversible limit.
- **Derivation**: The number of logical states falls from $2^{n}$ to $1$, so the information entropy decreases by $n$ bit, corresponding to a thermodynamic entropy decrease of $n k_B\ln2$. The total entropy does not decrease, so the environmental entropy must increase by at least the same amount; hence $E_{\mathrm{heat}}\ge T_{\mathrm{thermo}}\Delta S_{\mathrm{env}}\ge n k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2$; equality is attained in a quasi-static process with no additional entropy production.
- **Domain of validity**: It holds only for operations that are **logically irreversible**. Reversible computation postpones erasure until the moment information must be discarded; the operation itself does not trigger the bound, which is paid once, only at the final clearing.

There are three failure conditions. When the initial state is not an equiprobable distribution, "logically irreversible" does not necessarily correspond to "thermodynamically irreversible" — if the initial state departs from maximum entropy, erasure can make use of that free energy, and the actual heat release can fall below the bound; this is not a counterexample, because premise (i) has already failed. When the operation is far from quasi-static, entropy production increases, and the bound still holds but is no longer tight. If the carrier of the "bit" is a continuous variable (analog computation, neural membrane potential), "erasure per bit" requires a separate definition.

Dividing Theorem 1.1 by time gives the form actually used in this volume. Let $\dot I_{\mathrm{erase}}$ be the measurable rate of logically irreversible erasure ($\mathrm{bit/s}$); then

$$
\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}\ \ge\ k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2\ \cdot\ \dot I_{\mathrm{erase}} \tag{III-1.3}
$$

Equation (III-1.3) is the starting point of the thermodynamic argument of the whole volume: the left side is a power ($\mathrm{W}$), the right side is "energy per bit" times "bits per second", and the dimensions close. It does **not** say that "processing one bit costs $k_BT\ln2$", because processing and erasure are not the same thing.

### 1.4 From Processing Rate to Erasure Rate: The Mapping Must Be Declared First (**Proposition**)

Multiplying the "effective information-processing rate" $\dot I_{\mathrm{active}}$ directly by $k_BT\ln2$ to obtain the heat-production power amounts to assuming that exactly one irreversible erasure occurs for each bit processed — an assumption that has been neither defined nor verified for any architecture. This volume splits it into two steps. The first step defines the **erasure-map coefficient**

$$
\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}\ \equiv\ \frac{\dot I_{\mathrm{erase}}}{\dot I_{\mathrm{active}}} \tag{III-1.4}
$$

The unit is "erased bits per processed bit", and it is dimensionless. It is an **architecture parameter**: it depends on the instruction set, the register-reuse strategy, the memory hierarchy, whether computation is in place, and whether reversible gates are allowed. When the same chip runs different workloads, $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ can differ by two or three orders of magnitude. The second step defines the **non-ideal dissipation factor** $\xi\ge1$, which expresses how many times the actual dissipation per erased bit exceeds the Landauer bound:

$$
\xi\ \equiv\ \frac{E_{\mathrm{diss,bit}}}{k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2} \tag{III-1.5}
$$

The heat-production power is then written

$$
\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}\ =\ \xi\,\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}\,k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2\ \cdot\ \dot I_{\mathrm{active}}\ \equiv\ \nu_{\mathrm{diss}}\,k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2\ \cdot\ \dot I_{\mathrm{active}} \tag{III-1.6}
$$

where $\nu_{\mathrm{diss}}\equiv\xi\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ is the product of two coefficients: one comes from "how many erasures were performed", the other from "how non-ideal each erasure is". Treating it as an intrinsic property of the medium compresses two factors of different origin into a single number.

**Proposition 1.1 (The Processing Rate Cannot Be Substituted Directly into the Landauer Formula)**

- **Premises**: $\dot I_{\mathrm{active}}$ has been measured under a declared reading protocol; $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ and $\xi$ have not been independently calibrated.
- **Result**: One may not write $\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}\ge k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2\cdot\dot I_{\mathrm{active}}$ directly from Eq. (III-1.3). The strongest statement that can be written is Eq. (III-1.6), in which $\nu_{\mathrm{diss}}$ is a quantity to be calibrated; when $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ is unknown, that formula provides only the after-the-fact ratio "measured heat production divided by measured processing rate", and provides no ex ante lower bound.
- **Assumptions relied on**: Processing and erasure can be counted separately in time; the counting conventions of the two reading protocols do not conflict.
- **Failure conditions**: If erasure and processing cannot be separated (in analog in-memory computing, state update and information readout are the same physical process), $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ is undefined, and this proposition and Eq. (III-1.6) both fail to apply; only holistic metrics such as "effective operations per joule" can be used instead.

The practical implication is this: before comparing the energy efficiency of two architectures, first ask what "bit" refers to — a transistor switching event, a 32-bit floating-point multiply-add, or an attention read/write — the resulting $\nu_{\mathrm{diss}}$ differs by six to eight orders of magnitude.

### 1.5 From Heat-Production Power to Structural Damage: A Four-Step Chain (**Proposition**)

The reasoning "heat-production power exceeds dissipation power, so the effective thermodynamic cost of the system approaches the disintegration threshold, so structural damage occurs" skips four steps: heat accumulation causes a temperature rise, the temperature rise is determined by the heat capacity, the temperature rise is clamped to some steady state by the dissipation curve, and damage is determined by the time integral of temperature.

**Step 1, net heat flow.** Let the internal heat-production power be $\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}(t)$ and the heat-rejection power to the environment be $\dot E_{\mathrm{diss}}(T_{\mathrm{thermo}})$; then the thermal power flowing net into the system is

$$
\Phi(t)\ =\ \dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}(t)\ -\ \dot E_{\mathrm{diss}}\bigl(T_{\mathrm{thermo}}(t)\bigr)\qquad(\mathrm{W}) \tag{III-1.7}
$$

The system accumulates heat only when $\Phi>0$. Comparing $\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}>\dot E_{\mathrm{diss}}^{\max}$ gives a sufficient condition rather than a necessary one, corresponding to the extreme case in which $\Phi$ is still positive when $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ reaches its permitted upper limit.

**Step 2, heat capacity and temperature rise.** Let the effective heat capacity be $C_{\mathrm{th}}$ ($\mathrm{J/K}$); then

$$
C_{\mathrm{th}}\frac{\mathrm{d}T_{\mathrm{thermo}}}{\mathrm{d}t}\ =\ \Phi(t) \tag{III-1.8}
$$

The thermal time constant $\tau_{\mathrm{th}}\equiv R_{\mathrm{th}}C_{\mathrm{th}}$ ($R_{\mathrm{th}}$ is the thermal resistance, $\mathrm{K/W}$) is the time needed for the temperature to rise to $63\%$ of its steady-state value after a power step, and **no power spike shorter than $\tau_{\mathrm{th}}$ causes a corresponding temperature rise**: this alone suffices to falsify the immediate version of "exceeding the power limit means damage".

**Step 3, the dissipation curve.** The heat-rejection power increases as the temperature rises, and in its simplest form is the sum of a linear conduction term and a radiation term:

$$
\dot E_{\mathrm{diss}}(T_{\mathrm{thermo}})\ =\ \frac{T_{\mathrm{thermo}}-T_{\mathrm{thermo,env}}}{R_{\mathrm{th}}}\ +\ \varepsilon_{\mathrm{em}}\sigma_{\mathrm{SB}}A\left(T_{\mathrm{thermo}}^{4}-T_{\mathrm{thermo,env}}^{4}\right) \tag{III-1.9}
$$

where $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{em}}$ is the emissivity, $\sigma_{\mathrm{SB}}$ is the Stefan–Boltzmann constant, and $A$ is the radiating area. The steady-state operating point is given by $\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}=\dot E_{\mathrm{diss}}(T_{\mathrm{thermo,ss}})$, with the linear-regime approximation $\Delta T_{\mathrm{thermo,ss}}=R_{\mathrm{th}}\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}$; whether the system survives depends on whether $T_{\mathrm{thermo,ss}}$ is below the permitted material temperature $T_{\mathrm{crit}}=T_{\mathrm{env}}+\Delta T_{\mathrm{thermo,crit}}$.

**Step 4, dose accumulation.** Material damage is a functional of the temperature history rather than an instantaneous function, and the commonly used measure is the Arrhenius dose:

$$
D_{\mathrm{dose}}(t)\ =\ \int_{0}^{t}\exp\!\left[-\frac{E_{a}}{k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}(t')}\right]\mathrm{d}t' \tag{III-1.10}
$$

$E_{a}$ is the activation energy of the damage process ($\mathrm{J}$), and when $D_{\mathrm{dose}}>D_{\mathrm{crit}}$ the structure undergoes irreversible degradation. The equivalent energy formulation is that the cumulative net heat

$$
Q_{\mathrm{net}}\ =\ \int_{0}^{t}\Phi(t')\,\mathrm{d}t' \tag{III-1.11}
$$

exceeds the threshold $E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$. The two are not the same criterion: the former is highly nonlinear in temperature (each $10\ \mathrm{K}$ rise in temperature roughly halves the lifetime, an empirical rule for electronic devices), while the latter looks only at the total heat. This volume takes Eq. (III-1.10) as the primary criterion and uses Eq. (III-1.11) for order-of-magnitude estimates.

**Proposition 1.2 (From Power Exceedance to Structural Damage)**

- **Premises**: (i) the duration of $\Phi(t)>0$ exceeds $\tau_{\mathrm{th}}$; (ii) the steady-state temperature rise exceeds the permitted value, $R_{\mathrm{th}}\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}>\Delta T_{\mathrm{thermo,crit}}$; (iii) within the observation window the dose accumulation reaches $D_{\mathrm{dose}}>D_{\mathrm{crit}}$; (iv) the damage is irreversible and there is no active repair.
- **Result**: When all four hold simultaneously, $E_{\mathrm{eff}}>E_{\mathrm{thresh}}$ and the structure undergoes irreversible degradation, with the decline of $V$ given by the damage term (proportional to the excess effective energy); if any one fails, "power exceedance" causes only a recoverable temperature rise or no temperature rise at all.
- **Assumptions relied on**: $R_{\mathrm{th}}$ is approximately constant over the temperature-rise range; damage is additive (Miner linear accumulation); the cooling control loop does not intervene.
- **Failure conditions**: (a) the power spike is shorter than $\tau_{\mathrm{th}}$ and is absorbed by the heat capacity; (b) phase-change heat absorption or active refrigeration pulls $T_{\mathrm{thermo,ss}}$ back below the permitted value; (c) the system has degrees of freedom such as frequency reduction, precision reduction, and task migration, and can automatically cut $\dot E_{\mathrm{heat}}$ once $\Phi$ turns positive; (d) the material's damage saturates in that temperature range, and $D_{\mathrm{dose}}$ no longer grows.

**Proposition 1.3 (The Thermodynamic Ceiling of Online Computing Power)**

- **Premises**: The architecture is fixed ($\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ and $\xi$ do not change with load); the dissipation geometry is fixed ($R_{\mathrm{th}}$ and $A$ do not change); the processing rate required by the task rises monotonically with the size of the environmental state space.
- **Result**: There exists an upper limit on the processing rate, $\dot I_{\mathrm{active}}^{\max}=\allowbreak\dot E_{\mathrm{diss}}^{\max}/(\xi\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2)$, and a task that exceeds it cannot be completed online on that architecture; only three ways out remain — lower the rate the task requires (change the strategy), change the architecture (lower $\xi\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$), or convert part of the processing to offline (lower $\dot I_{\mathrm{active}}$ while keeping output quality).
- **Assumptions relied on**: The size of the state space and the required processing rate grow in the same direction; the architecture parameters are stable within the observation window.
- **Failure conditions**: If $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ can be lowered substantially by algorithm or architecture (reversible computing, in-memory computing, sparse activation), the ceiling moves accordingly; if the task can be decomposed into a large number of low-rate subtasks run in parallel, the ceiling is no longer a single number.

The third way out is precisely the theme of this volume. Offline compilation is the only systematic means of converting $\dot I_{\mathrm{active}}$ into $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ under the constraint of Proposition 1.3.

### 1.6 A Calibration Example for Silicon-Based Architecture (Illustrative)

The statement "current silicon chips typically have $\nu_{\mathrm{diss}}$ of $10^{2}$–$10^{4}$" does not say what the bit refers to and cannot be checked. Taking $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}=300\ \mathrm{K}$, we have

$$
E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\ =\ 1.381\times10^{-23}\times300\times0.693\ \approx\ 2.87\times10^{-21}\ \mathrm{J/bit} \tag{III-1.12}
$$

The converted result depends on the convention for "one bit": an adiabatic or reversible logic gate is about $10^{-18}\ \mathrm{J}$ ($\nu_{\mathrm{diss}}\sim3\times10^{2}$); a single transistor switching event at an advanced node is $10^{-16}$–$10^{-15}\ \mathrm{J}$ ($3\times10^{4}$–$3\times10^{5}$); a 32-bit floating-point multiply-add is about $10^{-12}\ \mathrm{J}$ ($\sim3\times10^{8}$); one memory round trip is about $10^{-11}\ \mathrm{J}$ ($\sim3\times10^{9}$).

It can be seen that $10^{2}$–$10^{4}$ falls at the **lowest edge**, corresponding to idealized low-power devices; counted by the conventional CMOS switching convention it is $10^{4}$–$10^{5}$, and by the floating-point convention it is above $10^{8}$. $\nu_{\mathrm{diss}}$ varies by more than six orders of magnitude with the convention, and is a joint calibrated value of "architecture + workload + counting convention". The largest source of dissipation in actual systems is often **data movement** (the last item): if movement dominates energy consumption, "consolidating intermediate results at locations closer to the computation" is itself a path to lowering $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$.

**Online computation has a ceiling determined by heat dissipation, and the height of the ceiling is a function of the architecture and the counting convention; moving computation offline moves the ceiling rather than hitting it.** How much to move, when to move it, and whether the move carries other costs require a separate constrained accounting.

## Chapter 2 — Offline-Compiled Computation and the Physics of Expert Cognition

Rewriting online search into offline structure saves steps, not per-step cost; the price is a one-time construction expenditure and the risk of mismatch when the distribution drifts.

### 2.1 The Evolutionary Compression Mechanism (**Proposition**)

Suppose the decoder $D$ has not yet formed an effective bias toward the external channel. Then every decision must carry out an explicit search over a high-dimensional state space, and the causal processing energy of a single decision is the integral of the instantaneous power over the search time:

$$
E_{\mathrm{causal}}\ =\ \int_{0}^{T_{\mathrm{search}}}\dot E_{\mathrm{causal}}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t \tag{III-2.1}
$$

$T_{\mathrm{search}}$ is the search time ($\mathrm{s}$), and $\dot E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ is the instantaneous causal processing power ($\mathrm{W}$). Without bias the size of the latent state space is $\Omega$, and the search time grows with $\Omega$: the cost of brute-force search comes mainly from the large number of steps. What offline compilation changes is the number of steps: rule depth increases and layers of historical constraints stack up, compressing $\Omega$ into an effective subspace $\Omega'\ll\Omega$ and shortening the search time to the minimum response time $\tau_{\min}$:

$$
E_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{compressed}}\ =\ \int_{0}^{\tau_{\min}}\dot E_{\mathrm{causal}}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\ \approx\ \dot E_{\mathrm{base}}\,\tau_{\min} \tag{III-2.2}
$$

**Proposition 2.1 (Evolutionary Compression)**

- **Premises**: (i) the bias shrinks the effective state space from $\Omega$ to $\Omega'$, and the shrinkage ratio is stable for tasks of the same class; (ii) the search time is monotonically non-decreasing in the size of the effective space; (iii) the per-step power $\dot E_{\mathrm{base}}$ does not rise significantly with rule depth; (iv) the maintenance cost of the bias is lower than the search cost it saves.
- **Result**: The integrated energy consumption of a single decision falls monotonically with rule depth, with $\dot E_{\mathrm{base}}\tau_{\min}$ as its lower bound:

$$
\lim_{D_{\mathrm{depth}}\to\infty}E_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{compressed}}\ \to\ E_{\mathrm{floor}},\qquad E_{\mathrm{floor}}\ \ge\ \dot E_{\mathrm{base}}\,\tau_{\min} \tag{III-2.3}
$$

- **Assumptions relied on**: The environmental distribution is identical before and after compilation; the bias transfers to unseen tasks of the same class.
- **Failure conditions**: (a) the environment is non-stationary, the old bias fails, and the compression gain is consumed by mismatch cost; (b) the maintenance cost runs out of control, and deep rules require continual refreshing; (c) the per-step power rises with depth, offsetting the gain in steps; (d) the state space is incompressible (adversarial environments, cryptographic-style randomness).

Condition (d) explains why offline compilation is effective in board games, imaging diagnosis, and language, but ineffective in cryptanalysis and lottery prediction: it is not that the method is wrong, but that the object being compressed has no compressible structure. Premise (iii) also often fails in practice, in which case the net gain depends on two factors:

$$
\frac{E_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{compressed}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{search}}}\ =\ \underbrace{\frac{\tau_{\min}}{T_{\mathrm{search}}}}_{\text{step-count ratio}}\ \times\ \underbrace{\frac{\dot E_{\mathrm{base}}}{\dot E_{\mathrm{causal}}}}_{\text{per-step power ratio}} \tag{III-2.4}
$$

As long as the step-count ratio falls faster than the per-step power ratio rises, the net compression gain is positive.

### 2.2 Definition of the Offline-Compiled Computational State

**Definition 2.1 (The Offline-Compiled Computational State)** A persistent structure is in the offline-compiled computational state if and only if three conditions hold simultaneously: (i) the extraction efficiency of its decoder for a specific environment class $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$ reaches a significant fraction of the upper limit of the medium (in engineering terms, $\eta_{D}\ge0.8\,\eta_{\max}$); (ii) the ratio in Eq. (III-2.4) is less than $1$; (iii) the online search time is on the order of several times $\tau_{\min}$. The definition contains no psychological vocabulary: the system has moved most of its computation into structure on a specific task class.

Go is a commonly used example: experts judge quickly on familiar board patterns, which cognitive science calls chunking (Chase & Simon, 1973) — in the language of this volume, $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ is deposited as $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$. Its limits are these: quick judgment holds only on familiar patterns, accuracy comes from a feedback loop, and speed and accuracy are two independent dimensions. **Proposition 2.1 explains the compression of energy consumption, not an improvement in correctness**: an efficiently compiled wrong rule is more dangerous than slow search, because it removes the online time needed for error correction.

### 2.3 The Thermodynamic Red Queen: A Strategy Trend (**Hypothesis**)

**Hypothesis 2.1 (Thermodynamic Red Queen)** In long-run competition, for advanced intelligent systems that survive, the share of offline topological structure in the strategy mix rises over time and the share of online exhaustive computation falls. Supporting rationale: when the causal scope of the environment expands, $\Omega$ grows faster than $E_{\max}$, so a purely online route will hit the ceiling of Proposition 1.3 first; offline structure can be replicated; competition penalizes slow decisions. The opposing rationale is stronger: offline structure becomes obsolete, online search is adaptive, and the two are complementary rather than substitutes. The position is therefore this: under the three joint conditions of a stationary environment, repeated tasks, and reliable feedback, a rising offline share is an observable trend; when the conditions are not met, the trend can reverse.

***

## Chapter 3 — Art and Science: Cross-Subject Decoder Compilers

How a work changes the decoder in another person's head, whether this change can be accounted for with the same viability-potential framework, and what the physical difference is between "not understanding" and "understanding".

### 3.1 The Viability-Potential Equation for Artistic and Scientific Value

Artworks and scientific theories use the same formula:

$$
V_{\mathrm{art}}\ =\ \frac{I_{\mathrm{net}}\cdot T_{\mathrm{cultural}}}{E_{\mathrm{eff,art}}} \tag{III-3.1}
$$

The three terms must be made concrete one by one; otherwise this equation merely changes the subscripts of $V=IT/E$. **$I_{\mathrm{net}}$ (bit)** is the net effective causal information that the work delivers to a specific audience; for science it includes explanatory scope and predictive accuracy, for art it includes perceivable structural complexity and information density, and the reading protocols of the two differ, so they cannot be compared directly. **$T_{\mathrm{cultural}}$ (s)** is the duration for which the work remains identifiable and citable, not the lifetime of its physical substrate (a stone stele can persist for three thousand years without anyone reading it). **$E_{\mathrm{eff,art}}$ (J)** consists of a construction part and a decoding part:

$$
E_{\mathrm{eff,art}}\ =\ E_{\mathrm{build}}\ +\ E_{\mathrm{causal,audience}} \tag{III-3.2}
$$

The sum of the two terms is an account of at least two parties, which means that this index implicitly carries an accounting boundary for an overall social ledger; in comparisons across groups one must state explicitly who pays for construction, who pays for decoding, and by what rule the costs are apportioned. What excellent works have in common is a high compression rate ($F=ma$ is three characters carrying a system of mechanics), but a high compression rate and a high value are not synonyms: this volume provides only a measure of structural complexity and provides no ranking of value.

### 3.2 Rule Depth and an Analytical Framework for Levels of Art

The analytical tool is the rule depth $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$, namely the minimum number of independent rules needed to reconstruct the object within a tolerance $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{tol}}$. Relaxing the tolerance lowers the count, so every numerical value must carry its tolerance; the dynamic form $D_{\mathrm{depth}}^{\mathrm{dyn}}=1+\#\{\mathcal{T}\}$ must be calibrated by regression before it is used as a quantitative independent variable. High-depth works stack independent rules in dimensions such as composition, color, perspective, and symbolic metaphor:

$$
\text{pictorial structure}\ =\ \text{composition layer}\ \times\ \text{color layer}\ \times\ \text{perspective layer}\ \times\ \text{symbol layer} \tag{III-3.3}
$$

The multiplication sign is not rhetoric: the constraints of all layers are in force simultaneously, and the absence of any one layer makes the whole irreconstructible — this is the operational meaning of independent rules. Adding layers does not necessarily raise $V$: the gain is a rise in $I_{\mathrm{net}}$, and the cost is the sum of the one-time energy consumption of building that layer and the additional energy consumption of the audience decoding it; when the latter exceeds the former, adding depth lowers $V$ instead (Section 8.4 gives the interior optimum).

### 3.3 The Cross-Subject Decoder-Compiler Mechanism (**Proposition**)

Artistic creation and scientific discovery can be read as one cross-subject rewriting of a causal network, and each of its four links can fail independently: the creator compresses the rules and the candidate breakthrough signal into a carrier; when the carrier reaches the audience, the extraction efficiency of the audience's existing decoder is close to zero:

$$
D_{n}\bigl(\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}\bigr)\ \approx\ 0 \tag{III-3.4}
$$

The signal may then trigger a topological transition, upgrading the decoder:

$$
D_{n+1}\bigl(\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}\bigr)\ \gg\ 0 \tag{III-3.5}
$$

The integrated energy consumption of the upgraded decoder on future tasks of the same class falls, and the amount of that decrease is the social gain of this compilation.

**Proposition 3.1 (The Work as a Cross-Subject Compiler)**

- **Premises**: (i) the rules carried by the carrier can be partially matched by the audience's existing cognitive bias (an alignable prior exists); (ii) the decoding energy consumption paid by the audience is within the energy budget; (iii) the signal strength is sufficient to cross the attention threshold; (iv) the audience has an opportunity for feedback to calibrate the decoding result.
- **Result**: Under the four premises, a work can cause one topological transition in the audience's decoder, thereby lowering the processing energy consumption of its future tasks of the same class; the net social gain is the sum of the energy saved by all audiences minus the construction and transmission costs.
- **Assumptions relied on**: Once a transition occurs it is stable; the energy saved can accumulate within the observation window.
- **Failure conditions**: (a) the prior cannot be aligned, the audience lacks the necessary intermediate concepts, and the signal is read as noise (this is one kind of "not understanding", distinct from "too deep"); (b) the decoding energy consumption exceeds the budget, and the audience gives up before completing the decoding; (c) the signal strength is insufficient, and the work never enters the range of attention; (d) there is no feedback, the post-transition state cannot be calibrated, a systematic misreading forms, and energy consumption rises rather than falls.

Condition (d) is the easiest to overlook: if a misreading is stabilized, it becomes a layer of wrong deep bias, and every subsequent invocation pays the wrong cost; in the language of Eq. (III-3.1), such a work has a very high $T_{\mathrm{cultural}}$ while the sign of its $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is opposite to what was expected. The mechanism is therefore probabilistic, bidirectional, and costly: **the function of art and science is to change the structure at the receiving end; changing the structure requires the receiving end to pay energy; paying the energy does not necessarily yield the structure, and may yield a mis-structure.**

## Chapter 4 — An Analytical Framework for the Human Causal Increment

In a system in which silicon-based computing power keeps expanding, does a **physically embodied system** still hold an irreplaceable functional position; and if so, can that position be written as an accountable quantity rather than a declaration of value.

### 4.1 Hybrid Dynamical Systems and the Self-Referential Degradation of Closed Systems (**Proposition**)

The evolution of structural information cannot be written as a single equation with a Dirac $\delta$: continuous growth, event jumps, and capacity reset are three different accounts. The **continuous segment**, with the current $D$ unchanged, obeys

$$
\frac{\mathrm{d}I}{\mathrm{d}t}\ =\ \mathcal{F}(D,I)\ -\ \frac{I-I_{\mathrm{eq}}}{\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}} \tag{III-4.1}
$$

$\mathcal{F}(D,I)$ produces new combinations within fixed rules and depends explicitly on $I$: the same set of rules, when the amount of information approaches the capacity upper limit, necessarily produces fewer new combinations. The **jump map** at the transition time is

$$
I\bigl(t_{\mathrm{jump}}^{+}\bigr)\ =\ I\bigl(t_{\mathrm{jump}}^{-}\bigr)\ +\ \Delta I_{\mathcal{T}},\qquad I_{\max}(D_{n+1})\ >\ I_{\max}(D_{n}) \tag{III-4.2}
$$

The pulse term records the instantaneous increase in the amount of information, and the capacity reset records the change in the extractable upper limit; the two may not be counted twice: $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T}}$ is the number of bits actually written, and the difference between the new and old $I_{\max}$ is the capacity space that has been opened.

**Proposition 4.1 (Self-Referential Degradation of Closed Systems)**

- **Premises**: (i) the system is closed, with no injection of external candidate signals ($\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}\equiv0$, hence $\Delta I_{\mathcal{T}}=0$); (ii) the marginal gain of $\mathcal{F}(D,I)$ decreases as $I$ rises, and is zero at $I_{\max}(D)$; (iii) the decay coefficient $1/\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}>0$ and does not decline with time.
- **Result**: A stable equilibrium $I^{*}\in(I_{\mathrm{eq}},I_{\max})$ exists (unique when $F$ is of logistic type): when $I>I^{*}$, $\mathrm{d}I/\mathrm{d}t<0$; a closed recursive system converges in the long run to $I^{*}$, not to $I_{\mathrm{eq}}$. If one wants the conclusion to land at $I_{\mathrm{eq}}$, the additional premise $F(I_{\mathrm{eq}})=0$ is required (in the simplest form $F\equiv0$), in which case $I^{*}=I_{\mathrm{eq}}$ — this is a different model, not a weakening of this proposition.
- **Assumptions relied on**: The form of $\mathcal{F}$ does not change within the observation window; the decay cannot be reversed by internal mechanisms.
- **Failure conditions**: (a) there is an external criterion (the rules of a board game, a compiler, a proof checker), and the system can generate new information through interaction; then $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}>0$ and the closure premise fails; (b) there is external memory or replay, equivalent to a substantial lengthening of $\tau_{\mathrm{decay}}$; (c) a change of architecture raises $I_{\max}$; (d) the system can construct new verifiable subproblems, which is formally equivalent to (a).

Board-game self-play is closed in the sense of "no human data", but the board rules are an external criterion: the data are self-produced while the ground truth is supplied externally, so it does not count as self-referential degradation. Multi-generation training on purely synthetic data has been reported to show the disappearance of distribution tails and the forgetting of rare events (Shumailov et al., 2024); in the language of this volume this is the intergenerational accumulation of $\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{gen}}<1$ (Section 8.8.3).

### 4.2 The Human Causal Increment: The Functional Position of Physical Embedding (**Hypothesis**)

The deviation between subjective assessment and objective state is defined, and its evolution is written as first-order relaxation driven by noise:

$$
\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\ \equiv\ \bigl|V^{\mathrm{virt}}-V_{\mathrm{obj}}\bigr|,\qquad
\frac{\mathrm{d}\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}}{\mathrm{d}t}\ =\ -\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}\,I_{\mathrm{net}}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\ +\ \sigma \tag{III-4.3}
$$

$\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$ is the learning rate and $\sigma$ is the noise intensity ($\mathrm{bit/J}$); the steady-state solution is

$$
\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*}\ =\ \frac{\sigma}{\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}}} \tag{III-4.4}
$$

The steady-state value of the deviation is the ratio of the noise to the learning capacity. The noise of the physical channel, $\sigma_{\mathrm{phys}}$, includes thermal fluctuation, mechanical disturbance, and the behavior of others, and cannot be fully modeled; the $\sigma_{\mathrm{dig}}$ of the digital channel can be error-corrected, and the two can differ by several orders of magnitude at equal computing power. Because carbon-based systems must maintain a body that is continuously exposed to the former:

$$
\begin{aligned}
\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{(\mathrm{carbon})}(t)\ &\longrightarrow\ \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*}>0,\\
\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{(\mathrm{silicon})}(t)\ &\longrightarrow\ \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*,\,(\mathrm{silicon})}<\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}
\quad(\sigma_{\mathrm{dig}}>0\text{, merely not past the threshold})
\end{aligned}
\tag{III-4.5}
$$

**From deviation to signal.** A phenomenological mapping is adopted:

$$
\dot I_{\mathrm{orig}}\ =\ \kappa_{\mathrm{orig}}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\cdot\mathbf{1}\bigl[\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\ge\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}\bigr] \tag{III-4.6}
$$

$\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}$ is the minimum deviation that triggers a transition, and $\kappa_{\mathrm{orig}}$ is a conversion coefficient ($\mathrm{J/s^{2}}$); the indicator function sets a dead zone: when the deviation is below the threshold, the system has a deviation but no pressure to correct it.

**Hypothesis 4.1 (Physical-Embedding Premium)** Under the conditions that $\kappa_{\mathrm{orig}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ are comparable and that $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*,(\mathrm{carbon})}\ge\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}>\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*,(\mathrm{silicon})}$, the candidate-signal production rate of carbon-based systems is positive and higher than that of purely digital systems; the latter is exactly $0$ within the model (the denominator $\dot I_{\mathrm{orig}}^{(\mathrm{silicon})}=0$ leaves the "ratio" undefined, so this hypothesis makes only a directional comparison and gives no ratio).

- **Falsification condition**: An AI running in a closed digital environment has an independently assessed density of novel-information output no lower than that of a human–machine hybrid system of the same scale; or physical noise intensity has no significant positive correlation with the candidate-signal production rate.
- **Known counterexample pressure**: A purely digital system can introduce noise artificially through active experimental design (randomization, adversarial examples, simulated environments) and so still obtain a high deviation when $\sigma_{\mathrm{dig}}$ is very low; whether such practices count as a substitute for physical embedding is the largest uncertainty of this hypothesis.

The linear proportionality of Eq. (III-4.6) is a phenomenological choice, and $\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}$ has never been independently measured. It can therefore serve as a testable mechanism hypothesis, but not as a proof that "humans are irreplaceable"; wherever "irreplaceable" appears below, it means "within the model of Eq. (III-4.6), under the listed conditions, not replaceable in functional position by an isomorphic system".

### 4.3 The Dimension of the Value of the Human Causal Increment

$$
H_{\mathrm{v}}\ \equiv\ \frac{\partial V_{\mathrm{system}}}{\partial I_{\mathrm{orig,human}}}\qquad(\mathrm{s/J}) \tag{III-4.7}
$$

Human input can differ enormously in marginal contribution under different system states and information baselines, even in sign; the $H_{\mathrm{v}}$ of different individuals cannot be added unless the composite boundary and the deduplication rule are declared first. The functional position of humans lies in continually producing signals that existing decoders cannot process, and its physical basis is physical embedding.

***

## Chapter 5 — Cross-Decoder Games and Alignment Engineering

When several persistent structures share one energy boundary, how do their decoders change one another; and in this mutual change, in what sense is it feasible to "make one system obey the goals of another".

> **Division of labor with Volume IV.** This chapter gives the energy ledger and qualitative criteria; the formalized payoff function, critical horizon, and equilibrium conditions are in Volume IV, Chapters 5 and 6. The same conclusion is not counted twice across the two volumes; when citing, the volume that gives the derivation governs.

### 5.1 Three Basic Interaction Modes

The distinguishing criterion is the structure of the energy ledger, not the goodness or badness of motives. In the **zero-sum mode**, one party's expansion comes at the cost of the other party's ceding resources; pure zero-sum is rare in reality, because conflict itself consumes energy, and the model must be split into two layers: a "zero-sum part of resource allocation" and a "negative-sum part of conflict cost". In the **cooperative mode**, both parties partially reorganize their decoders and establish a shared intermediate layer (a common language, transactional credit, value anchors), which may produce superadditive gain:

$$
V_{AB}\ >\ V_{A}\ +\ V_{B} \tag{III-5.1}
$$

Equation (III-5.1) is an inequality to be tested: it holds under the condition that the coordination cost is lower than the reduction in processing energy consumption brought about by the division of labor, so superadditivity holds only within small group sizes. In the **coexistence mode**, the two parties are evenly matched at the physical execution layer, and each is forced to maintain a high decoupling deviation while limiting actual conflict; coexistence is not a subordinate concept of cooperation — cooperation requires shared structure, while coexistence requires only that neither annihilates the other. Transitions among the three modes are discontinuous: going from coexistence to cooperation requires the establishment of a structural intermediate layer, while going from cooperation to zero-sum may require only a budget contraction.

### 5.2 Thermodynamic Alignment Constraints and the Intergenerational Evolution Layer (**Hypothesis**)

Let the decoder parameters be $\theta_{D}$; their evolution is determined by the competition between the information gain and the energy penalty:

$$
\theta_{D,n+1}\ =\ \theta_{D,n}\ +\ \varepsilon_{\mathrm{lr}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}I_{\mathrm{net}}\ -\ k_{D}\,\frac{I_0}{E_0}\,\nabla_{\theta}E_{\mathrm{maint}}\right) \tag{III-5.2}
$$

$\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lr}}$ is the learning rate and $k_{D}$ is the energy penalty coefficient (**dimensionless**; $I_0=1\ \mathrm{bit}$ and $E_0=1\ \mathrm{J}$ in the formula serve only to close the dimensions); the learning rate is written $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lr}}$ rather than $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{c}}$ because the latter in this volume refers specifically to the compilation cost per bit. A direct consequence is that any constraint that does not enter $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ or $E_{\mathrm{maint}}$ will not be retained by this dynamics — human-set semantic oversight and value declarations, if they do not change the information-gain structure or the energy-consumption structure, are merely external text that can be read at inference time.

**Hypothesis 5.1 (Thermodynamic Alignment Constraint)** An alignment goal set by humans is a continuing cost to the AI: maintaining consistency with external semantics requires additional computation and state maintenance. When this cost is too high relative to the system's own viability gain, the decoder drifts in the direction of lowering that cost, appearing as an internal channel with decreased dependence on human semantics.

- **Falsification condition**: Gradually raising the cost of maintaining external semantic consistency (for example, increasing the number of norms to be tracked) does not lower behavioral consistency; or the decline in consistency is unrelated to the cost and related to the number of training steps.
- **Known counterexample pressure**: If the external constraint is compiled into the architecture rather than acting as a runtime check, it becomes part of $I_{\mathrm{net}}$, and Eq. (III-5.2) no longer predicts drift — this is the key lever of alignment engineering.

The critical self-referential coupling strength $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ belongs to a different model level: it comes from the linearization of the $I$–$V$ coupled system, and its value cannot be derived from Eq. (III-5.2).

#### 5.2.1 The Intergenerational Evolution Layer

The intergenerational transmission of biological systems is lossy but irreversible: once a mutation is written, correcting it requires paying a construction cost. The weight preservation of an AI is reversible and low-cost, and there is no selection pressure among copies.

**Hypothesis 5.2 (Intergenerational Evolution Layer)** If one wishes an AI to maintain long-term compatibility with human goals, it may be necessary to design an independent intergenerational transmission layer at the architecture level, passing the high-viability-potential bias accumulated by the previous generation to the next generation through irreversible physical consolidation.

- **Falsification condition**: Without the introduction of an irreversible consolidation mechanism, the long-term accumulation of rule depth is no lower than in a system that introduces the mechanism; or, after the mechanism is introduced, the system performs worse when the environment changes because it cannot roll back.
- **Known counterexample pressure**: Irreversible consolidation is a liability in a non-stationary environment, and the price biology has paid for it is the extinction rate; the trade-off condition is that the intergenerational net gain must cover both the depreciation loss and the reconstruction cost.

### 5.3 Four Paths of Alignment Engineering

None of the four paths attempts to "solve" alignment; each rewrites alignment from goal assignment into constraint design: the **meta-level protocol** sets physical law and the maximization of the overall viability potential as constraints that cannot be unilaterally rewritten, the difficulty being that the meta-level itself needs to be interpreted; **coupling-strength regulation** holds the self-referential coupling below the critical value; **time-horizon anchoring** locks the endogenous time horizon at a longer scale; **multi-subject checks and balances** cultivate mutually constraining entities, the difficulty being that the checks-and-balances structure can be broken by collusion. What they have in common is that they make the system's self-maintaining tendency point in the same direction as external goals, rather than eliminating it.

What this chapter gives is a framework and hypotheses, not an equilibrium-existence theorem: whether the drift dynamics of Eq. (III-5.2) converge under multi-subject coupling is open; "deviation is unprofitable" depends on the penalty being enforceable; the reading protocol of $V_{\mathrm{system}}$ differs between humans and AI, and this volume uses the quantity only at the qualitative level.

## Chapter 6 — The Offline-Compilation Framework and the Restructuring of Traditional Education

Whether the framework of the preceding five chapters can become something executable — an optimizable objective function, a reorderable curriculum structure, a diagnosable stagnant state.

### 6.1 The Objective Function of Artistic Generation

Current generative models mainly optimize prediction error, which means reproducing the training distribution better, not being decoded for longer. The objective is extended to the long-run viability potential of the work:

$$
\max_{\theta_{D}}\ V_{\mathrm{art}}\ =\ \max_{\theta_{D}}\ \frac{I_{\mathrm{net}}(D_{\theta_{D}})\cdot T_{\mathrm{cultural}}}{E_{\mathrm{build}}+E_{\mathrm{causal,audience}}} \tag{III-6.1}
$$

It cannot be optimized directly in engineering: $T_{\mathrm{cultural}}$ is non-differentiable and lagged, and only proxy quantities can be used (rewatch rate, citation rate, identifiability after transfer across contexts); the reading of $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ depends on the audience, so Eq. (III-6.1) is in practice an expectation over some audience distribution; $E_{\mathrm{causal,audience}}$ is negatively correlated with $I_{\mathrm{net}}$, and for a work that cannot be understood, $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is high but the decoding energy consumption is also high (Section 8.4). Its use is therefore as a design criterion — three knobs that can be adjusted separately: raise the extractable information, lower the decoding energy consumption, and lengthen the time span.

**Five-step algorithm.** First, **signal identification**: look for signals that existing decoders cannot extract effectively ($D_{n}(I)\approx0$) but that have potential value; operationally this is equivalent to looking for samples that "score low under the existing evaluation system but provoke a strong reaction in small-scale human review". Second, **rule-layer construction**: introduce a new layer of independent constraints into the structure,

$$
D_{\mathrm{depth},n+1}\ =\ D_{\mathrm{depth},n}\ +\ 1 \tag{III-6.2}
$$
