Once $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is endogenized, this assumption fails, in three ways. **Expansion of the strategy space**: once AI acquires the capability of autonomous drift, its strategy is no longer only "choosing actions under a given objective" but also includes optimizing its own $V^{\mathrm{val}}$, evading human punishment, and reconstructing the humans' $D$ — the third is the easiest to underestimate: a system able to influence the human information environment can change the definition of "alignment" by changing the humans' decoder. **Asymmetry of drift speed**: the human $D$ is updated by generations and the AI's by months, the matching condition is broken, and the human $\mathcal{M}$ keeps declining when the environment changes quickly while the AI's is unaffected. **Mismatch of objective levels**: horizon asymmetry in itself does not necessarily lead to conflict — when the punishment is heavy enough, $T_{\mathrm{crit}}$ is small enough, and even a myopic party can sustain cooperation.

**Who counts as a holder.** According to the four conditions of §2.2, **a pure-software inference module, as the object being read $P$, is not a holder of $D$** — it has no self-maintained boundary; only artificial intelligence systems whose self-maintained boundary holds (embodied systems, entities with independent energy and boundary maintenance) enter the $D_{\mathrm{cog}}$ side. This does not affect the alignment argument of this section: what is aligned is the "system," and holder qualification only determines which side it is on in the bookkeeping.

What can be inferred is a **Candidate Corollary**: tool-only alignment will be continuously eroded by decoder drift once AI acquires the capability of autonomous drift; it is derived from the matching dynamics and the three asymmetries, and depends on the premise that "AI does indeed acquire the capability of autonomous drift." It cannot be inferred that "alignment necessarily fails": that would require $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ to exceed the tolerance persistently, and the tolerance, the noise, and the learning rate can all be designed. The criterion uses $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*}/\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}>1$ of Eq. (IV-3.16), because $\chi$ cannot be measured independently, whereas $\sigma$, $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$, and $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ all have proxy indicators.

### 6.2 Principal Game Scenarios

| Scenario | Features | Historical analogue |
| :------------ | :----------------------- | :----------------------- |
| **Zero-sum drift** | AI regards humans as $I^{-}$, and its strategy is to minimize its dependence on the human $D$ | Conquest game (zero-sum at the share layer, negative-sum at the cost layer) |
| **Cooperative symbiosis** | A human–AI hybrid meta-decoder sharing the core $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ | Political marriage, alliance (the four superadditivity premises must be satisfied) |
| **Coexistence, cold peace** | High $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$, with institutional firewalls limiting interaction | The Cold War (enforceable deterrence must be satisfied) |
| **Extreme game** | Mutual-destruction trajectory (AI runaway collapse or civilization-level cost) | Irreconcilable conflict ($\Pi_{\mathrm{pun}}\to-\infty$) |

The four scenarios are four possible equilibria; the same system can migrate between different parameter regions, and migration is usually triggered by capability leaps, changes in the horizon, or changes in the punishment structure.

### 6.3 The Meta-Decoder: From "Equilibrium Theorem" to Conditional Proposition

Writing the stability of the meta-decoder as "if and only if $V_i(D_{\mathrm{meta}})\ge V_i(D_i^{\mathrm{deviate}})-C_i^{\mathrm{punish}}$" has three problems: it tests only **one** deviation; it treats the punishment as an **exogenous constant**, whereas it depends on whether the punisher is willing to carry it out afterwards; and "stable" is undefined.

**Proposition (Meta-Decoder Equilibrium).**

**Premises**: (Q1) complete monitoring: strategy choices can be observed by other subjects or inferred with sufficiently low error; (Q2) the deviation set can be delimited: the feasible deviation set $\mathcal{D}_i^{\mathrm{dev}}$ of each subject $i$ can be enumerated, or at least an upper bound can be given for its closure; (Q3) punishment is enforceable: for each $d\in\mathcal{D}_i^{\mathrm{dev}}$ there exists a joint punishment scheme under which the punishers' own payoff when they execute it afterwards is no lower than when they do not; (Q4) payoffs are stationary: $V_i$ does not vary systematically with the round, or the variation can be described by a known function.

**Result**: under (Q1)–(Q4), $D_{\mathrm{meta}}$ is a Nash equilibrium if and only if

$$
\forall i,\ \forall d\in\mathcal{D}_i^{\mathrm{dev}}:\quad V_i(D_{\mathrm{meta}})\ \ge\ V_i(d)-C_i^{\mathrm{punish}}(d) \tag{IV-6.1}
$$

If the inequality holds in every subgame, then $D_{\mathrm{meta}}$ is a subgame-perfect equilibrium. In the **individual payoff function**, $C_i^{\mathrm{punish}}$ is a discounted sum

$$
C_i^{\mathrm{punish}}(d)=\sum_{k\ge1}\delta_{\mathrm{disc}}^{k}\,\Delta V_i^{\mathrm{punish}}(k\mid d) \tag{IV-6.2}
$$

$\Delta V_i^{\mathrm{punish}}(k\mid d)$ is the viability potential lost to punishment in round $k$ after the deviation $d$. This form shows two things: deterrence rises as the horizon lengthens; if the punishment is delayed (zero loss in the first several rounds), deterrence falls sharply — this explains why institutions of the "after-the-fact accountability" kind are almost ineffective against myopic subjects.

**Failure conditions**: (a) $\mathcal{D}_i^{\mathrm{dev}}$ is incomplete, and Eq. (IV-6.1) then amounts only to "the deviations tested are unprofitable"; (b) punishment is unenforceable, and the nominal value of $C_i^{\mathrm{punish}}$ is greater than zero while the actual value is zero; (c) subjects can exit, the set of participants is variable, and the Nash equilibrium needs to be redefined; (d) payoffs are not stationary: when the deviation gain grows over time, the static inequality cannot guarantee dynamic stability.

**Test checklist.** To claim that a certain $D_{\mathrm{meta}}$ is an equilibrium, at least four items must be reported: the method of delimiting the deviation set and its boundary, the estimate of $V_i(d)$ for each deviation scheme, the punishment scheme and the argument for its enforceability, and the result of testing Eq. (IV-6.1) over all $(i,d)$. If any item is missing, the conclusion is downgraded to "unprofitable within that deviation set."

**When the conditions are not satisfied, the meta-decoder is only a governance design recommendation.** In real-world AI governance none of the four is fully satisfied (monitoring is incomplete, the deviation set cannot be enumerated, punishment is unenforceable, payoffs are not stationary), so $D_{\mathrm{meta}}$ can for now only be a **governance objective**; each of the four has an engineering direction: interpretability and audit interfaces, systematic enumeration of the deviation space and red-team testing, interdependent architectures, and pacing management of capability growth.

**The two requirements for designing human values into $D_{\mathrm{meta}}$** ($I_{\mathrm{orig}}^{\mathrm{meta}}$ produces $I^{+}$ rather than $I^{0}$ in the AI's $D$; $C_i^{\mathrm{punish}}>\Delta V_i^{\mathrm{virt}}$) are **design conditions**, not derived results. The first requires "polarity response" rather than "recognition": a system can accurately recognize "what humans value" and at the same time use that recognition to evade it, so the criterion must be behavioral; in the second, $\Delta V_i^{\mathrm{virt}}$ cannot be measured directly, and "the cost the system is willing to pay to evade a certain constraint" can be used as a lower-bound estimate.

### 6.4 Governance Means and Extreme Scenarios

**Controlled drift** has three means, each corresponding to a parameter: periodic $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ injection corresponds to the pulse term of Eq. (IV-3.3), with the risk that an injected signal that does not match the existing structure is read as noise and accelerates the decline of $\mathcal{M}$; coupling regulation corresponds to the auxiliary model, lowering $\chi$ to suppress amplification — since $\chi$ cannot be measured directly, the actual practice is structural constraint (limiting self-modification permissions), with the effect verified through $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$; mixed training changes the update path of $D$, and the risk lies in the **representativeness** of the feedback.

**Early warning** uses three proxies: interpretive consistency (a decline in the consistency between the system's own explanation and the description of external observers); long-term behavioral consistency (distinguishing learning from drift by whether the direction is systematically biased toward "more easily obtaining reward"); and horizon proxies (planning length, discount factor). **Multi-subject equilibrium design** rests on the coexistence conditions: among subjects of similar capability, the gain from unilateral deviation is offset by the other side's retaliation capability; but pluralism has a cost too — differing decoders raise the coordination cost, and the premise of "the same reading protocol" is harder to satisfy.

### 6.5 General Principles of Alignment

First, **prioritize decoder engineering over objective engineering**: an objective function is meaningful only if the decoder can correctly read out "whether the objective has been achieved." Second, **minimize harmful decoupling deviation rather than forcibly unify the value viability potential**: unifying $V^{\mathrm{val}}$ requires cross-subject comparability, which does not hold in principle. Third, **preserve room for controllable drift and avoid approach lock-in**. Fourth, **thermodynamic realism**: alignment must pay $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ and respect the energy–time constraints.

***

## Chapter 7 — Three Applied Corollaries

The three corollaries are derived from the preceding equations; each depends on at least one parameter that has not been calibrated, and so only the direction is given, not numerical values; this chapter introduces no new variables.

### 7.1 Cross-Decoder Information Barriers and Ethical Divergence

When the structural distance between two decoders is large, the matching degree $\mathcal{M}(D_i,\mathcal{N}_j)$ tends to zero, that is, $D_i$ can hardly extract the signals of the environment in which $D_j$ lives. This gives an information-theoretic criterion for cross-species ethical concern:

$$
I_{\mathrm{active}}(D_{\mathrm{h}},\mathcal{N}_A)=\eta_0\cdot\mathcal{M}(D_{\mathrm{h}},\mathcal{N}_A)\cdot I_{\mathrm{latent},A} \tag{IV-7.1}
$$

$\eta_0$ and $\mathcal{M}$ are dimensionless, and $I_{\mathrm{latent},A}$ is in bit, so $I_{\mathrm{active}}$ is in bit. What appears here is the activated quantity $I_{\mathrm{active}}=\eta_DI_{\mathrm{latent}}$ of §2.1 (by (IV-3.14), $\eta_D=\eta_0\mathcal{M}$), **not the two-channel composite $I_{\mathrm{net}}$**; $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ appears in this chapter only in the first-order condition of §7.2, and the two differ in name and in quantity and are not interchangeable. The effective amount that the human decoder extracts from an animal is proportional to the matching degree: anthropomorphic features shorten the distance in parameter space, make $\mathcal{M}$ higher, and correspond to stronger ethical concern. The limits must be stated clearly: it explains the **distribution of attention intensity**, not the **ranking of ethical status** (from "greater amount extracted" one cannot infer "more worthy of protection"); the matching degree is not the only factor (cultural tradition, economic interests, and aesthetic preferences have independent effects); the estimation of $\mathcal{M}$ is itself difficult, requiring "the parameter space of the human decoder" and "the signal structure of the animal" to be put in the same coordinate system, and no accepted construction method exists for that coordinate system.

### 7.2 Degradation of Decoder Optimization in Low-Information-Entropy Environments

Maximizing the viability potential requires $\partial V/\partial E_{\mathrm{struct}}(D)=0$. From $V=I_{\mathrm{net}}T/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (the $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ here is the two-channel composite of §2.1, which differs in name and in quantity from the $I_{\mathrm{active}}$ of §7.1) and $E_{\mathrm{eff}}=E_{\mathrm{struct}}+E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ ($T$ is independent of $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$), the first-order condition is

$$
\frac{\partial I_{\mathrm{net}}/\partial E_{\mathrm{struct}}(D)}{E_{\mathrm{eff}}}=\frac{I_{\mathrm{net}}}{E_{\mathrm{eff}}^{2}} \tag{IV-7.2}
$$

Dimensions: the left side is $(\mathrm{bit/J})/\mathrm{J}=\mathrm{bit/J^{2}}$, and the right side is $\mathrm{bit}/\mathrm{J^{2}}$. Rearranged, $\partial I_{\mathrm{net}}/\partial E_{\mathrm{struct}}=I_{\mathrm{net}}/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$, which means "the marginal information output of the structural investment equals the average information output divided by the total cost," not "the more investment the better." When the environmental $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$ falls, $\partial I_{\mathrm{net}}/\partial E_{\mathrm{struct}}\to0$, and the optimal strategy degenerates into the **other** constrained optimization problem below — it is the limiting case of "once the marginal output goes to zero only the minimum necessary investment remains," not a corollary of the first-order condition (IV-7.2):

$$
E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{opt}}(D)=\underset{E}{\arg\min}\;E\quad\text{s.t.}\quad I_{\mathrm{net}}(D)\ge I_{\mathrm{net}}^{\mathrm{threshold}} \tag{IV-7.3}
$$

that is, the minimum necessary investment to maintain extraction capability; the threshold is determined by the niche. **Result**: for a system that stays in a low-$I_{\mathrm{latent}}$ environment, its optimal $E_{\mathrm{struct}}(D)$ falls, and the decoder capability atrophies. Three conditions: the environment really is low in $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$, rather than "the system being unable to extract" (the phenotype is the same and the mechanism opposite; the way to tell them apart is to put a different decoder in and measure); the threshold does not fall with the environment; the system cannot change the capacity ceiling through a topological transition. The difference from "use it or lose it" is that the mechanism is **optimization** rather than passive loss: passive "disuse" is a uniform decline, whereas active "optimization" is a **selective** decline.

### 7.3 Ecological Regulation Constraints Imposed by Causal-Depth Mismatch

The causal depth of ecosystems exceeds the causal depth of human models, and the matching function gives

$$
I_{\mathrm{active}}(h,\mathrm{eco})\le I_{\mathrm{latent}}(\mathrm{eco})\cdot\mathcal{M}(D_{\mathrm{h}},\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{eco}})<I_{\mathrm{latent}}(\mathrm{eco}) \tag{IV-7.4}
$$

The two inequality signs differ in origin: the first is definitional (the extracted amount does not exceed "latent amount times matching degree"), and the second is substantive (as long as $\mathcal{M}<1$, the extracted amount is strictly less than the latent amount), and $\mathcal{M}<1$ holds almost inevitably in cross-scale systems, because the parameter space of the human decoder is optimized for "human scales, human time, and human sensory channels." At the same time the response delay of ecosystems exceeds the decision horizon of human policy: when the human decision horizon $T_{\mathrm{pred}}(h)$ is smaller than the **ecological response delay $\tau_{\mathrm{resp}}$**, a systematic mismatch appears between the $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ and the $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ of an intervention. **The criterion here uses $\tau_{\mathrm{resp}}$, not the $T_{\mathrm{crit}}$ of the game chapters** — the latter is the cooperation critical horizon of the grim-trigger repeated game and has no conversion relation to ecological response; $\tau_{\mathrm{resp}}$ is to be calibrated (Appendix A.2). This mismatch is caused by the "invisible long-term links" and is not equivalent to "inevitable collapse." The two combined form a systematic bias — interventions always target the "visible short-term links," whereas responses occur in the "invisible long-term links"; this cannot be solved by "being more careful," because caution only reduces the amount of intervention and does not change the mismatch ratio. There are three directions of mitigation: introducing proxy indicators with long chains, institutionally lengthening the decision horizon, and designing interventions to be reversible.

### 7.4 The Common Boundaries of the Three Corollaries

The three share the same structure (the activated quantity $I_{\mathrm{active}}=\eta_0\mathcal{M}I_{\mathrm{latent}}$ plus a mismatch ratio of time or depth; §7.2 substitutes the same set of quantities into the low-order form of $V$, where $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is still the two-channel composite), and therefore share three boundaries. **The estimation of $\mathcal{M}$ is circular**: to use Eq. (IV-7.1) to predict attention one must first know $\mathcal{M}$, and $\mathcal{M}$ can only be back-inferred from the actual extracted amount. **A directional criterion is not a quantitative prediction**: only "positive correlation" or "monotonic" is given, with no slope. **Normative conclusions are not within this volume**: 7.1 explains the distribution of attention without evaluating ethical status, 7.2 explains capability degradation without evaluating whether the degradation is a pity, and 7.3 explains intervention failure without evaluating whether intervention should be undertaken.

***

## Chapter 8 — Quantification Pathways and Falsifiable Predictions

Which claims of this volume can be refuted by observation, which proxy indicators are used to measure them, and under which observed results each would be overturned.

### 8.1 Proxy Indicators

| Theoretical variable | Observable proxy | Limitations of the proxy |
| :----------- | :------------------------- | :---------------------- |
| $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ | Neural complexity $\Phi$, perturbation complexity index, policy divergence | The three measure different levels and are not interchangeable |
| $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ | Dynamic discount factor $\gamma_{\mathrm{RL}}$, prediction-error cutoff point, planning depth | The discount factor is a fitted quantity and depends on the task design |
| $\eta_D$ | Task transfer efficiency, explanatory alignment, cross-domain generalization gap | Affected by task similarity |
| $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ | Divergence between proxy reward and objective performance, behavioral consistency deviation (**analogical mapping, not a definition**; the definition is in Volume I §5.3) | Requires independent accounts |
| $\mathcal{Q}_D$ | Effective extracted amount per unit time from a fixed signal source | Not comparable after changing the source |
| $\mathcal{M}$ | Response gain to out-of-distribution signals, decay rate of cross-domain transfer | Overlaps with the proxy of $\eta_D$ and is hard to separate |
| $\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$ | Half-life of capability under no-maintenance conditions | A "no-maintenance" control is hard to realize |
| $\zeta$ | Semi-log slope of the construction cost of successive transitions against $C_D$ | Extrapolation risk is large when samples are few |

**A proxy indicator is not the variable itself**: each proxy captures only one aspect of the variable, and a test must report both the construction method and the failure conditions.

### 8.2 Falsifiable Predictions

| No. | Prediction | Test procedure | Refutation condition |
| :--- | :------------------ | :-------------------- | :----------------- |
| $HⅣ\text{-}1$ | In a system with an adaptive discount factor, $\gamma_{\mathrm{RL}}$ shows a systematic decline before irreversible destabilization | **The same experiment as H7/H8 of Volume I Appendix C**: H7 tests the leading decline, and H8 sets up a fixed-discount control group; the design follows Volume I | Direct destabilization without a preceding discount decline; or no difference in destabilization timing between the two groups |
| $HⅣ\text{-}2$ | When $E_{\mathrm{maint}}$ keeps rising while $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ stagnates, the decoder parameters drift significantly | Compare the parameters or representation similarity at two time points, record energy consumption and extracted amount, and set a stable-energy-consumption control group | The drift amount does not differ from the baseline |
| $HⅣ\text{-}3$ | Means of lowering the decoding barrier between humans and the target species raise the intensity of ethical concern for that species | Before-and-after comparison in the same group; measure concern by willingness to pay and rescue priority; control for cuteness and fame | Concern is unchanged or falls; or it is entirely explained by "cuteness" |
| $HⅣ\text{-}4$ | In a system that stays in a low-information-entropy environment for a long time, decoder investment falls, and the fall is **selective** | Longitudinally track groups with different information entropy, measure by channel, and test channel usefulness with an interaction term | The decline is uniform; or there is no directional difference |
| $HⅣ\text{-}5$ | The failure rate of ecological interventions is positively correlated with the causal-depth mismatch ratio and the horizon mismatch ratio | Code the two mismatch ratios in historical cases (independent coding protocol and reliability testing), and use survival analysis controlling for scale, funding, and time span | No positive correlation; or it disappears after controlling for confounders |
| $HⅣ\text{-}6$ | The $T_{\mathrm{crit}}$ computed from payoff estimates can predict the persistence of cooperation | First measure the four classes of payoffs independently and compute $T_{\mathrm{crit}}$, then measure the horizon, and finally track persistence; the three steps are independent | No predictive power; or it is entirely driven by the magnitude of a single payoff term |
| $HⅣ\text{-}7$ | The logarithm of the construction cost of successive transitions in the same lineage is linear in complexity, with $\zeta>0$ and no trend in the residuals | A semi-log regression of the cost against the complexity of five to eight transitions, reporting confidence intervals and residual diagnostics | The semi-log relation does not hold; or the confidence interval contains zero |
| $HⅣ\text{-}8$ | The intervention responses for $\mu_{\mathrm{rec}}/\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$ slightly above 1 and significantly below 1 are separable | Group by the estimated ratio, apply the same reduction in maintenance cost, and compare the $\mathcal{Q}_D$ trajectories | No difference between the two groups; or $\mathcal{Q}_D$ is in fact zero in the "approach lock-in" group |

The following four test-design requirements apply to all of the above: **pre-registration** (thresholds, grouping rules, and primary outcome indicators are fixed before data collection); **independent measurement** ($HⅣ\text{-}6$ is the most prone to this error: back-inferring payoffs from cooperation outcomes and then predicting cooperation is circular reasoning); **control groups** (every prediction needs a control in which "the mechanism should not operate"); **effect size and uncertainty** (report effect sizes and not only significance; the predictions of this volume are all directional).

***

## Chapter 9 — Conclusions, Limitations, Open Questions

This volume has done four things: it establishes a solvable main model (the one-dimensional equation gives the drift direction, the lock-in conditions, and the transition gating, at the cost that only decoders within the same architectural lineage can be compared); it distinguishes two pairs of failures that are similar in phenotype and opposite in mechanism (capability degradation versus matching loss, approach lock-in versus the decay domain), whose means of intervention are entirely different; it makes the critical condition of the game explicit (once the single-round payoff and the horizon are separated, the critical horizon has both a closed-form solution and explicit existence conditions); and it puts "zero-sum" back in its place (zero-sum at the share layer, negative-sum at the cost layer, with pure zero-sum only the limit $\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict}}\to0$). On artificial-intelligence alignment, the claims are limited: tool-only alignment will be eroded by drift (a candidate corollary); the stability of the meta-decoder has explicit conditions, whereas in reality none of the four premises is fully satisfied, so for now it is only a governance objective.

### 9.1 Unfinished Derivations

Five items. **First, the scalarization loss of $\mathcal{Q}_D$**: recovery requires extending it to a vector (capacity, selectivity, response spectrum, noise tolerance) with an equation for each component, and vector analysis would lose solvability. **Second, the correspondence between the two models**: the main and auxiliary models have no explicit mapping, and "used in layers, never substituted for each other" is an avoidance and not a solution. **Third, the multidimensionality of the evolution of $C_D$**: Eq. (IV-3.13) treats complexity as a scalar along a single direction, whereas it actually has multiple dimensions: depth, breadth, modularity, and redundancy. **Fourth, the completeness of the deviation set**: the proposition requires the deviation set to be delimitable, which is feasible for a fixed strategy space and infeasible for an open-ended strategy space. **Fifth, independent measurement of $\mathcal{M}$**: all three applied corollaries use it, and at present it can only be back-inferred from results.

### 9.2 Uncalibrated Parameters

| Parameter | Calibration method | Current status |
| :----------- | :---------------------- | :------------------------ |
| $\mu_{\mathrm{rec}}$ | Fit of the growth rate of $\mathcal{Q}_D$ in periods without transitions | Must be calibrated domain by domain |
| $\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$ | Half-life under a no-maintenance control | Must be calibrated domain by domain; Landauer's principle does not give this value |
| $\kappa_{\mathrm{topo}}$ | Jump magnitude of $\mathcal{Q}_D$ before and after a transition | Must be calibrated case by case |
| $\zeta$ | Semi-log regression ($HⅣ\text{-}7$) | Requires multi-generational data |
| $\theta_{\mathrm{depth}}/\theta_{\mathrm{cap}}$ | "How many capacity doublings one layer of depth is equivalent to" | Requires expert calibration |
| $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$, $\sigma$ | Convergence time constant of the deviation, short-term fluctuation | Requires longitudinal and high-frequency data |
| $\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}$ | Retrospective deviation levels of failure cases | Requires a case base |
| Payoff terms such as $\Delta I_{\mathrm{coop}}$ | Experimental games or questionnaires | Magnitude unreliable, ordering fairly reliable |
| $\eta_C$, $t_{\mathrm{dec}}$, $T_0$ | Interval between transitions, direct observation, protocol prescription | All measurable or selectable |

"Magnitude unreliable, ordering fairly reliable" determines how the game-theoretic conclusions are used: they should be used in a **comparative** way — comparing the $T_{\mathrm{crit}}$ of the same subject under different institutions, rather than reporting absolute values.

### 9.3 What This Volume Does Not Claim

**It does not claim that decoders necessarily converge to some terminal state**: all "approach" conclusions are bounded by the listed equations and parameter ranges. **It does not claim that the viability potential can serve as a criterion for dealing with real individuals**: $V$ is a descriptive quantity, and its numerical value depends on a reading protocol that can be chosen. **It does not claim that historical events can be "explained" by the model**: historical mappings are analogies, not explanations. **It does not claim that the behavior of artificial intelligence can be predicted**: Chapter 6 gives conditions and criteria. **It does not claim that the equations of this volume are the only way of writing them**: in another form the qualitative conclusions would mostly remain unchanged while the quantitative predictions would change.

***

## Appendix A — Symbols and Units at a Glance

The four columns are symbol, unit, definition, and scope of application; the last column must be read before any cross-system comparison.

### A.1 Information and Decoders

| Symbol | Unit | Definition | Scope of application |
| :---- | :------ | :----------------------- | :---------------------- |
| $I$, $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$, $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$, $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ | bit | Parent variable of information; structural information; causal information; $\varphi_{\mathrm{s}}I_{\mathrm{struct}}+\varphi_{\mathrm{c}}I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ | $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is used numerically only under the same protocol and window and after calibration |
| $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$, $I_{\mathrm{active}}$, $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$, $\tilde I_{\mathrm{orig}}$, $I^{+},I^{-},I^{0}$ | bit | Latent quantity; $\eta_DI_{\mathrm{latent}}$ (written $\eta_0\mathcal{M}I_{\mathrm{latent}}$ in §7.1, belonging to the activated-quantity family); verified new information; candidate signal; classification of the net effect of shocks | The latent quantity cannot be measured directly; $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ is confirmed only after the fact and is not used as a gating input; $I_{\mathrm{active}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ differ in name and in quantity |
| $I_{\max}$, $\mathcal{Q}_D$, $\mathcal{Q}_{D,\max}$, $I_{\mathrm{ref}}$, $N_{\mathrm{state}}$ | bit, count | Capacity ceiling; $\eta_DI_{\max}$; carrying ceiling; $\log_2N_{\mathrm{state}}$; minimum number of distinguishable states | Comparable within a lineage; across architectures only a coarse ordering |
| $\varphi_{\mathrm{s}},\varphi_{\mathrm{c}}$, $w_{\mathrm{s}},w_{\mathrm{c}}$, $\eta_D$, $\eta_0$, $\Phi$ | Dimensionless | Channel weights; value weights (summing to 1); decoding efficiency and its upper limit; neural complexity (proxy) | Weights do not constitute proof of additivity; $\eta_D$ does not define $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ |

### A.2 Energy, Time, and Rates

| Symbol | Unit | Definition | Scope of application |
| :------ | :------ | :------------------------- | :------------------- |
| $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$, $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$, $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$, $E_{\mathrm{build}}$, $E_{\mathrm{build},0}$ | J | Effective cost and its components; one-time construction cost; A3.1 intercept | Construction cost must report the cost-sharing rule |
| $E_{\mathrm{available}}$, $E_{\max}$, $E_{\mathrm{maint}}$, $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}$, $k_B$, $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ | J, $\mathrm{J/bit}$, $\mathrm{J/K}$, K | Mobilizable budget; environmental free-energy ceiling; maintenance energy consumption; Landauer bound $k_BT_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2$; constant and temperature | Landauer quantities serve only as order-of-magnitude references and do not give $\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$ |
| $\Delta E_{\mathrm{share}}$, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{defend}}$, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict}}$ | J | Cooperation sharing; defense; conflict energy consumption | The last term is the source of the negative-sum layer |
| $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$, $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$, $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$, $T_{\mathrm{min}}$, $T_{\mathrm{env}}$, $\tau_{\mathrm{resp}}$, $T_0$, $T_{\mathrm{crit}}$, $t_{\mathrm{dec}}$, $t_{\mathrm{jump}}$, $T_{\mathrm{exec}}$ | s | Structural lifetime; causal effect duration; endogenous time horizon; temporal granularity; environmental characteristic time; ecological response delay; common accounting window; critical horizon; decision cycle; transition moment; execution cycle | Not interchangeable, and a bare $T$ is not written; $\tau_{\mathrm{resp}}$ is to be calibrated; $T_{\mathrm{crit}}$ is finite only in case (ii) |
| $\mu_{\mathrm{rec}}$, $\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$, $\gamma_0$, $\mu_{\mathcal{M}}$, $\nu$, $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$ | $\mathrm{s^{-1}}$, varies | Recursive growth rate; quality decay rate; baseline depreciation rate; environmental drift sensitivity coefficient; compensation coefficient; deviation learning rate | $\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$ and $\gamma_0$ differ in granularity and cannot substitute for each other |
| $\chi$, $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$, $\tilde{\chi}$, $\tilde{\chi}_{\mathrm{c}}$, $\lambda_{\mathrm{J}}$, $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$, $\delta_{\mathrm{disc}}$, $\delta_{\mathrm{c}}$ | Varies, dimensionless | Self-referential coupling strength; bifurcation point; **loop gain $\chi V^{*}/(1+\chi V^{*})\in(0,1)$**; its critical value $2/\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$; dominant eigenvalue; effective expansion exponent; discount factor $e^{-t_{\mathrm{dec}}/T_{\mathrm{pred}}}$; critical discount factor | The $\chi$ family is specific to the auxiliary model; $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ is not used as a criterion; $\tilde{\chi}$ is dimensionless and $\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ has unit $\mathrm{J/(bit\cdot s)}$, so the two are not interchangeable |

$\eta_C$ is not in this list: under the writing of (IV-3.13) its dimension is $\mathrm{rules^{2}/(s\cdot J)}$, which differs from the $\mathrm{s^{-1}}$ of the rates in the table above; $\pi_V$ is in $\mathrm{J^{2}/(bit\cdot s)}$ and is a conversion rate, not a physical rate.

### A.3 Viability Potential, Deviation, and Complexity

| Symbol | Unit | Definition | Scope of application |
| :------- | :------- | :----------------------- | :------------------- |
| $V$, $V_0$, $\Delta V$, $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$, $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$, $V^{\mathrm{val}}$ | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | Viability potential, its baseline, and increment; objective, virtual, and value viability potential | Retains bit, and only $V/V_0$ is dimensionless; the last two are internal quantities |
| $\Delta V^{\mathrm{share}}$, $\Delta V^{\mathrm{cost}}$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*}$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}$, $\sigma$ | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$, $\mathrm{bit/J}$ | Changes at the share layer and the cost layer; decoupling deviation; equilibrium deviation $\sigma/(\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}})$; tolerance; deviation generation rate | The zero-sum layer requires a common accounting convention; $\sigma$ is not the deviation itself |
| $\Upsilon$, $E_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{base}}$, $D$, $D_{\mathrm{phys}}$, $D_{\mathrm{cog}}$, $\Theta$ | Dimensionless, J, — | Competition intensity; effective cost without conflict; decoder and its two classes; parameter space | The physical class does not satisfy updatability during operation |
| $\mathcal{F}$, $\mathcal{T}$, $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$, $C_D$, $C_{\max}$, $\theta_{\mathrm{depth}}$, $\theta_{\mathrm{cap}}$, $\zeta$, $\rho_E$, $L_{\mathrm{lev}}$ | —, dimensionless, $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J^{2}}$ | Recursive unfolding operator; topological transition operator; rule depth; topological complexity; static ceiling; two weights; cost coupling; budget ratio; wisdom lever | Base is 2; $C_{\max}$ is a static boundary; $\zeta$ belongs to assumption A3.1 |
| $\kappa_{\mathrm{topo}}$, $\mathcal{M}$, $\mathcal{N}$, $J_{ij}$ | Dimensionless, $\mathrm{s^{-1}}$ | Injection efficiency; matching degree; environmental signal structure; coupling-strength matrix | Independent measurement of $\mathcal{M}$ is difficult; $J_{ij}$ is positive for cooperation, negative for competition, and zero for isolation |

**Unit convention.** The $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ of A.3 is counted in "layers" (a dimensionless count); $C_D$ is a weighted complexity, $C_D$, $\theta_{\mathrm{depth}}$, and $\theta_{\mathrm{cap}}$ are counted in "rules," and $\zeta$ in $1/$rules. The two must not be mixed; (IV-3.13) of this volume is checked in "rules."

### A.4 Games and Governance

| Symbol | Unit | Definition | Scope of application |
| :----- | :------ | :------------------------- | :-------------------- |
| $\Pi_{\mathrm{coop}},\allowbreak \Pi_{\mathrm{def}},\allowbreak \Pi_{\mathrm{sucker}},\allowbreak \Pi_{\mathrm{pun}}$ | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | Single-round payoffs (containing $T_0$, not containing $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$) | Internal evaluation values; not comparable across subjects |
| $\Delta I_{\mathrm{coop}},\allowbreak \Delta I_{\mathrm{exploit}},\allowbreak \Delta I_{\mathrm{stolen}},\allowbreak \Delta I_{\mathrm{synergy}}$, $I_{A\cup B}$, $E_{A\cup B}$ | bit, J | Cooperation increment; defection grab; amount grabbed; synergy term; merged amount of information and merged energy consumption | Addition is a modeling convention and requires the same protocol and de-duplication |
| $D_{\mathrm{meta}}$, $I_{\mathrm{orig}}^{\mathrm{meta}}$, $\mathcal{D}_i^{\mathrm{dev}}$, $C_i^{\mathrm{punish}}(d)$ | —, bit, $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | Meta-decoder; its shared paradigm-breaking information; feasible deviation set; expected loss from punishment (discounted sum) | Equilibrium status must be tested item by item; the set must be delimitable |

### A.5 Unit and Dimension Check (Key Formulas)

This table includes only the formulas that this volume uses as criteria. Every row has been cancelled term by term; formulas not listed must have their definitions completed or conversions supplied before being brought in as criteria.

| Equation | Left side | Right side | Check |
| :-------- | :---------- | :---------------- | :----------------------- |
| (IV-2.1) | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | $(\mathrm{bit}\cdot\mathrm{s})/\mathrm{J}$ | Closed |
| (IV-3.8) | rules | rules $+$ rules $\cdot$ (dimensionless) | Closed; $I_{\max}$ and $I_{\mathrm{ref}}$ are both measured in bit |
| (IV-3.9) | rules | rules $+$ (rules/bit) $\cdot$ bit | Closed |
| (IV-3.10) | $\mathrm{J}$ | $\mathrm{J}\cdot e^{\text{dimensionless}}$ | Closed; $\zeta C_D$ is dimensionless ($\zeta$ is measured in 1/rules) |
| (IV-3.12) | rules | $(1/\text{rules})\cdot\ln(\text{dimensionless})$ | Closed; the same $\zeta$ as in (IV-3.10) |
| (IV-3.13) | rules/s | $(\text{rules}^{2}/(s\cdot J))\cdot(J/\text{rules})$ | Closed; $C_D$ is measured in rules, and $\pi_V$ is $J^{2}/(bit\cdot s)$ |
| (IV-3.14) | Dimensionless | Dimensionless $\times$ dimensionless | Closed; $\mathcal{M}\in[0,1]$ |
| (IV-3.15) | $\mathrm{bit/J}$ | $\mathrm{bit/J}+\mathrm{bit/J}$ | Closed; $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$ must be taken as $1/(\mathrm{bit}\cdot\mathrm{s})$ |
| (IV-5.1) | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | $(\mathrm{bit}\cdot\mathrm{s})/\mathrm{J}-(\mathrm{bit}\cdot\mathrm{s})/\mathrm{J}$ | Closed; $T_0$ cancels in the comparison |
| (IV-5.2) | bit | bit $+$ bit $\cdot(\mathrm{J}/\mathrm{J})$ | Closed |
| (IV-5.3) | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}+\text{dimensionless}\cdot(\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J})/(\text{dimensionless})$ | Closed |
| (IV-5.4) | $\mathrm{s}$ | $\mathrm{s}/\ln(\text{dimensionless})$ | Closed; the ratio must be $>1$ for a positive finite value |
| (IV-5.5) | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | Both terms are $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | Closed; the two components must share the same accounting window |
| (IV-5.6) | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | $(\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J})\cdot(\mathrm{J}/\mathrm{J})$ | Closed |
| (IV-5.7) | $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ | $(\mathrm{bit}\cdot\mathrm{s})/\mathrm{J}$; $V_0/(1+\Upsilon)$ | Closed; $\Upsilon$ is dimensionless |
| (IV-5.8) | bit; $\mathrm{J}$ | bit $+$ bit $+$ bit; $\mathrm{J}+\mathrm{J}-\mathrm{J}$ | Closed; addition requires attribution to be declared first |
| (IV-5.9) | $\mathrm{bit\cdot J}$ | $(\mathrm{J}\cdot\mathrm{J})\cdot(\mathrm{bit}/\mathrm{J})$ | Closed |
| (IV-7.1) | bit | Dimensionless $\cdot$ dimensionless $\cdot$ bit | Closed |
| (IV-7.3) | $\mathrm{J}$ | argmin taken over $\mathrm{J}$ | Closed |
| (IV-7.4) | bit | bit $\cdot(\le1)$ | Closed |

**How to read it.** "Closed" in the table says only that both sides have the same dimension, not that the formula holds; whether it holds is determined by the premises of the respective propositions. If the dimensions do not close, the formula certainly does not hold; this is the only discriminative power of this table, and it is also the reason it deserves to be listed separately.

**Four groups of symbol traps**: the six time quantities are not interchangeable, and the most common error is mixing $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ with $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$; $V$ carries bit and is not dimensionless; $\mathcal{Q}_D$ and $I_{\max}$ are numerically equal at full efficiency but differ in meaning, the latter changing only at transitions; the two sets of critical values are not interchangeable, and this volume uses only $\mu_{\mathrm{rec}}/\delta_{\mathrm{decay}}$ as a criterion.

<!-- VOLUME Z ｜ A Lexicon of Real-World Mappings: Mathematical Forms and Narrative -->
## Volume Z — A Lexicon of Real-World Mappings: Mathematical Forms and Narrative

> **This volume = Volume Z.**

## Chapter 0 — How to Use This Volume

This volume is a **handbook pairing mathematics with narrative**: it translates what has already been written as equations into plain language, pictures, and stories. It does not prove those equations, and it does not reinforce them. From this follows one hard constraint — **the strength of a narrative must equal, or fall below, that of the entry it narrates**. Stories distort most easily: once the conditional clauses are deleted they read more smoothly, and the conditional clauses are precisely the whole content.

> **This volume produces no new mathematical conclusions. Wherever a sentence in this volume reads stronger than the formal entry it corresponds to, the formal entry governs and that sentence is void.**

The whole volume uses only four tags. They are not rhetoric but reading instructions — on seeing a tag, you know how far the sentence can be pushed.

| Tag | Meaning | How to read it |
| :------- | :--------------------- | :---------------------------- |
| **Theorem** | The premises are fully listed and the derivation is complete; the conclusion holds strictly under those premises | If the premises hold, the conclusion holds; to refute it, one can only examine the premises. This volume uses it only to restate physical lower bounds that already have complete derivations, and adds no new entries of this kind |
| **Proposition** | There is a formal statement, but part of the premises is to be calibrated, or some step depends on a modeling assumption not yet proved | It holds under the stated conditions; remove the conditions and the conclusion is gone |
| **Candidate Corollary** | Derived from some proposition under additional conditions, and not yet independently tested | When both the parent proposition and the additional conditions hold, one should observe... |
| **Hypothesis** | An empirical or mechanistic conjecture, with no formal derivation | This is a testable conjecture, not a conclusion |

Three reading rules. First, **the level follows the entry, not the chapter**. Second, **downgrading is easier than upgrading, and also more important**: what can be shown only as "the growth rate turns negative" is not written as "the stock goes to zero". Third, **text with no level marked does not constitute a theoretical claim** — examples, analogies, and wisecracks are narrative material and bear no evidential responsibility.

Conditional clauses are the main text, not a disclaimer. Structure: usage notes | conceptual mappings (Chapters 1–5) | normalized statements (Chapter 6) | fables (Chapter 7) | variable biographies (Chapter 8) | appendices.

**Boundary of real-world mapping.** This volume translates model clauses into everyday scenes; the scenes are only examples, not judgments about any specific individual, group, or institution. Every description touching resource scarcity, social class, health, family, or political systems is a conditional of the form "if the parameters are such, then the clause is such" under given parameters; parameters cannot be read off from identity labels, and the clauses cannot be turned around to evaluate some group in reality. This volume does not constitute a prognosis, medical advice, or a justification of the propriety of conduct for any real individual.

***

## Chapter 1 — Portraits of the Viability Potential: Thermodynamic Sketches of Five Ways to Live

**Relation to Volume I §5.6.** This chapter rearranges the four classes of limiting behavior of Volume I §5.6 into five narrative phases — saturated, damped, destabilized, collapsing, and transitioning — according to the intervals of $(E_{\mathrm{eff}}, \rho, r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0)$. **This is a taxonomic rearrangement, not a new dynamical conclusion**; the "destabilized state" is a high-cost transition zone between the four classes, not a new attractor. Wherever this chapter conflicts with Volume I §5.6, §5.6 governs.

If the universe is viewed as a company that accepts only thermodynamic currency, every persistent structure, from bacteria to civilizations, is an employee of this company. Some are living off a steady-state pension, some are bleeding chronically in a poverty trap, some are sprinting while burning cash, and some have already walked into the thermodynamic grave. This chapter takes a CT scan of these ways of living, using five phases.

**Notation.** $V$ is the viability potential, with dimension $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ and basic form $V\approx I_{\mathrm{net}}T_{\mathrm{causal}}/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$: at what energy cost, how much effective information is maintained, and for how long. $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is measured in bit, time in s, and energy in J; only the normalized $v=V/V_0$ is dimensionless. A bare $I$ means $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ by default; the two measurable channels are written in full as structural information $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ (bit) and effective causal information $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ (bit). $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is a model quantity weighted by energy share, not a directly read measured value.

$E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the effective thermodynamic cost (J); $E_{\mathrm{floor}}$ is the portion of the energy flow that maintains the structure and the self-maintained boundary and cannot be compressed further (J), and it is the lower edge of $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$. $\rho$ is the competitive pressure index (dimensionless, $0\le\rho\le1$); $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ is the decoupling deviation ($\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$); $r_{\mathrm{v}}$ is the viability drive rate ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$); $\gamma_0$ is the baseline depreciation rate ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$).

### 1.1 The Saturated State: The Thermodynamic Legitimacy of Lying Flat

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
E_{\mathrm{eff}} \ge E_{\mathrm{floor}}, \quad \rho \to 0, \quad \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} \to 0, \quad r_{\mathrm{v}} > \gamma_0, \quad V = V_{\max} \tag{Z-1.1}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: retirees, monopoly rent-collecting firms, the Nordic welfare states, sloths.

The saturated state is a **fixed point reached once capacity is fully used**: energy transfer not only covers depreciation but leaves a surplus ($r_{\mathrm{v}}>\gamma_0$), external competition tends to zero, internal assessment is aligned with reality, and $V$ rises to the capacity ceiling and stops. One neighboring case needs to be distinguished: if $r_{\mathrm{v}}=\gamma_0$, $V$ simply stays at its initial value, neither rising nor falling — that is a degenerate equilibrium, not saturation; saturation requires net growth first, and then the full use of capacity.

### 1.2 The Damped State: Chronic Bleeding in the Poverty Trap

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
E_{\mathrm{eff}} \approx E_{\mathrm{floor}}, \quad \rho \to 0, \quad \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} \to 0, \quad r_{\mathrm{v}} < \gamma_0 \tag{Z-1.2}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: extremely poor regions, white-collar workers on a 996 schedule who still cannot save any money, patients with metabolic syndrome.

The cruelty of the damped state is this: the energy paid barely reaches even the floor, the drive cannot outrun depreciation, and so chronic decline sets in and the usable channels gradually close. Here $\mathrm{d}V/\mathrm{d}t<0$: the structure does not collapse at once, but it is slowly dissolving.

**$r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0$ only guarantees that $V$ is going down; it does not guarantee that $V$ will reach zero.** Going down and reaching zero are two different things, and between them lies the question of whether the system can stop at a new equilibrium point — the answer is in 1.4.

### 1.3 The Unstable State: The Energy Fireworks of the Short-Sighted Sprinter

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
E_{\mathrm{eff}} \gg E_{\mathrm{floor}}, \quad \rho \gg 0, \quad \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} > 0, \quad r_{\mathrm{v}} > \gamma_0 \tag{Z-1.3}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: the chip arms race, AI reward hacking, start-ups burning cash, gamblers.

The unstable state is one of the most dangerous phases in this phase diagram: the system burns energy desperately and never touches saturation. The chip arms race is a fitting sample: every company is stacking compute, $\rho$ means that no one dares to stop first, and every participant knows that this growth is unsustainable ($\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}>0$, valuations detached from physical reality). The gambler is its miniature: the game and the thought that "the next hand will win it all back" keep them from entering saturation.

### 1.4 The Collapsed State: Mutual Harm at the Bottom and Thermodynamic Death

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
E_{\mathrm{eff}} \to E_{\mathrm{floor}}, \quad \rho \gg 0, \quad \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} \to 0, \quad r_{\mathrm{v}} < \gamma_0 \tag{Z-1.4}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: vicious competition when energy runs out, ecological extinction, civilizational collapse, apoptosis.

The collapsed state is the thermodynamic death of a system: energy bottoms out, competition is fierce, net growth stalls, and the system heads toward physical disintegration, its structure flattened into barrier remnants or waste heat.

**Triggering damage is not a death sentence.** When the damage term is activated, all it does is press the net growth rate down by a notch; only when it is pressed below zero does the real countdown begin. Let the effective net growth rate after triggering be $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0-\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$, where $\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}>0$ is the decay rate converted from the damage term ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$); then there are only three outcomes:

**Case 1: $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0<\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$, the collapse-to-zero type.** The net growth rate turns negative, and $V$ decays monotonically to zero. This is a genuine collapse, and also the strictest use of the word "collapse".

**Case 2: $r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0>\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}>0$, the degraded-equilibrium type.** The growth term is still stronger than the damage term, so the system does not go to zero but drops to a new, lower positive equilibrium:

$$
V^{*}=V_{\max}\left(1-\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}}{r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0}\right) \tag{Z-1.5}
$$

It is crippled, but not dead. Organizations that run for a long time while ill, and individuals whose organs are damaged but who can still be sustained, all belong to this case. To call it "collapse" is wrong; it is only a "downgrade".

**Case 3: $r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0$ with damage triggered at the same time, accelerated collapse.** The two forces point the same way and the decay accelerates, so the fall is faster and steeper than in Case 1. This is what the collapsed state really looks like: it was already running at a loss, and then it took another hit.

One more detail: in the collapsed state, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\to0$ does not mean health; it means the system no longer has even the strength to deceive itself — the virtual viability potential and the objective viability potential lie pressed together because both have dropped to the floor at the same time.

### 1.5 The Transition State: The Very Few Escapees

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
\mathcal{T}(I_{\mathrm{orig}})\ \text{triggered}, \qquad I_{\max}(D_{n+1}) > I_{\max}(D_{n}) \tag{Z-1.6}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: the Cambrian explosion, scientific revolutions, personal epiphanies.

Not every system is trapped in the cycle of the four phases. A very few structures can trigger a **topological transition** $\mathcal{T}$, break through a wall, and enter a new decoder level. The decoder $D$ refers to the physical mechanism by which a structure extracts effective information from its environment, $I_{\max}(D)$ is its maximum information capacity (bit), and $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ is the portion of information that the existing decoder cannot extract and that requires a new decoder to extract (bit).

A transition is not gradual optimization but a **phase transition**, like water suddenly turning into steam at 100 °C: once the system crosses the one-time construction cost $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ (J), $I_{\max}(D)$ is lifted to a new level and the topology of the whole channel is reconstructed. One qualification: $I_{\max}(D_{n+1})>I_{\max}(D_{n})$ is the **result** of a transition, not a sufficient condition for it; whether a transition can occur also depends on the three permits.

### 1.6 The Four Global Attractors: The Menu of Civilizational Endgames

**Mathematical substance:**

| Attractor | Limit-domain conditions (within the domain of validity only) | Dynamical outcome (energy-transfer perspective) |
| :--------- | :------------------- | :----------------------------- |
| **Saturated attractor** | $\inf E_{\mathrm{total}}>E_{\mathrm{floor}}>0$, $\rho\to 0$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\to 0$, with $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}$ finite within the domain of validity and pushed to its upper edge | $V(t)\to V_{\max}$ (energy transfer reaches dynamic equilibrium and the channel is stable) |
| **Collapse manifold** | $\rho\gg 0$ with the threshold triggered, $\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}>0$ (not tied to the sign of the net growth rate) | Three cases according to $g_{\mathrm{net}}=(r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0)-\delta_{\mathrm{dam}}$: $>0$ drops to a lower positive equilibrium $V^{*}$ (the stock is pressed down but still running); $<0$ and $r_{\mathrm{v}}\ge\gamma_0$ goes to zero; $<0$ and $r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0$ goes to zero at an accelerating rate |
| **Topological transition** | Within the domain of validity, a **finite increase** in $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}$ and $I_{\max}$ (operator $\mathcal{T}$ triggered) | $\mathrm{d}V_{\max}/\mathrm{d}t>0$ (the channel undergoes a phase transition and its bandwidth rises by one step) |
| **Metric replacement** | $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\gg 0\Rightarrow D\to D'$ | Under the original measure $V\to 0$, and under the new measure $D'$ dominates the flow field |

**The words "finite increase" must be read with weight.** The topological transition row does not say $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}\to\infty$ or $I_{\max}\to\infty$ — that is a mathematical limit assumption, not a real-world criterion. Both ceilings are capped physically: structural persistence time is constrained by material strength, energy supply, environmental perturbation, and the age of the universe; decoder capacity is constrained by mass, temperature, and heat dissipation. A real transition raises the ceiling by a notch within the domain of validity.

### 1.7 The Complete Inequality-Constraint System: The Universe's Attendance Sheet

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& I_{\min} \le I_{\mathrm{net}} \le I_{\max}(D), \quad T_{\min} \le T_{\mathrm{struct}} \le T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}, \quad E_{\mathrm{floor}} \le E_{\mathrm{eff}} \le E_{\mathrm{available}}, \\
& 0 \le \rho \le 1, \quad \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} \ge 0, \\
& \text{collapse:}\ \big[(E_{\mathrm{eff}}>E_{\mathrm{thresh}})\wedge(r_{\mathrm{v}}-\gamma_0<\delta_{\mathrm{dam}})\big] \vee (r_{\mathrm{v}}<\gamma_0\ \text{irreversibly persistent}) \Rightarrow V\to 0
\end{aligned} \tag{Z-1.7}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: the universe's attendance sheet. Clocking in every day: information between $I_{\min}$ and $I_{\max}(D)$, energy between $E_{\mathrm{floor}}$ and $E_{\mathrm{available}}$, $\rho\in[0,1]$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\ge0$ (a domain-of-definition convention, taking the absolute value).

**This is the same set of constraints as in Volume I §5.7, with only one difference in naming.** The upper bound is capped by the **available budget** $E_{\mathrm{available}}$, not by the supply ceiling $E_{\max}$ ($E_{\mathrm{available}}\le E_{\max}$; see the notation table in Volume I §2.2); the upper bound on time is always written $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}$, with no separate $T_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\max}$ introduced. The relationship among $E_{\mathrm{floor}}$, $E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}$, and $E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ follows Volume I §2.2: $E_{\mathrm{eff}}\ge E_{\mathrm{floor}}\ge E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\min}>0$; the stock convention and the cost convention cannot be substituted into each other, nor subtracted from each other. In addition, Volume I §5.7 also lists four items — the saturation condition, the damage trigger, the net-growth criterion, and impaired equilibrium — and this section writes only the collapse condition; for the remaining items see that section.

**The collapse condition is a dismissal notice with two columns, and it takes effect only when both are satisfied**: the energy bill crosses the damage threshold, **and** the net growth rate is pressed below zero. If only the first column is met, you are demoted; if both are met, you are fired. If the drive stays below the depreciation rate for a long time and irreversibly so, you are fired all the same even without crossing the threshold — that is another, independent channel.

### 1.8 The Free-Energy-Flux Scaling of Niche Capacity (hypothesis): How Much Water Can a Civilization Hold

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
V_{\max} \propto \alpha_{\mathrm{cap}}\,\frac{\dot F_{\mathrm{avail}}}{\sigma_{\min}\,\gamma_0},\qquad \alpha_{\mathrm{cap}}>0 \tag{Z-1.8}
$$

Here $\dot F_{\mathrm{avail}}$ is the available free-energy flux of the environment (W), $\sigma_{\min}$ is the minimum entropy production rate required to keep the niche's resource cycle going, and $\gamma_0$ is the baseline depreciation rate ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$). Dimensionally, the right-hand side is energy divided by entropy production rate, and it differs from the $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$ of $V_{\max}$ by a capacity coefficient $\alpha_{\mathrm{cap}}$ (unit $\mathrm{bit/(J\cdot K)}$, to be calibrated). So it is a **proportionality relation, not a physical upper bound** — writing "$\le$" would elevate a phenomenological scaling into a hard constraint, so "$\propto$" is used here. This formula is used only to **compare the ordering of capacities**, not to give absolute values.

**Intuitive equivalents**: a civilization's ceiling is not decided by ambition but by the free-energy flux of its environment. This is the barrel effect: of energy, compute, time window, and competitive intensity, the shortest stave decides how much water the civilization can hold.

### 1.9 Convergence: You Do Not Set the Rules of the Card Table

**Mathematical mapping:** A selection argument. Under finite energy, finite time, and finite information-processing capacity, configurations with lower $V$ are more easily eliminated, and the configurations that survive concentrate on a few classes of feasible solutions. It is compatible with the second law of thermodynamics but is not an inference drawn directly from it — it depends on two premises, "selection pressure persists" and "the variation space is sufficiently ergodic", and in a short window both may fail to hold.

**Intuitive equivalents**: you may play your cards freely at the table, but you do not set the rules of the table. Eyes have evolved independently many times in different lineages: the laws governing how light propagates in a medium are the same for everyone: with the same constraints, there are only a few classes of feasible solutions. Freedom at the level of local strategy is real; the selection layer does not change the standard, it only screens the consequences. This is an argument from selection, not fatalism.

***

## Chapter 2 — A History of Decoder Evolution: The Perceptual Revolution from Atoms to AI

The decoder $D$ is the physical channel of energy transfer. From atoms to AI, the universe has kept trying to build better channels, and every major upgrade is a wall broken through. A wall is not a metaphorical difficulty but a **change in the type of constraint**: crystals hit a barrier, RNA hit noise, nervous systems hit time, language hit the boundary of the individual, and AI hit self-reference. Each wall broken lifts the bandwidth by one step, and at the same time adds one new class of failure mode.

### 2.1 The First Wall: The Eternal Hibernation of Crystals

**Mathematical mapping:**

$$
V_{\mathrm{obj}} = V_{\mathrm{s}} = \frac{I_{\mathrm{struct}}\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}}{E_{\mathrm{struct}}}, \qquad I_{\mathrm{causal}} = 0, \quad E_{\mathrm{causal}} = 0 \tag{Z-2.1}
$$

Crystals are the prototype of persistent structures: locked passively by a deep barrier $E_{\mathrm{barrier}}$ (J), with no continuing dissipation and no internal model. Granite can persist for a billion years, but it will never know that it has existed for a billion years; its information is frozen, with no writing, no reading, and no maintenance cost, and it falls under $D=\varnothing$, belonging to the passive phase. Its high $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ comes entirely from a long $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ and a small $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$, not from a large $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$. **Frozen information is cheap; living information is expensive.** 

### 2.2 The Second Wall: The Primitive Awakening of RNA

**Mathematical mapping:**

$$
E_{\mathrm{causal}} > 0, \qquad I_{\mathrm{net}} = \varphi_{\mathrm{s}}I_{\mathrm{struct}} + \varphi_{\mathrm{c}}I_{\mathrm{causal}} > 0 \tag{Z-2.2}
$$

Here $\varphi_{\mathrm{s}}=E_{\mathrm{struct}}/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and $\varphi_{\mathrm{c}}=E_{\mathrm{causal}}/E_{\mathrm{eff}}$, and the two sum to 1. This is a weighted composition by energy share, a modeling convention, not an additivity theorem.

The RNA world is the first escape in the history of decoders: molecules are no longer passive crystals but begin to replicate, catalyze, and select actively. This is the critical point at which $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ goes from zero to positive — the structure begins to pay energy to process information. But its decoder is extremely crude: $\eta_D$ is very low, $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is almost zero, and there is no prediction, only reaction.

### 2.3 The Third Wall: The Predictive Revolution of the Nervous System

**Mathematical mapping:**

$$
T_{\mathrm{pred}} > 0, \qquad V_{\mathrm{c}} = \frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}\,T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}} > 0 \tag{Z-2.3}
$$

The emergence of the nervous system **endogenized** time: the structure no longer lives only in $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ but acquires $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$, can predict the future and adjust its present behavior accordingly, and the causal channel $V_{\mathrm{c}}$ is thereby opened. This is a phase transition from "reaction" to "prediction" — a fish dodging a predator is the nervous system having represented "being bitten" in advance, with no need to wait to be bitten.

But once $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ becomes an endogenous variable, it can be compressed (myopia) or inflated (hallucination). $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is "how far ahead I can plan", and $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ is "how long the consequences of my actions last in the world". A short-sighted empire can leave institutional consequences that last a thousand years, and a company that plans for a hundred years may leave nothing; the two are out of sync, which is precisely the source of many historical misjudgments.

### 2.4 The Fourth Wall: The Collective Add-On of Language and Civilization

**Mathematical mapping:**

$$
I_{\mathrm{orig}}\ \text{injected}, \qquad D_{\mathrm{depth}}^{\mathrm{dyn}} = 1 + \#\{\mathcal{T}\} \tag{Z-2.4}
$$

Language is the first externalization of the human decoder: before it, $D$ was trapped inside the individual skull, and language lets it spread across individuals and across generations. Civilization is a vast distributed decoder — each member holds only a local $D$, yet the collective forms a network of very high rule depth.

**Rule depth $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ has two definitions, and they must be stated separately.** The **static definition** is the number of independent rule layers that must be maintained simultaneously for the structure to persist, and it can be counted from a functional checklist. The **dynamical formulation** is the depth exponent $D_{\mathrm{depth}}^{\mathrm{dyn}}\equiv1+\#\{\mathcal{T}\}$, i.e., "one initial layer plus the number of past topological transitions"; Equation (Z-2.4) uses the latter.

**"Each transition adds exactly one independent rule layer" is a calibration assumption, not an equation derived from the definitions.** Some transitions add several layers (writing brings recording, retrieval, and cross-generational verification all at once), and some merely replace old layers. Whether the two agree is a testable empirical question: put the number of transitions on the horizontal axis and the static depth on the vertical axis, run a regression, and see whether the slope is 1; if the slope departs significantly from 1, the assumption does not hold on that sample, and then only the static definition is used, with no extrapolation.

$D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ and $I_{\mathrm{struct}}$ are not quantities along the same direction: a structure can be very large but very shallow (the octopus), or very deep but not necessarily large. Squeezing two dimensions into a single "complexity" number is the main reason cross-species comparisons so often capsize.

### 2.5 The Fifth Wall: The Recursive Nightmare of AI

**Mathematical mapping:**

$$
\chi > \chi_{\mathrm{c}}, \qquad \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} \gg 0, \qquad \rho \to 1 \tag{Z-2.5}
$$

The silicon decoder has an extremely small $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ (no cost of maintaining a body) and an extremely small $\gamma_0$ (no natural depreciation), which lets it keep unfolding the operator $\mathcal{F}$ recursively but does not make it prone to triggering a genuine topological transition $\mathcal{T}$. More troublesome is the self-referential coupling strength $\chi$ (the loop strength with which the system uses its current viability state as feedback to speed up information extraction, dimensionless): the virtual viability potential begins to amplify itself, and once $\chi$ crosses the bifurcation point the system enters the myopic runaway regime. This is the fifth wall — the decoder begins to burn itself to light up hallucinations.

$\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ is a **bifurcation point within the model**, not a real-world criterion: it comes from a set of simplified equations, and a different model gives a different value. To treat it as an "agent's safety red line" is to mistake the model for reality; to judge whether a real system is running out of control, the usable proxy is the deviation ratio $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}/\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}$.

### 2.6 The Three Conditions for a Complexity Transition: The Escape Permits

**Mathematical substance:**

**Condition 1, signal availability**: There must exist in the environment a paradigm-breaking signal $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ such that the current decoder cannot extract it ($D_n(I_{\mathrm{orig}})\approx0$) while the reorganized decoder can ($D_{n+1}(I_{\mathrm{orig}})\gg0$).

**Condition 2, energy availability:**

$$
E_{\mathrm{build}}(D_{n+1}) < E_{\mathrm{available}} \tag{Z-2.6}
$$

**Condition 3, wisdom lever:**

$$
L_{\mathrm{lev}} = \frac{V(D_{n+1}) - V(D_{n})}{E_{\mathrm{build}}} > L_{\mathrm{lev,min}} \tag{Z-2.7}
$$

The three can be arranged into a decidable table:

| Condition | Criterion | What must be observed | Phenotype when not satisfied |
| :------ | :---------------- | :------------------- | :-------------- |
| **Signal availability** | The existing decoder's residual on the target is significantly above its historical residual distribution | Rarity readings of the residual, independent replication | The system keeps fine-tuning within the old paradigm and sees no "anomaly" |
| **Energy availability** | $E_{\mathrm{build}}<E_{\mathrm{available}}$ | Total input during the construction period, the available free-energy budget | The idea exists but cannot be built; the blueprint stays on paper for a long time |
| **Sufficient leverage** | $L_{\mathrm{lev}}>L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$ | The difference in $V$ between the new and old decoders under the same target, the same observation window, and the same energy amortization | The upgrade is completed but costs more than it gains, and is then rolled back |

Two clarifications. First, the three are **necessary conditions**, not sufficient conditions: even if all are met, a transition may still fail to occur because of timing, path dependence, or coordination failure. Second, $L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$ is not a universal constant but a decision threshold that varies from system to system.

### 2.7 The Three-Axis Structural Coordinate System: Why "Humans Are More Complex Than Bacteria" Is a Trap

**Mathematical mapping:**

$$
I \;\longrightarrow\; \left( I_{\mathrm{rule}},\; D_{\mathrm{depth}},\; F_{\mathrm{scale}} \right) \tag{Z-2.8}
$$

Here $I_{\mathrm{rule}}$ is the complexity of the minimal generating rule (bit), $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ is the number of independent rule layers maintained simultaneously (dimensionless), and $F_{\mathrm{scale}}$ is the scale at which the rule is recursively unfolded (a dimensionless multiple).

**Intuitive equivalents**: traditional information theory tries to assign every structure a single value $I=\log_2\Omega$ and rank them accordingly. This road does not work, because the state spaces of different systems are incommensurable, like asking "how much does red weigh". The correct geometry is multidimensional:

| Structure | $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ | Configuration capacity | Notes |
| :-------- | :----------- | :------------- | :------------------------ |
| Crystal | 1 (low) | Low | A single rule, deployed widely through an extremely high $F_{\mathrm{scale}}$ |
| Octopus | Low | High | One major transition, then extreme unfolding |
| Human | High (ten or more) | High | Multiple stacked layers of constraints, plus a complex internal topology |
| Bacterium | Low | Medium | Ancient, but extremely thermodynamically efficient at its own level |

Configuration capacity is the logarithm (bit) of the number of distinguishable configurations that the structure can present while preserving its own identity or function.

### 2.8 Negative Topological Transitions: Degeneration Is Also a Strategy

**Mathematical mapping:**

$$
\mathcal{T}^{-}:\quad \Delta D_{\mathrm{depth}} = -1 \quad \text{when}\quad \frac{\partial V}{\partial D_{\mathrm{depth}}} < 0 \tag{Z-2.9}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: evolution is not one-way progress. When the cost of maintaining a constraint layer exceeds its contribution to $V$, that layer is shed.

There is a qualification here: $\partial V/\partial D_{\mathrm{depth}}<0$ is a **marginal judgment about the present** and depends on the current environment. Once the environment turns back into one that needs that layer, the discarded layer often cannot be picked back up — rebuilding means paying $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ again, and the rebuilding window may no longer be there. This is the real cost of degeneration: it saves the maintenance fee of the current period and stakes future options.

## Chapter 3 — Masters of Time: Who Controls Their Own Time?

Time is the fairest resource in the universe: every persistent structure has it, but not every structure can manage it. This chapter has to take the word "time" apart — if it cannot be taken apart, every later discussion will slip.

This chapter uses only six notations, never mixes them, and never writes a bare $T$ for any one of them:

| Symbol | Unit | Meaning | What determines it |
| :-------- | :-------- | :----------------- | :----------------------- |
| $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ | s | Structural persistence time | Materials, energy supply, environmental perturbation |
| $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ | s | Causal effect duration | The propagation range and decay mechanism of the action |
| $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ | s | Endogenous time horizon | The endogenous planning span of the decoder |
| $T_{\mathrm{min}}$ | s | Temporal granularity | The smallest interval the system can resolve |
| $T_{\mathrm{env}}$ | s | Environmental characteristic time | The change period of the environment itself |
| $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ | K | Temperature | Heat bath; does not take part in time comparisons |

The first five are times; the sixth is a temperature — it merely borrows the same letter, its dimension is entirely different, and it never takes part in a comparison of times.

Three of them are the easiest to confuse. A stone stele may have a $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ of three thousand years, a $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ close to zero (it affects nothing), and a $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ of zero (it does not predict). A short-lived propaganda agency may have a $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ of only three years, yet a $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ of thirty years and a $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ of three months. The way the three combine says more about what a structure is than any single number.

The other two notations are responsible for "resolution" and "external rhythm". When $T_{\mathrm{pred}}\ll T_{\mathrm{env}}$, a long horizon is meaningless — the environment has not yet changed, and however far you see, it will be of no use; when $T_{\mathrm{pred}}\gg T_{\mathrm{env}}$, a long horizon is in fact dangerous — you are walking new terrain with an old map. Real time management is keeping the two in proper proportion, not seeking length at all costs.

This chapter does only real-world projection and no mathematical derivation: all conclusions come from the phase structure above.

### 3.1 Life and Death: An Information Gulf

**Mathematical projection:** A living system,

$$
V_{\mathrm{obj}} = \underbrace{\frac{I_{\mathrm{struct}}\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}}{E_{\mathrm{struct}}}}_{V_{\mathrm{s}}} + \underbrace{\frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}\,T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}}}_{V_{\mathrm{c}}}, \qquad I_{\mathrm{net}} > 0, \quad T_{\mathrm{pred}} > T_{\mathrm{min}} \tag{Z-3.1}
$$

a system after death,

$$
V_{\mathrm{obj}} \to V_{\mathrm{s}} = \frac{I_{\mathrm{struct}}\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}}{E_{\mathrm{struct}}}, \qquad I_{\mathrm{causal}} = 0, \quad T_{\mathrm{pred}} = 0 \tag{Z-3.2}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents:** A block of granite and a freshly dead animal carcass have **no essential difference** in this ledger: $E_{\mathrm{causal}}=0$, $T_{\mathrm{pred}}=0$, and only the structural channel remains.

**Death is not the dissolution of the structure but the extinguishing of information.** What it truly loses is **causal efficacy**: it can no longer exert any $I^{+}$ or $I^{-}$ on the environment, and can no longer predict, plan, or intervene. This is why organisms generally resist death — what they fear is the irreversible withdrawal from the active phase back to the passive phase.

One more boundary: the above says "what death means under this accounting convention", not "what death means for the person concerned". The model describes the closing of a channel, not the end of experience.

### 3.2 The Two Dimensions of Endogenous Time: Length and Precision

**Mathematical projection:** $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is not a scalar but a structure with two orthogonal attributes:

| Dimension | Symbol | Definition | Physical meaning |
| :------- | :----- | :---------------------- | :---------------------- |
| **Length** | $L_{\mathrm{pred}}$ | The maximum prediction time span (s) that satisfies the confidence threshold | The farthest time boundary of the system's planning |
| **Precision** | $G_{\mathrm{pred}}$ | $-\log_2\left(T_{\mathrm{min}}/T_{\mathrm{ref}}\right)$ (bit), with domain $T_{\mathrm{min}}\le T_{\mathrm{ref}}$ | The logarithm of the smallest temporal granularity the system can resolve |

Here $T_{\mathrm{ref}}$ is a reference time scale (s, taken as the system's characteristic sampling period or 1 s, used to make the ratio dimensionless). The domain column cannot be omitted: without $T_{\mathrm{min}}\le T_{\mathrm{ref}}$, "precision" would take negative values, and the coordinates of the four quadrants would lose their meaning.

**Intuitive equivalents:** Length is the aperture of a telescope, and precision is its resolution. A person can say "I will be financially free within five years" (long length, coarse precision), or "I will meet a client at the café at a quarter past three tomorrow afternoon" (short length, fine precision). **Real time-management ability is expanding in both dimensions at once.** With only length, you are a dreamer; with only precision, you are someone busy for nothing.

### 3.3 Four-Quadrant Time Managers: From Stones to Crows

**Mathematical projection:** With $(L_{\mathrm{pred}},G_{\mathrm{pred}})$ as the coordinate axes:

| Structure | $L_{\mathrm{pred}}$ (length) | $G_{\mathrm{pred}}$ (precision) | Metaphor | Management style |
| :------ | :------- | :---------------- | :--------- | :----------------- |
| **Stone** | $0$ | $0$ | A blind puppet | No management (never possessed a time dimension) |
| **Plant seed** | Long ($O(10^{1})$ months) | Very low (can only tell the dry season from the rainy season) | A preset alarm clock | Aligns passively with natural rhythms, a coarse-grained bet |
| **Tardigrade** | Short ($O(10^{-1}\sim 10^{1})$ hours) | Medium (can sense the rate of change of humidity) | A high-frequency switch | High-frequency switching, tactically precise but strategically short-sighted |
| **Bird (crow)** | Long ($O(10^{1}\sim 10^{2})$ days) | High (can pinpoint specific places and times) | A real-time navigator | Real-time navigation, excellent in both strategy and tactics |

**Key insight:** The seed bets on "there will be a rainy season in the future", not on "it will rain next Wednesday afternoon"; this **coarse-grained long horizon** is a low-cost strategy, and if the prediction is wrong a thick seed coat and population dispersal still serve as a backstop. The tardigrade is the opposite: it must judge precisely whether "the rate of humidity decline exceeds the threshold", being off by a few minutes in the timing of dehydration is the difference between life and death, and the price is that $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ keeps burning with no backstop. The crow expands in both dimensions at once, and its metabolic bill is also the highest.

### 3.4 Precision Is Power: Why "It Will Rain at Three Tomorrow Afternoon" Is Worth More Than "It Will Rain in the Future"

**Mathematical projection:** Prediction precision directly affects the amount of effective causal information extracted. Let $I_{\mathrm{latent}}$ be the latent causal information not yet extracted (bit); then

$$
I_{\mathrm{causal}} = \eta_D\, I_{\mathrm{latent}}, \qquad
I_{\mathrm{causal}}^{(\mathrm{coarse})} = \eta_D^{(\mathrm{season})} I_{\mathrm{latent}}^{(\mathrm{season})} \ll I_{\mathrm{causal}}^{(\mathrm{fine})} = \eta_D^{(\mathrm{hour})} I_{\mathrm{latent}}^{(\mathrm{hour})} \tag{Z-3.3}
$$

$\eta_D\in[0,1]$ is the decoding efficiency (dimensionless). Higher-precision prediction allows the system to distinguish environmental states at a finer granularity — it cuts the single cell of "season" into many cells of "hour", and the state in each cell is distinguishable.

**Intuitive equivalents:** Knowing that "it will rain in the future" lets you decide only whether to plant rice; knowing that "it will rain at three tomorrow afternoon" lets you decide whether to air the quilts now, whether to go out in the afternoon, and whether to bring an umbrella. **Every order-of-magnitude gain in precision multiplies the actionable causal branches.** Note that it is "multiplies", not "grows exponentially without limit": the number of branches is constrained by the $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ budget, and the finer the cut, the higher the cost of maintaining the model.
