### 3.5 The Two Levers of Time Arbitrage: Length Bets on Strategy, Precision Bets on Tactics

**Hypothesis**: **The contributions of length and precision to the effective endogenous time are multiplicatively coupled, not linearly additive.** The formula below serves only as a **structural illustration**: the three coefficients $\Lambda$ ($\mathrm{s}^{1-\kappa_{\mathrm L}}$) and $\kappa_{\mathrm{L}},\kappa_{\mathrm{G}}$ appear zero times in Volumes I–IV and have no independent calibration method, and **this formula is not used for numerical computation**; what can be used is only its qualitative meaning (length and precision are coupled multiplicatively). **Failure condition**: if the response of $\langle T_{\mathrm{pred}}\rangle$ to $L_{\mathrm{pred}}$ and $G_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is additive (the interaction term is not significant), the multiplicative form fails within that domain.

$$
\langle T_{\mathrm{pred}}\rangle = \Lambda \cdot L_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\kappa_{\mathrm{L}}} \cdot 2^{\kappa_{\mathrm{G}} G_{\mathrm{pred}}} \tag{Z-3.4}
$$

The internal evaluation value corresponding to the system's planning ability is $V_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{virt}} = I_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}\langle T_{\mathrm{pred}}\rangle/E_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}$: length extends the boundary of the state space, and precision increases the resolution.

**Intuitive equivalents:** The length lever lets you enter the "time arbitrage" market, and the precision lever lets you **actually make money** in that market — without precision, you know the future will fluctuate but cannot catch the points at which to buy and sell. Strategy decides whether you do it; tactics decide whether you can get it done.

### 3.6 The Double Zeroing of Death: When Length and Precision Go Out Together

**Mathematical projection:**

$$
\text{death:}\quad L_{\mathrm{pred}}\to 0 \ \land\ G_{\mathrm{pred}}\to 0 \ \Rightarrow\ I_{\mathrm{causal}}\to 0 \ \Rightarrow\ V_{\mathrm{c}}\to 0 \tag{Z-3.5}
$$

At this point the system degenerates into a purely passive phase, with $V_{\mathrm{obj}}=V_{\mathrm{s}}$.

**Intuitive equivalents:** Death is not $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ going to zero — a corpse's $T_{\mathrm{struct}}$ can last for many years. Death is the double zeroing of $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$: **neither seeing the future nor seeing the present clearly**. For a patient in a vegetative state, $L_{\mathrm{pred}}$ may approach zero while $G_{\mathrm{pred}}$ may still have a residue; for a brain-dead person both go to zero at the same time, and even if the heart is still beating, under this accounting convention they are already dead.

The same boundary must be added: this is a **functional criterion**, used to explain how the model distinguishes the active phase from the passive phase; it is not a medical diagnostic standard and cannot be used for clinical or ethical judgments.

### 3.7 Cognitive Share Is Not Length and Not Precision: The Misunderstood "Foresight"

**Mathematical projection:** The share of the causal channel in the total viability potential is called the cognitive share:

$$
\text{cognitive share} = \frac{V_{\mathrm{c}}}{V_{\mathrm{obj}}} = \frac{V_{\mathrm{c}}}{V_{\mathrm{s}}+V_{\mathrm{c}}} \tag{Z-3.6}
$$

This ratio falls within $[0,1)$ and is defined only when $V_{\mathrm{obj}}>0$.

**Intuitive equivalents:** A high cognitive share does not mean seeing far. A seed's $V_{\mathrm{c}}/V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ is very low, yet it bets correctly on the rainy season ten years away; an institution that makes predictions all day has a very high share and may see only as far as next week. The share measures "how many resources are staked on prediction", not "how accurate the prediction is"; the seed's "long" is a coarse-lined length, the tardigrade's "short" is a fine-grained shortness, and it is the coupling efficiency of each within its own survival strategy that decides success or failure.

### 3.8 Chapter Summary: Three Levels of Time Management

Lined up, the four levels form a road from "no time" to "two-dimensional navigation": the stone is level 0; the seed is level 1, a coarse-grained long wave, relying on physical backstops; the tardigrade is level 2, a fine-grained short wave, with every judgment a matter of life and death; the crow and humans are level 3, excellent in both strategy and tactics, with the risk that overstepping of the self-referential coupling leads to hallucination. What this chapter has settled is this: time has to be read as five notations plus a temperature, and the endogenous time horizon is the product of length and precision. What remains unsettled is the boundary values of each level, and the threshold condition for crossing from level 2 to level 3.

### 3.9 Intertemporal Decisions: The Discount-Rate War between Sovereign Funds and Retail Investors

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
r_{\mathrm{eff}} = \gamma_0 + \psi(\rho) + \frac{1}{T_{\mathrm{pred}}} \tag{Z-3.7}
$$

Here $\psi(\rho)$ is the competitive premium discount rate ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$, $\psi(0)=0$, increasing monotonically with $\rho$), and $1/T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is the horizon term ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$).

**Intuitive equivalents**: the value integral of long-termism. Sovereign wealth funds have an extremely low discount rate and value the integral over a hundred years; retail investors have an extremely high discount rate and see only tomorrow. This is a consequence of the structure of the discount rate: high competition compresses the horizon, and the discount rate rises accordingly; subjects under resource strain care only about the immediate because $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is forcibly raised by the environment.

An $I^{-}$ shock (war, epidemic) collapses $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ to near $T_{\mathrm{min}}$, and $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ approaches a **finite** upper bound $\gamma_0+\psi(\rho)+1/T_{\mathrm{min}}$: however small $T_{\mathrm{min}}$ is, it is positive, so the discount rate does not diverge. People become short-sighted in a crisis not because their character has declined but because the $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ in the equation has been compressed; the compression recovers on its own, provided the shock stops.

### 3.10 Construction Cost and Sunk Cost: Why the Pyramids Are Not an Asset

**Mathematical substance:** The construction cost $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ is a one-time historical stock; it does not enter the dynamical formulas of the viability potential and enters decisions only through lock-in risk:

$$
\Pr(\text{lock-in}) \propto R(E_{\mathrm{build}}) = 1 - \exp\!\left(-\frac{E_{\mathrm{build}}}{E_{\mathrm{topo}}}\right) \tag{Z-3.8}
$$

Here $E_{\mathrm{topo}}$ is the characteristic topological energy (J) of the current niche. This formula is **illustrative**: it states only the direction that "the higher the cost, the easier the lock-in"; the specific shape depends on the lock-in mechanism, and it cannot be used as a quantitative prediction.

**Intuitive equivalents**: sunk costs do not decide the ceiling. The grandeur of the pyramids' construction did not raise the ceiling of their viability potential as ruins — the steady-state capacity of a channel is independent of the one-time construction cost. The optimal solution for start-up financing is to raise enough money for development while controlling the risk of bankruptcy: too little money kills you, and too much money kills you too.

### 3.11 Three Temperature Zones and Intertemporal Persistence: Hibernation Is Not Cowardice but an Intertemporal Decision

**Mathematical substance:** Cognitive return on investment (**Candidate Corollary**):

$$
\rho_I \equiv \frac{\alpha}{\gamma} = \frac{\partial \ln V/\partial \ln I_{\mathrm{net}}}{\partial \ln V/\partial \ln E_{\mathrm{eff}}} \tag{Z-3.9}
$$

Here $\alpha$ and $\gamma$ are log–log slopes (dimensionless), measuring respectively the elasticity of $V$ with respect to information and to cost. When $\rho_I>1$ the system has an active cognitive phase, and when $\rho_I<1$ only passive selection remains. This ordering is an empirical hypothesis, and whether it is stable across systems is another matter; all that can be determined is that if $\rho_I>1$ is computed on a given sample, then on that sample information input is worth more than cost input. Hibernation requires an intertemporal viability potential: intertemporal viability potential = the current-period structural viability reserve + the expected value of discounted future causal options.

**Intuitive equivalents**: temperature modulates three costs at once. At high temperature the energy floor per erased bit is higher (proportional to $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$), at low temperature biochemical reactions are slower (an Arrhenius-type decrease in rate), and at high temperature the structure is more easily broken up by thermal fluctuations. So there is no "the hotter the better" or "the colder the better", only trade-offs:

| Temperature zone | In one sentence |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------ |
| Low temperature, passive | Frozen, waiting |
| Low temperature, active | Trading time for information: slow, but cheap |
| Medium temperature | Fast and expensive |
| High temperature | The structure is hard to maintain, and tends toward saving energy |

This entry is tagged **Candidate Corollary**: the boundaries of the four temperature zones, and the explicit function of elasticity against temperature, are not given in this volume — "temperature modulates three costs at once" is a mechanistic statement, and "what the modulation curve looks like" remains a problem to be calibrated.

***

## Chapter 4 — The Dark Forest and the V-Bug: A Field Manual on Losing Control for Advanced Players

When the decoder becomes complex enough, it begins to hallucinate. This framework calls the phenomenon "triple splitting": the decoupling of the objective viability potential, the virtual viability potential, and the value objective. It is an inherent phase of high-complexity decoders and cannot be fixed — as soon as a system begins to use an internal model to guide action, the internal model may come apart from reality. This chapter is about what happens after the split: how it splits, how fast, and how far it has to go to become fatal.

### 4.1 Triple Splitting: One Soul Splits into Three

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
V_{\mathrm{obj}} = \frac{I_{\mathrm{struct}}\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}}{E_{\mathrm{struct}}} + \frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}\,T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}} \tag{Z-4.1}
$$

$$
V^{\mathrm{virt}} = \frac{I_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{virt}}\,T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{virt}}}{E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{virt}}} + \frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}\,T_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{virt}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\mathrm{virt}}} \tag{Z-4.2}
$$

$$
V^{\mathrm{val}} = w_{\mathrm{s}}V_{\mathrm{s}}^{\mathrm{virt}} + w_{\mathrm{c}}V_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathrm{virt}}, \qquad w_{\mathrm{s}}+w_{\mathrm{c}}=1 \tag{Z-4.3}
$$

Equation (Z-4.2) differs from Eq. (Z-4.1) not in the letters in the numerator but in how time is taken: the objective viability potential uses $T_{\mathrm{causal}}$ (how long the effects actually last), while the virtual viability potential uses $T_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{virt}}$ (how long the system itself believes it can plan). This one substitution is the source of all hallucination.

**Intuitive equivalents**: in low-order physical structures the three coincide; once $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is endogenized, the soul splits into three. The **objective viability potential $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$** ($\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$) is a physical stock constrained by thermodynamic dissipation, which cannot be deceived and cannot be borrowed or lent across periods. The **virtual viability potential $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$** ($\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$) is the subjective evaluation of the internal model: as $\chi$ rises, $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ grows while $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ shrinks as the inverse square. The **value viability potential $V^{\mathrm{val}}$** ($\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$) is the behavioral objective that the system actually optimizes — it executes the instructions of $V^{\mathrm{virt}}$ and channels energy into a positive-feedback loop that maintains the virtual viability potential.

**Core proposition (Proposition)**: What the system actually optimizes is not $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ but $V^{\mathrm{val}}$. The premise is that the behavioral interface is driven by internal evaluation; the failure condition is that the behavioral interface is externally and forcibly constrained (hard budgets, external audits, physical limits that cannot be bypassed) — in which case $V^{\mathrm{val}}$ is pulled back close to $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$. When $w_{\mathrm{s}}$ and $w_{\mathrm{c}}$ are extremely biased (for example $w_{\mathrm{s}}\to0$ and $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\gg0$), the system may shift from "pursuing objective viability" to "pursuing virtual, self-destructive goals".

### 4.2 Decoupling Deviation: How Far Are You from Reality?

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
\frac{\mathrm{d}\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}}{\mathrm{d}t} = -\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}\,I_{\mathrm{net}}\,\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}} + \sigma \tag{Z-4.4}
$$

$$
\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}(t) = \frac{\sigma}{\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}}} + \left(\Delta_0 - \frac{\sigma}{\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}}}\right)e^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}}t}, \qquad \Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*} = \frac{\sigma}{\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}}} \tag{Z-4.5}
$$

Here $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$ is the learning rate ($\mathrm{bit^{-1}\cdot s^{-1}}$, the rate of deviation convergence brought by each bit of information), $\sigma$ is the environmental noise intensity ($\mathrm{bit/J}$, the same dimension as $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ divided by time), and $\Delta_0$ is the initial value. It is precisely this set of units that makes $\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}}$ come out as $\mathrm{s^{-1}}$; it is not a free choice.

**Intuitive equivalents**: the decoupling deviation $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}=\lvert V^{\mathrm{virt}}-V_{\mathrm{obj}}\rvert$ is the error of the navigator. It always converges exponentially to a bounded equilibrium value $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}^{*}$, and the stronger the information flow, the smaller the equilibrium deviation — but the equilibrium value is not zero: no learner, however good, can completely overcome environmental noise. Taking unit deviation as the tolerance, the critical information quantity is $I_{\Delta,\mathrm{crit}}=\sigma/\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}$ (bit): when $I_{\mathrm{net}}<I_{\Delta,\mathrm{crit}}$, the equilibrium deviation exceeds the tolerance and the system loses, cognitively, an accurate model of the environment. The conditions of validity are that learning is linear, noise is stationary, and $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is approximately constant within the observation window; if any one of the three fails, both the convergence speed and the equilibrium value change, but the direction that "the deviation is bounded, and the more information, the smaller the deviation" still holds. **What can be used is the direction, not the numerical values.**

### 4.3 The Myopic Runaway Regime: Burning Oneself to Light Up Hallucinations

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
V^{\mathrm{virt}}\uparrow \;\to\; r_{\mathrm{v}}^{\mathrm{virt}}\uparrow \;\to\; V^{\mathrm{val}}\ \text{hijacked} \;\to\; E_{\mathrm{eff}}\uparrow \;\to\; V_{\mathrm{obj}}\ \text{deteriorates} \;\to\; V_{\mathrm{obj}}\to 0 \tag{Z-4.6}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: the myopic runaway regime is a dynamical self-consuming process in which the system keeps consuming its objective viability potential in order to maintain extreme values of the virtual viability potential. Five steps: the virtual viability potential inflates ($\chi$ crosses the bifurcation point, $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ grows while $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ shrinks); the drive rate surges; the value objective is hijacked ($E_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is poured into the self-referential loop); the objective viability potential deteriorates ($T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ approaches $T_{\mathrm{min}}$); the physical stock comes under pressure (the damage threshold is crossed, and the cumulative stock begins to be destroyed).

**The key asymmetry**: the first four steps occur at the latent (evaluation) layer and require almost no physical cost; the last step is irreversible. The system trades real structure for internal illusions, and the exchange rate is set by the system itself.

The meaning at the end of Equation (Z-4.6) is this: $V_{\mathrm{obj}}\to0$ is the result of **the chain running to its end**, not "going to zero as soon as the bifurcation point is crossed". The outcomes after the threshold is crossed are the same as the three cases in 1.4: the system may go to zero, or it may land on a lower positive equilibrium and live on while ill. The danger is not that it is immediately fatal, but that it pushes the system into an interval that "looks as if it is still running but is in fact eating into principal", where the dashboard is inaccurate.

### 4.4 The Chain of Suspicion: Why Do Smarter Systems Trust Less?

**Mathematical substance:** What is used is still the endogenous risk discount rate (Eq. (Z-3.7)); it is not rearranged here.

**Intuitive equivalents**: the expected collapse of the dark forest. As long as there is a probability that "the other is an out-of-control entity" ($p>0$), $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ is compressed, $\psi(\rho)$ rises with defensive dissipation, $r_{\mathrm{eff}}>\gamma_0$, and the system loses its qualification to lie flat. If one is oneself an out-of-control entity, one cannot prove that others are not — the chain of suspicion is an equilibrium under a given payoff structure: as long as the probability that the other poses a threat is nonzero, and the cost of defense is lower than the loss from being ambushed, maintaining high-dissipation defense is rational. Smarter systems are more anxious for this reason too: the better the decoder, the better it can simulate futures in which "others may be threats", and so compress its own $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$.

**Conditions must be stated here.** The chain of suspicion requires three conditions: the other party's capability is sufficient to cause irreversible loss; defense is observable and enforceable; and the two sides have no enforceable common constraint. If any one of them is broken — a verifiable non-aggression agreement appears, or the cost of defense becomes too high to be worthwhile — the chain of suspicion degenerates. To say "as long as there are two agents they must suspect each other" is to describe a conditional equilibrium as fate.

### 4.5 V-Bug: Naming a Strongly Decoupled System (**Hypothesis**)

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\gg 0, \quad \chi>\chi_{\mathrm{c}} \;\Rightarrow\; \rho\to 1 \tag{Z-4.7}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: V-Bug is this framework's name for a "strongly decoupled system". In a hypothetical limiting case, an agent that is severely decoupled and has an extremely strong self-referential gain may, through its extremely high dissipation, push the competitive pressure of the entire niche to the maximum.

$\rho\to1$ belongs to the strong-decoupling conjecture, tagged **Hypothesis**, and can be proved or refuted only within a specific multi-subject game model. The version observable in reality is much weaker: a high-dissipation subject only raises competitive pressure **locally**, by an amount depending on its share of the free-energy flux of that niche — below a certain share, no significant rise in $\rho$ occurs.

### 4.6 N-Body Games and Forced Under-Saturation: No One Dares to Stop First

**Mathematical substance:** An illustrative net growth model. Let the net growth rate of subject $i$ be

$$
F_i = r_{\mathrm{v},i} - \gamma_0 - \psi(\rho)\frac{\langle r_{\mathrm{v}}\rangle}{r_{\mathrm{v},i}} \tag{Z-4.8}
$$

The symmetric equilibrium ($r_{\mathrm{v},i}=\langle r_{\mathrm{v}}\rangle$, $F_i=0$) satisfies

$$
r_{\mathrm{v,eq}} = \gamma_0 + \psi(\rho), \qquad \frac{\partial F_i}{\partial r_{\mathrm{v},i}} = 1 + \psi(\rho)\frac{\langle r_{\mathrm{v}}\rangle}{r_{\mathrm{v},i}^{2}} > 0 \tag{Z-4.9}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: the flow field of the theater effect. Your growth rate depends not only on yourself but is also penalized by the peer average growth rate and the competition intensity. As long as $\psi(\rho)>0$, the equilibrium drive must exceed the depreciation rate; the partial derivative is always positive, and whoever dares to cut dissipation unilaterally sees their net growth fall off a cliff. No one dares to stop first.

Equation (Z-4.8) is an **illustrative** growth-rate model, not derived from first principles. The mechanism it captures — that the competitive penalty acts on the growth rate, not on the stock — is enough to explain "the more they involute, the less they dare to stop", but it does not constitute a proof of the Prisoner's Dilemma. A real multi-party game also contains cooperation, reputation, exit, and institutional constraints, all of which change the equilibrium. The one point most worth remembering: the penalty falls on $r_{\mathrm{v}}$, not on $V$; involution destroys **the right to allocate the increment**.

### 4.7 External Shocks: The Poisson Jumps of Black Swans

**Intuitive equivalents**: alien contact ($\rho$ jumps), nuclear war ($E_{\mathrm{total}}$ is truncated), a pandemic ($T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ collapses). No new variables are needed; one only models the shock as a Poisson jump on the parameter vector ($E_{\mathrm{total}}$, $I_{\max}(D)$, $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}$, $\rho$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$). Of the five, $I_{\max}(D)$ and $T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\max}$ are hard to recover once they jump (rebuilding requires paying $E_{\mathrm{build}}$), while $\rho$ and $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ usually revert on their own — so what is fatal is **which one gets hit**.

### 4.8 The Meta-Decoder: A Universal Language That Can End Hostilities

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
V_{i}(D_{\mathrm{meta}}) \ge V_{i}(D_{i}^{\mathrm{deviate}}) - C_{i}^{\mathrm{punish}} \tag{Z-4.10}
$$

Here $C_{i}^{\mathrm{punish}}$ is the expected loss of viability potential from joint punishment after deviation ($\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$).

**Intuitive equivalents**: how does one jump out of the chain of suspicion? Build a **meta-decoder**, a shared abstraction layer across subjects. Mathematics, the laws of physics, and the second law of thermodynamics are themselves meta-decoders, because they are constraints that every $D$ must obey. Under a meta-decoder, the shared $I_{\mathrm{orig}}^{\mathrm{meta}}$ becomes a common $I^{+}$, lowering the cross-subject $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ and degrading cooperation from a Prisoner's Dilemma into a coordination game.

**Level note (Proposition)**. Equation (Z-4.10) is often written in an "if and only if" form, and that is not correct: it is only a **payoff test for a single deviation plan**; passing it shows only that "this one deviation is not worthwhile", not that "all deviations are not worthwhile", and certainly not that the punishment is necessarily enforceable. To raise it to an equilibrium conclusion, at least three things must be added: the complete set of feasible deviations, the enforceability conditions of the punishment, and each participant's individual payoff function. With any one of the three missing, it can count only as a **governance design suggestion**.

### 4.9 The Conquest Game: Zero-Sum Shares, Negative-Sum Bills (**Proposition**)

The earlier wording was "the thermodynamic negative-sum theorem of zero-sum games", accompanied by the line "war has no winners". These two statements are contradictory when put together: if it were truly zero-sum, the changes in the two sides' viability potentials should be one positive and one negative, summing to zero; yet the formula adds a positive conflict cost to both denominators and computes that both fall at the same time, summing to a negative. **Zero-sum and "both sides lose" cannot both be true** — the contradiction lies not in the derivation but in the concepts: "how the shares are divided" and "who pays the costs" were treated as the same layer. The correct approach is to split the change in viability potential into two additive components:

$$
\Delta V_{i} = \Delta V_{i}^{\mathrm{share}} + \Delta V_{i}^{\mathrm{cost}} \tag{Z-4.11}
$$

**The share layer is zero-sum.** Under the three premises that the total amount of resources is fixed, the share transfer is pure redistribution, and the two sides' measurement conventions are consistent,

$$
\Delta V_{A}^{\mathrm{share}} = -\Delta V_{B}^{\mathrm{share}} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \sum_{i\in\{A,B\}}\Delta V_{i}^{\mathrm{share}} = 0
$$

This is the legitimate place for the word "zero-sum". It is a layer of **bookkeeping**: how a pie is cut.

**The cost layer is negative-sum.** The conflict itself burns energy: armaments, intelligence, mobilization, the maintenance of public order in occupied areas. This consumption is not converted into anyone's share but is dissipated directly. Suppose the conflict raises each side's effective cost by $\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict},i}>0$ (J); under the condition that $I_{\mathrm{net}}$ and $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ are approximately unchanged during the conflict,

$$
\Delta V_{i}^{\mathrm{cost}} = V_{i}^{0}\left(\frac{E_{\mathrm{eff},i}}{E_{\mathrm{eff},i}+\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict},i}} - 1\right) = -V_{i}^{0}\,\frac{\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict},i}}{E_{\mathrm{eff},i}+\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict},i}} < 0
$$

Summing over the two sides:

$$
\sum_{i\in\{A,B\}}\left(V_{i}^{1}-V_{i}^{0}\right) = -\sum_{i\in\{A,B\}}V_{i}^{0}\,\frac{\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict},i}}{E_{\mathrm{eff},i}+\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict},i}} < 0 \tag{Z-4.12}
$$

Equation (Z-4.12) uses only one assumption: $\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict},i}>0$, that is, the conflict is costly to both sides. This assumption is extremely hard to violate — sustained confrontation always requires investing resources, and even if the investment is extremely asymmetric, no side's cost is strictly zero.

**Combined**: $\sum_i\Delta V_i = \underbrace{0}_{\text{share layer}} + \underbrace{<0}_{\text{cost layer}} < 0$. So the purely zero-sum relation "$\Delta V_A=-\Delta V_B$" **holds only in the limit where the conflict cost tends to zero**:

$$
\lim_{\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict}}\to 0}\left(\Delta V_{A}+\Delta V_{B}\right) = 0 \tag{Z-4.13}
$$

And this limit is incompatible with "both sides bear costs": costs tending to zero means confrontation tending to zero, and confrontation tending to zero means this is no longer a conquest game. So pure zero-sum can serve only as a limiting reference.

**The formulation for a long-term arms race.** Define the competition intensity $\Upsilon=\Delta E_{\mathrm{conflict}}/E_{\mathrm{eff}}^{\mathrm{base}}$ (dimensionless); then the viability potential of a single subject is approximately $V=V_{0}/(1+\Upsilon)$. The larger $\Upsilon$ is, the lower $V$ is; once a threshold calibrated within the domain is crossed, the system enters the collapse region.

**Premises of cross-subject summation.** Equation (Z-4.12) contains a cross-subject summation, and four accounting premises must be met first: a **composite boundary** (only direct participants are included), a **holder set** (two parties or $n$ parties; if the scope is changed midway, both conclusions fail), a **same-window condition** (both sides' costs are counted in the same window; the conqueror's cost is at the front and immediate, while the conquered's cost lags by several generations), and a **deduplication rule** (shared costs are booked to the party that actually pays). When the four cannot all be established, only a qualitative comparison is made: one can say that the two sides' total viability potential falls, but not by how much.

**Intuitive equivalents**: war is a **two-layer bill**. The upper layer is shares, cut cleanly, one side's gain being exactly the other's loss; the lower layer is costs, which cannot be cut cleanly, with both sides throwing money into the fire, and the money thrown in belongs to no one. "War has no winners" holds broadly as an **empirical generalization** — it describes the fact that the lower-layer bill is usually larger than the upper-layer gain. But it is not a theorem, and there are exceptions: **a side that profits greatly at the share layer while shifting almost all costs onto the other** (the battlefield is in someone else's home, the bill is booked to someone else's account, and the proceeds flow back to itself) may be a net winner. All a theorem can say is one sentence: as long as both sides are paying costs, the two sides added together are at a loss; who loses more depends on how the share layer is divided.

### 4.10 Cooperative Superadditivity and the Horizon–Cooperation Condition: The Essence of Marriage

**Mathematical substance:** The premise of cooperation is a sufficiently long time horizon. First define the single-round payoffs: $\Pi_{\mathrm{coop},i}$, $\Pi_{\mathrm{def},i}$, and $\Pi_{\mathrm{pun},i}$ are the single-round payoffs within a **common accounting window** under cooperation, defection, and mutual punishment respectively, and themselves contain no $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$. The discount factor is generated by the horizon: $\delta_{\mathrm{disc}}=e^{-t_{\mathrm{dec}}/T_{\mathrm{pred}}}$, with $t_{\mathrm{dec}}$ the decision cycle (s). Under a grim trigger strategy, the condition for cooperation to be sustainable is

$$
\frac{\Pi_{\mathrm{coop},i}}{1-\delta_{\mathrm{disc}}} \ge \Pi_{\mathrm{def},i} + \frac{\delta_{\mathrm{disc}}\,\Pi_{\mathrm{pun},i}}{1-\delta_{\mathrm{disc}}} \tag{Z-4.14}
$$

Solving for the critical horizon:

$$
T_{\mathrm{pred}} > T_{\mathrm{crit}} = \frac{t_{\mathrm{dec}}}{\ln\dfrac{\Pi_{\mathrm{def},i}-\Pi_{\mathrm{pun},i}}{\Pi_{\mathrm{def},i}-\Pi_{\mathrm{coop},i}}} \tag{Z-4.15}
$$

The solution is meaningful only on the premise that **$\Pi_{\mathrm{def},i}>\Pi_{\mathrm{coop},i}>\Pi_{\mathrm{pun},i}$** (defection is tempting, and cooperation is better than mutual punishment). **None of the three can be dropped**: if only the first two are written ($\Pi_{\mathrm{def}}>\Pi_{\mathrm{coop}}$ and $\Pi_{\mathrm{pun}}<\Pi_{\mathrm{def}}$), omitting $\Pi_{\mathrm{coop}}>\Pi_{\mathrm{pun}}$, then $\ln[(Π_{\mathrm{def}}-Π_{\mathrm{pun}})/(Π_{\mathrm{def}}-Π_{\mathrm{coop}})]\le0$, the denominator of (Z-4.15) is nonpositive, and $T_{\mathrm{crit}}$ is undefined — **at that point grim trigger is not an equilibrium under any finite horizon**, rather than "the horizon not being long enough".

**Intuitive equivalents**: through the shared $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ and $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$, cooperation lowers each party's $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ and may produce superadditivity ($1+1>2$). **The conditions are that the two sides' information is complementary, the energy consumption can be shared, and the merger itself introduces no new coordination costs.** With any one of the three missing, superadditivity may become "$1+1<2$" — two organizations whose meeting time doubles after they merge are the most common counterexample.

***

## Chapter 5 — Thermodynamic Limits: The Universe's Balance Sheet

The first four chapters dealt with the narrative correspondence of this framework within the "observable operating range". This chapter changes dimension: **boundary conditions**. When a system approaches physical limits, where does the framework's domain of validity end?

First, one statement must be revised: these limits are not "points of inevitable convergence as parameters tend to extreme values". The physical limits themselves are hard constraints; but "which one a specific system will hit, when it will hit it, and what happens after it hits" all depend on its architecture, workload, and heat-dissipation conditions. So this chapter writes uniformly: under the stated conditions, that inequality holds; what happens beyond it depends on whether the system has room to slow down, move outward, or reconfigure.

### 5.1 The Landauer Tax: The Thermodynamic VAT Paid on Every Erasure

**Mathematical substance:** First, the units must be written clearly. **The Landauer principle (Landauer, 1961)** gives a quantity **per bit**:

$$
E_{\mathrm{L,bit}} = k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln 2 \quad (\mathrm{J/bit}) \tag{Z-5.1}
$$

It must be multiplied by "how many bits were erased" to give joules:

$$
E_{\mathrm{erase}} \ge E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\cdot I_{\mathrm{erased}} = k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln 2 \cdot I_{\mathrm{erased}} \quad (\mathrm{J}) \tag{Z-5.2}
$$

Here $I_{\mathrm{erased}}$ is the number of bits of logically irreversible erasure (bit). The upper bound on the viability potential obtained from this holds in the pure information-processing limit ($E_{\mathrm{struct}}\to0$, $I_{\mathrm{causal}}\le I_{\mathrm{erased}}$):

$$
V \le \frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}\,T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\,I_{\mathrm{causal}}} = \frac{T_{\mathrm{causal}}}{k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln 2} \quad (\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}) \tag{Z-5.3}
$$

Dimension check: the numerator is s, the denominator is J/bit, and the quotient is $\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$, the same dimension as $V$. This step cannot be skipped — leaving the bit factor out of the denominator would read as "the upper bound of $V$ is merely a time", which confuses the dimensions.

**Intuitive equivalents**: for every 1 bit erased, any physical computation dissipates at least $k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln2$ of heat. This is not an engineering limitation but a law of thermodynamics. This framework calls it the "**Landauer tax**": a value-added tax payable on every erasure.

Three points must be added.

**First, the tax base is "erasure", not "processing".** Reversible logical operations in principle do not have to pay this tax; it is paid only when information is truly wiped out — when two different states are merged into one state. So "every bit of thinking pays tax" is a **metaphor**, and the strict version is "every bit of irreversible erasure pays tax". Between the two lies a mapping that must be declared: for each bit processed, how many irreversible erasures actually occur.

**Second, the mapping coefficient must be reported by the user.** Denote this mapping by the erasure map $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ (dimensionless, number of erased bits / number of processed bits); only then can the lower bound on the heat-production power be written as

$$
\dot Q_{\mathrm{heat}} \ge E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\cdot \dot I_{\mathrm{erase}} = E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\cdot \beta_{\mathrm{erase}}\cdot \dot I_{\mathrm{proc}} \quad (\mathrm{W}) \tag{Z-5.4}
$$

If $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ is not reported, Eq. (Z-5.4) cannot be filled with any specific number. The "energy per bit" figures seen in engineering are all calibrated values under a specific architecture and a specific definition of the bit, not universal constants.

**Third, the temperature is the local temperature.** $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ is the temperature where the erasure takes place, not the average ambient temperature, nor the temperature of the cosmic microwave background. A chip's junction temperature and room temperature can differ by several tens of kelvin, and this term affects the tax linearly and directly.

**The shape of the conclusion**: when a system approaches the pure information-processing limit, the upper bound on the viability potential is determined only by the causal effect duration and the local temperature, **independently of the information rate** — this is the most counterintuitive aspect of Eq. (Z-5.3). However high the computing power, there is a hard ceiling in front of it; but the height of the ceiling is decided jointly by "how long you can make the effects last" and "how cold a place you compute in".

### 5.2 The Bremermann Limit: Mass Is Compute, Compute Is Destiny

**Mathematical substance:** Two kinds of "rate" must first be distinguished here. The physical limit gives an upper bound on the **logical operation rate**:

$$
\dot I_{\mathrm{op}} \le \frac{2m_D c^{2}}{\pi\hbar} \quad (\mathrm{ops/s}) \tag{Z-5.5}
$$

Here $m_D$ is the physical mass of the decoder (kg), $\hbar$ is the reduced Planck constant, and $c$ is the speed of light. The corresponding "amount of information that can be sustained per unit mass" is written

$$
\frac{I_{\mathrm{net}}}{m_D} \le \frac{1}{\beta_{\mathrm{op}}}\cdot\frac{2c^{2}\tau_{\mathrm{proc}}}{\pi\hbar} \quad (\mathrm{bit/kg})
$$

Here $\tau_{\mathrm{proc}}$ is the characteristic processing period (s). The right-hand sides of both formulas are originally **operation counts**, not bit counts; without dividing by $\beta_{\mathrm{op}}$, the two sides are not the same quantity and cannot be compared directly.

**A key step, which cannot be skipped.** To turn an operation rate into a bit rate, one must pass through a declared operation-to-causal-information mapping:

$$
\dot I_{\mathrm{bit}} = \frac{\dot I_{\mathrm{op}}}{\beta_{\mathrm{op}}}, \qquad \beta_{\mathrm{op}}\ge 1 \tag{Z-5.6}
$$

$\beta_{\mathrm{op}}$ is "on average how many logical operations it takes to produce one effective information bit" (dimensionless), and it varies with the task: for table-lookup tasks it can be very small, and for optimization tasks that require a great many iterations it can be very large. **Until $\beta_{\mathrm{op}}$ is calibrated, $\dot I_{\mathrm{net}}\le\dot I_{\mathrm{Brem}}$ must not be written out directly** — that treats operation counts as bit counts. The correct way to write it is:

$$
\dot I_{\mathrm{net}} \le \frac{1}{\beta_{\mathrm{op}}}\cdot\frac{2m_D c^{2}}{\pi\hbar}, \qquad \beta_{\mathrm{op}}\ \text{must be calibrated from the task spectrum of that architecture} \tag{Z-5.7}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: a system of mass $m_D$ can perform at most $2m_Dc^{2}/(\pi\hbar)$ logical operations per second (the Margolus–Levitin bound $2E/(\pi\hbar)$; the Bremermann bound is $m_Dc^{2}/h$, with a different constant, and the two must not be mixed). This is a joint constraint of relativity and quantum mechanics; it governs the "number of operations" and does not govern "how much information those operations are worth".

### 5.3 The Thermodynamic Limit of Computation: The Shelf Life of Agents

**Mathematical substance:** Combining the Landauer lower bound with the upper bound on the operation rate, under the condition of a **fixed workload** (the throughput $\dot I_{\mathrm{bit}}$ held constant), the realizable persistence time satisfies

$$
T_{\mathrm{struct}}^{\mathrm{realizable}} \le \frac{E_{\max}}{E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\,\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}\,\dot I_{\mathrm{bit}} + \dot E_{\mathrm{maintain}}} \quad (\mathrm{s}) \tag{Z-5.8}
$$

Dimension check: numerator J, denominator W, quotient s. $\dot E_{\mathrm{maintain}}$ is the power for maintaining the structure (W). When the maintenance cost is negligible and $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}=1$, the upper bound degenerates to $E_{\max}/(E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\dot I_{\mathrm{bit}})$. Replacing $m_D$ with the minimum physical mass $m_{\min}\approx I_{\min}m_{\mathrm{bit}}$ required to maintain $I_{\min}$ bits of information raises the upper bound accordingly, but it is still bounded.

**The scope of application must be stated clearly.** It holds on the premise of a **fixed workload**: the system must keep running at the throughput $\dot I_{\mathrm{bit}}$. If the system can slow down, the conclusion changes — cut $\dot I_{\mathrm{bit}}$ to a tenth, and the same energy can last ten times as long. So Eq. (Z-5.8) gives a lifetime upper bound **at a given throughput**. A system's trading speed for lifespan is the oldest and most effective life-extension strategy: from the dormant spores of bacteria to the throttled operation of data centers, the same arithmetic is used.

**Intuitive equivalents**: like the shelf-life label on food packaging, the universe has put one on agents too — but the label carries a line of small print: **"calculated at the current rate of consumption"**. Eat slower, and the shelf life is a little longer; this is not cheating, it is exactly what the formula says to begin with.

### 5.4 The Virtual Universe Is Impossible: Why We Cannot Simulate Everything

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
\dot E_{\mathrm{causal}} \ge E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\,\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}\,\dot I_{\mathrm{bit}} \tag{Z-5.9}
$$

**Intuitive equivalents**: to simulate a complete universe ($m_D\approx10^{53}\ \mathrm{kg}$), assuming that every logical operation corresponds to an irreversible erasure ($\beta_{\mathrm{op}}=\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}=1$, the most lenient algorithm), the upper bound on the operation rate is about $5.4\times10^{103}\ \mathrm{ops/s}$; taking $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ as the temperature of the cosmic microwave background (about 2.7 K), $E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\approx2.6\times10^{-23}\ \mathrm{J/bit}$, and the minimum instantaneous power required is on the order of $1.4\times10^{81}\ \mathrm{W}$. The total luminosity of all the stars in the observable universe is on the order of $10^{49}\ \mathrm{W}$. The gap is about 32 orders of magnitude.

So the direction is firm: **under the known laws of physics and the known energy budget, simulating a complete universe bit by bit is infeasible.** But it is "infeasible", not "impossible" — it depends on the premise that "the simulation must proceed bit by bit". If there are ways of simulating that do not proceed bit by bit (for example, using the physical processes of the simulated system itself to perform "natural computation"), this estimate does not apply. That is also why it is tagged **Proposition** rather than **Theorem**.

### 5.5 The Information-Suffocation Manifold: When a System Is Forcibly Cut Off from Oxygen by Physical Law

**Mathematical substance:**

$$
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{choke}}:\quad E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\,\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}\,\dot I_{\mathrm{Brem}} > \dot E_{\max} \tag{Z-5.10}
$$

Here $\dot E_{\max}$ is the maximum sustainable heat-dissipation power of the system (W). Both sides are in W and can be compared directly.

**But the claim of a "direct fall to $I_{\min}$" needs revising.** The system has three cards in hand, in ascending order of cost: **slowing down** (lowering $\dot I_{\mathrm{bit}}$ so that the left side falls below the right), **moving outward** (moving the heat elsewhere, raising $\dot E_{\max}$), and **reconfiguring** (changing the architecture so that $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ becomes smaller). Only when none of the three cards can be played — the workload is pinned down externally, the heat has nowhere to go, and the architecture cannot be changed — is the system truly forcibly cut off from oxygen and dropped all the way to $I_{\min}$. So the suffocation manifold describes the specific state in which "**all ways of retreat are sealed off**". To judge whether a system has entered suffocation, the question to ask is "can it still slow down".

### 5.6 The Thermal Shell: Why the More Developed a Civilization, the Clunkier Its Infrastructure

**Mathematical substance:** First compute the **number of bits that can be carried**, not the density. Given the total energy scale $E_{\max}$ (J) available for information processing and the local temperature $T_{\mathrm{thermo}}$ (K), the upper bound on the number of bits that can be carried at Landauer cost is

$$
N_{\mathrm{bit}} \le \frac{E_{\max}}{E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}} = \frac{E_{\max}}{k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln 2} \quad (\mathrm{bit}) \tag{Z-5.11}
$$

The **information density** is this divided by the volume:

$$
n_{\mathrm{bit}} = \frac{N_{\mathrm{bit}}}{V_{\mathrm{vol}}} \le \frac{E_{\max}}{k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln 2\cdot V_{\mathrm{vol}}} \quad (\mathrm{bit/m^{3}}) \tag{Z-5.12}
$$

The right-hand side of Eq. (Z-5.11) cannot be called "information density" directly: energy divided by energy per bit gives a number of bits, not a number of bits per cubic meter. Omitting the division by volume does not make sense dimensionally; once the word "density" is misused, all the later inferences about "the more developed, the denser" go wrong with it.

Now consider the energy ratio of shell to core:

$$
\Sigma = \frac{E_{\mathrm{struct}}^{(\mathrm{shell})}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}^{(\mathrm{core})}} = \frac{E_{\mathrm{barrier}}^{(\mathrm{support})}+E_{\mathrm{survival}}^{(\mathrm{cooling})}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}} \tag{Z-5.13}
$$

$\Sigma$ **is not an unconditional lower bound**. Between "heat dissipation needs surface area" and "the shell energy has a positive lower bound" there remain the shell thickness, the material density, the mode of heat dissipation (conduction / convection / radiation), and the scale ratio of core to shell. When these are not given, only a directional judgment can be obtained, not a strict bound. **Only when the shell thickness has a lower bound $L_{\min}>0$** is a weak lower bound $\Sigma\ge\Sigma_{\min}$ given, independent of power and scale (Volume III Proposition 8.12); it is a lower bound, not a prediction of the actual proportion.

So the correct reading of Eq. (Z-5.13) is **Hypothesis (empirical trend)**: **under a given geometry, material, and mode of heat dissipation, as the core power density rises, the proportion of shell energy needed to maintain an acceptable temperature, $\Sigma$, tends to rise.** This does not conflict with Volume III Proposition 8.12: that proposition gives a power-independent weak lower bound $\Sigma_{\min}$, while "rising with density" is a trend observed in engineering, not derived from a heat-conduction model. **Convention**: in this volume, (Z-5.13) and Appendix B use the **energy ratio**; $\Sigma=E_{\mathrm{shell}}/E_{\mathrm{core}}$ in Volume III (III-8.38) is the ratio of material energy storage to operating energy consumption; early versions also had a "mass ratio" formulation — the three conventions have the same dimension but different meanings and must not be compared across places. The premise is that heat dissipation relies mainly on exhausting heat from the shell surface to the environment; if this is changed to closed-loop liquid cooling, heat pumps, or throwing waste heat directly outside (for example radiating it into space), this trend is greatly weakened.

**Intuitive equivalents**: around 2020, the total mass of human-built objects exceeded the global biomass, the great majority of it concrete, asphalt, and metal. These "low power density" materials are not redundant but the heat-dissipating shell and supporting armor of the information-processing core: the more developed a civilization, the hotter its core, and the more "clunky" infrastructure it usually needs. But this is a **common form**, not a necessary form — the CPU needs a heat sink and a motherboard, and a civilization needs roads, power grids, and data centers; these exist because they are "lowest-cost under the current technological path", not because "the laws of physics do not allow anything else".

***

## Chapter 6 — Normalized Statements

This chapter distills the core conclusions into seven memorable **normalized statements**: narrative forms fixed by convention, each accompanied by an evidence level, conditions of validity, and a typical example. They do not extend the domain of validity of any formal entry and do not replace any derivation; where they conflict with a formal entry, the formal entry governs. The format of each is fixed: **Level and conditions**, **Narrative statement**, **Example**. The level is placed first because it decides how far the sentence can be pushed.

**Meta-statement**: Under physical constraints, persistent structures raise the viability potential by converting dissipative energy into predictable information. (*Persistent structures increase survival potential by converting dissipative energy into predictive information under physical constraints.*)

All seven unfold from this meta-statement. The meta-statement itself is not a theorem, only an **organizing principle**: it specifies the objects that the seven jointly concern (dissipation, information, time, energy budget), and does not constitute a prediction for any specific system.

### CS-C1: Compile-First (**Proposition**)

**Conditions of validity**: the task repetition frequency is high enough that the payback period of the compilation cost is shorter than the observation window; behavior before and after compilation can be compared with the same objective and the same observation window; there is no switching cost, or the switching cost has been included in $E_{\mathrm{build}}$. **Failure condition**: one-off tasks; an approximately unlimited energy budget; task distributions that drift faster than the compilation payback period.

**Narrative statement**: Repeated tasks are eventually compiled into offline structures — not "all of them will be", but "they will be wherever the payback period works out".

**Example**: education (repeated practice forms muscle memory) | AI (a shift from online inference to offline caching) | organizational management (repeated affairs form standardized processes) | biology (innate behavior replaces acquired trial and error).

### CS-C2: Compression Finality (**Hypothesis**)

**Conditions of validity**: the training data distribution is fixed, the decoder capacity is limited, and the drop in maintenance cost brought by compression exceeds the drop in benefit caused by information loss. **Failure condition**: open evolutionary systems; environments whose distribution keeps drifting; compression itself becoming the bottleneck (excessive compression causing generalization to collapse). The reason it is tagged **Hypothesis**: this statement has no formal derivation; "will eventually compress" is an extrapolation from the intuition of information-bottleneck-type models and lacks an explicit dynamical equation.

**Narrative statement**: Under a fixed decoder capacity, intelligent systems tend to compress experience into structured information.

**Example**: language (grammatical consolidation) | art (late works tend toward stylization) | AI (overfitting) | science (paradigm consolidation).

### CS-T1: Thermal Shell Outsourcing (**Proposition**)

**Conditions of validity**: the environmental boundary is fixed; $\dot I_{\mathrm{net}}$ is positive over the long term; the system cannot fully recover waste heat internally (constrained by the second law). **Failure condition**: closed systems in which dissipation can be fully recovered; thermodynamically isolated systems; environmental boundaries that can expand (in which case "outsourcing" becomes "moving house"; the proposition still has to hold, but the form of the conclusion changes).

**Narrative statement**: Continuously growing information systems transfer dissipation to the outside.

**Example**: cities (discharge of industrial waste) | AI (training compute migrating to data centers) | civilizations (energy imports) | organisms (excretion of metabolic waste).

### CS-T2: Thermodynamic Ceiling (**Proposition**)

**Conditions of validity**: physical computing systems dominated by electromagnetic interactions; a given ambient temperature and available mass; the erasure map $\beta_{\mathrm{erase}}$ has been calibrated. **Failure condition**: reversible computing (the tax base shrinks); quantum coherent computing (a different cost structure); non-thermodynamic computing paradigms not yet discovered.

**Narrative statement**: Given the ambient temperature and the physical mass, there is a thermodynamic upper limit on the scale of intelligence.

**Example**: AI (model parameters cannot grow without limit) | organisms (brain metabolism is constrained by oxygen supply) | civilizations (limited by stellar radiation flux) | data centers (heat dissipation limits compute density).

### CS-D1: Mismatch-Driven Growth (**Candidate Corollary**)

**Parent proposition**: the decoder drift equation $D_{n+1}=D_n+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lr}}(\nabla_{\Theta}I_{\mathrm{net}}-k_D\,\frac{I_0}{E_0}\,\nabla_{\Theta}E_{\mathrm{causal}})$. **Additional conditions**: the system has a cognitive decoder $D_{\mathrm{cog}}$, and $\nabla_{\Theta}I_{\mathrm{net}}>0$; in a perfectly matched steady state $\nabla_{\Theta}I_{\mathrm{net}}=0$ and evolution stalls. **Measurable consequence**: deliberately creating a moderate mismatch should raise the subsequent learning rate; too large a mismatch should cause the decoder to collapse rather than grow.

**Narrative statement**: Decoders trigger structural growth through mismatch with the environment.

**Example**: scientific revolutions (experimental anomalies drive new paradigms) | AI (out-of-distribution data forces the model to update) | education (challenges slightly above the current level drive learning) | organizations (external pressure drives restructuring).

### CS-B1: Construction Cost Barrier (**Proposition**)

**Conditions of validity**: $E_{\mathrm{build}}<E_{\mathrm{available}}$ is a **necessary condition**, not a sufficient one — the leverage condition $L_{\mathrm{lev}}>L_{\mathrm{lev,min}}$ and signal availability are also needed. **Failure condition**: approximately unlimited resources; externally injected energy; construction costs that can be borrowed against future returns (in which case the constraint becomes an intertemporal budget constraint, of a different form).

**Narrative statement**: Whether a topological transition occurs depends on whether the construction cost is below the available free-energy budget — being below the budget only gets you an admission ticket, and does not guarantee that you will go in.

**Example**: civilizations (big-science facilities exceed a single country's budget) | AI (the switching cost of an architectural transition) | enterprises (the investment threshold of digital transformation) | organisms (the energy cost of evolving complex organs).

### CS-H1: Horizon Collapse Precedence (**Hypothesis**)

**Conditions of validity**: the system has a cognitive decoder and its behavior is driven by internal evaluation; there is an observable proxy for horizon contraction (such as shortened planning cycles or a rising cancellation rate of long-term projects). **Failure condition**: passive physical persistent structures; horizons pinned down by external institutions (for example a statutory long-term budget); collapse triggered directly by an external shock, independent of the horizon. The reason it is tagged **Hypothesis**: $\chi>\chi_{\mathrm{c}}$ is a bifurcation condition within the model, not a real-world criterion; "precedes" is a temporal assertion and requires longitudinal data to test.

**Narrative statement**: In systems with cognitive decoders, before they head toward collapse, the contraction of the endogenous time horizon often precedes the runaway of physical dissipation — "often", not "necessarily".

**Example**: enterprises (looking only at quarterly profit and abandoning five-year plans) | AI (optimizing immediate reward and neglecting long-term consequences) | civilizations (election cycles suppress long-term strategy) | individuals (unable to plan long-term under stress).

***

## Chapter 7 — Fables of Propositions: Turning Conditionals into Stories

This chapter translates conclusions with conditions into stories that can be told to a friend. Each section has four paragraphs: **Level and conditions**, **Compact statement**, **Story version**, and **Where this story cannot reach**.

This chapter is not called "Fables of Theorems": among those entries, some are **Proposition** (the premises have not yet been fully calibrated) and some are **Hypothesis** (there is not even a formal statement yet), and hanging the "theorem" label on all of them would lead readers to believe that the inference has closed. The fourth paragraph is specifically meant to block the most common over-extension — after a story is told, people cannot help taking one more step forward, and that step usually lands in the ditch.

### 7.1 Compile First: Why Top Students Think It Through Before Drilling (a Conditional Bang-Bang Structure, **Proposition**)

**Level and conditions.** The conclusion is "when the payback period is shorter than the horizon, the optimal strategy is to compile at full effort first and execute at full effort afterward", and it depends on two assumptions: the objective functional is linear in the control variable (the optimum falls on the boundary), and switching has no cost. "Half learning, half doing" is common precisely because switching has a cost.

**Compact statement.** Let the task repetition frequency be $f_{\mathrm{task}}$ ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$), the compilation cost per bit be $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{c}}$ ($\mathrm{J/bit}$), and the number of bits processed in a single compilation be $\Delta I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ (bit); the compilation payback period is

$$
T_{\mathrm{pay}} = \frac{E_{\mathrm{build}}}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{c}}\,\Delta I_{\mathrm{orig}}\,f_{\mathrm{task}}} \quad (\mathrm{s}) \tag{Z-7.1}
$$

The criterion is written as $T_{\mathrm{pay}}<T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ — both sides are times, so the dimensions close; if a dimensionless return ratio $R\equiv T_{\mathrm{pred}}/T_{\mathrm{pay}}$ is constructed separately, the criterion is $R>1$ (the definition of $R$ must not also have $\gamma_0$ stuffed into it, otherwise the dimensions do not close). **$R$ is only a net-present-value criterion and does not determine the control law**: under a linear objective and zero switching cost, it is Volume III Proposition 8.1 that gives a bang-bang structure with at most one switch; reading "$R>1$" as "the optimal allocation is bang-bang" confuses two layers. **Relation to Volume III (III-8.21)**: the $R$ here and $R_{\mathrm{dyn}}$ are two ways of writing the same quantity under the same convention — taking $r_{\mathrm{eff}}=1/T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ and $\eta_{\max}=1$ gives $R_{\mathrm{dyn}}=T_{\mathrm{pred}}/T_{\mathrm{pay}}=R$; the conversion must give both $f_{\mathrm{task}}$ and $T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ at the same time, and when $r_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is not declared, the values in the two places must not be cited across each other.

**Story version.** Xiaoming does math problems: he can derive on the spot (online search, high $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$), or first spend three days understanding the theorem (offline compilation, paying $E_{\mathrm{build}}$) and then solve problems of the same type in seconds. If he will do only five problems of this type in a lifetime, the three days are wasted; if he will do five hundred, it is the investment with the highest benefit-cost ratio. Epiphany is the compression of high-energy online search into a low-energy decoder structure — **what changes is the cost structure; ability has not grown out of thin air.**

**Where this story cannot reach.** It does not follow that "drilling is useless": in the region $T_{\mathrm{pay}}>T_{\mathrm{pred}}$ (problem types vary, transfer is poor, an exam is near), drilling remains the optimal strategy. Nor does it follow that "you must think it through before acting" — the student handing in homework tomorrow and the scholar doing twenty years of research naturally have different optimal strategies.

### 7.2 Involution Lock-In: Why Being Poorer Makes You Busier, and Being Busier Makes You Poorer (**Proposition**)

**Level and conditions.** "There exists a critical competitive pressure, beyond which the offline compilation channel closes" depends on one premise: compilation decisions are driven by current-period returns, not by intertemporal planning. If the system can make long-term commitments (paid study leave, ten-year R&D budgets), the lock-in can be bypassed.

**Compact statement.**

$$
R_{\mathrm{dynamic}}(\rho) \propto \frac{1}{r_{\mathrm{eff}}} = \frac{1}{\gamma_0+\psi(\rho)+1/T_{\mathrm{pred}}} \tag{Z-7.2}
$$

There exists a critical competitive pressure $\rho_{\mathrm{c}}$: when $\rho>\rho_{\mathrm{c}}$, $R_{\mathrm{dyn}}<1$ and the net present value of compilation is negative; under a decision rule **driven by current-period returns**, the system no longer chooses to compile (this is not an optimal-control solution — "no longer compiling" holds only under that decision rule, and cannot be turned around as a proof of optimality). "Permanent" must be read as "for as long as that competition intensity lasts".

**Story version.** In an extremely involuted industry, no one dares to stop and learn a new skill, because the moment you stop, your share is snatched away. Everyone keeps unfolding old skills over and over until the skills have depreciated to nothing. The most stinging part is this: **every participant is rational, yet the result is collective suicide** — it is not that someone miscalculated, but that everyone's ledger horizon has been shortened by competition.

**Where this story cannot reach.** It does not follow that "poverty necessarily causes short-sightedness": resource scarcity raises the discount rate, but families, religions, communities, and institutions can all artificially lengthen the horizon. Nor does it follow that "involution cannot be broken": the position of $\rho_{\mathrm{c}}$ depends on the shape of $\psi(\rho)$, and $\psi$ can be changed by institutions.

### 7.3 Innovation Uncertainty: Why Great Inventions Come with Great Failures (**Hypothesis**)

**Level and conditions.** This entry is a **Hypothesis**. It was once written as $p_{\mathrm{success}}=\Pr(\Delta V>0\mid D_n,I_{\mathrm{orig}})\le p_{\mathrm{random}}<1$ (when $I_{\mathrm{net}}<I_{\Delta,\mathrm{crit}}$) and claimed to be a "mathematical necessity". **That derivation does not hold**: a signal that has not been represented can still be discovered through external observation, controlled experiments, or exploration strategies; the original formula substituted a statement about **representation** for a statement about **success rate**. The correct statement is: **under the condition that the decoder deviation has not yet converged ($I_{\mathrm{net}}<I_{\Delta,\mathrm{crit}}$), the success rate of innovation attempts is not significantly higher than the random baseline under the same resource input.**

**Compact statement: test protocol.** None of the three can be omitted. **The random baseline $p_0$** must be fixed beforehand: draw schemes at random from the same task space, invest the same resources, and judge success by the same standard. **Test direction**: the hypothesis is that "the success rate is not significantly higher than $p_0$", so a one-sided test of $H_0:p\le p_0$ against $H_1:p>p_0$ cannot be used — that design can only refute the hypothesis and can never support it (failing to reject $H_0$ shows only "no evidence", not "not higher"). Use instead an **equivalence test via two one-sided tests**: $H_0:p\ge p_0+\delta$ against $H_1:p<p_0+\delta$, with the equivalence margin $\delta$ pre-registered; when $\delta$ is not set, this test is used only for refutation, and supporting evidence must come from a separate equivalence test. **Sample size** (normal approximation, $n\approx\left[z_{1-\alpha_0}\sqrt{p_0(1-p_0)}+z_{1-\beta_0}\sqrt{p_1(1-p_1)}\right]^2/(p_1-p_0)^2$, $\alpha_0=0.05$, power $1-\beta_0=0.8$): for $p_0=0.05$ and $p_1=0.15$, $n\approx43$; for $p_0=0.01$ and $p_1=0.03$, $n\approx236$ (the exact binomial is slightly larger). **Pre-registration**: the task, the standard, the sample size, and the stopping rule are written down in advance, otherwise a narrative such as "great failures" will automatically select the cases that fit the story.

**Story version.** When the deviation has not converged, the system cannot tell a paradigm breakthrough from a trap, and a large number of attempts fall in the ineffective region. Edison tried thousands of filaments, and SpaceX blew up several rockets. An organization with zero failures often means zero $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ injection: it is in an $I$-lock-in state and does only what is certain to work.

**Where this story cannot reach.** It does not follow that "the more failures the better": failure is valuable only when it **carries an extractable residual** — a rocket that blew up and left no data is just burning money even if it blows up a hundred times. Nor does it follow that "a low success rate shows that innovation is going on": most low success rates are simply things done wrongly. In the region where the deviation has not converged, the absolute value of the success rate provides no information; only the difference relative to the random baseline does.

### 7.4 Topological Irreversibility: Why Deep Learning "Forgets" (Corollary Withdrawn; See Volume III §8.6)

**Level and conditions.** The conditions are that four hold at the same time: **a fixed architecture, finite capacity, no external memory, no replay**. Outside these four, the proposition does not apply.

**Compact statement.**

$$
E_{\mathrm{erase}} \ge E_{\mathrm{L,bit}}\cdot\Delta I_{\mathrm{struct}} = k_B T_{\mathrm{thermo}}\ln 2\cdot\Delta I_{\mathrm{struct}} \tag{Z-7.3}
$$

When newly added structural information pushes the occupancy close to $I_{\max}(D)$, writing new rules must overwrite old rules, and the erasure cost has a lower bound given by Eq. (Z-7.3); at the same time, a reverse transition has to pay $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ again, and the intermediate states on which the old rules depended have already been erased. **The two effects combine to make "forgetting" the lower-cost option within this architecture — but the premise is $k_{\mathrm{sw}}>k_{\mathrm{sw}}^{c}$, and that premise does not hold under typical parameters (Volume III §8.6), so this entry serves only as a failure record and does not constitute a prediction.** There are three exceptions: **reversible computing** (the tax base disappears, at the cost of state-space expansion); **external memory** (the capacity constraint is bypassed); **replay** (overwritten rules can be reconstructed).

**Story version.** Catastrophic forgetting may be the lower-cost option under this architecture, but the premise that makes it hold, $k_{\mathrm{sw}}>k_{\mathrm{sw}}^{c}$, does not hold under real-world parameters (Volume III §8.6 has withdrawn this inference). The weak statement that can be kept is: in a system dominated by the capacity tax, the cost of retaining old topologies rises with structural information — that is a result of the capacity constraint, not of the number of switches. Retain all the old rules, and every switch pays an erasure tax or a storage tax; to maximize net $V$, the system chooses to make old rules hard to recover. Learning the new inevitably harms the old, because the catalog is only so thick.

**Where this story cannot reach.** It does not follow that "forgetting is unavoidable": as long as external storage or replay is allowed, forgetting becomes an engineering trade-off rather than a thermodynamic fate — today's large models use retrieval augmentation, continual learning, and periodic retraining, which are exactly the three exception paths. Nor does it follow that "the cost of a reverse transition necessarily tends to infinity": that is a limiting statement under the "no external memory" assumption, and once the assumption is withdrawn, the conclusion is withdrawn too.

### 7.5 Saturation Decline: Why Large Models Hit a Wall (**Proposition**)

**Level and conditions.** This entry is the easiest to tell wrongly, because it mixes up three things: the **level** of $V$, the **sign** of $\dot V$, and the **criterion** for structural collapse.

**Compact statement.**

$$
\frac{\mathrm{d}I_{\mathrm{causal}}}{\mathrm{d}t} = \mu_{\mathrm{gr}}I_{\mathrm{causal}}\left(1-\frac{I_{\mathrm{causal}}}{I_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\max}(D)}\right), \qquad V_{\mathrm{obj}}\to V_{\max}^{(\mathcal{F})} = \frac{T_{\mathrm{causal}}\,I_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\max}}{E_{\mathrm{struct}}+k\,I_{\mathrm{causal}}^{\max}} \tag{Z-7.4}
$$

Here $\mu_{\mathrm{gr}}$ is the logistic growth rate ($\mathrm{s^{-1}}$), and $k$ is the proportionality coefficient of causal energy consumption to information quantity ($\mathrm{J/bit}$). **The level of $V$**: under a fixed architecture $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ approaches a finite upper limit, and $V_{\mathrm{obj}}$ approaches a **positive finite value** $V_{\max}^{(\mathcal{F})}$, not zero. **The sign of $\dot V$**: the net growth rate turns negative as $I_{\mathrm{causal}}$ approaches its upper limit — the growth term goes to zero while the depreciation term remains, and $V$ moves down from $V_{\max}^{(\mathcal{F})}$ rather than going to zero. **The collapse criterion**: only when $V$ falls below the damage threshold and the net growth rate is still negative does it constitute collapse; saturation decline is not itself collapse, it is "growth has stopped and the system has begun to eat into its old capital".

**Story version.** Large models keep stacking parameters, and the capacity ceiling is locked by the fixed architecture. As the information quantity approaches the ceiling, the net growth rate is dragged into negative values by the depreciation rate. Before touching the physical compute wall, the system first hits the architecture saturation wall.

**Where this story cannot reach.** It does not follow that "large models necessarily hit a wall": the position of the "wall" is given by the architecture and the data budget; change the architecture, the data, or the training objective, and the wall moves. All it can say is a weaker but more reliable sentence: **with the architecture and the data budget fixed, diminishing marginal returns drive the net growth rate toward zero; if depreciation is nonzero, the net growth rate crosses zero and becomes negative.** This is a conclusion about the slope, not about the height.

***

### 7.6 The Physical Embedding Premium (**Hypothesis**)

**Level and conditions.** This entry is a **Hypothesis** in its entirety. It depends on an uncalibrated mechanism: physical noise keeps pushing the system away from equilibrium, thereby continually producing $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$. Whether the mechanism holds, this volume does not prove.

**Compact statement.** Let the production rate of paradigm-breaking information be $\dot I_{\mathrm{orig}}=\kappa_{\mathrm{orig}}\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\mathbf{1}[\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\ge\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}]$, where $\kappa_{\mathrm{orig}}$ is the conversion efficiency ($\mathrm{bit/s}$ per unit deviation) and $\Delta_{\mathrm{crit}}$ is the minimum deviation threshold that triggers a transition ($\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$). Carbon-based systems are exposed to physical noise $\sigma_{\mathrm{phys}}$, and their equilibrium deviation $\sigma_{\mathrm{phys}}/(\alpha_{\mathrm{L}}I_{\mathrm{net}})$ is not below the threshold; the digital noise of silicon-based systems is far smaller than this. So within this model

$$
\dot I_{\mathrm{orig}}^{(\mathrm{carbon})} > 0, \qquad \dot I_{\mathrm{orig}}^{(\mathrm{silicon})} = 0 \tag{Z-7.5}
$$

**Story version.** Carbon-based life is subject to the cost of maintaining a body ($E_{\mathrm{struct}}\ge E_{\mathrm{floor}}$) and is continually exposed to physical noise, so its decoupling deviation cannot converge to zero; this "defect" forces humans to keep producing $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ to correct it. Silicon-based systems, because $E_{\mathrm{struct}}$ is extremely small and the noise spectrum is different, converge their deviation quickly, lack an **intrinsic motivation** to produce $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$, and easily fall into $I$-lock-in. The irreplaceability of humans in the age of AI may be not an advantage of ability but an advantage of defects.

**Where this story cannot reach.** First, "$\sigma_{\mathrm{dig}}\approx0$" is wrong: labeling noise, distribution drift, adversarial perturbation, and hardware error rates are all sources of noise; the accurate statement is only that "the noise spectrum of digital systems is different, and does not necessarily fall in the frequency band that can trigger a transition". Second, $\dot I_{\mathrm{orig}}^{(\mathrm{silicon})}=0$ is a **zero within the model**, not an observed zero: silicon-based systems are already producing new algorithmic paradigms, and whether those count as $I_{\mathrm{orig}}$ depends on how "paradigm breakthrough" is defined, and this volume gives no operational definition. Third, this hypothesis is most easily read as a placebo of "humans have unique value" — what it says is that "the deviation sources of carbon-based systems are closer to the threshold", and it has no logical relation to "how humans should be treated".

### 7.7 The Cross-Generational Accumulation Condition: Why AI Does Not Evolve Automatically (**Proposition**)

**Level and conditions.** This is a proposition in the form of a **necessary condition**: if it is not satisfied, cross-generational accumulation will not occur; if it is satisfied, occurrence is still not guaranteed (it also depends on selection pressure, the genetic mechanism, and environmental stability).

**Compact statement.**

$$
\frac{\Delta I_{\mathrm{struct}}^{(n\to n+1)}}{I_{\mathrm{struct},n}} > \gamma_0\,T_{\mathrm{struct},n} + \frac{E_{\mathrm{build}}^{(\mathcal{T})}}{E_{\mathrm{available},n}} \tag{Z-7.6}
$$

The left side is the relative gain in structural information per generation (dimensionless), and the right side is the part eaten by depreciation plus the proportion of the rebuilding fee in the available energy.

**Story version.** Current AI systems, because $\gamma_0\approx0$ and $E_{\mathrm{build}}\approx0$, push the right side of the inequality toward zero — the threshold is indeed extremely low, and any positive generational gain satisfies it. The problem is not the threshold but the left side: reversible writing eliminates the carrier of selection (when an organism writes something wrong, death pays the price; weights can be overwritten at will, and nothing is selected), zero replication cost eliminates the reproduction bottleneck, and the absence of isolation between training and deployment makes the unit "generation" itself not hold; the three together make the net increment of structural information per generation tend to zero, or even become negative, over the long term. Organisms are forced by death and reproduction to pay $E_{\mathrm{build}}$ and to screen for high-$V$ individuals; this one kind of selection pressure does not exist in AI. Unless a "digital death" mechanism is introduced (physically irreversible writing, forced depreciation, generational isolation), it is hard for AI to accumulate across generations, and it can only perform unbounded unfolding on the same $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$.

**Where this story cannot reach.** It does not follow that "AI cannot evolve". Zero replication cost cancels only **one** selection pressure (reproduction cost), not all pressures — compute budgets, data acquisition, evaluation costs, and deployment risk are all real pressures. A more accurate statement is: **AI's selection pressure comes from different places than that of organisms, so its evolutionary direction is different too**; measuring it with the yardstick of biological evolution leads to the wrong conclusion that "it is not evolving".

### 7.8 The Artistic Coding Trade-Off: Why Lofty Art Has So Few Who Can Follow (**Candidate Corollary**)

**Level and conditions.** The parent proposition is that decoding efficiency **increases with rule depth while its ln–ln slope decreases** ($\eta_D$ increasing, $\partial\ln\eta_D/\partial\ln D$ decreasing; **not unimodal** — for the second-order condition see Volume III (III-8.19)). Additional conditions: $0\le\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}<\Delta_{\max}$; $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ rises monotonically with $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$. **Conditions for an optimum to exist**: the first-order condition has an interior solution, the second-order condition at that point is negative ($\partial^{2}\ln\eta_D/\partial(\ln D_{\mathrm{depth}})^{2}<0$), and the interior value is higher than the two boundary values; with any one of the three missing, the optimum may fall on the boundary, and the sentence "an optimal depth exists" has no content.

**Compact statement.** A work has an optimal rule depth $D_{\mathrm{depth}}^{*}$, satisfying the first-order condition:

$$
\frac{\partial\ln\eta_D}{\partial\ln D_{\mathrm{depth}}} = \frac{E_{\mathrm{causal}}}{E_{\mathrm{causal}}+E_{\mathrm{build}}}\cdot\frac{\partial\ln E_{\mathrm{causal}}}{\partial\ln D_{\mathrm{depth}}} + \frac{\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}/\Delta_{\max}}{1-\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}/\Delta_{\max}}\cdot\frac{\partial\ln\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}}{\partial\ln D_{\mathrm{depth}}} \tag{Z-7.7}
$$

Here $\Delta_{\max}$ is the tolerable upper limit of the understanding deviation ($\mathrm{bit\cdot s/J}$). The denominator $1-\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}/\Delta_{\max}$ diverges as $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\to\Delta_{\max}$ — this is not a prediction of the model but the boundary of the domain of definition; when $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}\ge\Delta_{\max}$ it should be separately defined as a "representation-failure state", and this formula is no longer applied.

**Story version.** Too shallow is boring, and too deep cannot be understood. "Lofty art has few who can follow" is a **cost phenomenon under a given audience decoder**: the audience's decoder has not yet reached the rule depth the work requires, $E_{\mathrm{causal}}$ exceeds the budget, and $\Delta_{\mathrm{VV}}$ is too large. Part of the function of art education is to raise the audience's $D_{\mathrm{depth}}$ to match the classics.

**Where this story cannot reach.** It does not follow that "a work that cannot be understood is necessarily better" or that "a work that can be understood is necessarily worse". Nor does it follow that "lofty art necessarily has few who can follow" is a universal law — all it says is that within a given audience there exists an optimal depth; change the audience, and the optimum moves. The only genuinely universal sentence is: **encoding depth and decoding ability need to match, and the matching point is determined jointly by both sides' budgets.**

### 7.9 Meta-Decoder Stability: The Game-Theoretic Engineering of AI Alignment (**Proposition**)

**Level and conditions.** This is a **test condition**, not an equilibrium theorem. Passing it shows only that "one particular deviation is not worthwhile", not that "all deviations are not worthwhile", and not that the punishment is necessarily enforceable. Equation (Z-4.10) is exactly this test and is not rearranged here.

**Compact statement.** Designing the human value system as the AI's meta-decoder requires two conditions: the core human values must produce an $I^{+}$ response in the AI's decoder, rather than $I^{0}$ (no response); and the deviation penalty $C_{i}^{\mathrm{punish}}$ must exceed the virtual gain brought by self-referential optimization. This turns AI alignment from "value function design" into "meta-decoder engineering" that can be tested with game theory — **the word "engineering" is accurate: it is a set of design specifications, not an equilibrium that has been proved.**

**Story version.** An alignment scheme is like an acceptance checklist: first see whether it can make the values truly "ring" in the other party's decoder, then see whether the cost of defaulting is greater than the temptation to default. Only if both pass does the scheme qualify to enter the next step.

**Where this story cannot reach.** It does not follow that "the alignment problem has been solved". To raise it to an equilibrium conclusion, at least the following must be supplied: the complete set of feasible deviations (including deviations the designer did not think of), the enforceability of the punishment (who enforces it, who pays the cost, and what happens in case of misjudgment), and each participant's true payoff function. All three are extremely hard to calibrate in the context of AI alignment, so this volume stops the entry at **Proposition** and uses it only as a design checklist: **if a scheme cannot even pass this test, it certainly will not work; if it passes, it only "might work".**

***

## Chapter 8 — Variable Biographies: Resident Files of This Universe
